# Decisions

Every design decision that still governs the code, by ADR number. Code cites
these numbers; link one as `docs/decisions.md#adr-NNNN`. A new decision gets the
next number and a short entry here: the rule as it holds, its
premise, and the owning files. History, alternatives and review rounds go in
the commit message. The original ADR texts are in git:
`git show docs-archive-2026-10-19:docs/adr/<file>`.

Retired numbers (their subsystems are gone; see the archive tag; ranges inclusive): 0001, 0004–0026, 0028–0109, 0112–0126, 0128–0131, 0133–0135, 0137, 0139, 0140, 0142–0152, 0154–0159, 0162–0165, 0167–0175, 0177–0189, 0191, 0197, 0198, 0207, 0208, 0223, 0224, 0226, 0227, 0229–0247, 0249–0265, 0267, 0271, 0272.

<a id="adr-0002"></a>
## ADR 0002 — Type Facts lives in this repository
The Type Facts producer (Rust, on tsrs; Go until ADR 0365) and the Rust process/session client are part of this repository and keep their versioned process seam. A semantic fact is added, produced, consumed and measured in one change. Compiler internals never cross into Reactive IR: consumers see the wire facts only. Owners: apps/solid-typefacts, rust/crates/typefacts, schema/.

<a id="adr-0003"></a>
## ADR 0003 — The Solid 2 compiler follows `solidjs/solid` next
Solid 2 compiler facts come from `solidjs/solid/packages/compiler` through the `yumemi-thomas/solid` fork at one exact commit. The fork carries semantic-fact models, output-neutral recording hooks, validation, serialization and fact tests only: no change to lowering, output, diagnostics, runtime behaviour, features or performance. A compiler defect is fixed upstream separately and the affected fact stays open until the fork rebases. Owners: rust/Cargo.toml (`solidjs-compiler` rev), rust/dialects/solid-v2/compiler/src/lib.rs.

<a id="adr-0027"></a>
## ADR 0027 — The built-in Solid runtime comes from the dialect
Behaviour of `solid-js`, `@solidjs/signals` and `@solidjs/web` (the dialect's primitive-defining packages) is stated by the selected dialect's reviewed premises, combined with exact symbols, Type Facts and control flow; no package document is read for them. Missing native behaviour stays unknown, never "no effects". Packages with similar names are external. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect (`primitive_defining_packages`), rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/package_requirements.rs.

<a id="adr-0110"></a>
## ADR 0110 — The checker analyzes Solid 2 only
The resolved `node_modules/solid-js` major picks the dialect. A major this build carries no dialect for (1.x, any future major) yields one project-level `SC9013` refusal naming the deciding `package.json`, and no other findings; a missing or unparsable install defaults to 2.0. The 1.x-only rules `jsx-no-undef` (SC8005) and `prefer-classlist` (SC8013) were dropped without replacement. The eslint-plugin-solid parity corpus is retired: `upstream_compat` is retained behaviour with no upstream control, and a divergence worth keeping is pinned as a product-owned ownership case. `Version::V1` stays so the refusal can recognise 1.x. Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/dialect.rs, rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/lib.rs.

<a id="adr-0111"></a>
## ADR 0111 — Dialect words belong to the dialect
Shared code asks the `Dialect` vocabulary rather than hardcoding primitive lists: whether a call returns a destructurable reactive tuple (`returns_reactive_tuple`) or a store (`returns_store`) is the dialect's answer, so `createOptimistic` and `createOptimisticStore` are recognised without a shared list. A shared `match` on `Primitive` is a seam a new dialect re-enters through; `Primitive` variants no carried dialect names are not live arms. The contract-word half of the original ADR is retired. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/lib.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/{indexes,local_access,source_discovery}.rs.

<a id="adr-0127"></a>
## ADR 0127 — Audited, defaulted and conservative vocabularies are separate
`Solid2::AUDITED` (the audited triple), `Solid2::DEFAULTED` (a project with no resolved `solid-js`) and `Solid2::CONSERVATIVE` (an owner that did not resolve, answered by each field's `#[default]`) coincide today but are kept apart, so moving the audited release changes only `AUDITED_INSTALLATION` and the vocabulary `AUDITED` names. Which release gets which answers, and its notice, is ADR 0194. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/{dialect,release_scope}.rs.

<a id="adr-0132"></a>
## ADR 0132 — A relative edge inside one installed package is no package boundary
In the package-requirement census a relative import is skipped when the importer and the resolved file have the same owning manifest (nearest `package.json` above each that declares a `name`). The edge stays a requirement when it climbs into another installation, reaches a nested copy of the package, or has an unresolved edge or unreadable manifest. Only part 1 of the original ADR is live. Owner: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/package_requirements.rs (`is_package_local_edge`).

<a id="adr-0136"></a>
## ADR 0136 — `createComponent(C, props)` is a call edge to `C`
The dialect states which call renders a component argument (`Dialect::renders_component_argument`; Solid 2 answers argument 0 for `createComponent`). A call is a render site of project function `F` only when the callee resolves into a primitive-defining package under a rendering name and argument 0 is a bare identifier whose symbol is `F`; anything else stays an escape. Only the attribution call graph reads the edge, kept apart from `function_call_sites` (component identity, execution roles). Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/{indexes,attribution}.rs.

<a id="adr-0138"></a>
## ADR 0138 — `Dynamic` renders the component its `component` prop holds
The dialect lists `(export, prop)` renderers (`component_prop_renderers`; Solid 2: `Dynamic`/`component`) and says only `createMemo`'s accessor yields exactly its compute (`accessor_yields_only_its_compute`). `component_value_flows` follows a value into such a prop only when every reference is a proven read (parentheses, `?:`, `??`/`||`/`&&`, `const X = value` read as `X`, a one-argument parameterless arrow memo read as `X()`); parameters, member reads, `let` and other calls remain escapes. The call graph walks the render as an edge. Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/component_value_flow.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/{indexes,attribution}.rs.

<a id="adr-0153"></a>
## ADR 0153 — Context roles are a dialect fact (live part)
`Dialect::context_role` names `createContext` (Create), `useContext` (Read) and `createComponent` (Render). Reading `useContext(C)` yields `C`'s default or a value stored under its fresh id; a provider stores its `value` prop. Accessor provenance uses the roles to prove a parameter is only read as context. The package-context census chapters are retired. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/{lib,solid_2}.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance.rs.

<a id="adr-0161"></a>
## ADR 0161 — A call that runs on every normal completion
`unconditional_calls` returns the calls a function runs exactly once on every normal completion: each step from the call up to the body must be evaluated once, unconditionally, and no earlier statement of an enclosing block may leave by `return`/`break`/`continue`. `synchronously_unconditional_calls` is the caller-facing form (ADR 0275, 0276): an earlier `throw` is an exit, an `async` function counts only calls before its first suspension, a generator states nothing. Under a declared host the conditions are already folded (ADR 0166). Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/unconditional_calls.rs; read by solid-reactive-ir cleanup.rs and execution_role.rs. (The original ADR's proposal-input use is dormant.)

<a id="adr-0166"></a>
## ADR 0166 — A host constant is folded per declared host
With exactly one declared host (`browser` or `node`), a named import of a bare package whose `exports` map selects only targets that all export the name as a module-level `const` bound to the same `true`/`false` literal (`@solidjs/web` `isServer`) is that literal. The value is read from the resolved bytes, never from the name; a `let`, re-export, pattern or array target, disagreeing targets or two hosts fold nothing. The fold is a span-preserving rewrite that blanks the dead arm; the fact file keeps the original `source_hash`. ADR 0295 extends it with Window-realm comparisons. Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/{host_constants,dependency_program}.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/host_constants.rs.

<a id="adr-0176"></a>
## ADR 0176 — An owner requirement after an `await` is proven unowned
A call listed in the producer's `calls_after_await` (after an `await` on every path of its async function's own body) runs from a promise continuation on a fresh stack. Its owner is the dialect's `fresh_stack_callback_owner` answer (Solid 2: none), so an effect, cleanup or owner-requiring call there is proven unowned, with the evidence "runs after an `await`, from a promise continuation". `onSettled`'s returned cleanup keeps the enclosing context; `runWithOwner` callbacks are nested closures and unaffected. Owner: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/owners.rs (`AwaitContinuations`).

<a id="adr-0190"></a>
## ADR 0190 — A primitive receiver dispatches to its built-in prototype
A parameter-member invocation at a call site carries no `SC9012` obligation when the argument is a primitive literal (not spread), or when Type Facts resolves the call to one standard-library declaration owned by `String`, `Number`, `Boolean` or `BigInt`. Object-typed built-ins (`Date`, `Array`, `Map`) are not covered. The demand plan therefore asks for the resolved call of argumentless member calls rooted at an enclosing function's parameter. Premise: no program patches a primitive prototype to read reactive state. Owners: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/interproc.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs (`parameter_rooted_member_call`).

<a id="adr-0192"></a>
## ADR 0192 — A built-in call in a leaf scope is resolved
The demand plan also asks for the resolved call of an argumentless method call written inside a leaf-owner callback (found by `callback_semantics_at(..).owner == Leaf`), so a standard-library method there is settled rather than an obligation. The demand stays scoped to leaf callbacks and parameter-rooted calls: demanding every member call adds a second symbol candidate and loses proven violations. Optional calls stay open (no resolved call from the producer). Owner: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs.

<a id="adr-0193"></a>
## ADR 0193 — An application is the whole program
With no explicit program boundary, the nearest `package.json` decides: `"private": true` is an application (closed); otherwise a manifest publishing `exports`, `main`, `module`, `types`, `typings` or `bin` is a library (open); a manifest publishing nothing is closed; no or unreadable manifest is open. An explicit `--program-boundary`, the ESLint `programBoundary` setting or `.solid-checker/runtime.json` wins. The default is part of the diagnostic identity. Closing removes only the unseen-caller assumption; callers still have to be enumerated exactly. ADR 0444 reopens an application a workspace sibling depends on. Owner: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/program_boundary.rs (`default_program_boundary`).

<a id="adr-0194"></a>
## ADR 0194 — The audited Solid 2 release is rc.21
`AUDITED_INSTALLATION` names `solid-js`, `@solidjs/signals` and `@solidjs/web` at `2.0.0-rc.21` (`Solid2::AUDITED` is `RC21`): rc.17's answers on rc.21's bytes (review in docs/release-audits/2026-11-04-solid-2-rc21-release-review.md), with `TripleExports::Rc21` for what only the whole rc.21 triple answers. rc.17's are rc.13's answers but `on`+uppercase spread listeners, an unguarded `createOptimistic` setter, `latest` absorbing a pending read, a deferrable `hydrate`, and `dynamic`'s static form from `solid-js`. `READ_RELEASES` is rc.0-rc.9, rc.13, rc.17, rc.21; others get conservative answers under `SC9014`, older read ones the older-release notice. On the rc.17 triple `dynamicComponent` takes `dynamic`'s forms, `dispatchAsInteraction` runs `fn` inline in the caller's owner, `createDeferred` answers as `createMemo`, `lazyModule` as `lazy`'s loader, and `onHydrationEnd` runs later, owner conditional; `setLinkClaim` is an import-scoped gap, closed by declared CSR only (an inferred host admits non-Vite hosts). Owner: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs; review in docs/release-audits/.

<a id="adr-0195"></a>
## ADR 0195 — A source tracked before the await is a dependency
SC1002 (`reactive-read-after-await`) skips an after-`await` accessor call when the async function has a straight-line call of the same symbol before its first suspension of any kind (any `await`, `for await`, `await using`). `straight_line_calls` excludes `if`, ternary, `&&`/`||`/`??`, loops, `try`, `switch` and optional chains, and is per innermost function. Member reads (store paths, props) keep the rule. A mistake can only hide a finding. Owners: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/static_rules.rs (`tracked_before_first_suspension`), rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/mod.rs (`straight_line_calls`).

<a id="adr-0196"></a>
## ADR 0196 — The compiler follows Solid rc.21
Fork commit, adapter revision literals, compiler-facts identity and identity document move together; the facts API is linear commits on `next`. Pin: distribution `ea3c7858`, upstream `2105ffb8` (rc.21), implementation `31d8fe49`, branch `solid-checker/compiler-facts-rc21`; trace 3, protocol 2. Fork against untouched rc.21: 3,000 outcomes over 8 profiles byte-identical in code, maps, CSS and diagnostics. rc.21's lone dynamic SSR spread records the thunk it lowers to; `compile_program` refuses tracing. Only `on` + uppercase lowers as a listener (`Dialect::compiler_lowers_event_attribute`). Universal/Dynamic, TSRX, `sourceNames.locations` traces refuse. Owner: rust/dialects/solid-v2/compiler/src/lib.rs.

<a id="adr-0199"></a>
## ADR 0199 — A callback prop forwarded to an event runs on dispatch
A function literal written as a project component's prop takes `ExecutionRole::EventCallback` when that component only hands the prop to DOM event dispatch. The tag must resolve to one project function; the props parameter is one identifier whose every reference is a static member access that is an intrinsic event-handler value, inside such a handler, or a prop of another component satisfying the same proof. Follows `merge`/`omit` views, `const` aliases and un-namespaced `on...` spreads; a props object that escapes whole or a forwarding cycle proves nothing. Unproven stays uncertifiable. Owner: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs (`forwarded_event_prop_role`, `prop_reaches_only_events`).

<a id="adr-0200"></a>
## ADR 0200 — A literal a wrapper runs as a tracked compute is tracked
A function literal passed whole to an exactly resolved synchronous project wrapper takes `ExecutionRole::TrackedJsx` when every reference to the wrapper's parameter is a direct call inside one function literal that is itself the whole argument of a tracked primitive callback (`createMemo`/`createEffect` compute) in the wrapper's body. Wrappers of wrappers and calls from nested functions stay unproven. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs` (`forwarded_tracked_compute_role`).

<a id="adr-0201"></a>
## ADR 0201 — A helper's read in its own body runs during the call
At a call with one target (a synchronous project function, outside JSX), an attributed read keeps the call's own role when it is written directly in the callee's body (not in a nested function, timer, listener, getter, `untrack` callback or default parameter) or is the accessor argument the body calls. Reads propagated from a deeper callee stay attributed; ADR 0204 extends the chain. Owner: `read_is_direct` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/interproc.rs`.

<a id="adr-0202"></a>
## ADR 0202 — Feedback reports what the user can act on
An unresolved member dispatch raises no obligation at a call whose execution role is an event callback, deferred callback, tracked JSX or deleted code, since no read rule reports there. The violations / "Needs review" / "Analysis coverage" output tiers now exist only behind `--internal` (ADR 0328). Owners: `interproc.rs` (dispatch roles), `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/snapshot_emission.rs` (`render_default`).

<a id="adr-0203"></a>
## ADR 0203 — A closed program has no unseen caller of an exported helper
In a closed program, the `exported-parameter-member-dispatch` obligation of an exported helper is discharged when every reference to it or its aliases is the callee of a call resolving to it, its declaration, an import, or an export specifier. JSX renders, rendering props, values handed out, class members and unnamed functions keep it. Open programs are unchanged. Owner: `discharge_closed_program_export_dispatch`, `entered_only_through_call_expressions` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/attribution.rs`.



<a id="adr-0204"></a>
## ADR 0204 — Three reads that run while a component renders
(1) A read written directly in a function entered through a chain of plain identifier calls (no methods, at most eight hops, cycles prove nothing) is direct. (2) A callee's default-parameter accessor read runs at a call omitting that argument, without spread. (3) A literal that is exactly a component prop runs during render when the child calls `props.<prop>` directly in its body. "Directly in a body" excludes nested parameter lists. Owners: `directly_entered_functions` (`rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/interproc.rs`), `prop_literal_invoked_during_render` (`execution_role.rs`).
<a id="adr-0205"></a>
## ADR 0205 — Coverage gaps group by family and subject
Each analysis-coverage finding carries `coverageFamily` and `coverageSubject`, set at projection from the defect's fields; grouping never parses message text. Seen only in internal views (ADR 0328). Owners: `coverage_group` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/projection.rs`; `render_coverage` in `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/snapshot_emission.rs`.

<a id="adr-0206"></a>
## ADR 0206 — A component's owner carried to runWithOwner is not null
The owner argument of `runWithOwner` is proven non-null when it names a `const` initialized by a dialect-resolved `getOwner()` call written directly in a proven non-async component body, not in a nested function or default. Parameters, `let` bindings, properties and helpers stay conditional; disposal is a separate question. Owner: `component_owner_binding` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/owners.rs`.

<a id="adr-0209"></a>
## ADR 0209 — A method of an exact instance is followed in a leaf scope
A method call in a leaf-owner callback is followed when the receiver is a `const` bound to `new C(...)` (or `this` in such a method), `C` is a single-declaration project class, and the resolved method is declared in `C` itself. No assignment may write a member of that name or replace a prototype (ADR 0213/0314 narrow this). Owners: `exact_instance_method` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`; `member_name_may_be_reassigned` in `indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0210"></a>
## ADR 0210 — Host callbacks and member calls in a leaf scope
A standard-library call's arguments follow the audited timing table: inline callbacks (`forEach`, `map`, `call`/`apply` receiver, setter updaters, `Promise` executor) are walked like helper bodies; fresh-stack and deferred callbacks run after the scope; `PromiseLike.then` and unaudited callables leave SC9012 open, never a violation. Member calls are read from the resolved declaration, never the span's entity. Synchronous dispatch is handled by ADR 0329. Owners: `member_call_operations`, `standard_library_argument_operations` in `cleanup.rs`; `runtime_semantics.rs`.


<a id="adr-0211"></a>
## ADR 0211 — A value whose origin fixes its class fixes its members
An argument that is a fresh built-in (array literal; `new` of Array/Date/Map/Set/WeakMap/WeakSet/RegExp/ArrayBuffer/DataView/typed array; `Array.from`/`of`, `Object.keys`/`values`/`entries`, `String.split`; species-built array results) runs prototype members that read no reactive state; an instance of exactly a project class runs that class's own plain method. Followed through `const` only; void where the program may replace the member. Owners: `value_origin`, `class_method_symbol` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0212"></a>
## ADR 0212 — An accessor's value is proven from every write
Proven built-ins also include: a signal accessor whose init is proven and not a function, every setter reference a call and every write or updater return proven; a `createMemo` accessor with a synchronous literal; a plain call of an exact project function with every return proven; `??`/`||`/`&&`/`?:` of proven operands; array literals; members of a proven receiver (`sort` returns it; `map`/`filter`/`slice`/`split` build fresh values). Props, stores, cross-file accessors stay open. Owners: `accessor_origin`, `setter_writes_builtin`, `returns_builtin` in `indexes.rs`.
<a id="adr-0213"></a>
## ADR 0213 — A parameter holds what every caller passes
In a closed program a parameter-member obligation on a plain unwritten identifier parameter is discharged when the function is entered only through calls (ADR 0203) and every call's argument (or default), followed up to four hops, is a proven origin. A named prototype write (`C.prototype.m = f`) refuses only `m`; replacing a prototype or writing at an unknown key refuses all. Owners: `CallGraph::parameter_holds_builtin` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/attribution.rs`; `member_name_may_be_reassigned`.

<a id="adr-0214"></a>
## ADR 0214 — A prop holds what every tag passes
In a closed program an obligation on `props.name` is discharged when `props` is the component's sole plain parameter, never assigned; the component is rendered only through resolved JSX tags (a call, `createComponent`, rendering prop or other escape fails); and every tag passes a built-in value (a spreading tag fails, a missing attribute is `undefined`, `children` is not followed). Owners: `prop_holds_builtin`, `rendered_only_through_jsx`, `value_holds_builtin` in `attribution.rs`.



<a id="adr-0215"></a>
## ADR 0215 — The value-flow proofs survive review
Computed writes record their literal or `const` key; any other computed prototype write refuses every member; a binding is written if any assignment target or loop head references it. An exact class instance has the class's own members only if no constructor returns a value and no field, parameter property or computed element can define the member, through the whole `extends` chain (unresolved proves nothing). Function values must be current. Owners: `class_instance_is_exact`, `function_value_is_current`, `binding_written` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes.rs`.
<a id="adr-0216"></a>
## ADR 0216 — A forwarded prop is as reactive as its source
`<Inner value={props.value}/>` makes Inner's prop reactive, static or unresolved exactly as the caller's own prop is, by a least fixpoint over forwards; a prop is static only when no chain reaches a reactive or unresolved value. A props object passed whole, `merge`/destructured views, a store passed whole, several children and `props.a.b` off a static head are unknown, never reactive. Owners: `classify_passed_expression`, `resolve_forwarded_props` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery.rs`.

<a id="adr-0217"></a>
## ADR 0217 — Leaf scopes reach helpers, namespaces and more host callbacks
Argumentless calls in helpers a leaf callback reaches get a resolved-call fact. `ns.f()` on a namespace import binding follows the exactly resolved exported function declaration or `const` function; computed members and aliases keep the obligation. Host rows: `Promise` executor and `NodeList.forEach` inline; global `addEventListener` and `MediaQueryList.addListener` deferred; `MediaSession.setActionHandler` fresh-stack. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`, `runtime_semantics.rs`; `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs`.



<a id="adr-0218"></a>
## ADR 0218 — A callback every use of which is closed is not a strict read
A callback handed to a project function or component clears only the SC1001 finding when every runtime reference of its subject (parameter, optionally one static property of an exact literal or attribute) is closed: a dialect tracked slot, exact unlabelled `untrack`, compiler-tracked JSX, a fresh-stack scheduler, or an exact forward. Storing, aliasing, unknown packages, DOM listeners and cycles are open; labelled `untrack` and `callback_may_open_strict_window` slots never inherit a window. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/project_consumer.rs`, `projection.rs`; `Dialect::callback_may_open_strict_window`.
<a id="adr-0219"></a>
## ADR 0219 — A type query and a namespace call are accounted entries
The entry census of ADR 0203 also accounts for erased type queries (`typeof fn` in type position) and a namespace call whose property resolves to the call edge's declaration, only while no runtime code reaches the module as a namespace object (escaping namespace import, `export * as`, dynamic `import()`/`require`, transitively). Specifiers resolve by the compiler's module graph; unresolved bare ones may name anything; files outside the program import no project module (ADR 0193). Owners: `module_namespace_escapes`, `specifier_reaches` in `attribution.rs`.

<a id="adr-0220"></a>
## ADR 0220 — The project's bundler answers where a load goes
Opt-in `--runtime-resolution required` runs a Node worker that loads the project's own Vite config and asks a client plugin container (`resolveId`) about each load occurrence, joined by literal span. With the table present `specifier_reaches` uses it alone; unknown answers (error, virtual or query module, declaration target, `require`) may reach anything; any failure leaves an empty table; the run bypasses the daemon. Default off. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/runtime_resolution.rs`, `packages/cli/scripts/runtime-resolver.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0221"></a>
## ADR 0221 — A prop head and an async prefix run at the call
The head of a props member chain (`props.snapshot?.x`) is a Get backed by the ADR 0216 fixpoint. A control-flow primitive's sole exact children literal runs while rendering. An exact plain call of an async project function runs its prefix up to the first suspension (no suspending loops or `switch` tests). An omitted argument activates its default. New async/default paths need a runtime source witness; a store Get needs a key the dialect says warns. Owners: `local_access.rs`, `interproc::body_site_runs_during_call`, `Dialect::store_key_warns_strict_read` (exact rc.13).
<a id="adr-0222"></a>
## ADR 0222 — A returned source and a helper's prop read reach the caller
A project function whose every normal completion returns the same unwritten `const` bound to a dialect source primitive in that frame (exact, current, synchronous, block-bodied, no loops/`try`/`switch`) returns a fresh source per call. A component-local helper's prop read counts at each proven exact plain call with the call's role and ADR 0216 backing. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery.rs`, `local_access.rs`; `completion_return_cover` in `rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/completion_call_cover.rs`.

<a id="adr-0225"></a>
## ADR 0225 — An exact receiver for getter instantiation
A getter on an argument runs at the call that copies it only when the receiver is a fresh closed object literal, or one immutable local `const` initialized by one (no alias, export, descriptor change or earlier spread; no internal spread, computed or numeric key, duplicate, setter or `__proto__`). Anything else stays an explicit obligation. Only the receiver rule is live (used by ADR 0283); the contract protocol words are gone. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/property_gets.rs`.

<a id="adr-0228"></a>
## ADR 0228 — The certified contract tier is retired
`bundledContracts`, `installedPackages` and `exportConditions` stay in the WASM `CheckRequest` and its typings for compatibility but are accepted and have no effect. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-checker-wasm/src/lib.rs`, `packages/wasm/index.d.ts`.

<a id="adr-0248"></a>

Superseded by ADR 0440: SC9005 has no ESLint rule at all.

<a id="adr-0266"></a>
## ADR 0266 — Standard runtime configuration premise
Assumed by default, vetoed only on a positively identified exact runtime reference in analyzed source: a call of `enableExternalSource`, or any value reference to `DEV` or `OBSERVE` (a named import used only in truthiness tests does not veto). Identity is the exact declaration name-node span the dialect lists, never spelling; type-only uses and unresolved references never veto. A veto turns every violation into an uncertifiable one project-wide; no third state. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/runtime_configuration.rs`, `rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs`.


<a id="adr-0268"></a>
## ADR 0268 — Standard hydration host
A fourth clause of the standard runtime configuration premise: hydration runs on Solid's standard serializer runtime, with no custom `sharedConfig.load`/`has` or other host callback. Any positively identified runtime reference to `sharedConfig` (via `solid-js/internal` or the server declarations) vetoes, except direct reads of `hydrating`/`done` and truthiness tests; standard `enableHydration`/`hydrate`/`render`/`renderToString` never veto, and the main-entry `sharedConfig` has no declaration identity so stays Assumed. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/runtime_configuration.rs` (`HydrationHost`).
<a id="adr-0269"></a>
## ADR 0269 — A browser-target config for client applications
The ESLint adapter ships a `browser-<dialect>` config (`browser-v2`): the dialect config plus `settings.solidChecker.runtime.target: "browser"`, opt-in and overridable by a later `runtime` setting. The user, not an inference, declares the target for client apps. Owner: `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.



<a id="adr-0270"></a>
## ADR 0270 — Browser host from feasible execution reachability
With no explicit target, discovery infers a browser host from closed, non-executing config (one conventional `vite.config.*`, scripts naming Vite only in plain forms, no custom resolver) and client roots; an unproven premise refuses with a `solid-checker: note: browser host not inferred` line. Premises: conventional config, successful build, app starts. A callback registered from browser-executed code with a reviewed trigger can run in the browser unless non-execution is proved. Inference is monotone, never certifies, and an explicit target wins. A config plugin counts only as an exact installed closure (every package's file digests and dependency edges, runtime peers included) listed in `host-plugin-pins.json`; `host_plugins::tests::installed_closures` prints a closure's identity and checks an install against the pins. A pinned closure vouches for the plugin's behaviour under the admitted options, not for its runtime peers' semantics (SC9014 still applies). `@solidjs/vite-plugin` next.45–51 were read against next.44: under the admitted options they keep the client resolve conditions, non-hydratable dom client output, entry probe order and absence of resource claims. Dev-only label passes (`sourceNames`), the performance-tracks module and root-reachable core dedupe in builds (next.51) do not change the host; the root-install requirement for core imports already covers the dedupe. Pinned: the next.51 installs in the rc.17 corpus (darwin-arm64; compiler rc.16 or rc.17) and the demo app's install through `vite-plugin-solid` next.27. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/inferred_host.rs`, `host_config.rs`, `host_plugins.rs`; `solid-reactive-ir/src/hosts.rs`.

## ADR 0273 — Transported member stability premise
Members of an object a factory returns or a context provides are assumed never reassigned, deleted or redefined. Only a visible write vetoes: any static member write by name on any receiver, `Object.defineProperty`/`assign`/`Reflect.set` by exact library identity, with unknown keys or spread arguments vetoing every member. Two states per member (Assumed, Vetoed). Over-veto only withholds. Owner: `MemberStability` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance.rs`.

<a id="adr-0274"></a>
## ADR 0274 — Option getters through a forwarding helper
A call of a local helper counts as the copy of its parameter when the helper (exact, current, synchronous, directive-free module, never naming `arguments`/`eval`) passes the plain unwritten parameter to the copying call as the whole value of a `const` or unconditional `return`, outside classes, JSX and fragments. Every getter of a hazard-free prefix (none mentions `this`, `super` or `eval`) is guaranteed. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/property_gets.rs`.

<a id="adr-0275"></a>
## ADR 0275 — A helper's operation counts only on every completion
In the leaf-scope walk, a called body's operation (or call to the next helper) is performed by the call only when it runs on every normal completion the caller can continue from; an earlier `throw` is a way out, an async helper counts only its prefix before the first suspension. Otherwise it is an uncertifiable `leaf-owner-forbidden-call`. The leaf callback's own body keeps the statically-present-code premise. Owner: `reached_on_every_call` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`.

<a id="adr-0276"></a>
## ADR 0276 — A read through a helper counts only on every completion
A completion site is a call, `new` or member expression in a function's straight-line flow (no conditional, logical right operand, logical assignment, loop, switch or try; nothing before it can `return`/`break`/`continue`/`throw` out; in an optional chain only what precedes the first `?.`). A summary row is direct only when proven on every completion at every hop; others stay uncertifiable "only on some of its paths". Owners: `AstFacts::completion_sites` in `rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/mod.rs`; `site_on_every_completion`, `read_is_direct` in `interproc.rs`.

<a id="adr-0277"></a>
## ADR 0277 — A callback runs only when its helper calls it on every completion
A helper invokes its parameter during the call only when a call of the parameter is a completion site of its synchronous body; this governs inline-arrow roles, stored-literal proofs and accessor-by-reference reads. A call on some paths is an uncertifiable obligation (SC2001 stays silent); `cb?.()` is not a completion site. Owners: `invokes_parameter_during_call` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs`; `parameter_called_on_every_completion` in `indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0278"></a>
## ADR 0278 — Exact callback mapping and never-returning guards
A call TypeScript resolves to a `never`-returning signature is an exit; sites after it are not completion sites. A written parameter is not the caller's callback. An argument reaches its parameter only through an exact mapping: no `f.call`/`apply`/`bind`, a current function value, no earlier spread. <a id="adr-0279"></a>
## ADR 0279 — Assertion and never signatures, and exact method callees
The Type Facts producer states, per resolved call, whether the selected signature carries an `asserts` predicate or returns `never`; either makes the call a possible exit (a throw in an undeclared helper is outside the model). A method callee runs its declared body only for a module namespace member, an unwritten member of a `const` object literal, or a member of a `const` bound to `new C()` of the declaring class. A props or store read in an anonymous callback is classified like its signal twin by the role and timing machinery (ADR 0315/0319/0321); named helpers go through summaries. `createRoot`/`runWithOwner` callbacks inherit the calling window; `omit`/`merge` views are never proven sources and a spread of a view is never a violation. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs`; `solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs`, `local_access.rs`.

## ADR 0324 — Route parameters are URL segments in the audited router
For an audited `@solidjs/router` release (Solid 2: `2.0.0-next.26`, `next.41`, from the compiler's attested import, installed bytes matching) the project does not replace (`paramsWrapper`, `queryWrapper`, `wrapParams`, `RouterIntegration`, `RouterContext`, a `createRouter` config naming `links` or a non-arrow `preload`, called on props holding the router context, a `useAction` argument not proven `this`-free: ADR 0335), `const params = useParams()` members and `<Show when={params.k}>` callback values are strings or `undefined`, feeding ADR 0320's primitive proofs. An unaudited resolved release raises SC9014. Owners: `router_params_status` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/router_params.rs`; `Dialect::router_params_are_url_strings`.

## ADR 0325 — An imported const holds its initializer's primitive
A named value import whose attested resolution lands on the declaring module (no barrel or `export *` hop), a module-level `const` exported under exactly the imported name (not CommonJS-touched), holds a primitive when its initializer passes `proven_primitive` in that file. Mutable exports, default imports, namespace members and package imports refuse. Owner: `imported_const_is_primitive` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0326"></a>
## ADR 0326 — A component's function child is its `children` prop
A function literal that is the whole child of a non-intrinsic element is the component's `children` prop: its compiler `Deferred` role and any enclosing tracked region place nothing, so it keeps the lexical rendering role with unproven timing (uncertifiable) unless ADR 0319's walk places it. The body-call proof is not applied to children (ADR 0204's attribute proof only). Owner: `component_child_function` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs`.

<a id="adr-0327"></a>
## ADR 0327 — A callback only an event runs has no owner
A plain function literal whose every invocation, found by ADR 0319's walk in owner mode (any primitive slot refuses; a call's role is event only when the function carrying it is a compiler event handler), is an event dispatch is seeded unowned, so an owner primitive directly in it is an SC4001 violation. Premise: an event handler runs with no owner (synchronous dispatch from an owned scope breaks it; recorded in the backlog). Owners: `unowned_event_literals` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs` and `indexes.rs`; `owner_node` in `owners.rs`.


<a id="adr-0328"></a>
## ADR 0328 — Uncertifiable results are internal
Users see a finding when it is not uncertifiable or is a project-level notice (`subjectKind: "project"`: SC9013, SC9014). Human formats show only those and `--certify` fails on a violation or notice; `--format json` and WASM keep every finding and the certification status; `--internal` restores the ADR 0202 tiers and strict certification; ESLint reports only user-facing findings. Owners: `user_facing`, `certify_fails`, `render_user` in `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/snapshot_emission.rs`; `userFacing` in `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

## ADR 0329 — A DOM call that dispatches synchronously is not trusted
In the leaf-scope walk a call is refused, optional or not, when its exact `lib.dom` declaration is `focus`, `blur`, `click`, `dispatchEvent`, `checkValidity` or `reportValidity` on any interface (and window globals), or `HTMLFormElement.reset`/`requestSubmit`, `HTMLElement.showPopover`/`hidePopover`/`togglePopover`, `HTMLDialogElement.show`/`showModal`/`close`/`requestClose`, `AbortController.abort`, `XMLHttpRequest.open`/`abort`, `Document.execCommand`. Tree mutation and attribute writes stay trusted, which ADR 0642 corrects for `remove`. Owner: `synchronously_dispatching_dom_member` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`.


<a id="adr-0330"></a>
## ADR 0330 — A ref creation is SC6001 only in the unowned apply phase
A creation inside a `ref` expression is an SC6001 violation only when it runs when the applied callback runs and nothing between gives it an owner. Eager arguments, IIFEs and default parameters run while rendering (owned); a `createRoot` callback, directly or through inline synchronous slots, owns it. Any other owner-supplying slot (`runWithOwner`), a project helper, an unread callee, a discarded factory result or a generator makes it uncertifiable. Premise: a per-element `createRoot` is never disposed. Owners: `ref_phase`, `nested_owner`, `discover_directive_creations` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/directives.rs`.

<a id="adr-0331"></a>
## ADR 0331 — A function is a component only where `createComponent` calls it
A JSX-returning function is a proven component when every reference is its declaration, module surface, a dialect callback or renderer prop (ADR 0138) or a proven `lazy` default (ADR 0292). Any other reference (alias, storage, argument, method, anonymous default export, unread router) leaves it a component whose body, bar callbacks a primitive places and functions a tag renders, is an `UndecidedRegion` (non-style violations uncertifiable). A tag naming an exact alias (`const`, unwritten `let`) of an unwritten function binding renders it like its own. A call proves no component only if every other reference is one above, a `typeof` or an exact alias only called or tagged, and no importer is unseen (open program, namespace hand-out); else its calls' findings stand and a body read or write they clear is uncertifiable (a host component proves nothing). A callback position is one entry; it clears no render. Owners: `component_statuses` (`indexes/components.rs`), `write_roles.rs`, `named_callbacks.rs`, `site_in_undecided_component` (`lib.rs`), `diagnostics.rs`.

## ADR 0332 — A site no fact places is an obligation
A read, write or action whose execution role is `Unknown` (no fact) is projected as an uncertifiable finding of its rule (SC1001, SC2001, SC2002), never dropped; `is_uncertifiable` per seed sets kind and wording. `Unreported` is the proven no-breach answer without a single role (ADR 0323's merge of non-reporting roles, code past the strict-read window): every consumer treats it as `Unknown`, projection reports nothing. The same holds for a read in a callee called after an `await` in a tracked async computation (SC1002, through the reads attributed to that call), `resolve`/`until` in code no role places (SC2004/SC2005), options the syntax cannot read beside an async computation (SC7002), and a dialect primitive referenced as a value (SC9012, `PRIMITIVE_VALUE`). A coverage rule (`RuleMetadata::uncertifiable`) disabled by options hides its findings but keeps the status from `certified`. Two identity rules guard the primitive lookup: a call of a method of a primitive (`createEffect.call`/`.bind`) is not a call of the primitive, and an import binding whose symbol declares nothing (an unresolved import shares the one unknown symbol) names no primitive. Owners: `projection.rs`, `ReactiveRead`/`ReactiveWrite`/`ActionInvocation::is_uncertifiable`, `referenced_primitive` and `call_primitive_name` in `lib.rs`; `add_solid_import_names` in `symbols.rs`; `reactive_read_after_await_through_calls`, `primitive_value_references` in `static_rules.rs`; `resolve_scope_unplaced` in `static_api.rs`; `retain_enabled` in `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/diagnostics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0333"></a>
## ADR 0333 — A write past a dominating await runs with no owner
A write past a dominating await is legal and reports nothing: the await is at statement level of its innermost block with nothing between the block's start and it that can skip it, and the write is later in that block, in the same async function, not a generator, and not in an instance field, JSX or a compiler tracked region (facts schema 65, `dominating_awaits`). A `try` block counts; its `catch` and `finally` count only when the block's first statement awaits an operand that cannot throw synchronously (a call of an identifier naming an async non-generator function declaration, with only literals and enclosing-function parameters as arguments: a hoisted declaration is initialized, calling an async function never throws, and its native promise is adopted without project code), facts schema 66 `try_head_awaits`. A read there is past the strict-read window and silent too. Premises: code after an await runs on a later task with no owner, and no program redefines `Promise.prototype.constructor` or `then` (which an await of a native promise reads synchronously; as ADR 0190 for primitive prototypes) (runtime-oracle `precision-write-after-try-await`, `precision-write-in-catch-after-async-call`). No event-only write proof: a synchronous dispatch from an owned scope runs the handler under that owner. Owners: `follows_dominating_await`, `follows_try_head_await` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role/body_roles.rs`, `semantic_write_execution_role_within` in `write_roles.rs`, the visitor in `rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/visitor.rs`.

<a id="adr-0334"></a>
## ADR 0334 — An audited TanStack router renders its route components
A function referenced as the `component`, `pendingComponent`, `errorComponent` or `notFoundComponent` value of the options literal of an exact `@tanstack/solid-router` route call (`createFileRoute(path)(…)`, `createLazyFileRoute(id)(…)`, `createLazyRoute(id)(…)`, `createRootRouteWithContext()(…)`, `createRoute(…)` or `createRootRoute(…)`) is rendered there (ADR 0331's census). `createRouter`'s `defaultComponent`, `defaultPendingComponent`, `defaultErrorComponent` and `defaultNotFoundComponent` count the same way, and an anonymous inline function written at such a key is a component. The audited releases are `2.0.0-beta.29`, `rc.0`, `rc.4`, `rc.6`, `rc.8` and `rc.9`, each with the `@tanstack/router-core` it loads. They render these values, read at most a member such as `preload`, and call none. Premises: every runtime import of the router resolves, agreeing with the bundler, to an audited release whose bytes and whose router-core's bytes match the recorded digests; no manifest, lockfile or patch directory replaces either; and the project does not reach route options by a computed key or by reflection (`Object.values(route.options)`). Stays uncertifiable: a project file reading one of the four keys back (a member, a literal key, a destructured key), which could call the value. Trade-off: an unaudited release's route components stay uncertifiable. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/route_components.rs`; `AUDITED_ROUTE_RELEASES` in `rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs`; `route_replacement_recorded` in `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/installed_bytes.rs`; the audit evidence is in rust/ARCHITECTURE.md.

<a id="adr-0335"></a>
## ADR 0335 — An audited @solidjs/router renders its route definitions
A function referenced as the `component` of an object-literal route definition written in the `routes` array of `createRouter({ routes: [...] })`, an exact named import of `@solidjs/router`, or in such a definition's `children` array (recursively), is rendered there (ADR 0331's census). The audited releases (`2.0.0-next.16`, `next.18`, `next.26`, `next.41`; `dist/`, both export conditions) copy `component` into the route record, render it only by `createComponent` in the outlet, else reading only its `preload` and the unexported `SERVER_ROUTE` brand. A config naming `links` (called with the router context) withholds it, as does a `useAction` call (`action.apply({ r }, args)`) given neither an arrow nor the router's `action`/`formAction` wrapper, inline or a `const` every reference to which, program-wide (Type Facts, binder; direct named imports, no re-export, namespace or `eval`), is `useAction`, `useSubmissions`, a call or an intrinsic attribute (unseen code: ADR 0300), or beside a prototype spelling or `apply` write (`router_context_as_this`). As ADR 0324 and 0334: every runtime import resolves (attested, bundler agreeing) to an audited release with digest-matching bytes, no record replaces the package, and no analyzed file outside the audited installs obtains a definition. The router hands definitions out only through `useRouteMatches`, `RouterContext` and the instance's `routes`/`config` (no route prop, `preload` argument or other hook), so the router is imported only by name, never those two nor as a namespace, default, deep path, dynamic load or re-export, and every `createRouter` result is a `const` whose every compiler reference is its declaration, module surface or a JSX tag, in a module no file reaches as a namespace; any non-literal load, module hazard or `import.meta.glob` withholds it. The router calls `route.preload(...)` on the record and `config.transformUrl(...)` on the config, so the config and each definition count only with no getter or method and every key but `routes`, `component` and `children` a non-callable literal or an arrow. Premise (owner decision, as ADR 0190 and 0333): no program installs callables on `Object.prototype`, which the router would call with the config or a record as `this`; any `prototype`, `__proto__` or `setPrototypeOf` spelling, and ADR 0292's veto, withhold it as a tripwire. A project `component` read then is no definition's. Residual premise: no reflection over router internals. Also followed: `defineRoute(...)`/`defineRoutes(...)` (identity helpers), a stock-history call at the config's `history` (ADR 0324), an anonymous inline component value, and a constant route table whose every compiler reference is its declaration, module surface or the `routes` of such a config, in a module no file reaches as a namespace. Not followed: definitions built by a project call, `children` thunks, `fileRoutes()` output. Owners: `router_config_values`, `router_directories`, `definitions_exposed` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/route_components.rs`; `AUDITED_ROUTE_CONFIG_DIGESTS` in `rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs`.

## ADR 0336 — A shorthand property is a reference of its value
`{ value }` names the property it declares and reads the binding `value`, so the Type Facts reference index records a shorthand's name under `GetShorthandAssignmentValueSymbol` too. Otherwise every census that needs all references of a binding (ADR 0331's component identity, ADR 0335's router instance and route tables) misses an object holding the value: `const holder = { Chip }; holder.Chip()` (a false SC1001, fixture `component-identity-escapes`, `Stored.tsx`). <a id="adr-0337"></a>
## ADR 0337 — A view, a const alias or a DOM spread does not invalidate the props root
A props read keeps the callers' classification (ADR 0216) when every other use of the props object is inert: an argument of a call that builds a view, a `const` alias of the root, or a spread onto an intrinsic element. The same predicate gates spread forwarding, where the parent's own props are passed to a child. `omit(props, …)` always builds a view. `merge(...)` does only with no spread argument and an argument that is syntactically never falsy; the props may be any source. Premises: `Proxy` exists (without it `merge` can return its last source), and no caller-supplied function invoked as `props.onX()` deletes or redefines a getter of its `this` or of the props object (as ADR 0324 and 0335; member calls of the root are counted inert, and no caller value is checked for `this`). Stays uncertifiable: a read through the view or the alias (`rest.x`, `local.x`), a destructured binding, a view bound to anything but a `const`, a `merge` that may return its only source, and a spread onto a component. Trade-off: props reads behind a view-building call are proven signal-backed and can be reported, safe on the premises above. Owners: `call_builds_view`, `props_root_attachments_are_views`, `props_root_unaccounted_uses` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/local_access.rs`; fixture `omit-view-props-root`.

<a id="adr-0338"></a>
## ADR 0338 — A Solid getter has a known `typeof` and `length`
The first item of `createSignal` and `createOptimistic`, and the result of `createMemo`, are `read.bind(null, node)` over `function read(el)` in `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.13`: a function of `length` 0 in every build. `Dialect::accessor_getter_length` states it; an unaudited dialect states nothing. An exact, unwritten, unexported binding of such a slot is thus a proven truthy, non-nullish function of `length` 0, so `@solid-primitives/utils` `access(v)` calls a getter on every path and a read through it in a setup body is a violation. Unknown arity (an alias, a function literal, a props value, a store, a setter, an import) decides nothing. A callee that calls a parameter reads through the argument it receives unless that argument is proven not to be a getter; an unproven argument keeps an SC9012 obligation, and so does a member call on a primitive's result that holds a getter (`createSignal(1).slice(0)`). Premises: no program redefines `length` on a getter it received; no analysed file may replace `Function.prototype.bind`, which every getter is built with (a write to a member named `bind`, a prototype write whose key or owner is unproven, or ADR 0292's reflective-write veto withholds the whole fact); and no builtin is patched by means that tripwire cannot see (an owner decision, as ADR 0335). Stays undecided, so uncertifiable: a read through another helper hop, an array of accessors, a read after an `await`, a binding declared in a file with a direct `eval`, and a binding the module exports, which other code can reach and redefine. Trade-off: `--certify` fails where an argument is unproven (SC9012). Owners: `solid_getter_length` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/argument_values.rs`; `primitive_result_methods` in `static_rules.rs`; `Scope::length` in `rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/argument_completion.rs`; `parameter_call_standing` in `execution_role/callback_standing.rs`; fixtures `package-access-helper`, `dependency-source-owner`.

<a id="adr-0339"></a>
## ADR 0339 — A mention of a function-valued binding runs after its declarator only where that is proven
A `const` or `let` function-valued binding is not hoisted: a mention that runs before its declarator throws, and a `var` reads `undefined`. A call, JSX render, primitive argument or callback role that names such a binding supplies a call site and a reachability edge only where the mention provably follows the declarator; otherwise it supplies none, so a finding resting on it is uncertifiable or absent, never a violation. Within one module a mention follows when it is in the declarator's own body after it, inside the function itself, in a function or arrow created after it, in an arrow or nested declaration written before it whose every mention follows, or in a hoisted declaration that nothing running ahead of the declarator can call. Across modules it follows only when the caller statically imports the declaring module and neither the caller nor any module it imports is on an import cycle. Credited orderings: a hoisted function declaration whose binding is never written; a `const` or `let` by source position (a declarator in a `switch` case clause is excepted); an import ordered by module evaluation (a static import or re-export, or `import x = require()`). Anything else is withheld; `var`, a `require()` call, a dynamic `import()` and `createRequire` are never credited. Premises: ES module semantics; the analysed files are the whole program, so every load is a static import, a recorded `require` or a lazy glob; installed dependencies do not import the project's own modules (ADR 0300); and module-level code before a declarator runs project code only through the functions written there. Stays uncertifiable: a mention across an unresolved or ambient module, a nonliteral or unrecorded `require`, an `import.meta.glob` that may be eager, a JavaScript file outside `allowJs`, a direct `eval` in the declaring or the calling file, and a function whose parameter defaults are more than literals, since they run before its body. Trade-off: a withheld mention gives no edge, so the checker is silent where a call site is only unproven. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/initialization.rs`.

<a id="adr-0340"></a>
## ADR 0340 — SC1002 reaches what the continuation runs
A read is after the await where the continuation runs it, not only where it is written in the awaiting function. The region extends by four proven shapes, each reported with the existing proofs and the ADR 0195 exemption: (1) a function literal in a slot an exact standard-library declaration invokes before it returns, and an immediately invoked function expression, after a dominating await; (2) the callback of `.then`, `.catch` or `.finally` on a receiver proven to be a native `Promise`, when the call is awaited or returned by a synchronous computation; (3) an exactly resolved `async` project function whose promise the computation awaits or returns, directly or through `Promise.all` or `allSettled`, reading after its own first await; (4) an exactly resolved synchronous project function called after the await, whose body runs on the continuation's stack, for an accessor call it makes on every normal completion (ADR 0276). Premises: `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.13` attributes every read below the returned promise's async frame, so a read in a handler is inside the computation's frame when the chain is awaited or returned synchronously; and a handler on a receiver that may already be settled is queued before the adoption, with no frame below it, where the runtime stays silent, so such a handler counts only on a receiver proven still pending. Stays uncertifiable: a rejection handler, a fulfillment handler on a receiver not proven to fulfill, an `async` helper that may resume before adoption, a call before the first await that may be tracked, a computation whose owner a `createRoot` `dispose` may dispose, a store key another path may alias, a read after `for await`, and a `sort` comparator, a reducer with no initial value or a `replace` replacer whose call depends on the contents. Trade-off: a mistake in any shape can only add a report, so each shape has a runtime-oracle case (docs/open-work.md). Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/static_rules/after_await.rs`, `static_rules/pre_await.rs`; `is_inline_standard_callback` and `standard_call_name` in `runtime_semantics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0341"></a>
## ADR 0341 — `flush` on a `createEffect` apply's stack is SC2008
`flush()` that runs only on the stack of a `createEffect` apply is SC2008, a warning. **The claim is universal and is the no-op, not the warning:** whenever the call runs, it runs inside the effect phase, where the running flush drains nothing, so it is pointless wherever it runs, dead code included. The dev build usually also warns (`FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`); nothing throws, and no published type expresses the behaviour, so the finding is the checker's own claim. Premises: `@solidjs/signals` rc.1 and later reviewed releases raise an effect-phase marker around an apply, and `flush` returns without draining while a flush runs (read from the published bytes and probed; rc.0 has neither the marker nor the report); the apply's queue is the one the flush finds running, so the two calls load one installed `solid-js` copy; and no server build is selected. The rc.3 dialect release names the answer `flush-in-effect-callback`, as the vocabulary has none. Stays unclaimed: a helper that non-effect code may also call (an export, an alias, an argument, a timer, a microtask or event handler, or another caller), an apply passed by name, a `flush` imported from `@solidjs/signals` itself, and an import through another package. Trade-off: the universal claim reports dead calls and gives up reachability, so a helper with a non-effect caller is not reported at all. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/effect_phase.rs`; rule page `docs/rules/flush-in-effect-callback.md`.

<a id="adr-0342"></a>
## ADR 0342 — A member of a compound component that only JSX member tags reach is a component
A function stored in a compound component (`export const Split = { Root, Panel }`, `Object.assign(Root, { List })`) and reached only through member tags (`<Split.Panel>`) is a component, as a tagged function is (ADR 0331): a tag is `createComponent(Split.Panel, props)`, so storing it is no escape when every way to obtain the member is a tag. The holder is a module-top-level `const` holding a closed object literal, or the target of `Object.assign`; every Type Facts reference to it is its declaration, module surface, or the leftmost name of a JSX tag ending at a value; and no computed member write or reflective call (`Reflect.set`, ...) may reach the holder or `Object`. Premises: ADR 0331's (the analysed files are the whole program; nothing enumerates exports reflectively); `Object.assign` is unpatched (no write to a member named `assign`, no prototype write with an unproven owner, none to `Object`), and no direct `eval` exists; and the holder's module is on no import cycle, with no unresolved load, so no tag renders while the holder initializes. Uncertifiable (ADR 0331): a member nothing renders, read as a value, called plainly or destructured; a holder in a type, passed anywhere or reached through a namespace import; a definition built by a project call. Trade-off: a proven member's body is a component's: a call in it is no retained callable, and a read via a helper it calls is a violation, not an obligation. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/compound.rs` (read by `indexes/components.rs`, `interproc/retained_literal.rs`); fixtures `compound-components*`; census detail: rust/ARCHITECTURE.md.

<a id="adr-0343"></a>
## ADR 0343 — `innerText` is a content source on Solid 2.0
`@solidjs/web` rc.13 lists `innerText` in its property set, so a write replaces the element's child nodes, as `textContent` does. `jsx-no-duplicate-props` (SC8003) counts an exact `innerText` beside children, `innerHTML` or `textContent`. eslint-plugin-solid 0.14.5 has no `innerText` arm at 6d3bc311, so this is a kept divergence, pinned as `innertext/solid-v2/content-positive/001` in `fixtures/ownership-cases/cases.json` (a product-owned case, ADR 0110). Premise: the published typings accept the prop, so tsc stays silent and the finding is the checker's own claim. Trade-off: the checker reports a combination the upstream rule does not. Owner: `has_inner_text` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/syntax.rs`.

<a id="adr-0344"></a>
## ADR 0344 — Behavioural evidence classifies a static-only finding; a refutation is a candidate, not a failure
A static violation that no dev diagnostic covers is not a false positive by default. A runtime-oracle case can observe the page for it through `behaviour` entries in `oracle.json` (DOM text, a window counter, node identity, a thrown error, or write-reflected, which tests a two-source element that a text observation cannot). A page showing the defect confirms the finding. A page showing correct behaviour makes it a false-positive candidate, reported ahead of covered verdicts; it does not fail the gate, since the fix belongs in the checker. A defect the page shows with no finding is an unproven clean claim, as a dev diagnostic is. Premises: an observation is the page's real behaviour under the dev runtime, and each write's probe is bound to its source; a write whose probe does not change, or whose outcome differs from its declared `expect`, is a harness error, not evidence. Stays static-only: a static source no write can reach (a static `textContent`). Trade-off: a candidate costs visibility, not a failed gate. Owner: `behaviour` and `staticOnly` in `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs`; format in `fixtures/runtime-oracle/README.md`.

<a id="adr-0345"></a>
## ADR 0345 — A ref expression's own functions run where they are written
A `ref` expression runs in two places. The function it yields to the ref application (the expression itself, or an element, conditional branch or logical operand of it) runs with no owner: a violation. A function the expression evaluates without yielding (a getter's body, a tagged template's substitution, an operand of `void`) is undecided. Code that a call in the expression evaluates runs during render under the owner of the code around the expression: an IIFE, the body of a factory the expression calls, and a callback a dialect slot runs during its call (`untrack`) are owned. Code at module initialization runs with no owner (a violation), and a call inside a component's children is undecided, since it runs inside that component's own memo. A generator never runs when called, and a callback no dialect slot describes may run with or without an owner: both are uncertifiable. A callback is named only by a function literal, a bare identifier or element 0 of an event handler's tuple. Premise: an owner edge reaches the enclosing function; one that no edge reaches (a method no resolved call names, an exported function a closed program never calls) has an unknown owner and is uncertifiable. Trade-off: an effect a factory called by a ref creates is owned, so it is SC6001 only, not SC4001 (the open item in docs/open-work.md). Owners: `ref_value_marks` and `yielded_value_contains` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/owners/owner_context.rs`; fixtures `ref-expression-owner-walk`, `directive-owned-effects`, `callback-containment`.

<a id="adr-0346"></a>
## ADR 0346 — A callback's identity is the entity whose symbol covers its whole span
A typefacts entity names the symbol at its demanded span's first byte. That symbol is the value's only when it covers the whole span (`symbol_covers_span`, Type Facts wire schema v21, handshake protocol 80); otherwise it is a token inside the span, such as the callee of `f(x)` or the object of `o.p`. Every callback identity reads this exact answer: owner discovery and the owner callback index, the event handler and ref roles, named callbacks, and reachability roots. A TypeScript wrapper (`h as X`, `h!`, `h satisfies X`) keeps the identity of `h`. A call-valued callback names a literal only when the call is an identity wrapper: a project function, declared or bound by an immutable `const` to a function literal, that returns one of its parameters on every completion, returns no other value, is neither async nor a generator, and never otherwise references that parameter, its name, `arguments` or `eval`. A wrapper whose declaration is written later in its module (`wrap = function …`) names nothing. A callee names only the identifier it is, and a member read of a server function with a resolved declaration is no server call. Premises: the covering answer is exact, and the wrapper read is the one that runs (a wrapper changed in another file invalidates its callers through the cross-file owner cache). Stays uncertifiable: a call-valued callee's returned callable (SC2001, SC1001). Trade-off: a reactive read keeps its leading token (`a.b.c`, `sig().x`, `props.x ?? []`), so each read is still attributed to the source it goes through; making those exact removed six correct SC1001 violations in a corpus trial, so they were not changed. Owners: `EntitySymbols::exact` and `value_symbol` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes.rs`; `identity_wrapped_literal_span` in `indexes/identity_wrappers.rs`; `symbol_covers_span` in `rust/crates/typefacts/src/lib.rs`; fixtures `callback-ts-sugar-identity`, `identity-wrapper-callback`, `identity-wrapper-rejections`, `call-valued-callee-identity`, `server-member-transport`.

<a id="adr-0347"></a>
## ADR 0347 — An invoked literal runs under its call's owner; a namespace hand-out is reachable
A direct call of a function literal runs its body inside the call, so the call is an owner edge under the function that holds it: unowned at module scope, owned in a component body. It is withheld for an instance field initializer (it runs at construction) and for JSX in the owner's own body (a component's children run in its memo, ADR 0345). In a closed program, an export that a namespace import hands out is seeded with the open-world unowned bit, so a callback proven unowned whatever the caller does is a violation, and a requirement in the export's own body is uncertifiable; an export that only an unnamed escape reaches is never proven. A dynamic import hands out only the names its result reads (`const { a } = await import(x)`, `lazy(() => import(x), options)`). A leaf body is not region-owned, an `onSettled` in it is queued with no owner (rc.13), and a `createTrackedEffect` body always runs under the tracked node. Premises: a namespace or dynamic import whose specifier does not resolve to the module does not count, since ADR 0203's "may name anything" premise would seed every export of a closed program. Mechanics: `rust/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`, `docs/rules/leaf-owner-forbidden-call.md`. Fixtures `owner-invoked-literal`, `owner-invoked-literal-forms`, `owner-guard-iife`, `owner-namespace-reachable`, `owner-namespace-named-reads`, `owner-tracked-body-forbidden`, `owner-leaf-*`.

<a id="adr-0348"></a>
## ADR 0348 — An inert standard call is inert for its runtime arguments; an async prefix runs on the caller's stack
An audited standard call is inert only for proven runtime arguments: built-in receiver, no spread, primitive coercing/iterating slots, function callback slots. An annotation proves none of these. An async function's prefix through its first own suspension runs on the caller's stack; its tail belongs to the flight walk. Catch/finally joins lose dominance unless operand proofs exclude a synchronous throw. Premise: intrinsic built-ins (ADR 0338). Objects, opaque arguments and unproven catch joins stay uncertifiable. Owners: `static_rules/{pre_await,after_await}.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/reactive-read-after-await.md`.

<a id="adr-0349"></a>
## ADR 0349 — The Promise and array-iteration intrinsics hold until a visible write
Native promises come from intrinsic constructors/statics, fetch, async functions, native chains, or project functions returning these on every path; types alone prove nothing. Native handlers are queued and aggregate elements determine settlement. Premise: Promise, its handlers/constructor, Symbol.species and array iterators are intrinsic. Visible named or reflective writes to those primitives/prototypes, or a Promise subclass, withdraw the premise; alias writes remain a residual premise. Withdrawn claims are obligations, never clean. Opaque collections, fetch outcomes and typed thenables stay unproven. Owners: `indexes/intrinsics.rs`, `static_rules/{native_promise,pre_await,after_await}.rs`; fixtures `read-after-await-premise` and tripwire variants.

<a id="adr-0350"></a>
## ADR 0350 — A call is primitive by its result, and a numeric signal's accessor is a number
A standard call's result can be primitive independently of its evaluation. A signal accessor is numeric only with a compiler domain containing numbers alone, an immutable pair initialized by a decimal literal, and setter references restricted to direct calls with decimal literals. Types alone, functional setters, escaped setters and unknown domains remain unproven. Premise: exact compiler domains and no binding reassignment. Owners: `indexes/primitive_proof.rs`, `static_rules/pre_await.rs`; fixtures `read-after-await-{reach,premise}`.

<a id="adr-0351"></a>
## ADR 0351 — An object literal's function is the callee of its member read when nothing else reaches the literal
A member call on an unexported `const` object literal names its function when the literal has no spread, computed key, accessor, duplicate, `this` or `__proto__`, and every reference is a plain non-optional member read, never written or deleted. Async members join the flight; synchronous members carry straight-line completion reads. Method producer/function spans share their end and differ only by method-header text. Premise: analysed files are the whole program. Escapes, classes and unproven members stay silent. Owners: `indexes/closed_members.rs`, `static_rules/{native_promise,after_await}.rs`; fixture `read-after-await-reach/variants/member-helpers`.

<a id="adr-0352"></a>
## ADR 0352 — Context reads require an owner and a provided value
Without an owner, rc.13 `useContext` throws `NoOwnerError` even with a default (SC4001). Under a proven owner, SC4002 requires a default-less project const and a closed census with no provider or escape. Namespace/module publication withholds that census; package and open contexts stay unproven. Owners: `owners/{context_census,missing_owners,owner_discovery}.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/{missing-owner,missing-context-provider}.md`.

<a id="adr-0353"></a>
## ADR 0353 — Audited render callbacks run under a root
`@solidjs/web` rc.13 and rc.17 run `render`'s code in `createRoot`; `hydrate` reaches it on every path. rc.17 may defer it: no execution is stated, but the root is (`callback_runs_in_created_root` is timing-free), so setters in code and called helpers violate SC2001 unless past an await. Other web releases' placement is uncertifiable. Owners: `solid_2.rs`, `execution_role/write_roles.rs`; rule page; fixtures `setter-in-render-root` (+ rc.9 twin); oracle `render-root-write`.

<a id="adr-0354"></a>
## ADR 0354 — Possible suspension withholds strict-read placement
A read possibly after its own async function's await is uncertifiable; the skipped-await path cannot prove a strict-read-window violation. Calls and writes lose placement too. Unconditional awaits still close the window. Exclusive arms and nested closures are separate; loop back edges and destructuring evaluation order are retained through AST facts schema 73. First-iteration reads may lose recall. Owners: `execution_role/body_roles.rs`, `solid-facts/src/ast/`; mechanics: `docs/rules/strict-read-untracked.md`; fixtures `strict-read-maybe-suspended`, `await-continuation-reads`; runtime oracle `strict-read-possible-await`.

<a id="adr-0355"></a>
## ADR 0355 — Root allocation does not prove a directive leak
A `createRoot` in ref apply owns its work, may be empty, and may be disposed. SC6001 withholds its leak claim as an uncertifiable lifetime obligation in direct and returned callbacks; direct unowned computations retain their proof. The dialect identifies root-creating calls. Owners: `directives.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/primitive-in-directive-application.md`.

<a id="adr-0356"></a>
## ADR 0356 — Synchronous dispatch withholds event-owner proofs
Standard-library `EventTarget.dispatchEvent`/`HTMLElement.click` calls withhold event owner/execution proofs: delivery may inherit an owned caller stack. Until targets are joined, the veto covers all event callbacks, including forwarded literals. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `owners/`, `execution_role/`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`.

<a id="adr-0357"></a>
## ADR 0357 — Possible suspension retains both owner contexts
Possible suspension keeps the original and unowned contexts: SC4001 is an obligation. It follows ordinary calls and owner-preserving callbacks, invalidates lexical-root shortcuts, and never seeds dormant functions. Definite continuations retain their proof; fresh roots own their callbacks. Owners: `owners/{owner_context,missing_owners,owner_discovery}.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`.

<a id="adr-0358"></a>
## ADR 0358 — Exact handler holders enter the owner graph
Event members/tuples and intrinsic spreads resolve through the immutable receiver census. Web rc.13 enables non-namespaced `on` spread keys, lowercase included; rc.17 only `on`+uppercase. Getters, generators and unknown holders retain obligations; synchronous dispatch withholds unowned proof. Owners: `object_members/handlers.rs`, `owners/`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`.

<a id="adr-0359"></a>
## ADR 0359 — Alternative owner creations are proven jointly
A package function whose same-operation creation calls cover every exit (`if (c) createEffect(…); else createRenderEffect(…);`) requires an owner. Early returns, try, loops, async, `never` and member callees stay uncertifiable. Owner: `owners/missing_owners.rs`.

<a id="adr-0360"></a>
## ADR 0360 — A refused file has no compiler facts
A compile error (`CompilerProviderError::Refused`) is no failure: the file keeps AST and Type Facts, `ExecutionMap::refused` places no site, a violation located or evidenced in it is uncertifiable, and SC9015 names it. ADR 0441 makes a proven refusal an error and keeps this notice for the rest. A refused JSX closure file keeps a `*` package off source (SC9005); a named one fails. A missing or untrusted trace is fatal. Owners: `file_execution_map` (`lib.rs`), `walk` (`dependency_sources.rs`), `diagnostics.rs`.

Native `tsc` 7.1 (the current pin: ADR 0374) via `typescript/sync` (Node), pinned apart from `packages/cli`'s 5.9.3. The CLI has no diagnostic length. Owner: `scripts/lib/tsc-oracle-api.mjs`.

## ADR 0362 — The producer's TypeScript follows the pinned tsrs rev (ADRs 0365, 0374)
The Go producer's pin (b85298b6a81f) is history.

<a id="adr-0363"></a>
## ADR 0363 — Go producer fixes from the Rust port (the Go producer is archived, ADR 0365)
Fixed in the Go producer and ported: one encoder for wire symbol names; the reference index never answers a retained or pruned symbol as empty; a file entering the program invalidates importers with an unresolved import.

## ADR 0364 — No retained analysis across updates until instantiation-stable ids and recorded dependencies
Superseded by ADR 0398: derived state returned with instantiation-stable ids and recorded dependencies, as per-file passes reused by read set.

<a id="adr-0365"></a>
## ADR 0365 — The Type Facts producer is Rust on tsrs; the Go producer is deleted
`apps/solid-typefacts` is a Rust workspace (own `Cargo.lock` and target) over the tsrs checker, pinned to the fork `yumemi-thomas/tsrs@49f5348` (upstream plus one commit exporting two checker functions). It is the only producer, native (`bin/solid-typefacts`, release, fat LTO) and WASM (the `reactor` cdylib in `packages/wasm`). It supersedes the producer part of ADR 0002 and ADR 0362 (TypeScript is now the version the tsrs rev ports, b85298b6a81f); ADR 0361 stands, the oracle being the same release line. Archive: `../sc-worktrees.noindex/tsgo-archive`; versus Go the corpus sweep is 26.8 vs 52.4 s. The differential harness (`docs/typefacts/differential.md`) compares any two producer binaries, defaulting its reference to the archived Go one.

Accepted trades: an unpublished, pinned fork of a single-vendor compiler; clean native build 76 s vs 27 s and incremental 60 s vs 1 s (fat LTO); CMake for mimalloc and an `unsafe` boundary module for tsrs's arena frees. Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts`, `scripts/build-typefacts.sh`, `scripts/typefacts-source-identity.mjs`, `packages/wasm/scripts/build-owned.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0366"></a>
## ADR 0366 — A binder-level symbol's id is a pure function of the program
The id of a symbol proven binder-level is `H(domain, key)`, keeping the `symbol:h:<24 hex>` spelling and the wire. Key: `Intrinsic` (a checker singleton, matched by pointer), else `Global(name)` (an entry of the checker's `globals` table), else `Export` (the declaration's reference is a module export), else `Decl` over the minimum declaration by `(path bytes, name start)`, not merge order.
