A pre-merge binder symbol maps to its merged symbol first (one `JSON`, one `Set`). Private and unique-symbol names are hashed without the checker's counters (`#<classId>@x`, `@sym@<id>`). Which symbol asked first decides nothing. Proof of binder-ness: not `Transient`, or a merge clone (a declaration's binder symbol is recorded as merged into exactly it) with no check flag or derivation link. Anything else, instantiations, `this` clones, mapped and spread properties, takes its exact derived key or no id (ADR 0398) and never shares a binder id; so does an id whose key collides, and a symbol that does not own every declaration it lists (each declaration's binder symbol merges into it, or it is that declaration's local half): a parameter property's parameter shares its node with the property, so only the property keeps the hash. Derived symbols: ADR 0372; multi-origin properties: ADR 0371. A property read through an instantiation or a class instance's `this` type has a derived id. Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/binder_key.rs`, `src/identity.rs`, `tests/binder_ids.rs`.

<a id="adr-0367"></a>
## ADR 0367 — A table's facts do not depend on demand order, batch order or checker warm-up
Superseded by ADR 0398 for check-first: a file is checked first by a checker of its own, not every demanded file in path order by one. Still in force: a type descriptor is its location's type (no batch-wide sharing by symbol id); tsrs runs its canonical history; no checker counter reaches a fact (runtime identities, wire symbol names and library type names drop `#<id>@` and `@<id>`); type text is display only (`descriptor_text_gate`). Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/passes.rs`, `src/durable.rs`, `src/tests/history_order.rs`.

<a id="adr-0368"></a>
## ADR 0368 — A dependency-source program lists the project's roots first, in tsc's order
TypeScript orders a program's files by its root files, and its answers can depend on that order. The generated sibling tsconfig (ADR 0284) listed its roots by path, so closure files under `node_modules` came before `src`: in probus-hk (TanStack Router) TypeScript 7.1, on that program, reports 188 router call errors that `tsc` on the project does not, and the checker took 21 calls as TypeScript-owned (`recovery`), hiding 5 SC9012 findings. Root order causes it, not check order. The project's files now come first: its configured roots in `ParsedCommandLine.fileNames` order (the producer's `ModuleFact.root`), then its other files by path, then the closure. TypeScript then holds the project's files in `tsc`'s order (measured on probus-hk, unproven for every closure). Owners: `dependency_program.rs` (`program_modules`), `dependency_sources.rs` (`program_document`), `apps/solid-typefacts/src/module_graph.rs`.
Review: a root is placed as the loader places it (an extensionless name's first existing extension, a project-reference redirect); a root it cannot place (`unmappedRoots`, protocol 83) refuses the sources (`ProgramPreparationUnavailable`), never path order. A root under `node_modules` stays listed. Every project call's validity is the project's own program's (kept open): `Recovery` there is TypeScript's (silent), `Recovery` only in the generated program is SC9012 `dependency-source-program-call-error`. A `Recovery` call in selected package source to a non-callable callee refuses that package (SC9005). Callee a call (`get()(x)`): the outer call's fact is demanded at the whole call and the producer marks it on a callee-spanning error.

<a id="adr-0369"></a>
## ADR 0369 — Binder-level answers are reused across updates under recorded dependencies
Superseded by ADR 0398 and deleted: a whole per-file pass is reused under its read set, so the binder-level halves (reference spans, entity symbols, symbol declarations kept with `Text`, `Exports`, `Ambient` and `Options` digests) have nothing left to save.

<a id="adr-0370"></a>
## ADR 0370 — A parameter that may reach an undescribed callee is an obligation at its callers
A proven source passed to an analyzed function (project or selected package source, also via `.call`/`.apply`) is SC9012 `forwarded-to-undescribed-callee:<callee>` at the argument when its position may reach an argument SC9012 `package-callee` would report: the parameter, a binding initialized with it, a rest element or spread, or `arguments`, anywhere in the body, or a reached position of a further analyzed function (after a spread, any later one). No link (current callee, unkilled value, the call running, the final callee) is proven, so never a violation. Owner: `ForwardedUncaptured` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`.

<a id="adr-0371"></a>
## ADR 0371 — A multi-origin property's id is its combination and origins, when they fix its type
A union, intersection or spread property is `H("synth", combination, name, origins)`: the binder keys (ADR 0366) of its declarations' own properties (not a late-bound `__computed`), whole, sorted for a union, in written order for an intersection (overloads) and a spread. The container is not keyed, so the key must fix the type (id-keyed consumers read one type): no `Partial` flag; every constituent's own property (resolved member or a generic target's own member) and spread side a declared property or an instantiation of one, never mapped, inherited from a mapped type or spread-merged; each origin a TypeScript interface, type-literal or class member (or parameter property) with a written type (a method: return and parameters), outside any function, mapped or conditional type, with no `this`, `super` or identifier spelling an enclosing type parameter. Else a counter. A `Set` id is never re-resolved; a call keeps its own target; declarations are sorted. Owner: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/binder_key.rs` (`set_key`).

<a id="adr-0372"></a>
## ADR 0372 — A derived symbol keeps its own id
Policy E (STABLE-IDS) rejected. A derived symbol (instantiation, clone, mapped property) may differ in type from its source, and the client joins by id: typed-source tables and the descriptor and callability fallbacks apply one entity's type to the whole id; component identity and write roles take an id's resolved call sites as all its entries. Erased, object methods reached through a cloned or instantiated receiver went silently clean (`MethodAlias.tsx`; rc17: 3 SC2001). Retention gains nothing (ADR 0369). Revisit when those consumers read types per entity and need a census.

<a id="adr-0373"></a>
## ADR 0373 — The stable-id scheme as built (ADRs 0366-0372)
A symbol id is a hash (`symbol:h:`) that names one symbol in every checker of the program: binder keys (0366), set keys (0371), exact derived keys or no id (0398); no erasure of derived symbols, which id-keyed consumers would conflate (0372); roots first (0368). `retainedFiles` stays on the wire at zero. Type-derived facts are reused only as whole per-file passes under their read sets (0398).

<a id="adr-0374"></a>
## ADR 0374 — tsrs 3bbcc754, TypeScript 7.1.0-beta, and the oracle moved with it
The producer pins `yumemi-thomas/tsrs@db57178` (upstream `3bbcc754`, v0.19.0, plus the export commit). Its TypeScript is `3e9236361fa9`, so the tsc oracle moved with it (ADR 0361): typescript 7.1.0-beta. Lib offsets and `symbol:h:` ids moved, so differential tests take only a reference on the same tsrs rev. Supersedes the pins in ADRs 0361, 0362, 0365.

<a id="adr-0378"></a>
## ADR 0378 — A write's legal role needs all of its function's entries
An event-only proof (ADR 0327) decides first. Else an unplaced write takes its function's call sites' and naming positions' role (a nested closure: its own, unless each layer between runs inline or on a fresh stack). An owned-scope entry is a violation if the binding holds only it. An unplaced entry, a recursive JSX render, or other reference to the binding or a named expression's outer one (prop, store, alias, method, open or escaping export): uncertifiable. Owner: `execution_role/write_roles.rs`.

<a id="adr-0379"></a>
## ADR 0379 — `make verify` skips WASM parity for analysis-only changes
When every path the branch changes against origin/main is in `solid-facts`, `solid-reactive-ir`, the v2 rules crate or prose (`verify-delta.mjs --wasm-scope`), `make verify` builds no WASM package and runs no parity gate; CI's `wasm-parity` job (macOS 27, image `xcode-27`; the gate's path rows follow the host OS) runs the gate on every pull request instead, over `wasm-package`'s built files. Parity compares two builds of one analysis, and the WASM-only behaviour there is threads: `Scope::spawn` panics on wasm32-wasip1. Each of those crates' `clippy.toml` disallows the thread APIs, so a new site needs an allow that names its sequential arm. Accepted trade: a 32-bit or WASI-only analysis difference surfaces in CI, not locally. `SOLID_CHECKER_VERIFY_WASM=1` forces the gate. Owners: `scripts/verify.sh`, `scripts/verify-delta.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0380"></a>
## ADR 0380 — The runtime oracle reuses a case's browser half by content
`feedback run --execution-cache` stores the browser half of a run (Vite, Chromium, scenario, frame attribution: raw records only) keyed on everything it reads: sources and manifest, the native development models (`feedbackFacts`, which instrument the sources), the TypeScript error count, scenario and supplied responses, runtime options, browser binary, tooling manifests, the CLI's scripts and Node. A hit re-hashes every recorded runtime input; a changed or missing one is a miss. Findings, observations and automatic warnings are recomputed from the current analysis every run. A failed scenario is never stored. `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs` uses it by default (`--cache off` disables). Owners: `packages/cli/scripts/feedback-execution-cache.mjs`, `feedback-browser.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0381"></a>
## ADR 0381 — Catalog names follow eslint-plugin-solid where accurate; scope is a superset
A rule that maps onto an eslint-plugin-solid rule (0.18.1, its `v2` config) takes the upstream name unless that name no longer describes what the checker proves; the former name stays a `RULE_ALIASES` entry. Where upstream reports a shape we do not, we add it, subject to invariants 1 and 2: a shape `tsc` reports or the runtime refutes is not adopted, and the refusal is pinned in `fixtures/tsc-oracle` or `fixtures/ownership-cases`. Where we prove more than upstream, we keep it. The policy covers the semantic rules only; the policy rules ADR 0110 retired stay retired. Owners: `rust/dialects/solid-v2/rules/src/rules.rs`, `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/dialect.rs` (`RULE_ALIASES`), `docs/rules/README.md`.

<a id="adr-0382"></a>
## ADR 0382 — A retained daemon keeps its session across an edit
The resolution witness observes program files outside the tracked sources only (a tracked edit is an overlay update; observing it made every edit a rebuild). It reads the `Modules` demand `distinctBareImports` (protocol 84): non-relative imports, one row per importer, text and resolved, included and symlink path, so a `.d.ts` repeating `import("pkg")` no longer overflows the 64 MiB frame and drops the daemon to one-shot. An update that keeps the program's files keeps the witness and adds the edited files' imports: a superset of a fresh one. A dangling symlink's cache digest is its link text (inference still refuses on it). Owners: `daemon.rs` `answer`, `daemon_resolution.rs`, `inferred_host_input_digest`, `module_graph.rs`.

<a id="adr-0383"></a>
## ADR 0383 — A retained IR build is proven equal to a fresh one
`SOLID_CHECKER_IR_SHADOW` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/shadow.rs`): after every incremental build, rebuild fresh and compare `Program` table by table. `panic` names the first table and row (process tests, `.cargo/config.toml`); any other value logs, drops the caches and serves the fresh program (the daemon, opt-in: it costs one fresh build per edit). The cache domain holds `RuleOptions` and `runtime_symbol_redirects`, and one predicate decides "TypeScript unchanged" (`cache::typescript_unchanged`): both stages' reuse had ignored them (`incremental_staleness_process.rs`). `scripts/ir-edit-fuzz.mjs` (random edits, daemon against one-shot) and `scripts/ir-read-lint.mjs` (a ratchet on raw cross-file reads) gate `make verify`.

<a id="adr-0384"></a>
## ADR 0384 — The IR's project-wide facts are folds of per-file contributions
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base`: a path is a `FileId`; a base index is `key -> [(FileId, values)]`, folded from `fn(&FileView, &BuildConstants)` contributions that by type read one file. An update replaces the changed files' contributions and returns the exact Δ of keys. Indexes: references, callees, module edges, members, hazards, definer keys, items. The eval, dispatch, reflective-definition and prototype-write vetoes, assigned member names, `MemberStability`, `ModuleCensus` and the namespace census read them; a build reads a fresh fold. Shadow mode updates the previous build's indexes and compares them with the refold: a difference is a shadow mismatch.

<a id="adr-0385"></a>
## ADR 0385 — Derived answers are memos with recorded reads
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base/memo`: a memo table maps a key to a value computed through a `Ctx`, the only way its code reads the build: a file section (source, compiler map, entity rows; the key's own file is free), an L1 index key, a file's slots, a build constant, another memo. Reads are kept as `QueryId`s with the answer's fingerprint (for a memo, the revision its answer last changed in) and a reverse map. A build's changed inputs (section fingerprints, the store's index Δ) mark readers dirty and their readers maybe-dirty; an answer is reused, verified through its memo reads in recorded order, or recomputed, and an equal recomputed value stops propagation. Keys and values are `Canonical` (no hash containers). Only context-free answers are memos, so reads form a DAG; a cycle answers the table's fail-closed value, is reported and empties the store. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_VERIFY_REUSED` recomputes each reused answer and names the table and key that differ.

<a id="adr-0386"></a>
## ADR 0386 — Member resolution is memoized; an unported read is a probe
`object_members/memo.rs`, `assigned.rs`: object-member targets, spread handlers and literal receivers are one memo per file slot, `ReceiverClosed` one per receiver (owner, literal, declaration, symbol), `PrototypeOwnerAt` one per `R.prototype` write, and the assigned-member census one project memo. The lookup's `Targets`, `SpreadTargets`, literal-receiver set and `AssignedMembers` are assemblies of these answers in file order (a later slot of a path still overwrites an earlier one). A receiver's uses come from the base `symbol_uses` index plus TypeScript's references of its symbols, and the host-prototype proof from the `spellings` index, never a scan of every file. A table not ported yet is read through a **probe** (`base/memo/probes.rs`): a keyed question the lookup answers from its table, recorded as an input with the answer's fingerprint; the store asks every recorded probe again in `begin`, so an answer that moved dirties exactly its readers, and a probe may not read a memo.

<a id="adr-0387"></a>
## ADR 0387 — Project-wide answers are memos; what is not yet narrow reads the whole program
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/project_memos.rs`: the effect-apply tables, exported primitive consts, withheld initialization mentions, parameter-completion calls, never-returning calls, the exempt store-key census and the any-file vetoes (intrinsics, string code, tuple iteration, the `bind` patch, objects assigned into, router parameters) are memo tables (ids 32+). Never-returning calls are per file and read only that file's source and its `Signatures` section (each entity's location and whether its resolved call never returns or asserts). The rest read the lookup through `Ctx::project`, recorded as `Input::Project`: it moves on any file change, any TypeScript table not reported unchanged (`cache::typescript_unchanged`), or a closed-program flip. Such an answer is reused only across builds in which nothing moved, and narrows as the tables under it port. Fixed points (parameter completion) and the recursive initialization proof stay one whole-program answer each (cone recomputation is S7). Answers are sorted vectors; the lookup keeps per-build hash views. Premise: module-resolution attestations change only with a source edit or a cache-domain change.

<a id="adr-0388"></a>
## ADR 0388 — The component census is memos
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/census.rs` (ids 64+): component identity (per file, and per function whose direct JSX return needs its references), call-graph entries per function, and per file the tag renders, prop render sites, compound holders' values, lazy `default` candidates and witnesses and result-access forwarding are memos (ADR 0385). An identity reads its own file, the reference index's key of its binding symbol, and the files holding those references, calls or tags with their per-file answers: an edit elsewhere leaves it unrun. Calls and tags come from the L1 callee index, keyed by the symbol a call's callee resolves to (a build with runtime redirects corrects it through the redirected calls). Initialization reads ADR 0387's memo, the module census and `eval` ADR 0386's probes; other unported tables (functions by symbol, primitives, TypeScript symbols and descriptors, route values, namespace hand-outs, the `Object.assign` and prototype-write vetoes) are adapter memos over `Ctx::project`. The entity section also covers resolved-call declarations and references. The slots of one path are one source (the join checks each), so the memos read a path's first slot.

<a id="adr-0389"></a>
## ADR 0389 — The client patches its symbol closure from exact per-file changes
The per-file passes (ADR 0398) answer `referenceChangesExact`: every id whose `symbols` answer may differ from the previous table's (a moved merged row, a moved reference bucket's holders, every scratch-answered id). Inexact for a first table, a restarted merge, a rebuilt reference index, the sequential run. The client then patches its closure (`typefacts/src/session/closure.rs`): it asks changed and newly reached ids, and references of tier ids that changed or entered; a `ClosureIndex` of counts (seeds, alias in-edges, roots per path, one-hop aliases) makes the cost the changed paths'. The client asks every retained analysis for them. `TYPEFACTS_VERIFY_SYMBOL_PATCH=1` compares each patch with a full closure (the IR edit fuzz sets it). The TypeScript index patch takes an added or removed import alias; the balanced idle policy keeps those indexes.

<a id="adr-0390"></a>
## ADR 0390 — Returned-callback uses are a memo; the digest keeps its source hashes
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/returned_uses.rs` (table 128): the uses of the function a factory call (`createReaction`, `lazy`, `lazyModule`, `mapArray`, `repeat`) returns are one memo per call, as `Call`, `Argument(i)`, `Preload` and `Jsx` uses. It reads the call's file, the exact symbols holding the result (the bindings it initializes, then bindings initialized from one of them, in any file) and, per symbol, two L1 keys: TypeFacts' references and the new `use_spans` index (spans of callees, arguments, tags, `preload` objects and alias initializers whose entity is the symbol). Only files holding such a span are read. What a use inherits (the callback primitive it is handed to, that callback's owner) depends on tables that are not memos, so the consumer assembles it (`callback_owners.rs`) into a per-build cache.

<a id="adr-0391"></a>
## ADR 0391 — The invocation-flow answers are memos
`solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role/flow_memos.rs` (ids 96+): each file's ADR 0319 answers and event-only census are memos, each literal computed from an empty context. Per file, not per literal: on solid-groove 29k per-literal entries cost 150 ms of bookkeeping and lock contention; the walks cost 25 ms. The walk runs over a world trait: the lookup's for a query nested in another computation, or the memo context, where every file the walk holds is a recorded read, component identity the census's per-file memo, the primitive at a call the census adapter, `eval` the hazard probe, a tag's component its entity row and the `FunctionForSymbol` probe, a primitive slot the call's signature section. Call-site roles, project callees at a parameter, window-inheriting callbacks, member tags' declared methods and absent-member vetoes read `Ctx::project`. A budget-bound walk records what it read. The lookup assembles them.

<a id="adr-0392"></a>
## ADR 0392 — The returned-callback and returned-source digest sections bind answers
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/returned_invocations.rs` (tables 160, 161). The binding-chain walk (`returned_primitive_invocation`) is a memo per file reading the resolved binding through a probe (`BindingNameAt`, asked only where the file has an entity row or reference at the span), that binding's file and `CallPrimitives`. Returned sources (ADR 0222, 0287) are a memo per file through `Ctx::project`. `returned-callbacks` digests each positive answer some consumer can read (an execution or owner for an argument at the call's count) and each factory call's sites; `returned-source-inputs` each positive answer and what a fragment reads of the creating call (`returned_source_facts`). An inline factory callback with a site in another file is classified in that file's context, so it brings back every participating file's source hash.

<a id="adr-0393"></a>
## ADR 0393 — Project passes solve on kept state and publish per key
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base/fixpoint`: three solvers keep nodes, ordered definitions and values between builds, keyed by stable keys (`NodeKey`, `DenseIndex`; slot order never reaches an answer): a keyed fold of per-file rows in path rank (K1), a monotone least fixed point re-solved on the changed definitions' forward cone (K2) and a Jacobi loop replayed from per-round trajectories (K3, lemma 4′); an ordered fold (K4) was removed in the 2026-10-25 audit: it had no production user. A cone over a quarter of the nodes, reordered surviving files, a K3 stopped at its cap and a poisoned store solve fresh. Output is published per key (`Input::Derived`, `memo/derived.rs`): an equal value keeps its revision, a moved key marks its memo readers after `begin`; a read before its pass published fails closed. Shadow and verify-reused modes compare each kept solve with a fresh one, naming the first differing key. Proof of use: the resolver's handoff join (`Resolver::join_handoffs`, derived table and solver 224).

<a id="adr-0394"></a>
## ADR 0394 — The retained daemon verifies its answers while idle
`daemon/idle_shadow.rs`. After a materialized answer and 300 ms idle, a background-priority thread opens a fresh state from the same request, answers the same check and compares findings JSON, status and notice byte for byte and the IR `Program` table by table (ADR 0383's `first_difference`). Comparable only when both builds saw the same inputs (tracked source and config hashes, package inputs); otherwise inconclusive, never a mismatch. A request cancels the check (a flag read between the fresh answer's phases); it restarts after the next debounce. It runs only while the machine has room for a second build: available memory (Linux `MemAvailable`; macOS free, inactive and speculative pages; purgeable ones are already among those) of at least the daemon's resident size plus `SOLID_CHECKER_IDLE_SHADOW_MIN_FREE_MB` (default 1024), whatever the daemon's own limit; the watchdog does not hold a running check to that limit while the room lasts, and abandons it first when it does not. One connection per request leaves no push channel, so the next request delivers a mismatch: logged (stderr, `<socket>.shadow.log`, first differing table and row), the whole state dropped (memos, indexes, per-file caches, producer), the answer opened from scratch, response header `recovered`. Accepted: the stale answer was already served. Off by default, like oxlint's language server (each check costs about a cold check of CPU); `SOLID_CHECKER_IDLE_SHADOW=1` turns it on, `panic` ends the daemon (tests, CI); off when the inline shadow is on. Debug builds carry `_FAULT` and `_DELAY_MS` hooks. Cost: a transient second build (+0.8 GB on solid-groove, +1.4 GB on app-game since ADR 0399), none on the next edit once it ends; an edit arriving mid-check measured +10% on app-game.

<a id="adr-0395"></a>
## ADR 0395 — Interprocedural summaries are canonical sets
`SummaryReads` (`interproc.rs`) used to keep or drop a row by arrival: a later dominating row was kept beside the weaker one before it, so the rows, and the `origin`, `origin_context`, `declaration`, `kind` and structured-return label ties picked, depended on worklist order. It is now, per key `(display, origin, declaration, transported)`, the rows no other row dominates, where domination is the product order on `local`, `conditional` and a rank (hops from the seed, origin, declaration, display, symbol, kind, context, owner, undecided). A product order, not "flags, then a tie-break": every propagation transfer is monotone in each component, so a dropped row only ever yields rows the survivor's yield dominates, and the fixed point is order-free. Rows sit in canonical order, so emission's stable sort and structured returns' first row read the canonical first row; delta propagation reads an arrival log.

<a id="adr-0396"></a>
## ADR 0396 — The accessor-provenance resolver is built from per-file memos on kept folds
`solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance/resolver_memos.rs` (tables 240–246, derived tables 240–247, state 240). The resolver's walks run over a world trait (`resolver_world.rs`): the resolver over the lookup, or the memo context, where each answer is recorded. Its stages are memos per file (bindings, parameter inputs, receiver census, origins with blocked contexts) and per context (object contexts); their rows are folded in path order on kept K1 folds, whose output the next stage reads per key: binding locations, direct uses, providers, the handoff join's `HandoffUses` (K2, ADR 0393). The pass publishes the accessors, source kinds and each file's lookup answers (binding resolutions, call primitives, context targets, iteration receivers), so a reader moves with its keys; a parameter channel's Type Facts half and a vetoed dependency member are adapters. A duplicated path or foreign symbol names builds fresh; shadow and verify-reused modes compare both builds.

<a id="adr-0397"></a>
## ADR 0397 — A stage chunk records what it read; the cross-file digests are gone
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base/reads.rs`: a per-file stage chunk (discovery, reachability, local accesses, graph, results, owner fragments) records its cross-file reads while it computes: a memo answer read outside every memo (table, query, the revision it last changed in) and a whole-table view of the lookup (`Section`: components, member and spread targets, literal receivers, flow answers, event literals, never-returning calls, returned sources and callback sites, the dispatch and reflective hazards, reflective prototype writes, imported primitive consts), fingerprinted from its value once per build. A per-build lookup cache (`Recorded`) and a parallel helper's workers hand their reads to whoever reads the result, so a reader records what the one that filled it did. A chunk is reused while its file is unchanged (`unchanged_paths`) and every read answers as recorded. `cross_file_proof_digest`, `member_resolution_digest`, the exempt-store census memo, `transport_identity_matches`, `retained_source_paths` and `changed_source_symbols` are deleted: result chunks record their transported-call, source-origin and accessor reads (`interproc/transport_reads.rs`); graph chunks the accessor entries they embed (closing ADR 0383's latent key); local accesses diff source discovery's tables by symbol. Kept: `call_role_syntax` (result chunks read other files' syntax outside the lookup); the late aggregates' whole-stage reuse is removed (saved under 5 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit); the leaf-owner and static-API passes stay unchunked for the same reason as the first. Reachability survives the balanced idle policy. Discovery's chunk reuse is removed (its unchanged-file identity stays): saved under 9 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_READ_MISSES=1` names each rerun's first moved read.

<a id="adr-0398"></a>
## ADR 0398 — Per-file checkers with exact ids are the producer's only mode
A file's facts are a pass: a fresh checker of its own checks the file first, then answers its demands, async facts and reference scan, and computes the rows of every id they name; the program's other non-declaration files get a reference pass. Answers that read every file (reference spaces, the default-library write census, structural-accessor descriptor suppression) are deferred and completed from the merged passes. Ids: binder (0366) and set (0371) keys, else the exact derived key (root and canonical type context, `derived_key.rs`); no key, no id (an absent fact), never a counter. A pass is reused while its read set (the forward closure over recorded resolutions of the file and of every global contributor) is unchanged path by path in `Text`, `Exports` and `Resolutions` (every resolution record of the file and the files its edges land on), with `Options`, `Ambient` (program order), its census answers and its own demands; the rest run on a pool (`SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_WORKERS`). This holds across a rebuilt chain (an import edit, a deleted or new file, a rebase): a kept read set's members kept their edges, so it is the same paths (computed again and compared); a newly imported file enters only read sets that reach the edited file. Two passes minting one id for two keys make the table the sequential shared-identity run's. Supersedes ADR 0367's check-first and ADR 0369; the selection flags are gone (no escape hatch). Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/per_file.rs`, `passes.rs`, `derived_key.rs`, `identity.rs`, `rust/crates/typefacts/src/session.rs`.

<a id="adr-0399"></a>
## ADR 0399 — The idle check's fresh build ends its producer after the facts
The fresh state (ADR 0394) closes its producer once the facts and the resolution witness are answered: package inputs, analysis and emission read only those, and a closed session fails instead of relaunching, so a later exchange is inconclusive, never a verdict. The fresh state is dropped before the comparison, which serializes one row pair at a time. Daemon RSS peak, with the slice's sharing commits: app-game 4.5 to 3.6 GB, solid-groove 2.3 to 1.9 GB. Owners: `daemon.rs` (`AfterFacts`), `daemon/idle_shadow.rs`, `shadow.rs` (`compare_tables`).

<a id="adr-0400"></a>
## ADR 0400 — The daemon's residue per edit
Outside the reactive IR and the producer an app-game body edit cost resync 19 ms, package inputs 23, solve 17, emit+cache 12, and a 5.5 MB answer per linted file. Now 10, 13, 6, 10 (release, interleaved against the parent, loaded machine). Each is exact: (1) `snapshot_of` placed each location by scanning its file; it computes each file's line starts once per answer, O(log n) (line starts kept per source `Arc` were removed: saved under 3 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit, groove 7 ms faster without). (2) The package boundary read each import's manifest and scanned every file per import: one read per manifest, a set of held files. (3) The resolution witness drops absent entries under an absent ancestor (the ancestor's entry fails first) and resolves a directory entry's canonical path as its parent's, once per check; tracked sources' and directories' stamps are read on six workers. (4) The inference closure's digests and the imported manifests' hashes were kept by file stamp; removed: saved under 6 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit. Kept again by ADR 0600. (5) `--only-file <abs path>` (`--format json`) answers the report with only the findings that can matter to that file: project-scoped, no path, primary or related location equal after lexical normalization, or a path it cannot settle (relative). Positions are recorded as the report is written (`FindingIndex`); everything else, and each kept finding's bytes, is the full report's. The ESLint adapter sends it for the native checker only, and projects per file as before. An app-game answer is 12 KB, not 5.5 MB; parse in Node 5.3 ms to 0.03; a repeat request 36 ms to 17.
Accepted: resync keeps a floor of about 10 ms on app-game (6,000 witness stats; every request, cache hit included) until an event watcher can prove what did not change; the boundary still re-derives per request (about 6 ms).

<a id="adr-0401"></a>
## ADR 0401 — The invocation-flow walks read what they ask
`execution_role/flow_memos.rs`, `indexes/callee_world.rs`. Of ADR 0391's whole-program questions, a project callee at a parameter and a member tag's declared method resolve over a `CalleeWorld` (the lookup, or the memo context: the call file's member targets, literal receivers and entity rows, the `FunctionForSymbol`, `BindingAt`, `ClassFor` and `PathFile` probes, the files they name, the assigned-member memo; the lookup under runtime redirects). An absent member reads that memo and a `DefinedReflectively` probe; a window-inheriting callback the dialect and the effect-apply memo. A site role is a memo per file, call and the walk's sorted `classifying` context (table 256), the one left on `Ctx::project`. A body edit now recomputes 1 flow and 1 event answer (app-game: was 879 and 715; groove: 466, 419) and reruns every site role (5,278; 2,199): IR 1,373 to 1,275 ms on app-game, groove unchanged, one-shot +1-3%. ADR 0387's premise holds: the daemon reopens its state when the resolution witness fails or a tracked directory changes, and an import edit (ADR 0398) is a source edit.

<a id="adr-0402"></a>
## ADR 0402 — Transport and the member-invocation refresh are kept between builds
`solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance/transport_memos.rs` (table 384), `interproc/refresh.rs` (state 384). Transport: each file's call answers are a memo over the resolver's memo world (ADR 0396), which serves a query after construction with stage 4's blocked contexts (derived 384). A row keeps the targets that need a summary node; the graph's function set decides them at the fold, so the memo reads nothing the stage computes. Refresh (ADR 0320): each file's invocation contexts, unmodeled uses and forwarding edges are kept with its recorded reads and every callee answer they read, re-asked before reuse (the function table and a callee's syntax are not recorded reads). Asked in every build: a consumer's tracked-or-fresh proof (never cached) and each edge's callee among the nodes. The 8-round forwarding loop runs on the K3 solver (ADR 0393), truncation replayed. The node index's in-place patch was removed (saved under 5 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit). A path listed twice runs fresh; shadow and verify-reused modes compare with a fresh build and poison on a difference. Import edit: refresh 246 to 9 ms (app-game), transport 90 to 3.

<a id="adr-0403"></a>
## ADR 0403 — Source discovery's post-passes keep their rows
`solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery/post_passes.rs`, `post_rows.rs`, `trace.rs` (solver slot 320). Each post-pass is rows plus a fold. A file's rows (per TypeScript path for typed sources, per file for the rest, components included) are kept while the file is one of the build's `unchanged_paths`, its memo and section reads hold, and every other answer it recorded (`trace::Dep`: another file's facts, references, declarations, entity sites, the tuple veto, an earlier pass's table at a key; a symbol's function and a binding resolution are the lookup's hand-out reads, ADR 0408) still answers so. Each distinct answer is asked once per build, and only its readers recompute. Folds run in file order with each pass's own rule. The groupings are kept K1 folds (ADR 0393). The alias rounds are solved from their sources over kept candidate indexes: a Jacobi round's only adding rows hold a key the round before added, and pass 8's Gauss-Seidel sweeps fire each row at its earliest (sweep, position). Pass 10's forwarded-props loop replays, because its unresolved sets depend on round order. A K2 cone is not used there. Pass 4's fallback for an identifier return with no entity takes the least-located same-name declaration, not a `HashMap` hit; no fixture or corpus project reaches it. Duplicated paths, dependency-source builds and a moved domain (closed program, caller proof, redirects) recompute every row. Shadow and verify-reused modes compare with a run on empty state.

<a id="adr-0404"></a>
## ADR 0404 — The owner graph is patched per file
`solid-reactive-ir/src/owners/owner_state.rs`, `owner_emission.rs`: the owner stage keeps its fragments, resolved edges, symbol winners, final contexts and each file's emission between builds, keyed by `(interned path, span)`. A recomputed fragment equal to the kept one changes nothing downstream; otherwise its own edges are re-resolved, and every file's only when its shape (node spans, symbols, generator flags) moved. Context bits are solved by the plain worklist in file order on the patched graph (the cone solve on two kept `Monotone` solvers was removed: saved under 5 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit); a node whose context moved dirties its file, and a file's emission also re-runs when the closed provider census or an `onSettled` callback's file moved, and its lookup reads fold into the fragment's proof. Fragment reads now merge once (`Reads::extend_all`): the per-file `extend` sorted the whole set each time (9 of 14 ms on app-game).

<a id="adr-0405"></a>
## ADR 0405 — Final ordering is cheap because its two whole-program answers are chunked and the memo sweep is incremental
Measured on app-game, the 101 ms of final ordering was 40 ms `gate_diagnostics` and 55 ms `end_memos`, not row sorting (`into_program` 0.8 ms). The gate's cost was recomputing the two whole-program apply memos (`effect_apply_calls`, `effect_apply_regions`) each build. `effect_execution.rs` now keeps, in the retained store (`SemanticLookup::with_kept_state`, solver ids 448 and 449), a chunk per file with its recorded reads (ADR 0397), its own file's facts included (another file can re-point its rows): the file's calls that carry a defer option, and the callbacks it supplies and may escape to; the memo folds them (a callback's region needs a supplying site anywhere, an escape only adds `Unknown`). The proofs of the defer calls stay computed fresh (they read other files' bodies, which a chunk would not see). A one-shot build, a probe and a path held by two slots keep nothing. The memo store's `end` released unread inputs by scanning all of them (250k) and walked its live set in ordered maps; it now looks only at inputs that lost a reader or were allocated since the last end (`Graph::unread`), and its graph and live set are hash containers. Rows keep their production order and the stable sort by location (`into_program`); per-path run assembly was not built, as it would save under 1 ms.

<a id="adr-0406"></a>
## ADR 0406 — The memo begin and the read sets cost what changed
`base/memo/build.rs`, `base/reads.rs`. (1) A file's TypeFacts fingerprint parts are kept with the identity of the rows each digests: its entities by address and length, sound because the store keeps the last build's table (every allocation named stays alive and immutable), and its entity-symbol rows by `Arc` (`PathSymbols`, changed only through `make_mut`, so the store's clone forces a copy). The same rows reuse the digest; anything else, a full table replace included, digests again. The producer's change list is not read, so a path it leaves unnamed is still seen (`bookkeeping_process`). References are still digested every build: they are assembled per build from the whole symbol table. Shadow and verify-reused modes digest each reused part again and compare. (2) A read set is sorted runs shared by `Arc` plus a tail of at most 64; adding a set adds its runs by pointer, so the stage reads every result chunk carries are neither copied nor sorted per chunk; past 16 runs the smaller half merge. A check may ask a read held twice twice. (3) `SymbolUses`, `Spellings` and `GlobalWritten` are no longer probes: the store keeps their L1 indexes, whose Δ marks exactly the moved keys. The other probes are re-asked: their answers depend on other files' rows, and most of their cost builds tables the build needs anyway. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_BOOKKEEPING_LOG` logs these costs (the begin phases then run on one thread, so a time is CPU).

<a id="adr-0407"></a>
## ADR 0407 — The interprocedural fixed points stay whole; graph chunks re-ask the function table
`interproc/reads.rs`, `structured_returns.rs`, `graph.rs`. The structured-return Jacobi loop (up to nodes+1 rounds over every node) had no reader since contract emission went (D2): `StructuredReturnUnresolved` reads only the property's file, the import's symbol chain and the declaring files' syntax. It is deleted, so no K3 replay is needed. The parameter-member obligation asks its pure conjuncts cheapest first. `fixedPointsMs` times each pass: on an app-game import edit forwarding, calls, dispatch, factory and returned propagation take 2.3 ms together (seeds: returned 9, transported 3.6), so their cone solve (S7 design §5) is not built. A body edit that keeps the late inputs skips the stage. A graph chunk records which function a callee symbol names and each ADR 0285 forward decision (`GraphCalleeRead`, removed by ADR 0408) and is reused only while each answers so: a written callee binding in another file had left a forward stale.

<a id="adr-0408"></a>
## ADR 0408 — What the lookup hands a chunk of another file is a recorded read
`indexes/hand_outs.rs` (tables 640-643), `base/reads.rs`, `cache.rs`. The role machine (and any lookup reader) reaches another file through `function_for_symbol`, `binding_at_reference`, `class_for_symbol`, `file_by_path`, `symbol_references`, a function's callers, a returned use's or binding's file, then reads that file's syntax, compiler map and entity rows directly; nothing recorded that. Asked in a recording, each is now the question's answer (a symbol's bucket of `functions_by_symbol` answers, the asking file's binding rows, else a probe memo; absent answers included) plus each handed file's four section fingerprints, deduplicated per frame and asked of the store once per build. `call_role_syntax` (ADR 0397) is deleted. Two aggregations drop these reads: the stage reads a result chunk inherits (now one proof per build, checked once), and the namespace census and resolver builds. Reachability's whole-stage comparison (`same_reachability_ast`) now compares every AST table, not twelve: a same-length `&&` to `||` in a callee kept a stale role. Route component values are a section. Removed: `GraphCalleeRead`, the refresh rows' callee re-asks. Re-parses (completion covers, `synchronously_unconditional_calls`): a chunk re-parses its own file, or another reached through a hand-out; the rest run in memos or unchunked passes. App-game same-length TSX edit: result reads 143 to 16 ms; body +4%, cold within noise.

<a id="adr-0409"></a>
## ADR 0409 — The memo store's hot path is sharded and records inputs without fingerprints
`base/memo.rs`, `base/memo/build.rs`, `base/memo/locks.rs`. An input read records no fingerprint: `begin` (and `invalidate`) push input changes, and the recorded value was never compared; each input is recorded once per entry. Reverse edges are applied in a batch (`Graph::flush`) before `mark` and `end`, their only readers; `mark` skips it when no changed input is interned. Tables and polled keys are 16 key-hash shards (a key's query id names its shard), on the heap: a store built on the idle check's small stack overflowed. The store's locks are parking_lot's. A first build of the generated 1,000-file corpus waited on these locks for more thread time than it worked (14 cores); first IR 1.68 to 1.36 of the main merge base (CI's ratio gate is 1.25). The rest of the cold-build gap is the memo stages' per-entry cost and the kept solvers, not locks.

<a id="adr-0410"></a>
## ADR 0410 — The producer's per-edit work follows the edit
`apps/solid-typefacts`: `identity.rs` (`ExportScan`), `completion.rs`, `passes.rs`, `per_file.rs`, `inputs.rs`. (1) Exported identities are kept per module with each root's lasting stand-in (`binder_key::lasting_root`: a binder symbol owning its declarations, or the least binder symbol merging into a merge clone; one symbol per stand-in in every checker of a chain). A parse-reusing update rescans the edited module and the modules reaching it; others (and every module after a rebuilt chain, on the pass workers) as before. (2) A pass prepares its run's entity rows and records which rows the deferred answers (spaces, library writes, structural suppression) change; a run's answers are asked again only when its output changed, a changed output's references name an id it asks, or a symbol it reads changed structural standing. (3) After parse-reusing updates the read-set graph, global contributors and census are kept; only replaced files and paths whose export digests moved are compared. (4) A compact demand group keeps its one expansion; kept passes hold it and compare by identity. (5) Text digests after a rebuilt chain are computed on workers; read sets are compared as bitsets. Each kept answer is checked against a recomputation under `SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_VERIFY_REUSE`. App-game: body edit update 35 to 3 ms, analyze 104 to 18; an import edit's update stays about 200 ms (the re-parse: tsrs reuses a file in place only with the same imports, and handing parsed files to a new chain breaks the per-chain file regions).

<a id="adr-0411"></a>
## ADR 0411 — A cold build's memo bookkeeping is accepted for incremental edits
`base/memo.rs`, `base/memo/derived.rs`, `base/memo/build.rs`, `base/memo/serial_digest.rs`, `base/index.rs`, `base/reads.rs`, `pipeline.rs`. Cuts: an install interns its inputs in 16 hash shards and queues its reads per table shard, never taking the graph lock; the graph is 16 shards that take the queues and apply reverse edges (sorted runs, readers a sorted `Vec`) on the analysis workers at `mark` and `end`; `end` skips the live walk when every entry was asked, and walks memo reads only (an input read's fingerprint is `u64::MAX`); derived tables are 16 key shards; index folds group per file on the workers, and a first build folds its four eager indexes side by side; the Signatures section digests a tagged binary serialization (field names, variant indices, lengths) instead of JSON; read-set merges sort adaptively. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_WORKERS=<n>` caps the analysis workers to reproduce a smaller runner. Accepted: on a generated 1,000-file corpus a first IR stays 1.19x (14 cores) to 1.35x (four workers) of a build with no memo tables, the memo stages' per-entry bookkeeping and kept solvers; on real projects the same machinery makes edits 3-9x faster and their first IR faster than before (app-game body edit 3,930 to 457 ms, first IR 1,636 to 837 ms).

<a id="adr-0412"></a>
## ADR 0412 — An edit reruns the chunks whose reads moved; the balanced policy keeps them
`interproc/reads.rs`, `transport_reads.rs`, `indexes/components.rs`, `census.rs` (`component_spans`, 91), `hand_outs.rs`, `runtime_configuration.rs`, `cache.rs`. (1) A result chunk's proof is its own reads, the result view's dependencies, and each entry it asks of the stage's tables: transport, the resolver's origins and transported reads, discovery's accessors and source kinds (`AccessorsRead`, `SourceKindsRead`; no other path to them). What building the resolver, transport, discovery and the refresh read, and what a recomputed graph chunk read, is no longer part of it: one binding moved in the edited file reran all 1,611. Narrowing it exposed reads no chunk recorded: `hazard_present` (the eval hazard of nested member dispatch) is now its probe's answer, and the alias-set filter of `callee_symbols` reads the callee symbol's declarations and their files. Instrumented over coverage, the process tests, app-game and groove, a result chunk reaches no other whole-program lookup table. (2) A question about one file's component identities reads `component_spans` of that file, not the whole census: an import edit reran 1,406 local-access and 1,444 owner chunks. (3) The exported-primitive-consts section and memo are deleted: the imported constant's file is a `binding_at_reference` hand-out. (4) The runtime-configuration scan keeps each asked symbol's identity with its alias chain and asks again only those whose chain read a row that moved (`FactTable::symbols_with_moved_declarations`, from the index leaves two tables do not share): 42 to 3 ms. (5) Timings: `namespaceHandOuts` held the memo store's begin (the census is 5 ms) and `reusePlan` the warm-up (`memoWarm`, 33 ms; the plan is under 1 ms). (6) `Balanced` keeps the graph and result chunks (about 95 ms per app-game edit for about 50 MB; the daemon already exceeds the 15% limit below about 19 GB of RAM either way). Open: a prefix or import edit renumbers the file's local symbol ids, which moves their `hand_out_function_bucket` reads (110 graph chunks on app-game).

<a id="adr-0413"></a>
## ADR 0413 — The static prepass's file rules are per-file chunks, and their reads are per-file
`static_rules.rs`, `late_chunks.rs` (new), `pipeline.rs`, `cache.rs`, `timings.rs`, and the per-file questions of `base/memo/build.rs`, `base/reads.rs`, `execution_role/flow_memos.rs`, `indexes.rs`, `indexes/{completion,hand_outs,project_memos}.rs`, `interproc/reads.rs`, `object_members.rs`. The prepass's three file rules (props destructure, primitive value references, primitive result methods) walked every file on every build. Each file's output is a chunk (`late_chunks::FileChunks`), reused only while the file is unchanged (`unchanged_paths`, source hash, compiler semantics), its props-discovery answers hold (`DiscoveryRead`) and its recorded lookup reads hold (`ReadProof`); a kept chunk replays its reads, a moved one reruns. A member target, a literal receiver and a never-returning call are asked of their file's memo, and a flow site role and a parameter-completion slot are per-file memos (`project_file_recorded`), so an edit moves no whole-table section for every chunk. The after-await rule and the leaf, owner, API and server passes stay whole-program. `reactiveIrReuse.ownerMs` emits the owner stage's four sub-timers, which were kept but never emitted.
Static prepass: app-game 106 to 39 ms, groove 37 to 15.

<a id="adr-0414"></a>
## ADR 0414 — The memo begin's index builds and the warm-up's component lane use the analysis pool
`indexes.rs` (`declaration_symbols`, the warm-up lanes), `indexes/callees.rs` (`bindings_by_reference`), `indexes/components.rs` (`functions_by_symbol`). (1) The whole-program indexes that the first probe or warm-up lane touches are built on the analysis pool with the serial builds' answers: bindings by reference per file, then per path shard (one path is one shard, so each key's first writer is unchanged), then completed per path; declaration symbols per path run, a second run of a path merged in the table's order; functions by symbol per file, merged in file order. (2) The warm-up's components lane, last to finish once the other lanes are done, takes the whole pool rather than its share of the lanes. (3) Not built then: partial re-attestation of import identities. An edited file's ambient module or reference directive can change another file's resolution, so an unchanged importer is exact only with the producer's word on its resolution inputs (open-work section 2); ADR 0419 takes that word for in-place updates.
App-game code edit: memo begin 72 to 45 ms, warm-up 32 to 26.

<a id="adr-0415"></a>
## ADR 0415 — A source file's local ids survive an insert above them
`apps/solid-typefacts/src/binder_key.rs` (`LocalChains`, the `Local` key), `identity.rs`, `derived_key.rs`. An unexported binder symbol whose minimum declaration is in a non-`.d.ts` file has key `Local(path, chain, name)`, not `Decl`'s name span. A chain hashes the declaration's position (its enclosing named scope's position, its kind, its name's text and its rank among that scope's declarations of that kind and text) and its own source text. No two declarations of a file share a position (by induction on the nesting), so no two share a chain; the `Local` hash input starts with a NUL, which no other key's input does. A key survives an edit only while its declaration's own text and position hold: an inserted earlier declaration of the same kind and text moves the later ranks, and the text keeps a displaced id from naming another declaration (`an_inserted_same_named_declaration_hands_out_no_id`, `identical_declarations_in_one_scope_get_distinct_ids`). Re-resolution walks the file, and `symbol_for` still requires the resolved symbol's key to be the requested one. A `.d.ts`, a declaration no scope scan reaches (a JSDoc tag), a synthesized position, or two declarations sharing a name span keep `Decl`. An edit inside a declaration moves its own id and its enclosing declarations' ids; nested declarations keep theirs (`an_edit_inside_a_declaration_moves_only_its_own_id`). The IR is unchanged: the chunks that read an id that did not move hold.
The cost is in the IR. A moved id leaves one `function_for_symbol` bucket and joins another (`hand_outs.rs`: 1024 buckets by a hash of the id string), so a body edit inside an unexported declaration reruns those buckets' readers (app-game: 28 to 77 graph chunks). Widening the buckets to 16384 restores them (3.5 chunks) but adds 13 to 20 ms to mid-file and import edits, so it is not taken (open-work, section 4). Not kept: a per-path slice of `initialization_rejected` cut groove's reachability reruns from 518 to 3 with no change in graph, result or wall time, and added as many slice memos.
App-game mid-file edit 616 to 557 ms, import 899 to 796; its IR equals an append's, and the rest of its gap over an append is the TypeFacts exchange (65 against 16 ms: every moved span's answer changes), open.

<a id="adr-0416"></a>
## ADR 0416 — The changed-reference scan answers from a reverse index of the served canonical ids
`apps/solid-typefacts/src/passes.rs` (`Merged::canonical_index`, `ids_with_canonical`, `reindex`), `per_file.rs` (`per_file_symbol_changes`). `Merged` keeps the reverse map (canonical id to the served ids that name it). It is built by the first query after a merge that started over (`None` until then, so a full merge does no extra work) and moved by `Merged::update` for exactly the ids whose served row changed: the candidates the changed-rows report already names (the old and new rows of every changed position). Any other served row keeps its providers and its output, so its canonical id is unchanged. Under `SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_VERIFY_REUSE` and in every test build each query is compared with the scan (`ids_with_canonical_scan`); a mismatch is a reuse mismatch and serves the scan. A unit test drives random merges, incremental and starting over, with queries between them.
The type-facts request (`daemonPhases.facts.typeFactsMs`) falls from 110 to 63 ms on import, 78 to 30 on mid-file and 83 to 33 on body.

<a id="adr-0417"></a>
## ADR 0417 — Verification reads clean memos by stamp; the graph is an RwLock
`base/memo.rs`. `reads_unchanged` answers a clean dependency from its stamp, cloning neither key nor value (not in verify-reused mode). Verifications read-lock the graph; `synced_graph` and `clear` write-lock it. Saves 5 to 10 ms on a code edit.

<a id="adr-0418"></a>
## ADR 0418 — A program build asks the disk each question once
`apps/solid-typefacts/src/overlay.rs` (`OverlayFs::build_scope`, `Lookups`), `project.rs` (`build_program`, `update_inner`, `rebase_if_due`). A program build asks the disk the same questions again and again: every importer of `./util` probes the same candidate paths, and every bare specifier walks the same `node_modules` ancestors (an app-game import edit rebuilds its chain with 66,400 lookups, 23,500 of them distinct). Inside one build the overlay now keeps what its base answered (`file_exists`, `directory_exists`, `stat`, `realpath`, `get_accessible_entries`, a missing path's answer too) in sharded maps owned by a `BuildScope`. A scope is opened around a configuration parse plus program build, an update's candidate (`prepare_update` or `build_program`) or a rebase, and its maps are dropped when the build returns. They sit below the overlay's own state, which is read first, so an edited or deleted file never reaches them; a duplicate starts with none; a query outside a build (the module graph's root positions) reaches the disk as before. TypeScript assumes one file system snapshot per build, and the next build asks the disk again: a file created or a package installed between two builds is seen by the second (`a_file_created_after_a_failed_probe_is_found_by_the_next_build`, `answers_last_one_build`). File contents are not kept (a rebuild reads each of its 4,465 files once). `typefacts:update` reports `fsHits`, `fsMisses` and `fsReads` under the timings trace.
App-game import edit: program rebuild 173.5 to 149.8 ms, from the 42,900 repeats; the 23,500 distinct lookups stay (stat and lstat, about 150 ms of thread time per rebuild).
A symbol id hashes its file's path, and `function_for_symbol` buckets by that hash, so a body edit's graph reruns depend on the project path: the same binary and edit reran 21 to 27 chunks on one copy of app-game and 91 to 131 on another (IR 335 against 385 ms; an A/A of 9 body edits differed by 44 ms). Arms are compared on one project copy; an A/A there is within 5 ms on every kind.
Not built: a cache that outlives a build (open-work section 2); one `stat` answering `file_exists`, `directory_exists` and `stat` of a path (19,000 distinct paths behind the 23,500 misses: about 1 ms); a directory listing answering probes beneath it (on a case-insensitive volume a probe of another case finds the file, and the listing does not).

<a id="adr-0419"></a>
## ADR 0419 — An update the compiler made in place keeps the attested imports
`apps/solid-typefacts` (`UpdateOutcome::graph_kept`, `module_graph.rs` `omit_modules`), `typefacts` (protocol 85: `Response.graph_kept`, `ModuleGraphRequest.omit_modules`, `Session::last_update_graph`), `solid-facts-backend` (`lib.rs` `kept_attestation`, `patch_import_identities`; `daemon.rs` `answer_with`). The compiler replaces an edited file in place only when the new text has the old one's imports (specifier, mode, order), module-augmentation names, ambient module names, reference, type and lib directives and check-js directive, needs the same synthetic JSX-runtime and import-helper imports, and no package redirect group holds the file (`can_replace_file_in_program`, `can_keep_synthetic_imports`); the new program then shares the previous one's processed files and module resolutions. The producer reports that case (the update was `Reused`, one changed file, and no rebase followed it) as `graphKept` on the update's own answer. Any other update, a producer restart, and an update re-sent after one say false.
On that word the session keeps the previous generation's attested imports and asks the producer for the edited file's rows alone, without the module inventory (`omitModules`): only the edited file's own import positions can differ, and an unedited file's rows are the ones held. It does so only while the previous generation's attestation (full or itself kept) is for the producer process still running and the generation directly before this one, over the same files this generation asks about; else it asks about every file. The daemon, on the same word, skips the `program_modules` comparison (the file set is the one it read before the edit) and records the witness's edited-file imports without the inventory the witness already holds. This is the producer's word on its resolution inputs that open-work section 2 asked for; nothing is re-derived from the edit's text. A package manifest an unedited row names is a witnessed path, so a changed one rebuilds the program before any kept row is served. Under shadow mode (`SOLID_CHECKER_IR_SHADOW`, the fuzz's) the full attestation is asked beside the kept one and compared; a difference is reported (`kept attestation mismatch`) and the full one served.
App-game code edit: the facts phase 77 to 51 ms (the attestation asks 1 file and 3 rows, not 1,611 files and 6,495 rows).
Not built: a partial attestation after an update the compiler did not make in place (an import edit rebuilds the chain and every file is asked again).

<a id="adr-0420"></a>
## ADR 0420 — Entity rows are indexed per path, in the build caches
`solid-reactive-ir` (`indexes/entity_locations.rs`: `EntityLocations`, `PathLocations`; `EntitySymbols::locations`; `symbols.rs`; `cache.rs`), `solid-facts` and `typefacts` (`entities_shared_for_path`). Two questions ask the entity rows by position: the entity at an exact `(path, start, end)`, the first in the table's order, and the smallest entity inside a span. Each was a whole-project map built over every row on every build (414,000 rows on app-game: both maps and their drop, about 40 ms of an edit, inside the memo begin's world). A path's rows change only where the table delta names the path (`TypeScriptChanges::entity_paths`, the premise `EntitySymbols` is patched under), so each path keeps, beside the symbol maps, the shared slice of its own rows (the table's own `Arc`, not a copy), the ordinal of the first row at each exact span and the ordinals by start byte. The patch replaces the named paths in the loop that already patches the symbols; a first build, a full or inexact transition and an alias graph the symbol patch refuses rebuild every path. The lookups answer as the maps did (the same first row; the smallest contained entity, ties by start byte and then row) and record no read, as the maps' initializers recorded none. Under shadow mode the patched index is compared with one built from the whole table and the fresh one is served on a difference. `first_entity_by_location` is gone.

<a id="adr-0421"></a>
## ADR 0421 — A retained edit's disk checks run beside the producer's update; its bookkeeping runs after the answer
`solid-facts-backend` (`daemon.rs`: `State::edit_overlapped`, `After`, `module_lines_kept`; `lib.rs`: `NativeIncrementalSession::edit_during`, `Edited`), `solid-reactive-ir` (`MemoStore::finish_deferred_end`, `IncrementalBuilder::defer_memo_end`). When every edited file keeps its module lines (lines naming `import`, `from`, `require`, `<reference` or `declare`), the witness check and the closure read run while the producer applies the update, and a stale witness abandons that session for a fresh one once the update has landed, before any analysis. When the lines change, the witness is checked first as before: beside a program rebuild those reads slowed its file-system lookups by more than they saved (import edit +19 ms). The choice is only of order; both are exact. The memo sweep runs once the answer is written, or its write failed: the daemon answers one request at a time, and a build begins by running a sweep still due. A failure there discards the state, as a failed request does. The witness's extension with the edited files' imports stays inline, right after the facts (review R1 finding 2): deferred, it observed the disk after analysis and emission rather than when the producer resolved, and it costs about 0 ms. Its prune drops only absent entries under an absent one, never one an earlier request recorded present. The cached answer is still built before replying (the idle check records it with the answer), and the idle check's fresh build defers nothing.

<a id="adr-0422"></a>
## ADR 0422 — The synthetic base-versus-head race is removed
`.github/workflows/performance.yml`, `benchmarks/compare-performance.mjs`, `benchmarks/accepted-regressions.json`, `codspeed.yml`, `benchmarks/run-codspeed-case.mjs` and `scripts/performance-workflow.test.mjs` are deleted. The workflow raced the merge base against the head on the generated 1,000-file chain corpus (each module imports the one before it, the edit lands at the chain's root, one same-span body edit, measured in-process) and gated on the ratio. That corpus did not show main's real cost (main answered its edit in about 150 ms while app-game took 6.9 s), so it could not see the instant-edits stack's gains, and its worst-case dependency shape made the stack's reuse bookkeeping the whole signal: 1.75x on a four-core runner, 1.13x locally, against real projects where the stack is 1.4x to 18x faster. A gate that cannot see the work users wait on is removed rather than retuned. `make verify-performance` keeps its structural invariants and ceilings where the machine is controlled.

<a id="adr-0423"></a>
## ADR 0423 — CI gates edit latency on real projects, base against head
`.github/workflows/real-projects.yml` times what users wait on: one CLI check with the retained daemon, full report, after a code, mid-file, body (where the manifest names an anchor) or import edit, on app-game (`apps/web`, 1,611 program sources), sensor-sim (`packages/frontend`) and solid-groove. `benchmarks/real-projects/manifest.json` pins each repository commit, the Solid 2 release-candidate patch, the install command and the lockfile digest; `benchmarks/real-projects-prepare.mjs` fetches, patches, installs and refuses a tree whose lockfile or diff moved. The merge base and the head run interleaved on one runner, one project copy, their own daemons; the gate (`benchmarks/lib/real-projects.mjs`) fails when the median of paired head/base ratios exceeds 1.25 for any project and edit kind, or when a head answer did not come from its retained daemon. Differing answers are gated at a looser ratio (ADR 0451). Absolute head ceilings (app-game code 1,000 ms, every edit 2,500 ms) only report until calibrated on runners. app-game's daemon and producer peak at 3.2 GiB, so CI sets `SOLID_CHECKER_DAEMON_MAX_RSS_MB=8192`. Owners: the workflow, `benchmarks/real-projects*.mjs`, `scripts/real-projects.test.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0424"></a>
## ADR 0424 — SC2001 is `no-write-in-pure-computation`; an unreadable source option is uncertifiable
SC2001 takes upstream's name; `reactive-write-in-owned-scope` becomes an alias. The scope stays a superset of upstream's (root bodies, `render`/`hydrate` code, `untrack`, `flush(fn)`, `refresh`, helpers placed by their entries). A setter whose source's options argument is not a readable object literal may carry `ownedWrite: true`, so its write is uncertifiable, not a violation (upstream is silent there; a setter found by its type alone has no source in view and is uncertifiable the same way). Options held in a variable stay uncertifiable where upstream resolves one level of `const` and reports (ownership case `no-write-in-pure-computation/const-held-options/001`). The store setter guard does not honor `ownedWrite`, as upstream says: on `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `devGuardStoreSetterWrite` (`dist/dev.js:5811-5825`) has no exemption, `storeSetter` (`:5847`) calls it unconditionally and `createStore$1` (`:5904-5944`) reads no `ownedWrite`, while `setSignal` honors the signal option (`dist/dev-shared.js:5622`); rc.0, rc.9 and rc.13 read it in no store code either. So a store is never exempt and its options are never an unknown (ownership case `…/store-owned-write/001`). Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/lib.rs` (`owned_write_option`, `OwnedWriteOption`; the facts-side boolean is gone, AST facts schema 74), `projection.rs`, `static_api.rs`; fixture `owned-write-option`.

<a id="adr-0425"></a>
## ADR 0425 — SC2003 is `no-store-mutation-outside-setter`, with upstream's write shapes
SC2003 takes upstream's name; `no-direct-mutation` becomes an alias. It gains two of the shapes upstream reports and the checker did not: a call of one of the nine in-place `Array.prototype` methods on a path of a `createStore` read proxy, and writes (assignment, update, mutator) through a `createProjection` result (its own finding wording). The array method must resolve through Type Facts to the standard library's `Array` method at the exact store symbol, never by name; the argumentless `pop`/`shift`/`sort`/`reverse` calls are demanded too (`demand_plan.rs`). Probed on `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` (`dist/dev.js`): `delete`, all nine mutators and a projection write outside its compute are dropped (`set` trap `:5691-5700`, `deleteProperty` `:5761-5767`); a write through the store's own proxy inside its own setter callback commits (`storeSetter` `:5847-5866`, `inDraft` `:4955-4957`), through another store's it is dropped, and a write through a projection result or draft inside its compute function commits (`:6675-6678`). So the own-setter exemption is kept against upstream (which reports it) and joins an equal exemption for a store's own compute function, both pinned as ownership cases. `delete store.path` is not built: tsc reports TS2790 on a required property under strictNullChecks, and no Type Fact says whether a property's type includes `undefined` at a delete operand (a producer demand, wire and protocol change); open-work records it and the oracle pins the refusal. The accessor-member arm (`memo.a = 1`) is deleted too, in the review round: TS2339/TS7053 uncast, and a cast write lands on the function object (`removed-because-redundant` case, ledger row). The accessor-binding reassignment arm is deleted: rc.17 gives TS2588 for a `const` binding and TS2322/TS2356/TS2365 for a `let`, and what type-checks is a deliberate rebinding (`removed-because-redundant` case, ledger row). Props writes stay uncertifiable (2026-10-19). Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`, `docs/rules/no-store-mutation-outside-setter.md`; fixtures `store-mutators`, `projection-writes`, `accessor-rebinding`.

<a id="adr-0426"></a>
## ADR 0426 — SC2004 and SC2005 are one rule, `no-await-primitive-in-tracked-scope`
`resolve` and `until` share one dev guard (`getObserver()`) and one scope proof, so they are one configurable rule. Both codes stay: a finding names `resolve` (SC2004) or `until` (SC2005). `resolve-in-tracked-scope`, `resolve-in-reactive-scope` and `until-in-tracked-scope` become aliases of the new name, each limited to its own code: the first two configure SC2004 alone and the third SC2005 alone, in `rule-options.json` (`CODE_SCOPED_ALIASES` in `dialect.rs`, `RuleOptions::is_enabled_for_code`) and as ESLint deprecated keys (each reports, owns and takes the severity of its code only), so a project that turned one off keeps the other. The new name governs both codes; an old name's `enabled` wins for its own code, and a disable among two names of one code stays. Fixtures `rule-name-scopes`; tests in `diagnostics/tests.rs`, `rule_options.rs`, `packages/cli/test/adapter.test.mjs`. Release gating (`until` from rc.5) is unchanged. Owners: `rust/dialects/solid-v2/rules/src/rules.rs`, `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/projection.rs`.

<a id="adr-0427"></a>
## ADR 0427 — SC7006 is `valid-use-server`, with upstream's placement checks
SC7006 takes upstream's name; `server-function-module-directive` becomes an alias. It gains upstream's placement checks: a `"use server"` string statement outside the prologue (after another statement, in a block or loop), a template-literal directive, and a directive in an object method, getter, setter or class method, constructor or accessor (never extracted; rc.17 rejects it with a compile error). Premises are read from `@solidjs/compiler@2.0.0-rc.17` at the pinned fork revision (`packages/compiler/src/directives/{transform,validate}.rs`; the compiler version a build runs is not observable, so claims hold on the audited one and fail closed elsewhere) and RFC 10, with `@solidjs/web@2.0.0-rc.17`'s `registerServerReference` (`server-functions/dist/server.js:1567-1571`). The exemptions mirror upstream's (nested in an extracted function; a directive file suppresses function-level placement) and widen it in one place: a stray string in the extracted function's own block is silent, as the pass skips it. **Premise correction:** the earlier rule reported a wrapped export (`export const x = wrap(fn)`) as dropped from the client build, from RFC 10's first text; the audited compiler registers each export's evaluated value and never inspects its shape (`transform.rs:411-420`, rc.4; the rc.3 compile error was superseded), so it is no defect and is gone, an unproven value being an uncertifiable result. Kept: a re-export is a compile error (`transform.rs:1377-1412`, rc.8; upstream accepts it, pinned as an ownership case). Added with upstream: a non-function export (literal, template, array, object) throws at boot, and a module-scope call to a client-side wrapper in a directive file never runs on the client. The built-ins (`GET`, `live`, `withMeta`, `query`, `action`, `liveQuery`) match the callee's exact import binding from `solid-js`, `@solidjs/web`, `@solidjs/router` or `@solidjs/start` (or a subpath), not its spelling. The `clientWrappers` option (`rule-options.json`, upstream's grammar: exact, `*` over `[a-zA-Z0-9_$]`, unanchored `/regex/`, invalid regex compared exactly; the regex arm a JavaScript subset matched without backtracking) matches the callee's local spelling, the user's own declaration. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/valid_use_server.rs`, `name_matcher.rs`, `upstream_compat/rule_options.rs`; facts `stray_directives` (AST schema 75); `docs/rules/valid-use-server.md`; fixtures `valid-use-server-placement`, `valid-use-server-wrappers`, `server-function-directive`. An export whose value no fact shows to be a function is uncertifiable, `server-export-value-unproven`.

<a id="adr-0428"></a>
## ADR 0428 — `reactivity` is a group key over SC1001, SC1002, SC1005 and SC1007
`rule-options.json`, the ESLint plugin and inline directives (ADR 0429) accept `reactivity` as a key that enables or disables `strict-read-untracked`, `reactive-read-after-await`, `uncalled-accessor` and `reactive-handler-frozen` together (`RULE_GROUPS`): the four defects upstream's `reactivity` reports. Expansion is two-phase (`RuleOptions::parse_with_groups`): the group sets its members, then every member the document names wins, whatever order the keys sort in. A finding names the member, never the group. ESLint cannot report a member under the group's id, so `solid-checker/reactivity` is a registered no-op rule, valid in a migrated config and in a disable comment; severities stay per member. SC4001 is not a member (upstream has no owner model); SC9005 and SC9012 are obligations, not rules (ADR 0440). SC1003 is not a member, but not because upstream keeps `no-destructure` apart: upstream's `reactivity` also files destructured props as `untrackedReactive` (its test rows 5 and 63). SC1003 is a different claim (a setup-time snapshot of props, decided by callers) under its own upstream key `no-destructure`, so disabling `reactivity` leaves it on. This reverses the README sentence that one key never suppresses unrelated defect classes: the four are one defect class in upstream's vocabulary, and a project migrating its configuration needs the key. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/rule_options.rs`, `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/dialect.rs`, `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

<a id="adr-0429"></a>
## ADR 0429 — Inline directives are honored by the checker, under both plugin prefixes
The native checker reads `disable`, `enable`, `disable-line` and `disable-next-line` comments in two families: `eslint-*` naming `solid-checker/<name>` or upstream's `solid/<name>`, and `solid-checker-*`. ESLint's grammar (solid-facts `SuppressionCommentFact`, facts schema 76) and matching (`suppression.rs`) apply, so a comment means the same in both tools; no names means every rule, and the two families scan independently. A name resolves through the catalog, `RULE_ALIASES`, the group key (ADR 0428) and, in a `solid-checker-*` comment, SC codes. A comment removes site violations only, after every reclassification: never an uncertifiable result, a notice or a project finding. A `solid-checker/*` name nothing resolves is the project notice SC9016 (`--certify` fails on it, like SC9014); accepted silently are the pinned v0.18.1 `solid/*` names (`UPSTREAM_RULE_NAMES`; `no-single-arg-create-effect` has no alias, ADR 0432), adapter ids, retired ids (an obligation's names and codes included: SC9005 and SC9012 are never suppressed, ADR 0440) and another plugin's names, and other `solid/*` names are not read. ESLint applies `eslint-disable solid-checker/<key>` to the key that reported, so the adapter forwards the keys it enables for the file (`--host-suppresses`) and native skips those; a member reported through `certification`, and the no-op `reactivity` rule, are native's, and ESLint's unused-directive warning on them is accepted. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-facts` (`ast/suppression.rs`), `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/suppression.rs`, `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

<a id="adr-0430"></a>
## ADR 0430 — SC5003 reports no violation it cannot read, and follows the audited routers upward
An async source whose options argument the analysis cannot read may declare `loadingValue` or `seedLoadingValue`, so its bare render is uncertifiable, not the informational violation the rule page called "the honest default" (that contradicted invariant 2; `projection.rs` downgraded SC5001 only). The dominant uncertifiable cause is a rendering component never traced to a `render`/`hydrate` root (269 of 271 fixture findings; the corpus stops at routed pages). ADRs 0334 and 0335 prove a route component is rendered; the proof is extended to what the audited router renders above it (layouts, the root route, the `Router` element's children), so a `Loading` there covers the page and its absence is a violation. `<FileRoutes />`, with no source on disk, leaves the chain uncertifiable. Built: the first half (`projection.rs` downgrades SC5001, SC5003 and SC5005 for unreadable options; fixture `async-options-unreadable`); the router chain is not. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/projection.rs`, `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery/async_server.rs`, `docs/rules/async-outside-loading-boundary.md`.

<a id="adr-0431"></a>
## ADR 0431 — SC7005 proves one shape: a call dominated by a pending read under a boundary in streaming SSR
`http-response-after-flush` was uncertifiable by construction and so, under ADR 0328, never shown. It becomes a violation under four premises: the rendering selection is `streaming-ssr`; the call sits below a `Loading` boundary; on every path the call is dominated by a read of an async source proven pending at first flight (no `loadingValue`/`seedLoadingValue`, no `deferStream`, a compute returning `fetch(...)` or an `async` function's promise, ADR 0340's still-pending proof); and that source is read nowhere outside a boundary. The first pass then throws `NotReadyError` before the call, the boundary is pending at the shell flush, and the re-run is post-commit, where `@solidjs/web`'s server build gates the write on `!response.committed`. Every other shape stays uncertifiable. Ships only after a Node probe of `renderToStream` on the rc.17 server build records the drop. Owners: `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/server_rules.rs`, `docs/rules/http-response-after-flush.md`.
