## ADR 0432 — SC7001 keeps `missing-effect-function`
Upstream's `no-single-arg-create-effect` reports the plain one-argument `createEffect`/`createRenderEffect` call. The published `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` typings declare only the two-argument overload, so that call is TS2554 and invariant 1 keeps the checker silent on it; the rule's residual claims (a cast-hidden non-callable apply, an exact one-element spread, the rc.0–rc.5 overload) are not what the upstream name says. The name stays; the rule page records the difference. Owners: `docs/rules/missing-effect-function.md`, `fixtures/tsc-oracle`.

## ADR 0434 — SC3001 and SC4002 stay although the runtime throws; SC3001's throws are development-only
`missing-context-provider` claims a throw the development and production builds both raise (`ContextNotFoundError`), and `leaf-owner-forbidden-call` claims throws that only the development build raises: in the `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` registry bytes (read from the tarball, not copied into a fixture) `CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` is at `dist/dev.js:2391-2403`, `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` at `dist/dev-shared.js:1485-1494` and `:4916-4925`, and the leaf `flush()` throw at `dist/dev-shared.js:2671-2676`, while `dist/prod` holds none of those messages or guards (no `FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` string, no tracked-callback `flush` check; its `onCleanup` is `dist/prod/signals.js:65`), so production does not throw there. <a id="adr-0435"></a>
## ADR 0435 — The dispatch veto excludes sites that cannot deliver a handler under an owner
ADR 0356's veto is the set of its dispatch sites, and a site leaves it when one of two facts holds. Receiver: the global `window` (an event dispatched on it reaches only `window`'s listeners, never a JSX handler), or the global `document` where the dialect says so (`document_dispatch_reaches_no_element_handler`, read on the rc.17 bytes: `eventHandler` walks `composedPath()` from the event's target and calls only the `node[$$type]` handlers `addEvent` sets on elements, and the document carries none; other releases keep the veto). The receiver resolves through its `lib.dom.d.ts` `var` declaration with no project binding, never by spelling: a parameter or constant named `window` keeps the veto. Caller: a callback handed directly to a reviewed fresh-stack scheduler, or module top level outside any function, class and JSX element, has no owner and no strict-read window; the callback of `runWithOwner(null, …)` has no owner but leaves its enclosing window open, so it excludes owner and write proofs only. A site in a function nothing places keeps the veto. A caller that is an event handler is excluded only by ADR 0437, and a test runner callback by ADR 0438. Every site and its classification enter the report's `premises`. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `solid-dialect` (`document_dispatch_reaches_no_element_handler`), `solid-facts-backend/src/diagnostics.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `event-dispatch-*`.

<a id="adr-0436"></a>
## ADR 0436 — A spread with a complete declared key set and no listener key is not a handler site
A spread onto an intrinsic element that no exact object resolves (ADR 0358) holds no handler, and raises no SC4001 obligation, when Type Facts reports the key set of the spread argument's declared type as complete and the dialect lowers none of its keys as a listener (`spread_key_is_event == Some(false)`; an unaudited release answers `None` and keeps the obligation, and rc.13's lowercase `onclick` stays a listener). Complete means every value-carrying constituent is an object type with no index signature of any key type and a fully resolved property list: unions and intersections add their members' keys, nullish members add none. `any`, `unknown`, primitives, type parameters and other instantiable types, reverse-mapped types, a mapped type whose constraint is not a union of literal keys, and an `as`-clause mapped type unless it instantiates a top-level alias with type-variable-free arguments (`@solidjs/signals`' `Omit<T, K>`, `[P in keyof T as Exclude<P, K[number]>]`, rc.17 `store/utils.d.ts:226`; lib's `Omit` is `Pick<T, Exclude<keyof T, K>>`, no `as` clause, judged by its constraint), give no set, as do an object type with no property and no signature (`{}`, an empty interface: it admits any non-nullish value and declares nothing; `object` is not an object type here) and a type whose only members are nullish. The proof reads the declared type, not the value: a value typed `{ class?: string }` may carry more keys at run time (structural subtyping, excess keys), the same premise as every checker-resolved type here; direct `eval` withholds it. The producer answers `declaredKeys` as sorted names, a pure function of the type (ADR 0367), demanded only at spread arguments of intrinsic elements in a dialect that sets `spread_declared_keys`; the wire carries it from handshake protocol 84 (Wire table schema v23, row flag 16, compact demand bit 19). Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/type_facts.rs` (`declared_keys_at`), `rust/crates/typefacts`, `demand_plan.rs`, `object_members/handlers.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `spread-declared-keys-*`.

<a id="adr-0437"></a>
## ADR 0437 — A dispatch written in an event-only function leaves the veto in one step
A synchronous dispatch written directly in a function literal that ADR 0327's census (`event_invoked_literals`: closed program, compiler facts not withheld) finds invoked only as event deliveries, outside a class element and JSX the compiler wraps, is classed `event handler` and leaves ADR 0356's veto for owner and read proofs. A handler also called from a component body keeps it (mixed invocation); one under a component's children leaves it, as ADR 0327 proves such a handler ownerless wherever it is written (the owner expected the veto to stay there; not built). ADR 0435's rounds (remove a site once it is proven ownerless under the larger set, repeat) cannot start, since the proof they reuse is withheld while any site stays; the census reads no veto, so one induction replaces them. Soundness: while any non-excluded site remains every event proof is withheld, as before. With none, each delivery comes from a host event (no owner, no window; ADR 0327's premise), a window or document dispatch (no element handler) or a site in a scheduled, module-level, test-runner or event-only function; a site in an event-only function runs only inside a delivery, so by induction on time no site runs on a stack with an owner, and removing sites only strengthens ownerless proofs. The answer is one `OnceLock`, independent of demand order (ADR 0367), enters the digest as the owner and read bits, and a debug build fails if the census reads the veto it computes. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `execution_role/event_literals.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `event-dispatch-handler-*`.

<a id="adr-0438"></a>
## ADR 0438 — A callback a reviewed test runner registers runs with no owner and no strict-read window
A function literal handed directly to a test runner's registration (`test`, `it`, `describe`, `suite`, hooks, through members such as `only`, `skip`, `serial`, `each(rows)(…)`) is run from the runner's scheduler: no Solid owner, no strict-read window. That holds for a test, a hook and a suite body only where the runner's bytes show it (below): a suite body its runner calls inside the registration call (jest-circus, mocha, `node:test`, playwright) takes the role of the call's own position, and `bun:test`'s is not placed. A dispatch site in it is classed `test runner callback` and leaves the veto, and its body takes a scheduled callback's role for every rule (owner, write and read; fixtures `test-runner-vitest/variants/role-*`). The registration's root identifier must resolve through its imports to declarations that all lie in the installed declaration files of `vitest`, `bun-types` (`bun:test`), `@types/node` (`node:test`), `@playwright/test`/`playwright`, `@jest/globals`, `@types/jest` or `@types/mocha` (`TEST_RUNNER_DECLARATIONS` in `runtime_semantics.rs`), never by spelling: a project `it` or a wrapper (`it("x", wrap(fn))`) is not one, and only the outermost call of a curried registration registers (`describe.each(rows)` and `it.skipIf(cond)` take data, so a function written there is no callback). Reviewed premise, read on the published bytes the corpus installs (vitest's runtime on 5.0.0; the fixture `test-runner-vitest` pins only the 5.0.5 declaration files, which carry the registrations and no runtime): vitest 5.0.0 calls a test as `fn()` inside `runTest` (`dist/chunks/run.CQOUYP-x.js:3876`), a hook in `callSuiteHook` (`:3584`) and a suite body in `runWithSuite` (`:2763`, `:3248`); playwright 1.63.0 calls `fn(testFunctionParams, testInfo)` (`lib/worker/workerProcessEntry.js:1655`), a hook through `resolveParametersAndRunFunction(hook.fn, …)` (`:1775`); no Solid API is on any of those stacks. Suite bodies are per runner (`SuiteBodyRun` in `runtime_semantics.rs`). vitest records the suite and runs the body in `collect` (5.0.0 `run.CQOUYP-x.js:2763`), called from `collectTests` after `importFile` (`:3446`, `:3449`, `:3454`; 4.1.7 `chunk-artifact.js:1888`, `:2457`, `:2462`, `:2469`), so it runs later wherever the registration is written. playwright 1.63.0 calls `body()` inside `_describe` (`lib/common/index.js:2308`), jest-circus 30.5.2 `blockFn()` inside `_dispatchDescribe` (`build/index.js:1792`), mocha 11.8.0 `opts.fn.call(suite)` in `suite.create` (`lib/interfaces/common.js:153`, from `bdd.js:46`), and `node:test` (Node 24.22.1, `internal/test_runner/test.js`) builds the suite in its constructor (`:1804`; `createBuild` runs the body at `:1840`, from `createSubtest` `:1052`): the body runs inside the registration call, so it takes the role of that position (owned under `createRoot` or a component body, ownerless at module level; a dispatch in it is classed as the call's position, the owner graph gives it a `Preserve` edge, and an `async` body is not placed, its resumptions run elsewhere). `bun:test` is native, with no JavaScript to read; a probe of bun 1.4.0 ran the body after the file loaded, which earlier releases are not shown to do, so its suite body is not placed. Tests and hooks are recorded and run later in every runner read: jest-circus `add_test` (`:1867`) then `_callCircusTest` (`:965`), mocha `suite.addTest` (`bdd.js:93`) then `Runnable.run` (`lib/runner.js:806`), `node:test` from `Test.start` (`:1262`) once the root has concurrency and the build phase (`harness.js:53`, `:1238`) is over, which a registration made while the file loads never finds. Fixtures `test-runner-suites`, `test-runner-node/variants/suite-inline*`, `test-runner-vitest/variants/suite-scheduled`, `curried-argument`, `test-runner-bun/variants/suite-unproven`. Async callbacks: an `async` registered callback is in the premise whole, since its prefix up to the first await runs on the runner's stack and every resumption (after `await`, `for await`, `await using`, in a loop) runs with no owner (ADR 0333) and no strict-read window, so a call written anywhere in it is a satisfied entry, unlike the event census, which refuses one a suspension may precede. vitest 4.1.11 declares the registrations in `@vitest/runner`, which `vitest` re-exports, so that package is a reviewed location too; the same read of its bytes: `fn()` in `runTest` (`@vitest/runner/dist/chunk-artifact.js:2956`), `await hook(...args)` in `callSuiteHook` (`:2608`), the suite body in `runWithSuite` (`:2255`). Test-only helpers: a project function literal whose every invocation, followed as ADR 0327's census follows it (`test_invoked_literals`: the same walk in owner mode, closed program, compiler facts not withheld, the same budget, with a call written directly in a registered callback as the one entry), is a call in a registered callback or in another such helper, so it runs on the runner's stack or a continuation of it and a dispatch written directly in it is classed `test-only helper` and leaves the veto. Soundness: by induction on the call chain every invocation starts in a registered callback, and the callback's owner and window are the runner's; an export of an open program (ADR 0442 follows one in a closed program), a rebinding, an alias or a stored value the walk cannot follow, an invocation from a component body, an event handler (a mix of the two entries), a primitive's callback or an unplaced caller, and a program not declared closed all refuse (a call past a suspension in an async helper is a satisfied entry since ADR 0442); a helper handed to a project function that only tests call stays test-only, as the walk follows the parameter to that function's call. The census reads no veto (ADR 0437's argument; the debug tripwire covers it), the sites' classes enter the same two answers `Section::SynchronousDispatch` binds, and a helper's own reads and writes already took their role from ADR 0319's flow. Residual: a runner configured to wrap each test in a Solid root or component, a patched runner, a `node:test` test registered after its build phase ended (it may run inside the call), and a helper also called from an event handler (an exported helper module of a closed program is followed since ADR 0442). Sites and class enter the report's `premises`. Owners: `runtime_semantics.rs`, `indexes/test_runner.rs`, `execution_role/callee_roles.rs`, `execution_role/event_literals.rs`, `owners/owner_context.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `test-runner-*` (`test-runner-vitest/variants/async-*`, `helper-*`, `test-runner-vitest4`).

<a id="adr-0439"></a>
## ADR 0439 — An optional DOM call in a leaf scope is read like the non-optional call
In the leaf-scope walk an optional call `el?.m()` (an optional chain, or a `let` typed `T | undefined`) whose exact declaration is a `lib.dom` member is judged by that declaration alone, as the plain call is: only ADR 0329's synchronous dispatchers refuse, in either form, so `dialog?.focus()` stays an obligation and `el?.scrollIntoView()`, `setAttribute`, `append`, `select` and `observe` are certified. `getContext` of `HTMLCanvasElement` and `OffscreenCanvas` keeps the optional refusal (a failed WebGL context fires `webglcontextcreationerror` at the canvas synchronously); the plain call keeps its trust, an approximation. Premise: ADR 0329's, that a member outside its list dispatches no event and custom-element reactions are not modeled. Demanding resolved calls in every file moved other rules (ADR 0192) for 7 fewer obligations and was not built. Owners: `member_call_operations`, `refused_when_optional` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`; fixtures `leaf-scope-optional-dom`, `leaf-scope-project-callees`.

<a id="adr-0440"></a>
## ADR 0440 — SC9005 and SC9012 are obligations; SC9011 is SC9012's context `package-callee`
An uncertifiable result is internal (ADR 0328): a user sees none of it, `--certify` ignores it, and only `--internal` and the JSON keep it. A rule key for one promised a switch that did nothing for the user and, for the native checker, one that hid the gap but not the `uncertifiable` status (`withheld_coverage`). SC9005 `package-behavior-unavailable` and SC9012 `reactive-dispatch-unresolved` therefore become obligations: they keep their code, name and metadata (`RuleMetadata::configurable` is false), are never filtered by enablement, and have no rule-options key, no plugin-manifest entry and no ESLint rule. SC9011 `reactive-source-uncaptured`, a source handed to a package callee, merges into SC9012 with `analysisContext: package-callee` (`PACKAGE_CALLEE`); its internal defect kind is unchanged. Every former name still parses and does nothing: `RETIRED_RULES` gains the three names and SC9005's four former names (they were `RULE_ALIASES`, whose target is no longer a rule), and the ESLint plugin keeps four deprecated no-op keys (`RETIRED_RULE_KEYS`), so no existing configuration breaks. This supersedes ADR 0248; SC9011's page content is SC9012's. Owners: `Rule::metadata`, `static_defect_wording` (`rust/dialects/solid-v2/rules/src`), `retain_enabled`, `RETIRED_RULES` (`rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src`), `rule_manifest_json` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/findings.rs`), `RETIRED_RULE_KEYS` (`packages/cli/eslint.cjs`); `docs/rules/README.md`.

<a id="adr-0441"></a>
## ADR 0441 — SC9015 is an error for a removed 1.x attribute `tsc` accepts
The pinned compiler errors on `on:`, `oncapture:`, `attr:`, `bool:`, `use:` and `classList` on a native element in the dom and ssr generates, whatever the options (`packages/compiler/src/shared/removed.rs:19-52`, `dom/element.rs:226-285`, `ssr/transform.rs:1634, 2238` at the pinned fork; solidjs/solid #4199, merged before rc.17). The file does not build, so SC9015 is a violation (error), one per file, judged against the pinned compiler, not the installed one: a project on an older compiler (corpus `app-game`, rc.4, which accepts `attr:`) still gets it. Narrowed by invariant 1: TypeScript exempts a name containing a hyphen from the excess-property check, so only `attr:data-z`, `on:custom-event` and their kind are errors; `attr:title`, `bool:hidden`, `on:click`, `use:tip` and `classList` are TS2322 on the rc.17 typings, and keep ADR 0360's notice (a different claim: the file has no compiler facts). Every other refusal keeps the notice too: malformed markup is an error only while `validate` is on, which the checker does not model, and a fragment under an element, an unimplemented form and a namespaced component callee belong to a generate or a limitation it does not claim for the project. Classification is by the pinned compiler's own messages (`classify_refusal`, pinned against the real compiler by a test), and an unrecognized message is unproven. Premise: the project's generate is dom or ssr, the only two the checker compiles. Owners: `classify_refusal` in `rust/dialects/solid-v2/compiler/src/lib.rs`, `compiler_refused_finding` in `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/diagnostics.rs`; oracle cases in `fixtures/tsc-oracle/rule-cases.json`; fixture `compiler-refused-file`.

<a id="adr-0442"></a>
## ADR 0442 — A function handed to project code takes the role of each place that code invokes it
A function literal, or a same-file function named by identifier that the file hands on (a whole argument, a non-intrinsic JSX attribute value, a `const` alias), takes for a write the join of the roles of every invocation ADR 0319's forward walk finds: through the callee's parameter (every use a call, or handed on to another exact project callee), through `props.<name>` of an exactly resolved project component (an intrinsic `on…` attribute or a call in the child's handler is a delivery, a call in the child's body or a tracked computation is owned, a call in a leaf scope is exempt, a prop forwarded to another component recurses within the walk's budget), and through a reviewed scheduler's callback slot (`setTimeout(fn)`, a fresh stack). A call is placed by what its own position proves, without a census of the other entries of the function holding it, and a call in a function the walk entered through a parameter takes the role of the call that handed the value over, never of that function's other callers. The join follows ADR 0378: an owned invocation (the body, a tracked computation) makes the write the violation whatever else invokes the value; otherwise every invocation must be placed (several non-reporting roles give `Unreported`), and a census gap, an unplaced invocation, a destructured or `let` or stored or escaped use, a `merge`/`omit` view, a spread or repeated attribute on the element, a package component known only by declarations, a primitive's callback slot, or an unresolved child keeps the obligation. A prop literal needs no closed program (what a child does with a prop does not depend on its other callers); ADR 0450 makes the answers one memo per function. The same walk now follows an exported function declaration through its other modules' references in a closed program when every one is a call the graph accounts for (`export *` is no hand-out; an alias, a re-export specifier, a value use, an escaping namespace or an open program refuses), so ADRs 0437 and 0438's censuses place a dispatch in `fireAndFlush(() => el.dispatchEvent(…))` called only from test callbacks (class `test-only helper`, leaving the veto) and in a handler-only `withTx(() => el.click())`, and flows through exported helpers (the returned closures of `makeNavigation`) become placed. Two supporting steps of the flow: a `const` object pattern that holds an object the value is a member of binds the member by its plain-identifier key (`const { add } = renderPanel()`; a rest element, a quoted, computed or nested key refuses), and in the test census only, a call written past a possible suspension in an async helper is a satisfied entry, as in a registered async callback (the resumption has no owner and no window, ADR 0333; the part before the first await runs on the caller's stack, which the walk still requires to be a runner's). A write inside a layer the walk runs in place (an immediately invoked function, a callback the standard library runs inline) takes the answer of the function around it only when no layer's position follows a suspension: an `await` that may precede the write in the write's own function, or in any function around it up to the answered one, puts it in the ownerless continuation (ADR 0333), which no invocation of that function places, so it stays unplaced (`write_flow_role`; fixture `prop-callback-roles/variants/after-await`). A host event delivery is gated the same on both routes under a synchronous dispatch (ADR 0356): the invocation the walk finds through `onClick={props.onPress}` and a call in `onClick={() => props.onPress()}` (placed by ADR 0327's census, which refuses then) are both unplaced (`variants/sync-dispatch`). Premises: ADR 0319, 0327, 0333, 0378 and 0438's. Not built: reads for a forwarded or aliased owned call (SC1001 stays uncertifiable; a direct body call is ADR 0204), `merge`/`omit` views for an owned role, a registered test callback named by identifier, and every unmodeled position the walk already refuses. Owners: `write_function_role`, `write_site_role`, `exported_call_sites` in `rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role/event_literals.rs`; `write_flow_answers`, `write_flow_role` in `execution_role/invocation_flow.rs`; `semantic_write_execution_role_within` in `execution_role/write_roles.rs`; digest in `indexes.rs`; fixtures `prop-callback-roles`, `helper-literal-roles`, `test-runner-vitest/variants/helper-literal-*` and `helper-exported-*`; `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/tests/invocation_flow_process.rs`.

<a id="adr-0443"></a>
## ADR 0443 — Three untracked reads rc.17 leaves silent are SC1001: a map function, a provider's `value`, a `runWithOwner` callback in a tracked scope
Probed on the rc.17 browser dev builds (`scripts/probes/rc17-untracked-reads`): (1) the map function of `mapArray` and `repeat` runs under `runWithOwner`, which sets `tracking = false` (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:5779`), inside the map computation whose recompute clears `strictRead` (`:4196-4200`); a read there subscribes nothing, and only a call passing `name` opens a window (`dist/dev.js:3093-3102`, `3623-3633`), so unnamed it is stale and silent. The vocabulary said the opposite (map callbacks "run tracked"). (2) Solid 2's context element reads `value` once under `<provider>`'s window (`solid-js@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/solid.dev.js:9-16`, `48-49`, `65`) and stores a snapshot (`dist/dev.js:1834-1844`); a child's `useContext` subscribes to nothing, and the dev build warns. (3) `runWithOwner` clears tracking and leaves the window as the call had it, so in an effect or memo compute, or a JSX expression, a read in its callback is untracked with no warning. All three are SC1001 violations, by new `Dialect` questions (`callback_runs_untracked_without_window`, `callback_clears_tracking_in_calling_window`) and a role, `UntrackedUnwindowed`, whose hint promises no dev warning where none is certain; a provider's value is placed through the exact `createContext` binding of its tag. Not reported: JSX the callback writes (it tracks itself), `untrack`, a stored function, a read after an await the call follows (including through an immediately invoked function; that is SC1002's), a call `tsc` rejects (the one-argument `createEffect` of upstream's case 32 is TS2554), and `createRoot`, whose code read at `dist/dev-shared.js:3478-3481` was not probed in a computation. Legal on rc.17, kept as divergences from eslint-plugin-solid 0.18.1's `badUnnamedDerivedSignal` (ownership cases `strict-read-untracked/unnamed-derived/*`): an effect compute or memo returning a function, and a function a component returns and a tracked consumer calls. Case 34's condition `props.big` is a defect and its arrow is not; case 35 is undecided (an uncertifiable result). Owners: `reports_untracked_reads_at`, `callback_clears_tracking_in_calling_window` (`solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs`); `semantic_role_before_awaits`, `unwindowed_role_at_call`, `context_provider_value_role` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role`); fixtures `mapper-untracked-reads`, `provider-value-untracked`, `run-with-owner-in-computation`; oracle and ownership cases.

<a id="adr-0444"></a>
## ADR 0444 — A workspace package a sibling depends on is an open program
ADR 0193's default made every `"private": true` package, and every manifest publishing nothing, a closed program. In a workspace a sibling the analysis never sees imports it, so closed-program proofs (ADR 0203, 0213, 0214, 0442) certified "no other caller" falsely (kui's `kernel`, imported by every `feature-*`). Now an application is closed only if no package of a workspace that holds it declares a dependency on it. The workspaces read are `package.json` `workspaces` (array or `{ packages }`; npm, yarn, bun), `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `packages` (without the key, pnpm's default of every subdirectory) and `lerna.json` `packages` (default `packages/*`), at the package's directory or any directory above; a pattern is `*`, `?`, `**` and `!` negation over directories holding a `package.json`. The package is a member when a pattern lists it, or it is the root; its dependents are the other members and the root manifest. A dependency is a name in `dependencies`, `devDependencies`, `peerDependencies` or `optionalDependencies`, a `workspace:` or `npm:` spec naming it, or a `file:`, `link:`, `portal:` or `workspace:` path resolving to its directory. Open instead: a workspace this does not read exactly (brace, class or other pattern syntax, an anchored or multi-line YAML list, a `workspaces` of another shape, a sibling or ancestor manifest that is invalid or unreadable, more than 20,000 directories listed), and `rush.json` or an `nx.json` beside no workspace patterns, whose project graph is not package-based. An explicit boundary still wins. An open boundary only withdraws proofs, so a result moves to uncertifiable, never to a violation. Premises: a package imports a sibling only through a declared dependency, as the managers require (not by a relative path out of its directory, a `tsconfig` `paths` alias or an undeclared hoisted install); Deno and Bazel workspaces are not read. The daemon keys its answer on one digest of every file and member listing the decision read (`program_boundary_digest`), so a sibling that starts depending on the package reopens it at once. Owner: `rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/program_boundary.rs`; fixtures `program-boundary-workspace`, `program-boundary-pnpm`, `program-boundary-standalone`.

<a id="adr-0445"></a>
## ADR 0445 — A census with no entry proves no component
ADR 0331's census is vacuously complete for a function whose references are its declaration and module surface, so it needs a reference that renders the function: a tag, a dialect renderer prop, a proven `lazy` default, an audited route option, or a compound member a tag reaches. A JSX-returning function with none is a component only as an exported capitalized binding, the host's by module surface (a file route's default export, a library component). Else (dead, unexported, lowercase, or bound to a call's result, `const rows = items.map((x) => <li/>)`) its body is an undecided region as for an escaped reference: non-style violations stay uncertifiable. An anonymous function literal called where written has that call as its only entry and is no component: its caller's role is its role, and module scope has no owner. Premise left: an exported capitalized function nothing renders that unseen code calls plainly is judged a component. ADR 0203, 0213 and 0214 discharge toward silence for a function with no caller and never report; the closure they rest on is ADR 0444's. Owner: `compute_function_component_status` (`indexes/components.rs`); fixture `component-census-no-entry`.

<a id="adr-0446"></a>
## ADR 0446 — Precision is measured on a reproduction set, refreshed by one pre-release run
`fixtures/uncertifiable-repro` stands in for the corpus: a slice iterates on it in seconds on any machine, and each case is the test the slice must move. A case is one cause or shape, never one finding, of a `kind`: `uncertifiable` (a cause that holds results, ADR 0328), `violation` (a triaged real defect) or `clean` (a certified shape a runtime probe or mutation confirmed; no later change may make it uncertifiable or a violation). A local case is one short file in the shared project of its main rule; a cause that acts on the whole project gets a project of its own: the dispatch veto (ADR 0356), one project per site kind since one unexcluded site holds every handler proof, and the open program boundary (ADR 0444), under which the test-only-helper exclusion (ADR 0438) also fails. The component census (ADR 0445) moved no corpus result and gets none. The one `node_modules` holds published bytes only (the files `tsc` and the browser/development conditions read, each with its sha256 under the package's version and integrity, `packages.json`); every case type-checks against them and runs under the corpus configuration. `cases.json` is the one table: kind, cause, files, source (app, file:line, commit), weight (ADR 0448's for uncertifiable cases), expected results, status; snapshots pin the findings. A cause is what `scripts/uncertifiable-causes.mjs` reads around a site with TypeScript's symbol resolution, from the premises and from a second sweep with `--program-boundary closed`: a grouping label, never evidence. A case is faithful when its result has its source's rule, analysis context and message family and reads as the same cause; `make repro-causes` checks the set side, `--report` the corpus side. No continuous intake: issues with minimal repros carry what users see, and once before each release a pinned, versioned manifest of public Solid 2 repos is restored, swept, reproduced and discarded (CONTRIBUTING.md): a known cause updates its weight, a new one becomes a case unless it cannot be reproduced faithfully (it then joins `notReproduced` with why), a false positive blocks the release. Budgets, with lines of their own for violation and clean cases: `scripts/repro-budget.mjs` in `make verify`. Owners: `fixtures/uncertifiable-repro`, `scripts/{uncertifiable-causes,repro-reduce,repro-budget,corpus-sweep}.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0447"></a>
## ADR 0447 — Veto weight is what a veto-off sweep clears
A result is the dispatch veto's (ADR 0356) only if a sweep by a scratch build whose sites withhold nothing (`withholds_owner_proofs`/`withholds_read_proofs` answer false; never committed) no longer has it; one it keeps takes its other cause (`--noveto-report`). On the rc.17 corpus the shape reading gave it 787 results, 108 of which the veto-off sweep keeps, and missed 279 it clears; the sweep gives it 958. app-game holds event-target and helper sites together, so its case's project holds both kinds; excluding one moves nothing. Owner: `scripts/uncertifiable-causes.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0448"></a>
## ADR 0448 — Coverage counts sites whose mechanism a source read verified
A label is a grouping: the rc.17 corpus gave 95.7% of results a case's label, yet sampled sites read as their case's mechanism in 315 of 551 reads. So an uncertifiable case records `labelWeight` (results its label takes), `sites` (distinct app, file, line and rule: a file several projects analyze counts once) and `verification.samples`, a seeded draw of 5 sites (all when fewer) each read from source against the case's file (`same`, `different`, `unsure`, with the mechanism). `verifiedWeight` is `sites` when every sample reads `same`, else 0; `unsure` counts against. A mixed label is split by the classifier until each case is one mechanism; a mechanism under 10 distinct sites gets no case and joins `notReproduced` with its reason. `--report` checks that the counts and samples still match the sweep; `--draw` redraws. Only verified coverage is quoted: 2,350 of 5,183 sites (45.3%) at 187 cases; label coverage 4,834 of 6,734 results (71.8%); 1,494 sites sit under 611 labels of fewer than 10. A source read can miss a second blocker the label hides, so every verified case was also tested by experiment: in a corpus clone, remove the case's blocker at a recorded or drawn site and see the site move (859 site runs). A stay sets the case's weight to 0 (74 cases, 1,427 sites refuted: writes unplaced before the await, other obligations on the line, a project-wide veto or census). 2,247 verified sites are experiment-confirmed; 103 (25 cases) had no faithful edit and rest on reading. Owners: `scripts/{uncertifiable-causes,repro-budget}.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0449"></a>
## ADR 0449 — The classified dispatch veto and the write-flow table are read sections
The veto of ADR 0356 is the dispatch hazard's sites classified by receiver, caller and the two censuses (ADRs 0435, 0437, 0438); it is a whole-lookup computation, so `Section::SynchronousDispatch` binds its value (the raw hazard, the owner answer, the read answer) and a memo that read the section reads the whole program when the hazard is present (the hazard's probe alone when it is not). The test-runner census runs over the lookup and is read only through the veto. A walk that follows an exported function declaration to its other modules asks the census's own answers in the memo world (`exported_call_sites_of`: hand-outs, the symbol's references, the call graph), so an unrelated edit leaves it unrun. The `declaredKeys` demand (ADR 0436) takes protocol 86 on Wire table schema v23, after protocol 85; its keys are part of a file's Entities fingerprint. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `execution_role/{invocation_flow,flow_memos,event_literals}.rs`, `indexes/census.rs`, `base/{reads.rs,memo/build.rs}`.

<a id="adr-0450"></a>
## ADR 0450 — Edits on a project with dispatch sites cost what they cost on one without
The veto's cost was the write-flow table, not its reads: only a write's enclosing functions are ever asked. The answer is one memo per function and guard, `WriteFlowAnswer` (table 47, key `(path, span, exempts root)`, computed through the lookup by `write_flow_answer`, its walk cache cleared after each answer), and `write_flow_role` asks it of the functions that enclose the write, so a build computes the answers its chunks ask. For the veto (classification is 12 ms on app-game): (1) `Section::DispatchVeto` is what the two accessors note, and `Section::SynchronousDispatch` is the raw hazards index again, the hazard's probe alone; (2) `DispatchVetoAnswer` (table 46) holds `(owner proofs, read proofs)` and `project_file_recorded` reads it while the hazard is present, so a reader reruns only when the hazard flips or one of those two bits moves (equal reruns keep their revision, ADR 0385; sound because a reader sees the veto only through the bits); (3) the test-runner census is asked of the one function a dispatch is written in (each literal's answer is independent; union over a path's slots), not computed for every function, and placed sites come from the hazard index's `SynchronousDispatch` entries. The default program boundary (ADR 0444): a request decides once (the analysis takes the boundary, the answer's inputs the digest, of that one read), and the daemon keeps the decision (`BoundaryCache`) while every observation it rested on still holds: a file's or listed directory's device, inode, length, modification and change times (the daemon's own file stamp, ADR 0400), a path's absence, the real path the project canonicalizes to; a decision that listed a symbolic link is never kept, and a file is read once per decision. Test: `dispatch_veto_reads_process.rs` (retained against fresh with every reused memo answer recomputed, `IncrementalBuilder::verify_reused_memos`; a reader recorded without the veto fails it by name). Owners: `indexes/{event_delivery,project_memos}.rs`, `execution_role/{event_literals,invocation_flow}.rs`, `base/{reads.rs,memo/build.rs}`, `cache.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/{program_boundary,daemon}.rs`.

<a id="adr-0451"></a>
## ADR 0451 — A change that moves findings is gated on edit latency at a looser ratio
The ratio is gated whenever the base answered from its retained daemon: at 1.25 where the answers are equal, at 1.5 (`DIFFERING_RATIO_LIMIT`) where they differ; a head answer outside its retained daemon still fails outright. Why the ratio and not a ceiling: ceilings are absolute times of one runner image and are not calibrated (ADR 0423), the paired median needs no calibration and is what the check already measures. Why 1.5: moved findings can add work (a new rule, more results to emit), so the bound is looser than 1.25, and a real 2.0 to 2.7x regression sits over it. The ceilings stay reported. Owners: `benchmarks/lib/real-projects.mjs`, `scripts/real-projects.test.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0452"></a>
## ADR 0452 — A closure a tracked scope builds runs where it is called
A tracked scope (a memo or effect compute, a tracked JSX expression) gave every write and read written in it its role, a proof for code it runs. Building a closure runs nothing. `enclosed_by_stored_function` left an anonymous stored literal to its invocations but skipped a function bound to a name ("the call sites' business"), and the lexical role was returned before any call site was consulted (`write_roles.rs`, `direct`), so `SC2001` reported `const openFn = () => setOpen(true); return openFn` (rc.17 runs it from a click, legal), and a read in a returned or stored closure was certified tracked although rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` when the body calls it. Writes: the named closure's entries decide (`named_closure_in_tracked_scope`): a call in the scope's own pass is a violation, also through layers that run in place (an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback), an alias or an outer closure; a call in a leaf scope or handler is not; a closure only returned or stored is uncertifiable. A name decides nothing (a capitalized closure is judged by its calls; one that returns JSX is a possible component). Reads (`unrun_closure_within`, in both tracked arms of `semantic_role_before_awaits`): inside the region, a stored function, or a named closure not entered only by calls in tracked positions, takes no role, so the read is uncertifiable; an IIFE and a name every reference of which is a tracked call keep `Tracked`. A default value (`(x = set(1)) => x`, `({ a = count() }) => a`) lies outside its function's body but runs only when that function is called, so every closure question looks at the functions whose body or parameter list holds the site (`functions_around`: the stored-literal, JSX-attribute, region and named-closure guards). `functions_around` finds the owners of a parameter list by their whole span, so a function written inside a default (an IIFE, an inline callback) belongs to the closure too, and a write there is unplaced whatever role its lexical position or the function around the closure has (`write_flow_role` and the walk's entries took the body-based function, so a helper returning the closure placed the default by the helper's own calls). A call in the owner's own parameter list is no call in its body for the summary rows (`interproc/graph.rs`, `callback_chain.rs`): it runs only when the caller omits the argument. The `containing_ast_function` lookups (about 150) still placed a site in a nested parameter list in the function around it: ADR 0454. The same query now serves the component-child, provider-value, `merge` argument, named-callback, retained-literal and summary-owner questions. A named closure with a default in it is unplaced for writes and reads: its parameter list is not inert, so the call graph withholds its call sites (ADR 0339), which also costs the violation for a write in the body of a closure with a non-literal default called in the scope. Costs: an object method the scope runs by coercion (`valueOf`) and a recursive closure (its own calls are in progress, so unproven) are uncertifiable too; the corpus gains 17 uncertifiable results, no violation. Not changed: `project_consumer` and `runs_outside_owner_call` still read `attribute_function_within` alone. Fixtures `write-named-closure-in-tracked-scope`, `read-closure-in-tracked-scope`; rule page `no-write-in-pure-computation`.

<a id="adr-0453"></a>
## ADR 0453 — The runtime oracle runs in its own pinned Solid rc.17 install
The oracle borrowed corpus apps' `node_modules`, whose compilers were not rc.17: `@solidjs/vite-plugin` 3.0.0-next.51 asks for `@solidjs/compiler` and `@solidjs/babel-plugin` `^2.0.0-rc.16`, which resolves to rc.18 (helge-dev) or, in an older install, rc.4 (probus-hk). Invariant 7 forbids a newer prerelease and the compile is part of the evidence. `scripts/runtime-oracle-install` (manifest and lockfile; `make runtime-oracle-install` runs `npm ci`) pins every Solid package, by `overrides` the compiler and babel plugin, and the tooling and packages cases import, at rc.17 or exact versions. A run on it refuses a compiler that is not `solid-js`'s version. `oracle.json` `install` is removed; `--install` still names another directory to compare, unasserted. Owner: `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0454"></a>
## ADR 0454 — A site in a parameter list belongs to the function whose list it is
A default value (`(x = cb()) => x`, `({ a = count() }) => a`), and a function written in one, runs only when its function is called without that argument. The lookups through `containing_ast_function` (the innermost function whose *body* holds a site) put such a site in the function around it, so `function runNested(cb) { const g = (x = cb()) => x; return g; }` as `createMemo(() => { runNested(() => setV(1)); return 1; })` was an `SC2001` for a write that never runs (`calls_parameter_in_body` took the call for one in `runNested`'s body, and `write_site_role` placed it in `runNested`'s activation). rc.17 throws nothing for it, nor for the forwarded `(x = runBody(cb)) => x`, the closure in a branch, returned directly, destructured, declared, with an IIFE in the default, or called with the argument supplied (`oracle-behaviour-parameter-list-owner`: the counters stay 0 where the checker said violation). The owner primitive is `AstFacts::innermost_function_owning` / `functions_owning`: the innermost function whose body holds the site or whose parameter list does (from its first parameter to the body; its name and type parameters are bound around it). Over it, `owners.rs`: `owning_function`; `containing_body_function` (the owner when its body holds the site, else none); `owned_by_body` (`containing_ast_function(span) == function` for a call, member, JSX element or binding, true only for a site in the body); `in_parameter_list`; `functions_around` is `functions_owning`. `containing_ast_function` stays the lexical lookup the role pipelines use: a list site takes the role of the code around its function and is then withheld (`parameter_default_owner`, now the owner of a site directly in a list, `unrun_closure_within`, the write guard); a global switch of it to the owner moved `prop-head-get`'s dormant default from uncertifiable to a violation. A call in the owner's own list (`run(cb, x = cb())`) runs when a caller omits `x`: no body call, kept unplaced; no proof that every caller omits it is built. A parameter called in a list is a call that may be made: `calls_parameter_in_parameter_list` keeps the summary row uncertifiable, as the misplaced call did. The write guard covers a site in the list of any function, not only a closure bound to a name, so an anonymous callback's default is uncertifiable (`forEach((item, i = setV(1)) => …)`, whose index is supplied), and so is a true one (an IIFE that omits the argument, `createMemo((prev = setV(1)) => …)`). Costs: those true violations; a forbidden call in an IIFE's default in a leaf callback is one obligation at the IIFE, not also one at the call (`DefaultImmediate`). The owner graph (SC4001) places a call by its owner too (`owner_discovery.rs`): a call in a list is an edge from no source that may be unowned (`MayBeUnowned`), its requirement is uncertifiable, held by the owning function's context (zero, never called, counts as unowned) and not by a providing region around the function; a component's own list under its owned body stays silent, unless the call is a function literal invoked there (`m = (() => onCleanup(…))()` is legal and uncertifiable: the in-place invocation has no source). The SC3001 leaf walk judges a call in the callback's own list like a call in its body, kept uncertifiable: rc.17 calls an `onSettled` callback with no arguments (`@solidjs/signals` dist/dev.js `onSettled`, `untrack(callback)`), so its default runs in the leaf scope (`oracle-parameter-list-owner-leaf`; it was silent), but no dialect fact states the arguments a primitive passes, so no violation is proven. A component's own list is a possible component's (`in_component_parameter_list`), no module initialization: a read there is uncertifiable, not silent. `reachability.rs` and the summary graph (`interproc/graph.rs`) place a list call by `owning_function` too: reached when its function is, the graph edge is not a guaranteed one, so the result is uncertifiable (also for a legal tracked or handler call, a cost, and for a call that supplies the argument). Unchanged: `async_context.rs` `inside_non_component_function` (no probe differed), and `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs` is retained behaviour. Fixtures `parameter-list-owner`, `write-default-in-helper-closure`; rule page `no-write-in-pure-computation`.

<a id="adr-0455"></a>
## ADR 0455 — A write's owner is the one its callback's call gives it, through the layers that run in place
A callback's lexical hold does not place a write (`write_region_adjustment`, `write_roles.rs`). The root, call-site, first-run and unaudited-root arms use `direct_callback_runs`, so a list site reaches the write guard and is uncertifiable, for every scope those arms cover (`createRoot`, `render`, `hydrate`, `untrack`, `dynamic` static, `flush`, `dispatchAsInteraction`, `createRenderEffect`'s first-run apply, an unaudited root; `createRoot` and `untrack` pinned). New arm `detached_in_place` climbs from the site through layers that run on the callback's stack (`layer_run`, shared with `runs_in_place_within`: an IIFE, an argument a standard-library call runs inline) and through calls that keep the owner and run the callback during the call (`untrack`) to the primitive callback holding them. Owner `None` (`runWithOwner(null, …)`) is `Detached`: legal wherever the call is. Owner `Conditional` (may be null, or a bound `createOwner()` result, which `run_with_owner_callback_owner` does not prove non-null) through a layer is `OwnerUnknown`: uncertifiable; it was a violation unproven for null. A proven owner (`createOwner()`, a component's `getOwner()`) is a root whenever the callback runs, so the write is `CreatedRoot`'s: a violation, directly in the callback and through layers, also when the call sits in a handler (rc.17 throws; not a layer in a parameter list, which needs the omission proven). The owner that may be null is uncertifiable directly too, and a write in a layer under `untrack` takes the role at the `untrack` call (`CallSite`), as a direct one does. A layer that stores the function, hands it to an unknown call or runs it on a fresh stack ends the climb. A closure bound to a name inside an `untrack` callback and called there (rc.17 throws) is served by ADR 0452's walk (`named_closure_in_owner_keeping_callback`, beside `named_closure_in_tracked_scope`): the closure's call sites decide, a call in the callback's pass takes the role at the `untrack` call (a violation under an owned scope, legal in a handler or under a detached owner, on a fresh stack legal), a closure only returned or handed by name is uncertifiable, a site in a list stays unplaced. An anonymous literal the callback returns, stores or hands to a call it does not run in place is unplaced too (`unrun_layer_in_owner_keeping_callback`); one handed to a project helper that calls it is a violation by the helper's summary (rc.17 throws). Costs: a closure with a default called in a root or `untrack` body with the argument omitted is uncertifiable where it was a violation (rc.17 throws; nothing proves the omission, as in a memo), and so is a write under a bound `createOwner()` owner (directly or in a layer), which `run_with_owner_callback_owner` does not prove non-null. Fixtures `write-detached-owner-layers`, `write-default-in-root-closure`. In the oracle's own harness (a dispatching entry) every handler write is uncertifiable, an `untrack` IIFE in a handler too.
Disposal (`fixtures/reactive-ir/write-owner-in-cleanup`): rc.17's write guards (`setSignal`, `refresh`, store setters) test `!disposalDepth`, which `runDisposal` raises around every `onCleanup` callback (`dev-shared.js:5620-5626`, `:1314`; rc.13 throws), so a write a cleanup runs under a proven owner lands. `Dialect::callback_runs_only_in_disposal` (`onCleanup` only) and `write_guard_in_disposal` (release-keyed: `Rejects` through rc.13, `StandsDown` on rc.17, `Unknown` for an unread release and the conservative vocabulary; tokens `disposal-rejects-writes`, `disposal-stands-down`, none, so an id spelled before decodes `Unknown`) place it. Legal only when the climb through inline layers ends at an `onCleanup` call whose result is discarded (rc.17 returns the callback) with no await ahead of the write, and the release stands down; `Unknown` there is uncertifiable. A violation needs proof the write is outside a disposal (a component body, a fresh stack, a scheduled callback, a past-await continuation, an effect's returned cleanup, a function only events call); a named, stored or handed-on layer is uncertifiable. An action call keeps its violation (its guard has no `disposalDepth` test). Cost: a named root helper called from a body is no longer reported. A function passed by name to `createRoot` asks the same disposal question at its call; an effect created inside a cleanup is unplaced, since a flush there applies it in the disposal. An effect's returned cleanup also runs when its owner is disposed (a memo that reruns, a `Show` turned off, a removed row, a disposed root), inside a disposal, and nothing orders that against a rerun, so on a stands-down release it is unplaced whatever the effect tracks (`effect_provably_reruns` no longer proves a violation there; rc.13 reports it, a release nobody read keeps the proof). The rerun that finds the owner alive throws and goes unreported (`docs/open-work.md`).

<a id="adr-0456"></a>
## ADR 0456 — A called `onCleanup` handle runs its callback where it is called
rc.17's `onCleanup(fn)` returns `fn` (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:2377-2405`, prod `signals.js:65-67`). So `const h = onCleanup(() => setV(1)); h();` in a memo runs the write at `h()`, with `disposalDepth` 0, and throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`. A write in an `onCleanup` callback took the callback's lexical `Deferred` role, though, and the handle was never read, so 60 of the 75 items of the disposal fuzz run (Chrome-confirmed, 7 of them `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`) were silent. Now `Dialect::disposal_callback_returned` (`Returned`, `NotReturned`, `Unknown`) says what the call returns. Solid 2 answers `Returned` for `onCleanup`'s callback when the read signals release is rc.17, and `Unknown` otherwise. The answer keys on `write_guard_in_disposal`'s `StandsDown`, which is exactly `Read(RC17)`: a digit of its own would double the variant table. `cleanup_handle.rs` takes the census by exact resolution. The callback runs only in the disposal when the result is discarded or bound to a `const` nothing reads. It also runs at the handle's calls when the result is a `const` whose every reference is a call in the file (not exported, no `eval`, no other-file reference), or when the call is written on the result (`onCleanup(fn)()`). Anything else (stored, passed, returned, aliased, an unread release) is unplaced. The write walk climbs from the write to the callback through layers that run in place (an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, `untrack`) and judges the write at each handle call: one that rejects it is the violation, an unplaced one leaves it uncertifiable, and calls that are all legal keep the disposal's role. A closure the callback builds, or a write under an owner a call in it gives (`runWithOwner`, `createRoot`), is left to its own entries: a named closure is judged at its calls, which take the handle's role. `runs_in_disposal` asks the same of an owner-given write. A handle call proven outside a disposal is `NotIn`, a violation. Costs: a passed, aliased or object-member handle, and any called handle off rc.17, is uncertifiable. Not this mechanism (`docs/open-work.md`): an action in a closure a cleanup hands to `createRoot` by name; `flush()` in an effect's cleanup. A handle call in an instance field, a possible or undecided component or a refused file is unplaced, by the backend's own predicates (it withdraws by the write's location, never the call's). A callback registered by name is judged like an inline one when `fn` is exactly resolved, else uncertifiable; a write that may follow an await is placed at no handle call. A handle call in a dead branch (`if (false)`, after a `return`) or in a function proven not to run (every call and render unreached, or unreferenced) is unplaced too. Fixture `write-cleanup-handle-call`; rule page `no-write-in-pure-computation`.
<a id="adr-0457"></a>
## ADR 0457 — A helper that calls a callback in its own body is a layer that runs it in place
A fuzzer-found `SC2001` false positive (Chrome-confirmed; rc.17 throws nothing and the write lands): `runWithOwner(null, () => { inline1(() => setW(17)); })`, with `inline1` a project function or a package export read from source (`export function inline1(cb) { return cb(); }`). ADR 0455's climb (`detached_in_place`) crossed only an IIFE or an inline standard-library callback (`layer_run`), so it ended at the helper and the write took the lexical role, which `nested_literal_runs_during_body` proved through that same helper: the component body's, a violation. A maybe-null owner there was a violation too. Now `layer_run` also takes a direct argument whose exact callee, not a primitive, calls that parameter in its own synchronous body on every completion (`invokes_parameter_during_call`, the proof `callee_callback_timing` takes for reads; ADR 0266, 0300 for dependency source): the call is on the callback's stack and under its owner. Every walk `layer_run` serves gains it: the owner climb (null legal, proven owner a violation, maybe-null uncertifiable), the disposal climb (`runs_in_disposal`: a cleanup that runs a helper that runs `runWithOwner(o, …)` is legal on rc.17), the `untrack` layers, and `runs_in_place_within`. A helper that keeps, schedules, hands on the callback or calls it in a closure (`createRoot(() => cb())`) proves nothing and the climb ends there; the write's invocations decide, else it is uncertifiable. Review amendment: that proof is "called at least once", not "every run is that call". `parameter_uses_during_call` splits a callee whose every use of the parameter is a direct call in its own body (`InPlace`) from one that also passes it on, stores it or closes over it (`InPlaceAmongOthers`); a callee that reassigns it or reads `arguments` proves nothing. Among others only a violation reads the in place run (a proven owner, `NotIn`); every legal arm (null owner, `Only`, `untrack`, `runs_in_place_within`) leaves the write unplaced. Fixture `write-detached-owner-helper`;

<a id="adr-0458"></a>
## ADR 0458 — A violation at a site no execution reaches is uncertifiable
Five fuzzer-found false positives (Chrome-confirmed on rc.17: every flagged site had 0 hits and no diagnostic; rendering the function or removing the dead guard raises it) had one cause: nothing pruned code no execution reaches. `false && setW(1)` in a called handle's callback (SC2001), `return; class K { static { onCleanup(…) } }` and `return; onCleanup(…)` in a timer (SC4001), `f1()` written only in a `Child1` nothing renders or names (SC2001), and an async `Child1` returned from an IIFE whose result is discarded, or never rendered (SC4001). ADR 0456 had built that reachability for handle calls alone. One query now answers it (`SiteReach`, `execution_role/site_reach.rs`): `Unreached` is a proof the site never runs, `MayRun` proves nothing. Syntax decides the first half (`AstFacts::unreachable_regions`, facts schema 77, `solid-facts` `ast/unreachable.rs`): statements after one that never completes normally in the same list (`return`, `throw`, `break`, `continue`, a block or an `if` whose arms all do; a hoisted `function` declaration is not dead), and arms a literal test rules out (`if`, `?:`, `while`, `for`, the right of `&&`, `||`, `??`; literals are booleans, `null`, numbers, strings, `void`, function, object and array literals, and `!`/`&&`/`||` over them; no identifier, `undefined` included). The second half is whether the function running the site runs, a least fixed point: never when it is written in dead code or in a function that never runs, when it is a constructor, method or accessor of a class whose name is not exported and has only unreached references (no static block, static initializer or `@` in its scope), when it is bound to a name that is not exported, no module calls `eval`, every call or render the call graph resolves is unreached, and every reference is unreached, in a type query or what a discarded IIFE returns, and when it is an anonymous function a discarded IIFE returns. A function at module scope may run (modules and hosts this does not follow; ADR 0445 keeps the module surface), and so may any other unnamed value once created. The pipeline asks it of the location of every row the projection may report, after every producer and gate (`unreached_report_sites`, `Program::unreached_sites`, compared by the shadow build). The backend makes a violation at an exact unreached location uncertifiable, whatever the rule, beside the undecided-component downgrade (`diagnostics.rs`); style rules (SC80xx) and SC2008, which claims a no-op wherever it runs, are left alone. The handle census (ADR 0456) asks the same query, and no longer takes a named function whose calls are all unreached for unreached when it is also handed on as a value. Not covered: a site after a call that always throws (SC3001's dead tracked-effect body), a `try` that cannot complete, `undefined` or a constant binding as a test, a module-scope function nothing references, a site reached only through an unreached call of a function also called elsewhere (the finding's location is reached), and an evidence step in dead code. Fixture `dead-code-sites`.
<a id="adr-0459"></a>
## ADR 0459 — A `const` destructuring of a namespace import binds the export it names
`const { onCleanup: cleanup } = S`, with `import * as S from "solid-js"`, holds exactly the export `S.onCleanup` reads: a module namespace object is immutable, a `const` slot is never reassigned, and the copy equals the export while the export binding is never reassigned, true of Solid's function exports (the dialect's primitive list gates it). So `cleanup(…)` names the primitive wherever `S.onCleanup(…)` does. solid-facts records the slot (`AstFacts::namespace_member_bindings`, facts schema 77) only when the binder resolves the initializer (an identifier under TypeScript wrappers) to a value namespace import binding, the declarator is a non-ambient `const`, the key is an identifier or a string literal (cooked) and the value a bare binding; a computed key, a default, a rest element, a nested pattern, a `let`/`var`, a shadowing `S` and an exported slot are absent, so every mention is in the declaring file. The IR names the slot's symbol after the export (`add_solid_import_names`, `referenced_primitive`), `effect_phase::declaring_file` follows it to the namespace's import, and the backend's demand plan asks for it (`PrimitiveImports`). A mention that may run before the declarator throws `ReferenceError` instead of calling: ADR 0339's proof decides each one (`namespace_slot_mention_withheld`), and a withheld call names no primitive, which leaves the reference an uncertifiable `SC9012` obligation. Only claims about the call itself (`missing-owner`, `flush-in-effect-callback`) need that guard, and they read the guarded path (`call_primitive_name_cached`, the primitives memo). The JSX control-flow, post-row and async-context sites read names unguarded: everything they claim runs only after the tag or call succeeded. A new claim about a call site must read the guarded path. Not this: a namespace of a project module that re-exports Solid (the namespace form is not resolved either), and a local value read through a literal (`const { useIt } = { useIt: r1 }`). Fixture `namespace-destructured-api`; `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback` gains the destructured `flush`.
<a id="adr-0460"></a>
## ADR 0460 — A write through in-place layers is placed where the layers run, in a render-effect apply and a control-flow row
The misuse fuzzer found writes (signal and store setters, `refresh`) silent in two places, for every binding. (1) In a `createRenderEffect` apply, a write written directly was already uncertifiable (its first run is synchronous under the component's owner only when no `defer`/`schedule`, no pending compute and, on rc.17, no lane seats it), but one layer down (an IIFE, a helper calling its callback, a named closure the apply calls) took the apply's lexical deferred role, which is legal. `detached_in_place` now places a climb through in-place layers that reaches a callback sharing its call's owner on its first run (`CallbackOwner::InheritsFirstRun`) as a write at the call, which keeps it uncertifiable; a site in a parameter list or a callback a helper also hands on gets no placement. `callback_takes_call_owner` lets ADR 0455's named-closure and unrun-layer rules cover that apply too. (2) `control_flow_execution_role` gives every function nested in a control-flow children callback (`For`'s row) the deferred role, including an IIFE in the row's JSX child, which runs in place in a render computation. `write_role_walk` now follows the layers between the write and the row (`control_flow_nested_layers`): all in place, the write takes the role at the outermost call (a violation in a JSX child, as a write written there already was); one bound to a name, stored or handed to an unknown call, uncertifiable; a primitive's callback, a JSX attribute value (a handler) or a scheduler's callback keeps its own role, so handlers, timers and effect applies in a row stay legal. Fixture `reactive-ir/write-render-apply-row-layers` (4 uncertifiable, 7 violations, negatives); every case checked on the rc.17 fast path.
<a id="adr-0461"></a>
## ADR 0461 — `delete` of an optional store property is a store write
rc.17's `deleteProperty` trap drops a delete outside a setter behind the `set` trap's gate (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:5761-5767`, `inDraft` `:4955-4957`), so `delete store.path` is reported where `store.path = x` is, with the same store proof (bare root identifier, static member path) and exemptions (own setter, compute function, rc.0-rc.6 readonly root). Upstream reports every delete; tsc reports a required operand (TS2790) and a readonly one (TS2704), so the operand must carry the new Type Fact `writableOptionalProperty`: the access's resolved property symbol is `Property | Optional` and not readonly, which type-checks under every compiler option for a bare or parenthesized operand (tsc sees through parentheses only: `delete store.v!` and `delete (store.v as T)` are TS2703 and stay silent). A chain past the root (`store.a.b`) is silent when a link's primitive value domain may hold `null`/`undefined` (TS18047/TS18048) and otherwise uncertifiable, never a violation: without `strictNullChecks`, which the facts do not record, a link's type proves nothing about its runtime value, and an `undefined` link throws before any write (the tsc oracle's loose project); a store delete whose facts were not served is uncertifiable. A non-optional property whose type includes `undefined` is TS2790 under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` and stays silent. Wire table schema v24 (row bit 17, no payload), demand bit 20, protocol 87. Refused, silent: element access, optional chain, cast root, a `const` alias (the assignment form follows none). Owners: `writable_optional_property_at` (`apps/solid-typefacts/src/type_facts.rs`), `no_store_delete` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`), `collect_delete_spans` (`demand_plan.rs`); fixture `store-delete`.
<a id="adr-0462"></a>
## ADR 0462 — `Loading`/`Errored` called as functions need an owner; an async read in a rendered local function is uncertifiable
The misuse matrix found two codes with no recall. (1) `NO_OWNER_BOUNDARY`: rc.17's `Loading(props)` and `Errored(props)` (`solid-js` `dist/solid.dev.js`) call `createLoadingBoundary`/`createErrorBoundary`, whose `createBoundary` (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev.js`) warns when `getOwner()` is null; the hydrated wrappers pass straight through with no owner (`noHydrationId`). The owner walk asked only about JSX `<Loading>`, so the call form was never a subject. The dialect now answers `OwnerRequirementRole::Boundary` for `Primitive::Loading | Primitive::Errored`, and owner discovery records the call as operation `boundary-call` (reported as `boundary`), placed like an effect: unowned contexts, `runWithOwner(null)`, and after an `await` (`calls_after_await`). JSX `<Loading>` keeps its placement: rc.17 dev's `createComponent` runs a component in a transparent root, so a tag in a handler does not warn and is not claimed here; the module-scope tag claim stays (prod creates the boundary with no owner, and the dev root is never disposed either). `createLoadingBoundary`/`createErrorBoundary` are not in the published typings. The typings accept `Loading({ fallback: 0, children: 1 })`. (2) `ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY`: an async read was claimed only where the accessor call itself sits in rendered JSX. A read written directly in a named local function (`const wrapUse = () => { a1(); }`), not a generator or async, whose binding is never written, is now followed through direct calls (up to four named layers) to a call in rendered JSX, and recorded there as an `AsyncRead` with `call_attributed`: nothing proves the read runs pending while the call renders, so SC5003/SC5005 are uncertifiable, never violations (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`, `local_access.rs`). Silent at this entry, resolved later in the same batch: an alias (`const useIt = a1`) and an object-literal destructuring (`const { useIt } = { useIt: a1 }`) of the accessor (ADR 0463), and a callback handed to a project helper for the boundary call (ADR 0474), as for every owner requirement (rule page). Placing the fuzzer's own render loop (`Object.entries(mod)` in `main.tsx`) is whole-program flow the mount census does not follow, so even the direct read stays uncertifiable there. Fixtures `boundary-call-owner`, `async-boundary-call-attributed`.
<a id="adr-0463"></a>
## ADR 0463 — An exact `const` alias of a local reactive binding is its source
The misuse matrix found a local value read through an alias silent for every read and store rule (`const useIt = r1; useIt()`: `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`, `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`, `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT`, `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER` 0% agree), and its object-literal form silent for every rule (`const { useIt } = { useIt: r1 }`, which ADR 0459 left out), while a write or action call through `const set = setW1` was followed: source discovery's pass 5 (`resolve_setter_action_aliases`) copies a setter's or action's entry to any binding initialized by its identifier, but no accessor, and no row existed for a literal slot. solid-facts now records `AstFacts::local_alias_bindings` (facts schema 79): a non-ambient `const` that holds exactly a binding the binder resolves to a `const` variable of the same module, either `const a = b` (under parentheses and TypeScript wrappers) or a static slot `const { k: a } = { k: b }` of an object literal whose properties are all static data properties (no spread, computed or numeric key, getter, setter, method or `__proto__`), the key present once with an identifier value; an exported local is absent. Pass 5 reads one row per fact and, for those rows only, also gives the alias the source's accessor entry and every per-source fact (kind, primitive, phase, async provenance and options, origin, runtime standing, summary and return eligibility), including when the typed pass (ADR 0294) already proved the alias holds the source; setters and actions follow the literal slots too. Every rule then sees the source. A mention that may run before the alias's declarator throws `ReferenceError`: ADR 0339's proof now also covers aliases (`names_local_alias`), and a withheld mention (`local_alias_mention_withheld`) is unplaced in local access, so its read, write or action is uncertifiable. SC1002's pre-await tracking compares sources by their accessor declaration (`source_identity`) and store paths after the root's name, so a key read before the await through the source or the alias tracks it for both (the `after-await-tracked` runtime case). The store mutation rule (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`, product-owned proven-source logic, code unchanged) now sees an alias store, so ADR 0461's refused `const` alias delete is reported. Refused: a `let`/`var`, conditional, parameter, imported or exported alias, a `let` source, a default, a nested pattern. Not fixed: a direct read before its source's own declarator is still reported (open-work). Fixture `local-alias-bindings`; `store-delete` and `store-strict-read-keys` gain an alias violation each (rc.17 fast path confirms both).
<a id="adr-0464"></a>
## ADR 0464 — A site that names a binding before its declarator is proven to have run is uncertifiable
`[1].forEach(() => r()); const [r] = createSignal(0)` was SC1001 (Chrome on rc.17: "Cannot access 'r' before initialization", no Solid diagnostic; `tsc` is silent inside a closure). ADR 0339 ordered mentions of a function-valued binding only, and only for call edges: a read, write or store access of a destructured binding, and any rule other than the call graph's, never asked. Now the proof (`indexes/initialization.rs`) also decides every binding an array or object pattern declares with an initializer, and answers two sets per path: the mentions the call graph withholds (ADR 0339's order for a function-valued binding; its non-literal-default withholding, which says nothing of the mention's own order, stays there alone) and those whose order is unproven under the full order, which also places an object or class method at its creation, as an arrow is, and credits a project module's mention of an installed dependency's binding (ADR 0339's premise: the package imports no project module, so the load that makes the name usable completed its evaluation). The pipeline asks the second set of every reportable row's location and of a propagated read's origin (`early_mention_report_sites`, `Program::early_mention_sites`, compared by the shadow build); the backend makes a violation there uncertifiable, whatever the rule, beside ADR 0458's downgrade; style rules (SC80xx) are left alone. A mention anywhere in the site counts (`setX(r())` throws before the write). Not covered: an identifier bound to anything but a function or call (`const r2 = r`, `const r = pair[0]`); a site after an unordered mention in the same run (a throw ends it; ADR 0458 does not prune it either); a `for (const [k, v] of …)` binding, which has no initializer and is bound before each iteration. Fixture `destructured-binding-tdz`. Review: a mention inside a function written within the reported site does not count (it runs when that function is called; the frozen rc.17 corpus lost its one SC1001 to such a callback); a decided binding with no compiler reference list has its same-file binder mentions withheld and unordered; under the full order a `var` whose initializer is a call in its function's straight-line flow is ordered like a `const`.

<a id="adr-0465"></a>
## ADR 0465 — A store write past the root is TypeScript's where a link may be nullish, and proven only under strictNullChecks
SC2003 reported `store.a.b = 1` with `createStore<{ a?: { b?: number } }>({})`, which strict `tsc` reports as TS18048, and the same for `store.a.push(1)` and a nullable link (TS18047). ADR 0461's rule for `delete` now covers the assignment and the in-place array call: each link the write's member chain reads past its root (`AstFacts::member_chain_links`: every object below the written member that is itself a member, as written, through transparent wrappers) is asked for its primitive value domain (demand plan, capability `store_write_chain_links`), and Type Facts now states the program's effective `strictNullChecks` (protocol 87: the analyze answer's `strictNullChecks`, kept with the retained table, `TypeScriptTable::strict_null_checks`; the producer reads the checker's own rule, `get_strict_option_value`: the option, else `strict`, which TypeScript 7 turns on unless it is `false`, so a tsconfig with no `strict` key is strict). `chain_links_verdict` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`): no link (`store.v = 1`, `list.push(1)` on an array store, a cast root) keeps the violation; under strictNullChecks a link whose domain may hold `null` or `undefined` makes the write TypeScript's, so the rule is silent, a link whose domain excludes both keeps the violation (Chrome on rc.17: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`), and a missing or unknown domain, an element-access link (no `undefined` without `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`) or a chain under a type assertion (`store.n!.b`, `(store.o as T).b`; Chrome: they throw `TypeError`) is uncertifiable; without strictNullChecks, or with no answer, every write through a link is uncertifiable, since a type then proves nothing about a link's value at run time. Moved: `rows[props.index].done` (solid2-precision) to uncertifiable. Residual: a project without strictNullChecks gets an internal uncertifiable result where strict `tsc` would report; the tsc oracle requires silence in both passes, so no `removed-because-redundant` case pins TS18048/TS18047 (fixtures `store-write-chain-links`, `store-write-chain-links-loose`). Review: a nullish link is TypeScript's only in a chain with no optional access (`store.list?.push(2)` short-circuits, so it is uncertifiable) and in a file `tsc` reports in (a JavaScript file, whose `checkJs` the facts do not hold, and a file containing `@ts-nocheck`, `@ts-ignore` or `@ts-expect-error` are uncertifiable). The effective option follows TypeScript 7 (`strict` on unless `false`), the oracle's authority (typescript 7.1.0-beta); a TypeScript 5.x project with no `strict` key is a residual (docs/open-work.md). Merged with ADR 0461: `delete` keeps its parenthesis-only operand and `writableOptionalProperty`, and its links past the root take this verdict, so a nested delete through non-nullish links under strictNullChecks is a violation (Chrome on rc.17: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER` at `store.a.b` in a body and a handler and at a narrowed `store.o.b`), where ADR 0461 had it uncertifiable. Amendment (recall batch): the directive veto reads the parser's comments (`AstFacts::typescript_directive_comment`, facts schema 80) instead of scanning the source text, so a string literal naming a directive is no directive, and the answer is computed once per file at extraction.

<a id="adr-0466"></a>
## ADR 0466 — A callback passed by name is placed as the inline callback in its position
The containers fuzzer found a write in an IIFE or `forEach` inside a memo compute passed by name silent (`const cb1 = () => { (() => setW(1))(); return 1; }; createMemo(cb1)`; rc.17 throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`), where the inline compute and a direct write in `cb1` were reported. Comparing inline and by-name forms over every write host and layer (none, IIFE, `forEach`, a named closure, a helper calling its callback) found the shared cause: ADR 0455/0457's climbs (`detached_in_place`, `runs_in_disposal`) found a callback's primitive only when the function literal was the argument, and the memo arm of `write_role_walk` dropped a write nested in the named compute. Silent too where rc.17 throws: `untrack(cb1)` with a layer, and a render-effect apply by name (inline: uncertifiable, ADR 0460); uncertifiable where inline is a violation: `createRoot(cb1)` with a layer, an effect or render-effect compute by name; uncertifiable where legal: `runWithOwner(null, cb1)`. `missing-owner` had no by-name gap. Now `by_name_position` resolves the one primitive position whose bare identifier argument is exactly the function's binding (the value current, a declaration or a `const` arrow whose argument follows the declarator in the same function; not a method, a generator, a named function expression, or two positions), and both climbs treat it as the inline argument. A position that is not the function's only entry (other references, hand-out, export) is one run among others (ADR 0457): a rejecting run is a violation (`CreatedRootAmongOthers`, new `CallSiteAmongOthers` for `untrack`, whose legal answer is a final `Unknown`), and a legal one proves nothing (`runWithOwner(null)` then leaves the write to the function's entries, whose census does not count the position). The memo arm takes a write a named compute runs in place (`runs_in_place_within`), and `NamedCallbackRole::effect_compute` (an effect's tracked compute named as the whole argument; reads still not admitted) answers `TrackedJsx` for a write it runs in place. A cleanup's `onCleanup(f)` as `f`'s only entry runs `f` only in the disposal, so `write-owner-in-cleanup`'s `CleanupNamedOwnerHelper` and `CleanupNamedRootBody` and rc.3's exempt store setter in a by-name root body went from uncertifiable to legal (fast path: writes ran, no diagnostic). Costs, uncertifiable where rc.17 throws: an alias, a named function expression, a `const` named in a nested function. Fixture `write-callback-by-name`, every case checked on the rc.17 fast path.

<a id="adr-0467"></a>
## ADR 0467 — An effect apply's read of an async source stays a strict read
