The misuse matrix counts `const a1 = createMemo(async () => 1); createEffect(() => 1, () => { a1(); })` (and the render-effect form) as wrong-rule: rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` and the checker reports `strict-read-untracked` (SC1001). rc.17's `read()` (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev-shared.js:5320-5325`) throws for a pending source in a strict-read window before it would warn (`:5441-5446`), so one run raises one code, never both, and which one is timing: on the fast path the same apply throws when it first runs while the source is pending (plain, module-scope source, render-effect apply even with the source rendered) and warns when the mount waits on a rendered async read, under a `Loading`, when the compute reads the source or another async source, or on a guarded later run. Nothing local orders the apply's first run against the source's settling (a held mount anywhere above, a compute that waits, ADR 0460's unproven first-run premises), so `pending-async-unsuspendable-read` claims no apply read and SC1001 stays the violation: the read is untracked in the apply's strict-read window in both outcomes. Its message gains a sentence when the projection holds an async read row of proven async provenance at the same site (`FindingSeed::StrictRead::apply_reads_async_source`) and the compute does not read the source: a pending run throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` instead. Not built: a pendingness proof for the first run (it needs every async read that can hold the mount). Fixture `strict-read-async-apply` (rc.17 fast path for every case).
<a id="adr-0468"></a>
## ADR 0468 — A callee spelled in a runtime-equivalent way is the function it names; an unnamed callee is an obligation, never nothing
Equivalence mode and the containers fuzzer found every rule silent on `(void 0, setW)(1)`, `(0, f)()` and, for missing-owner, `(f1)()` over a helper registering `onCleanup`: the fail-closed part was that an unresolved callee was dropped (no local-access row, no owner edge; a function with no owner context counted as never running), and `call_edge_symbol` and reachability took a callee's first token for its target. Now (1) `AstFacts::composite_callee_operands`/`composite_callee_references` record what a comma, conditional or logical callee may evaluate to (not `&&`'s left); when an operand may be a local setter, action or accessor the call is an SC9012 obligation, and a new owner edge `MayRun` passes the caller's owner context minus "proven unowned" to every function it may run, also for a callee binding written anywhere, a mention before its declarator (ADR 0339/0464) and an exact object member tail whose method does not read `this` (a comma loses the receiver; one that reads it throws); the first-token guard applies to owner edges and reachability. (2) solid-facts records `(void 0, f)`, `(0, f)` and nested inert prefixes as a direct call of `f` (facts schema 81), never a member tail, an effectful prefix or `(0, eval)`; the demand plan asks the symbol of an identifier under a TypeScript wrapper, so `setX!(1)` and `(setX as T)(1)` resolve. Parentheses and an optional call of a proven non-nullish binding already resolved. Three missing-owner false positives on main are gone with (1): `let k = () => onCleanup(…); k = () => {}; k()`, its `k?.()` form after `k = undefined`, and `(f = h)()` (rc.17's fast path reports none). Moved: two TypeScript-wrapped callees in `async-call-prefix` and `own-setter-wrapper-identity` become uncertifiable (were silent). Not covered: a returned callable (`(void 0, q.bind(null))()`, RC1), `const fn = o.m; fn()`, a conditional alias. Fixture `callee-spelling` (39 cases).
<a id="adr-0469"></a>
## ADR 0469 — A read through layers that run in place is placed where they run
The containers fuzzer (RC7) and equivalence mode found SC1001 silent for a read in an IIFE, a `forEach` callback or a helper's callback inside a `createRoot` or `runWithOwner` body or an effect apply, and for `setK(r())` wrapped in an IIFE in an apply, while the direct read is reported: those role checks matched a read written directly in the callback, so the layered read took the surrounding callback's deferred (silent) role. `climb_in_place_layers` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) walks out through the layers the write rule already trusts (`layer_run`: an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, a helper calling its callback, also one that keeps it), stopping at a primitive's callback, a stored or handed-on function, a fresh stack, a generator or a layer called inside JSX, and records a parameter-list site and an await before the site (always or possibly). `callee_roles.rs` (reads only; writes untouched) places such a read as one written at the outermost layer's call before it falls back to the deferred role: a reporting place stays a violation, an await on every path makes it legal, a possible await or a parameter-list site uncertifiable. TDZ (ADR 0464) and the render-effect apply's first run (ADR 0460) are unchanged. Not this: an action in a named closure called in place inside a callback `createRoot` receives by name (the write engine's climb does not cross a named closure; equivalence item 2), a callable returned through a primitive and called later (RC1a) (`createRoot(() => <div>{r()}</div>)`, a pre-existing false positive, is ADR 0470). Fixture `read-in-place-layers` (13 violations, 8 legal, 3 uncertifiable; each on the rc.17 fast path).
<a id="adr-0470"></a>
## ADR 0470 — A read in JSX a callback writes takes the compiler's tracked role
A Chrome-confirmed false positive on main: `createRoot(() => <div>{r()}</div>)` was SC1001, while rc.17 is silent, because the pinned compiler compiles the child to `insert(_el$, r)` (an attribute to `effect(() => …)`, a component prop or child to a getter), which tracks the read. In `semantic_role_before_awaits` the effect-apply and the `createRoot`/`runWithOwner` branches ran before the compiler's tracked-region check and placed the callback, so a read in JSX it writes took the call's window; the existing escape ran only for a callback that clears tracking (`runWithOwner`). `own_jsx_compiler_role` (`execution_role/callee_roles.rs`, reads only) now runs first: when the compiler's narrowest placement of the read is in the read's own function, a tracked region is `TrackedJsx` and a prop or child getter `DeferredCallback`; an untracked once-evaluated value or a handler factory falls through unchanged. A read in the callback's body beside the JSX stays a violation. Not this: JSX in an effect apply, where rc.17 throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`, a code no rule claims (open-work). Fixture `read-jsx-in-callbacks`.

<a id="adr-0471"></a>
## ADR 0471 — A closure in a root's body runs under the root where it is called, whatever region holds the root call
The disposal fuzzer found SC2002 silent for `onCleanup(() => createRoot(() => { const g = () => a1(); g(); }))` and its by-name, function-declaration and effect-cleanup forms, and for an IIFE in `f1` with `(() => { createRoot(f1); })()`; rc.17 throws `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` at disposal. `write_allowed_spans` drops a root body from the regions a write may treat as imperative, but the cleanup, effect apply or handler around the root call stays one, so the lexical role (`DeferredCallback`) covered the closure and was returned before its calls were walked. A root body in a component body was right (its rendering role is no proof for a nested literal). Now, for a guard that rejects in a root (actions; setters, whose disposal exemption the walk still reads at the call), `named_closure_in_root_callback` takes the innermost closure bound to a name whose innermost primitive callback, literal or named by its binding, is a root body, as ADR 0455 does for `untrack`: its calls decide. A site a function runs in place that a primitive names as a root's body in a position the by-name climb does not admit is unplaced instead of legal (`in_place_in_root_named_callback`). `by_name_position` admits an argument in in-place layers (IIFE, inline standard-library callback, helper) whose outermost call follows the declarator in the binding's scope, with no await on the way and not in JSX. A closure stored, returned or kept and run elsewhere stays uncertifiable; one called in place and also kept is a violation (one run is enough). Not this: `createRoot` written directly in an effect apply throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` before its body runs, yet a write or action in the body is reported, inline (before) and now through a named closure (open-work). Fixture `write-named-closure-in-root`.

<a id="adr-0472"></a>
## ADR 0472 — An owner operation in a returned function no owner edge reaches is uncertifiable
`const mk = () => () => onCleanup(f); const h = mk(); setTimeout(() => h())` was certified, as were the same returned through `runWithOwner(null, …)`, `createRoot`, `untrack` or a memo, while rc.17 reports `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP`. The owner graph gives a call an owner edge for an exact function symbol, an invoked literal or a modeled callback slot; a binding that holds a call's result is none of them, so the returned function kept context zero, which emission reads as "no syntax runs it" (no report bit), and a lexical providing region around it discharged it too. Context zero is a proof only for a function nothing can call. `emit_file` now treats an operation whose function (or an enclosing context-zero function it is nested in) is a `return` argument or a concise arrow body as unowned and runtime-uncertain, and keeps the providing region from discharging it: uncertifiable, never a violation. A sound violation needs the returned value's exact transport to its calls (every return the same function, a `const` result, every use a call, and each primitive's result contract behind `Dialect`), which this does not build (open-work `review-r1-owner-mix`). Cost: a result called under an owner, in a root or never is uncertifiable. Fixture `owner-returned-callable` (14 uncertifiable, 1 violation, 2 clean; each on the rc.17 fast path); three existing findings were added, all uncertifiable SC4001 at returned functions (`callback-ts-sugar-identity` `makeHandler`, `directive-phases` `innerComputation` beside its SC6001, `object-member-import` the getter's returned closure).
<a id="adr-0474"></a>
## ADR 0474 — A helper call runs the callback the helper calls, for the owner graph
Containers RC2: `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP` was silent for an `onCleanup`-registering callback handed to a project helper that calls it (`function h1(cb) { cb(); }`) in a timer, a promise callback, an effect apply, a handler or `runWithOwner(null)`: the owner graph had an edge from the caller to `h1` but none from the call to the function literal it receives, so the callback kept owner context zero, which emission reads as never entered. ADR 0457's census (`helper_argument_run`, now shared by the write and read role walks and owner discovery, `execution_role/callback_standing.rs`) proves the exact helper calls the parameter on every completion or on some paths, and whether it also stores or hands it on. `owner_discovery.rs` adds, for a non-primitive call with no other callback edge, an edge to each function-literal argument the helper calls: the call's own kind when it always calls it, `MayRun` (ADR 0468) when only some paths do, plus a `MayBeUnowned` root edge when the helper also keeps or hands it on; no edge when it never calls it, reassigns the parameter or reads `arguments`. Moved: `uncertifiable-repro/sc4001`'s clean `withOwner(fn)` (`getOwner() ? fn() : createRoot(fn)`) gains an internal uncertifiable (the census proves neither the guard nor the hand-on). Fixture `owner-helper-callback` (7 violations, 6 silent, 2 uncertifiable; each on the rc.17 fast path). Amendment: a call behind an exact `getOwner()` test (the guard `push_owner_requirement` reads) adds only the source's owned part, one behind a call behind `!getOwner()` is not recognized (the facts record no unary expression and the source text is not read), so it keeps the "some paths" edge, and a hand-on to a `CallbackOwner::Creates` position (`createRoot(fn)`) an `Owned` edge, not `MayBeUnowned` (`helper_argument_owner_runs`).

<a id="adr-0475"></a>
## ADR 0475 — A creation the runtime refuses enters nothing
Chrome-confirmed false positives: a write or action in a `createRoot` body written in an effect apply (inline, through a named closure, by name) and a write in a component rendered only in an owner-backed `onSettled`, a `createTrackedEffect` callback or an effect apply were SC2001/SC2002 violations, while rc.17 throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` or `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` at the creation (rendering creates a transparent root, `solid.dev.js:44-69`, `dev-shared.js:1455-1495`) and the body never runs. ADR 0458's `SiteReach` now asks whether an entry is refused (`refused_entry`): a component tag, or a callback argument (literal or reference) of a primitive SC3001's `cleanup_rule` forbids under a leaf (`creates_forbidden_primitive`, not `onCleanup`/`flush`), written in the own extent of an effect apply or a `CallbackOwner::Leaf` callback. Such a callback is never entered; a named function, also at module scope, whose calls and renders are all refused or unreached and whose references are all refused, unreached or erased, is never entered. The backend makes every rule's violation there uncertifiable with its own wording (`UnreachedSite::refused_creation`). A render elsewhere keeps the violation. Lexical: an `onSettled` called out of band is no leaf at runtime, so a write in a root there is uncertifiable where it may be a violation (a cost, never a new report); an effect cleanup, a helper or an IIFE between the apply and the creation is not followed (as today). Fixture `write-in-refused-creation` (8 uncertifiable, 4 violations; each on the rc.17 fast path). Review amendment: the refusal also holds through layers the apply or leaf callback runs in place (`creation_refused_at` climbs `layer_run`'s in-place layers: an IIFE, a helper calling its callback), since rc.17's guard covers the whole callback run; a layer that also runs elsewhere, or a site past an await or a possible suspension, keeps its answer. Before this, `(() => createRoot(f1))()` in an apply was a violation on main, and ADR 0471 extended it to a named `f1`.

<a id="adr-0476"></a>
## ADR 0476 — The store setter's window is a dynamic extent, and a write past the root is unproven
The fuzzer's Chrome probes (witnessed: the raw value read back) found SC2003 wrong both ways around the own-setter exemption (ADR 0425), which was lexical. A write the setter's callback hands to `queueMicrotask`, `setTimeout` or `.then` runs after `storeSetter` closed the window (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:5847-5866`, `inDraft` `:4955-4957`): it is dropped with STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER, and the checker was silent. A closure defined outside `setStore(…)` and called inside it commits, and was reported. `store_write_standing` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`) now answers exempt, dropped or unproven. The write is climbed out through the layers that run in place (`layer_run`: an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, a helper calling its callback), crossing JSX, to the setter's own callback or the store's compute function. A fresh-stack scheduler, or an await on every path before the write, drops it; a possible await is unproven. A named function is in the window when every reference is a call in one, by name or as a method, or the setter's own argument (`setStore(bump)`); exact `const` aliases of the setter open the same window, and a write through an exact `const` alias of the store is the store's (legal.mjs found it reported, Chrome-confirmed). One call outside drops the write on that run. A hand-out, an export, a binding that may change, a method an unresolved call in the window may reach, a chain of callers deeper than three, an IIFE in JSX inside the callback, and an await in an async compute function all leave it unproven, never dropped. Separately, a write past the root (`store.a.b = 1`, `store.items.push(1)`, `delete store.a.b`) is uncertifiable unless a nullish link makes it TypeScript's. The proxy serves a child only through `isWrappable` (`:2272-2283`), which refuses frozen and non-plain values, and the value's plainness is no type's: a frozen intermediate was reported although the write lands. The root stays a violation, because `createStore` wraps its initial value unconditionally (`:5904-5915`). Owners: `shared_reactivity.rs`, `docs/rules/no-store-mutation-outside-setter.md`; fixture `store-setter-extent`; ownership cases `…/own-setter-deferred-write/001`, `…/own-setter-outside-closure/001`.

<a id="adr-0477"></a>
## ADR 0477 — A dispatch on an await continuation leaves the dispatch veto
A synchronous dispatch (ADR 0356) written in an async, non-generator function after an `await` of that same function that dominates it (`follows_dominating_await`, ADR 0320's continuation: outside JSX, a compiler tracked region and an instance field) leaves ADR 0435's veto as caller class `await continuation`, for owner and read proofs: the continuation resumes from the microtask queue on an empty stack, so the delivery it makes has no owner and no strict-read window (the premise ADR 0320 already rests on). A dispatch before the await, after an await under a condition, or in a closure written after the await (it runs on its caller's stack) keeps it. Found by the fuzzer: its entry (`scripts/fuzz/checker.mjs` `MAIN_SOURCE`, the oracle's `mainSource`) drives handlers from `async function exercise() { await pause(200); … element.dispatchEvent(…) }`, so every fuzzed and oracle program had its handler proofs withheld (an inline `onClick` write was SC2001 uncertifiable); the fuzzer now records such a premise per run (`entryVeto`). Owner: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `event-dispatch-await-{continuation,before,conditional,closure}`.

<a id="adr-0478"></a>
## ADR 0478 — flush-in-action climbs the layers that run in place
SC2006's position proof read the call itself, so a `flush` in an IIFE, a `[x].forEach` callback or a helper's callback in an action step was silent, though the rule page's probes show a synchronous helper throws `FLUSH_IN_ACTION`. The call now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers (`climb_in_place_layers`: an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, a helper that calls its callback in its own body, in any module) and the step proof reads the outermost layer's call. A layer in a parameter list (ADR 0454) and a site after an await in its layer are not claimed; a helper that also hands its callback on still is, as one in-place run throws. Found by the fuzzer's helper layers (the cross-file one included), which were certified false cleans. Owner: `static_api.rs` (`action_step_body`); fixture `rc9-flush-in-action-layers`.

<a id="adr-0479"></a>
## ADR 0479 — A named function with one call runs in place there
`layer_run` (ADR 0469) answers `InPlace(call)` for a function bound to a name when its binding holds it at every call (`function_value_is_current`, ADR 0221), it has exactly one call site (ADR 0448 credits only calls after the declarator), that call is in its file, and every other reference to the binding is its declaration: nothing aliases, passes, stores or exports it, so its only run is on that call's stack. Excluded: methods, generators, a named function expression (also entered by its own name), an exported function or one a namespace hands out (a consumer outside the analysed files leaves no reference), and a symbol whose references are not known. Every climb over in-place layers reads it, for violations and legal proofs alike; `layers_run_inline` now steps to the function owning the layer's call, not its lexical parent, since the one call may sit in a nested function (a review found a write placed in the component body when the closure's only call was in an effect). Found by the fuzzer's closure layer (`const f = () => …; f()`), whose `flush-in-action` cases were certified false cleans. Moved: SC2006 on a named closure (`rc9-flush-in-action-layers`); clean where the write is legal at its one call (`store-setter-extent` DeepChain, `write-owner-in-cleanup` CleanupCalls*); violations where rc.17 throws (`write-detached-owner-helper` NullHelperOwnRoot, `write-named-closure-in-root` CleanupRootByNameInNestedFunction). Owner: `execution_role/write_roles.rs` (`sole_call_of_named_function`); fixture `write-named-function-one-call`.

<a id="adr-0480"></a>
## ADR 0480 — Literal slots and object-literal arrows are exact local aliases
ADR 0463's `local_alias_bindings` (solid-facts schema 82) also records a slot of a destructured `const` array literal (`const [a] = [r1]`; a spread ends the slots), a slot of a `const` holder of an array literal every reference of which is a `const` array pattern's initializer (`const pair = [0, r1] as const; const [, a] = pair`: nothing writes, passes or exports it), and a slot of a destructured call of a `const` arrow whose expression body is an object literal of static data properties (`const useThing = () => ({ a: r1 }); const { a } = useThing()`: each call builds the literal fresh, and its values are `const` variables, never the arrow's parameters). Pass 5 gives each the source's facts, as for 0463's aliases. The store mutation rule's own-setter exemption (ADR 0476) closes its store set over these aliases in both directions and reads a setter reached through one (`literal_alias_is_own_setter`): the legal-context matrix found 36 Chrome-confirmed false positives there in the first cut (a write through the alias inside its own setter). Not followed: a member read of the call's result (`thing.useIt()`), a block-bodied or imported hook, and a holder that is read any other way. Owners: `solid-facts` `ast/visitor.rs` (`record_local_alias_candidates`), `ast/extract.rs` (`finish`); `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`; fixture `local-alias-literal-slots`.

<a id="adr-0481"></a>
## ADR 0481 — A member read of an exclusive arrow result is the const it names
solid-facts (schema 83) records `local_alias_members`: a static member read `thing.key` is exactly the `const` variable the literal's `key` names when `thing` is a non-ambient, unexported `const` bound to a non-optional call of an ADR 0480 object-literal arrow, and every resolved reference to `thing` is the object of a static, non-optional member read that is neither written nor deleted (so nothing reaches the object to replace the property). `callee_symbol` and `callee_symbols` answer such a callee with the source's symbol, so setter and action calls through it are judged as direct ones. Not followed yet: a store written through such a member (the store rule's ported root walk wants a bare identifier). Owners: `solid-facts` `ast/{visitor,extract,types}.rs`; `indexes/callees.rs`; fixture `local-alias-member-reads`.

<a id="adr-0482"></a>
## ADR 0482 — A member alias is a root for the ported rules
In `upstream_compat`, `member_root` stops at a member read that is exactly a `const` (ADR 0481) and returns it as the chain's root, and `source_symbol_at` resolves that span to the `const`'s symbol, so `holder.state.v = 1` is a write to the store's own property, as `state.v = 1` is. The store rule accepts such a root wherever it required a bare identifier (`bare_root`: the rc.0-rc.6 readonly-root exemption, the mutator and delete paths), `chain_links_verdict` counts no link at or inside it, and the `delete` path ends its link walk at that root (a follow-up: it walked down to `holder` and skipped every delete through a member, 78 grid false cleans), the own-setter exemption reads an alias-member callee as the setter slot it names (`holder.setState(() => { holder.state.v = 1; })` commits). Upstream eslint-plugin-solid roots the write at `holder` and stays silent: a kept divergence, pinned by ownership cases `no-store-mutation-outside-setter/hook-member-write*`. Owners: `upstream_compat/mod.rs`, `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`; fixture `local-alias-member-reads`.

<a id="adr-0483"></a>
## ADR 0483 — A computation created with no owner in a createEffect apply is SC6002
`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` in dev from `assertNotInEffectCallback` (`dist/dev-shared.js:3947-3957`: `inEffectCallback && context === null`), which `createOwner` (`createRoot`) and `setupComputedNode` (every computation) call at creation. New rule `primitive-in-effect-callback` (SC6002, error) reports `createMemo`, `createEffect`, `createRenderEffect`, `createRoot`, `createProjection` and function-form `createSignal`/`createStore` (`Dialect::throws_creating_in_effect_callback`, read on rc.17 only; it rests on the answer that is `StandsDown` exactly for rc.17 signals, tested beside it) when the call runs on a `createEffect` apply's stack with no owner set in between: SC2008's effect-phase walk in an owner-preserving mode (`ownerless_effect_phase_origin`), which crosses `untrack`, IIFEs, inline standard-library callbacks and helpers but no other primitive callback. The marker is one global a nested synchronous effect run lowers, so the throw is proven only when no call runs earlier in the apply; otherwise, and through a helper, it is uncertifiable. A `createRenderEffect` apply (its first run is owned) is not claimed. An effect created there is SC4001 too (a different claim: the leak). Premise, shared with SC4001 and recorded in open-work: the apply runs from an ownerless drain (a synchronous `flush()` under an owner would run it under that owner; the review found this, and no census of such calls exists). A tagged template before the creation counts as an earlier call. Found by the misuse matrix, where the code had no rule. Owners: `solid-dialect` (`dialect.rs`, `solid_2.rs`), `effect_phase.rs`, `static_api.rs`; rule page `primitive-in-effect-callback`; fixture `primitive-in-effect-callback`.

<a id="adr-0484"></a>
## ADR 0484 — The effect-phase walk crosses nested closures and in-place helpers
The walk SC2008 and SC6002 share (`effect_phase.rs`) refused every function nested in an exported one: its `exported` test read an export statement's span, which holds the whole exported function, so a closure written in an exported component and called in place was never followed. It now asks `module_exports`, which excludes nested functions. A literal handed to a project helper that calls it in its own body on every completion and never hands it on or keeps it (`layer_run`'s `InPlace` at that call) is crossed to the helper's call, one helper layer. Found by the misuse matrix's closure and helper layers (certified false cleans for `FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` and `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`). Owner: `effect_phase.rs`; fixture `effect-phase-layers`.

<a id="adr-0485"></a>
## ADR 0485 — A helper's in-place callback after an await carries the continuation's reads
The read-after-await walk (`static_rules/after_await.rs` `visit_call`) followed IIFEs, `runWithOwner` and standard-library inline callbacks but no project helper's: `run(() => count())` after the computation's `await` was silent. A literal a project callee calls in its own synchronous body on every completion (`layer_run`'s in-place answers at that call, ADR 0457: the exact callee, resolved from the project or a dependency's source) runs on the continuation's stack, so its synchronous prefix is reported like the continuation's own (SC1002); one in-place run is enough, as for every violation. A helper that keeps or hands on its callback is not claimed. Moved: `solid2-precision` `ShadowedFilterAfterAwait` (a project `filter` method whose exact body calls the callback) is now SC1002, by that proof and not by the name. Found by the misuse matrix's helper layers (certified false cleans: 46 -> 10). Owner: `static_rules/after_await.rs`; fixture `after-await-helper-callback`.

<a id="adr-0486"></a>
## ADR 0486 — An accessor a primitive returns directly is summary-eligible
The interprocedural read summaries carried only the accessors of tuple-returning primitives (`go_returned_source`, a Go-producer parity marker no ADR explains), so a memo read inside a helper was invisible to every read rule: `const wrap = () => { m1(); }; wrap();` in a component body was silent while the direct `m1()` is SC1001 and rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`. An accessor-kind source bound to a plain identifier (`const m = createMemo(...)`) now enters `summary_source_symbols` (`discover_file.rs`); return eligibility is unchanged. Moved: `uncertifiable-repro/sc9012` (`useQuery`'s body reads its memo, called from a component body: SC1001; the escaped `Overview` stays uncertifiable). Corpus cost: 57 more SC1001 uncertifiable (memo reads through functions placed nowhere proven). Accepted: an async memo read through a helper is SC1001 where its direct read is SC5001 (ADR 0467), a defect either way since rc.17 throws or warns. Owner: `source_discovery/discover_file.rs`.

<a id="adr-0487"></a>
## ADR 0487 — The read-after-await rule reads member aliases
The read-after-await rule resolved a member callee to TypeScript's property symbol in both of its paths (`after_await.rs` `report`'s dominance loop and `static_rules.rs` `call_accessor_symbol`), so `thing.useIt()` on an ADR 0481 member alias was a typed obligation directly after the await and silent in a closure or helper. Both now ask `local_alias_member` first and read the `const` it names. Making only one path alias-aware left the two disagreeing and dropped the direct case's obligation (the misuse matrix showed two recall gaps turning into false cleans), so both change together. Owner: `static_rules.rs`, `static_rules/after_await.rs`; fixture `local-alias-member-reads`.

<a id="adr-0488"></a>
## ADR 0488 — A settled cleanup after an await is unowned
The owner emission applied ADR 0320's await continuation to effects, cleanups, context reads and boundary calls by the operation's span, but never to `onSettled`'s returned cleanup, whose operation span is its callback, not a call. An `onSettled` call that runs after an await of its async function on every path (its callee is among the producer's `calls_after_await`) is now in that continuation: no owner is current, so a callback that returns a cleanup throws `SETTLED_CLEANUP_UNOWNED` (rc.17), as anywhere unowned (SC4001). Only the definite continuation counts, not a possible one. Owner: `owners/owner_emission.rs`; fixture `settled-cleanup-after-await`.

<a id="adr-0489"></a>
## ADR 0489 — The stored-literal walk follows a named function's one call
`runs_in_unproven_stored_literal` (`interproc/retained_literal.rs`) decides whether a call inside a function literal written in a component may be attributed through summaries. It continued outward from an IIFE's call but treated every named closure as stored, so a read in a component-level closure called only from a closure written in an effect apply (`const relay = () => { peek(); }; relay();`) was skipped at its call and never claimed at `relay()`, whose summary did not carry it. A function bound to a name whose only run is its one call (ADR 0479, `layer_run`'s `InPlace`) now continues the walk from that call, as an IIFE does. Owner: `interproc/retained_literal.rs`; fixture `write-named-function-one-call`.

<a id="adr-0490"></a>
## ADR 0490 — An async read in a named local function is unsuspendable where its call is
ADR 0462 followed a read written directly in a named local function (not a generator, binding never written) through direct calls only to a call in rendered JSX, and dropped calls in a leaf owner. A call in a component body's strict-read window (`f1();`, also through a further named layer), at module scope, or in a leaf owner (`createTrackedEffect(() => { wrapUse(); })`, `onSettled`) therefore reported nothing, though the call runs the read there and rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` or `PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` (Chrome-confirmed in the misuse matrix). `push_call_attributed_async_reads` now records the read at such a call with the role and leaf owner a direct read at that call gets: a body call counts only where `nested_literal_runs_during_body` holds, as for a direct read, and every other call keeps walking outward. The read is reported on the same premises as a direct read (SC5001 violation); `call_attributed` still makes only SC5003/SC5005 uncertifiable. Owner: `local_access.rs`; fixture `async-unsuspendable-call-attributed`.

<a id="adr-0491"></a>
## ADR 0491 — A store link the store's own initializer wrote, and nothing can reach raw, is wrapped
ADR 0476 left every store write past the root (`store.a.v = 1`, `store.items.push(1)`) uncertifiable: the proxy serves a child wrapped only when `isWrappable` accepts its raw value (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:2272-2283`), and frozen or non-plain values are no type's. A proof from the initializer was not built because a handed-out setter or store can install any value later. `links_proven_wrappable` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`) now builds it where nothing can: the write's root resolves, through exact `const` aliases, to slot 0 of a function-local `const` array pattern initialized by `createStore` (exact primitive) with one argument, a closed object literal with no getter; the pattern has at most two slots and no rest, and the setter slot is absent or never referenced; every reference to the store or an alias is an alias initializer or the root of a static, non-optional member path that is written, deleted, called as an in-place array method other than `sort` on an array literal, or read where the literal holds a primitive; and each link of the write is a data property holding an object or array literal. Then the raw value is the fresh inline literal, which no code can reach (no proxy leaves as a value, so no `$TARGET` and no freeze), and no write lands outside the setter, so the link is wrapped and the write is dropped (`STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`, Chrome-confirmed in the misuse matrix). Applied after the ADR 0465 checks, only under strictNullChecks in a file `tsc` checks, so a nullish link stays TypeScript's. Moved: `solid2-precision` `profile.user.name` to violation. A store reached through a hook, object or tuple stays uncertifiable (the store leaves as a value). Review: a link must hold an object or array literal (a regular expression, which the facts class as primitive, and a cast primitive are served raw), a direct `eval` in the file refuses the proof (it reaches the setter with no reference), and a reported array call must write `length` (`push`, `pop`, `shift`, `unshift`, `splice`; `reverse`, `fill`, `copyWithin` may write nothing). Fixture `store-write-literal-links`.

<a id="adr-0492"></a>
## ADR 0492 — A leaf owner's callback runs the callbacks project helpers call in place
`leaf-owner-forbidden-call` (SC3001, `cleanup.rs`) judged a call in a leaf callback (`onSettled`, `createTrackedEffect`) only where it sits in the callback's own synchronous extent, and followed a non-primitive call into the helper's body. A primitive written in a callback the helper receives (`run(() => { createMemo(() => 1); })`, `function run(cb) { cb(); }`) was in neither: the helper's body calls a parameter, which names no operation, and the callback is a nested function. rc.17 throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` (Chrome, misuse matrix). The call now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers (`climb_in_place_layers`) when every layer crossed is a callback handed to a project function (no standard-library or dependency declaration) and the outermost helper call sits in the callback's own extent, not in a parameter list and not after an `await`; the operation is then reported where it is written. An IIFE, an inline standard-library callback (`forEach`, a `Promise` executor) and a named function keep their paths (`helper_forbidden_operations` with its argument-sensitive verdicts, the host-callback census), so nothing is reported twice and an IIFE whose argument disables the operation stays silent. A helper that hands its callback to a timer, stores it, or calls it after an `await` is not crossed. Fixture `leaf-scope-helper-callback`.

<a id="adr-0493"></a>
## ADR 0493 — An owner requirement in layers run after an await is on the continuation
Owner emission (`owners/owner_emission.rs`) placed an effect, cleanup, context read or boundary call on an await's continuation (ADR 0320) only when the operation itself was written after the `await` in the async function. One in an IIFE, a project helper's callback, or a function bound to a name and called once after the `await` got the possible-continuation answer, a conditional owner: uncertifiable, though rc.17 warns `NO_OWNER_EFFECT` / `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP` / `NO_OWNER_CONTEXT` there (Chrome, misuse matrix). The operation now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers (`climb_in_place_layers`) and is after the await when the climb follows an `await` on every path (`suspended == Some(true)`) and crosses no parameter list. An await on one path keeps the conditional answer. Review: a climb through an inline standard-library or dependency callback (it may run zero times: `[1].reduce(fn)`, an empty `forEach`) is not taken (`climb_crosses_library_callback`, also for ADR 0494), and neither is one ending in a function that calls `getOwner()` anywhere (the push-time guard test follows only IIFEs). Amendment: ADR 0488's settled cleanup takes the same climb from its `onSettled` call. Fixture `owner-after-await-layers` (a helper handing its callback to `setTimeout` through a parameter stays a known miss there).

<a id="adr-0494"></a>
## ADR 0494 — `resolve`/`until` in layers run in place inside a tracked callback
SC2004/SC2005 (`static_api.rs`, `resolve_tracked_scope`) asked only whether the call sat directly in a tracked callback; in an IIFE, a helper's callback or a function bound to a name and called once inside a memo compute it was unplaced (uncertifiable), though the observer is active there and rc.17 throws. The call now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers, as ADR 0478 reads them (not a parameter list, not after an `await`), and the outermost layer's call is judged. Moved: `unplaced-site-obligations` `waitReady`/`readNow` (each called once from a memo) to violations; exported helpers with no caller keep the uncertifiable case.

<a id="adr-0495"></a>
## ADR 0495 — SC6002's earlier-call test runs per layer
SC6002 (ADR 0483) proves `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` only where no call runs in the apply before the creating call, since a synchronous effect run lowers the marker; any helper on the walk made it uncertifiable. The effect-phase walk (`effect_phase.rs`) now records an earlier call per layer it crosses (calls written in that function before the path, not inside functions nested in it, and tagged templates) and, for a helper, whether the helper's own body calls anything before its parameter (`parameter_called_first`, `callback_standing.rs`: exact project callee, not async or a generator, an identifier parameter, no `arguments`). The apply's own positional test stands where no helper was crossed. Review: what may run first is anything written in the layer before the path, its parameter list and nested functions included (a getter or JSX component runs with no call written), except the inner layer the walk came from, plus what the path call's own arguments evaluate outside the functions they create; in a helper, anything in the function before its first call of the parameter, that call's arguments included; JSX counts as a call. Moved: `effect-phase-layers` `MemoThroughHelper` to violation; `MemoAfterHelperCall` and `MemoAfterEarlierCall` pin the uncertifiable cases.

<a id="adr-0496"></a>
## ADR 0496 — The call-attributed async walk starts from any named local function
ADR 0462/0490's call-attributed async walk (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`, `local_access.rs`) started only where `inside_non_component_function` held, which refuses a read nested anywhere inside a primitive's callback. A read in a named closure written in a `createRoot` callback (`createRoot(() => { const f1 = () => { a1(); }; f1(); })`) never started it, though the closure's call runs in the root callback the body runs synchronously and rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` there (Chrome, misuse matrix). The walk now starts where the function holding the read is no component, no compiler callback role and no primitive's callback argument (`read_in_named_local_function`); every call it follows is still judged by its own role, so a closure called in a memo compute (which suspends) or from a timer stays silent. Fixture `async-unsuspendable-call-attributed` (`RootClosure`, `MemoClosure`, `RootTimer`).

<a id="adr-0497"></a>
## ADR 0497 — ADR 0491's store-link proof follows ADR 0480's literal slots
ADR 0491 proved a store write past the root only where every reference to the store was a static member path or an exact `const` alias's initializer, so a store handed through an object-literal arrow (`const useThing = () => ({ useIt: store }); const { useIt } = useThing()`) or a tuple holder (`const pair = [0, store] as const; const [, held] = pair`) stayed uncertifiable (the misuse matrix's `hook` and `tuple` bindings, 312 programs, Chrome: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`). `links_proven_wrappable` now follows the root through `local_alias_binding` facts and adds every exact alias of the store to the checked set, and `consumed_by_alias_slots` accepts a store reference that is a direct element of an array literal, or a static data property's value in a closed object literal, when that literal is consumed only by `const` destructuring: a tuple holder every reference of which initializes an array pattern, or the expression body of a non-async `const` arrow every reference of which is the callee of a call initializing an object pattern, each pattern with no rest element, default or nested pattern, and every slot receiving the store an alias in the set (so its references are checked too). A rest element, a call that keeps the object, or a nested pattern leaves the write uncertifiable. The hook-member form (`thing.useIt.a.v`, ADR 0481) is not followed. Review: a slot is matched only by a key written as a plain identifier on both sides (a quoted or escaped key beside a method left the receiving slot unrecorded and unchecked), and an array literal with a spread or a `<` (a generic or JSX comma the index scan miscounts) is refused. Fixture `store-write-literal-links` (`HookSlot`, `TupleSlot`, `TupleRest`, `HookKept`, `HookNested`, `TupleSpread`, `HookQuotedKey`, `HookEscapedKey`).

<a id="adr-0498"></a>
## ADR 0498 — The object of a written member is read
`discover_member` (`local_access.rs`) skipped a member that is another member's object, since the longer path is reported instead, and skipped a plain assignment, delete or iteration target, which reads nothing. A store link in a write past the root (`store.a.v = 1`) fell through both: `store.a` is `store.a.v`'s object, and `store.a.v` is a write target, so the Get of `store.a` was never judged, though rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` for it in a component body (Chrome: replaying the misuse matrix's store-write cases). A member whose immediate parent member is such a target is now read on its own. The misuse matrix scored only each program's target code, so it never saw this: `judge` now also records, per program, every other claimed code Chrome reports on the site's lines that the checker misses (`false-clean`) or declines (`recall-gap`), totalled in `matrix.json`'s `secondary` (test `matrix-secondary.test.mjs`). Moved: `solid2-precision` `profile.user` (violation) and `rows[props.index]` (an undecided store key, uncertifiable).
