ADR 0498 judged the link Get of a nested store write written in a body (`store.a` in `store.a.v = 1`), but one written in a closure the body calls once (`const write = () => { store.a.v = 1; }; write();`) stayed uncertifiable, and through a member alias (`holder.state.a.v`) silent, though rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` there (the misuse matrix's secondary codes: 36 programs). Two summary facts refused it. `body_site_runs_during_call` (`interproc/result_reads.rs`) withheld every site inside an assignment target, for the evaluation-order reversal of destructuring patterns; a member target is no pattern (its object and key are evaluated before the right-hand side), so a site in one now runs during the call as any other does. And the summary's store reads (`direct_reference_contributions`, `interproc/reads.rs`) walked only the store's identifier references; a member read that is exactly the store (ADR 0481) is now a reference too. A write on one path of the closure stays uncertifiable (ADR 0276), and a closure run from a handler is silent. Fixture `local-alias-member-reads` (`StoreLinkInClosure`, `StoreLinkInClosureThroughMember`, `StoreLinkInClosureConditional`, `StoreLinkInHandlerClosure`).

<a id="adr-0500"></a>
## ADR 0500 — The store-link proof follows a member-read hook holder
ADR 0497 followed a store handed through an object-literal arrow only into destructuring; the misuse matrix's `hook-member` binding (`const thing = useThing(); thing.useIt.a.v = 7`, 156 programs, Chrome: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`) stayed uncertifiable. `links_proven_wrappable` now starts the root walk at the `const` an ADR 0481 member alias names, and `held_by_member_holder` accepts an arrow call that initializes a function-local `const` holder every reference of which is the object of a static, non-optional member read that is not written, deleted or iterated, each read at the store's key being an exact member alias that is itself a contained store use. So the holder never leaves, and the store reaches code only through that read. A holder that escapes, is written or is read by a computed key has no member alias, so its write's root is not proven to be the store, and it is silent (a known miss, docs/open-work.md). Review: ADR 0481's member alias is no longer recorded (solid-facts schema 84) when the holder is read at a key the literal does not define (`thing.valueOf()` reads `Object.prototype` and hands the holder out) or the literal holds a `function` or class value (called as `thing.reset()`, it runs with the holder as `this` and may replace a slot); that also guards ADR 0498/0499's strict reads through the alias. `held_by_member_holder` further requires every other key read through the holder to hold a primitive, object or array literal or an arrow function. Fixture `store-write-literal-links` (`HookMember`; `HookMemberPassed` uncertifiable; `HookMemberKept`, `HookMemberWritten`, `HookMemberComputed`, `HookMemberValueOf`, `HookMemberThisReset` silent).

<a id="adr-0501"></a>
## ADR 0501 — The fuzzer's entry renders each component by name
The runtime oracle's entry (`scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs` `mainSource`) mounted every export through `Object.entries(mod)`, a namespace escape (ADR 0347) that leaves no mount the checker can place, so no proof that needs a component's mount (ADR 0460's first run, ADR 0467's pending apply) could ever hold in a fuzzer case. A case's `oracle.json` may now list `mount`, its exported components: the entry imports them by name and renders each by its own top-level `render(() => <C />, host)`, in the namespace's sorted order, and the namespace loop that follows skips them, so every other export renders as before and the namespace hand-out stays in the program. Without `mount` the entry is byte-identical to before. The fuzzer's generator lists every `export function` component (`scripts/fuzz/emit.mjs`), and the checker materializes the same entry (`scripts/fuzz/checker.mjs` `mainSourceFor`).

<a id="adr-0502"></a>
## ADR 0502 — `schedule` withholds a render effect's first run as `defer` does
rc.17's `effect()` runs a render effect's first apply on the call only when neither `options.defer` nor `options.schedule` is set (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:1865-1868`); with `schedule` the run is enqueued and runs from the flush, not under the caller's owner. The checker read only `defer`. The dialect now answers `effect_apply_schedule_options` (`createRenderEffect`'s apply), and a `schedule` that is set or cannot be read makes the apply's owner conditional (`callback_owner_at_call`), never none, since which releases honour the key is not modelled per release; a first-run read with it is not the caller's (`read_escapes_synchronous_extent`). It is a precondition for proving a first run (ADR 0503).

<a id="adr-0503"></a>
## ADR 0503 — A render effect's first run is on the call under a module-scope render
ADR 0460 left a write in a `createRenderEffect` apply written in a body uncertifiable: the first run is on the call (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:1851-1869`, `runEffect` keeping the caller's `context`) unless `defer` or `schedule` is set, the first pass is born staged (`dist/dev-shared.js:4438-4462`) or lane-seated (`:4388-4396`), the compute errors or goes pending (`:4227-4240`), or a boundary above shows its fallback (`dist/dev.js:1957`), and nothing placed the component's mount. `first_run_proven_at_call` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) now proves it where every one of those is excluded: the call has no options argument; its compute is a function literal, not async or a generator, that calls, reads, references, awaits, renders and throws nothing; the call is on every completion of its component's body; and the component is rendered by a call of a synchronous mount (`Dialect::mount_runs_component_synchronously`: `render`, not `hydrate`, `@solidjs/web` `dist/web.dev.js:542-568`) written at a module's top level, whose first argument is an arrow whose whole body is the component's self-closing tag (`mounted_by_module_render`, `owners/async_context.rs`). Module-scope evaluation is outside every flush, lane, transition and boundary, so the first run happens during the call and a write there throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` (an action, `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`). One such mount suffices: the claim is about a run that happens in it. A `schedule` that may keep the first run off the call (ADR 0502) leaves a write directly in the apply unplaced (it was silent). The fuzzer's entry mounts components by name (ADR 0501), so its cases now carry such a mount. Fixture `render-apply-first-run` (violations `FirstRunWrite`, `FirstRunWriteLayered`; uncertifiable `Unmounted`, `Scheduled`, `OptionsVariable`, `ComputeReads`, `ConditionalEffect`, `Hydrated`, `RenderedLater`). Review: the compute must be one primitive literal, returned or as the whole body, with no parameter or one plain one (a dynamic `import()`, a literal thenable, or a destructuring parameter passed the first cut and keep the first run off the call); the rendered binding must hold its function (`function_value_is_current`: a `let` reassigned before the render); and the `render` must run on every module evaluation (outside a class, in the module's straight-line flow, a completion site; the module now has its own frame for `try` blocks and exits, solid-facts schema 85, so a top-level `try { render(…) }` is straight-line as a function's is).

<a id="adr-0504"></a>
## ADR 0504 — A render-effect apply's first read of a source pending since the body is SC5001
ADR 0467 kept an effect or render-effect apply's read of an async source as SC1001: rc.17's `read()` throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` for a pending source before it would warn the strict read (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:5320-5325`, `:5441-5446`), and nothing ordered the first run against the source's settling. For a render effect ADR 0503 orders it: the first run is on the call (`dist/dev.js:1869`), which a halt does not stop (`schedule` alone checks it, `dist/dev-shared.js:2038-2042`). `pending_apply_first_read` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) proves the read is in that first run: in the apply of a `createRenderEffect`, a function literal, directly or through in-place layers that cross no library callback, list, await or helper that also hands the callback on, with no call of the apply written before it (an earlier throw ends the run); the call placed on the mount by ADR 0503; the source a `const` of the same body, declared on every completion before the call, initialized by `createMemo` with one argument, an async function literal, pending from its creation until a microtask (`:4626`, `:3642-3660`) with no `loadingValue` to mask it; and nothing in the body between the source and the call that runs code (each call there creates a signal or memo, or calls a `const`-bound local function that calls nothing). The row (`AsyncRead::apply_first_run_pending`; at the call a closure is run from, `pending_origin`/`pending_apply`) is SC5001's violation, and the strict read at the site, or with that origin inside that apply, is withdrawn, since the run throws before it warns. Not built: a `createEffect` apply, whose first run is in `render`'s tail flush, which a pending render effect anywhere in the mount (a fragment, a helper's JSX or effect, a module-level effect) holds until the source settles (review). Fixture `strict-read-async-apply` gains `main.tsx` (moved: `RenderApplyReadsAsync`, `RenderApplyReadsAsyncRendered` SC1001 -> SC5001; the rendered reads' SC5003 becomes a violation, the mount now resolved; `ApplyReadsAsync`, `ApplyReadsAsyncRendered`, `ApplyComputeReadsAsync`, `ApplyComputeWaitsOnOtherAsync` and the review's `SharedClosure`, `ReadAfterCall`, `BodyReadsFirst` and the unmounted `UnmountedApplyReadsAsync` keep SC1001 where they read in an apply).

<a id="adr-0505"></a>
## ADR 0505 — A user effect's first apply under a static first mount meets its source pending
ADR 0504 left a `createEffect` apply as SC1001. Its first run is queued at the call, and `render`'s tail `flush()` runs it (`@solidjs/web@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/web.dev.js:542-568`). A pending render effect in that flush, or a transaction an earlier pass left, holds it (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:1690-1733`, `:2180`). `user_apply_runs_in_mount_flush` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) adds premises to ADR 0504's proof so that nothing can create such a node:
- the component renders a static intrinsic tree: lowercase tags only, with no attribute, spread, expression child, fragment or component;
- the body runs only the effect, the source and signals or memos of primitive literals, plus calls of inert local `const` functions; it reads no member, writes no `throw`, class or tagged template, and destructures only local literals;
- the source's async compute is one primitive literal;
- the `render` mounting it is the first code that can create a node. The memo `ModuleScopeRuns` lists the project files whose module scope may run code (a call, member, reference, JSX, class, `await`, `throw`, method or hazard), and whether any file loads a module other than Solid's own (whose evaluation only installs hooks) or an analyzed project file. Only the entry may run code, and before the call it may run only standard-library code and local `const` functions that do, referencing no import but `render`.

solid-facts schema 86 records `throw_statements`. Fixture `effect-apply-first-run` holds the two positives and one variant per premise; `strict-read-async-apply`'s `ApplyReadsAsync` (the first render) moves SC1001 -> SC5001. Mounts by a child, router, loop or `hydrate` stay SC1001, as do later renders. Review (also ADR 0504): the read must be on every completion of the apply and of each in-place layer up to it (`read_on_every_completion_of_apply`). A read a closure carries to its call must be on every completion of each function up to the call too (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`). Before this, `if (v === 1) return;` or a loop that may not run before the read, in the apply or in a closure it calls, was SC5001 although the first run skips the read. Fixture rows: `RenderApplyGuarded` and the `guarded` variant. Fuzzer: legal contexts `{effect,render}-apply-guarded` and `-guarded-closure` (container `guard-return`); the legal judge counts an SC5001 violation the runtime never matches as a false positive, at an unexecuted site or anywhere else; probes `pending-async/apply-guarded-first-run`, `apply-guarded-closure`.
<a id="adr-0506"></a>
## ADR 0506 — Reachability follows function literals a call runs or receives; a call-result binding's calls run nothing
The fuzzer's mount axis renders each misuse component as a parent's child, not exported. It found two defects:
- **False cleans (364 per mount, confirmed in Chrome).** A write in an IIFE or a helper's callback inside such a component was dropped, and the program was certified. Reachability (`reachability.rs`) reached such a function only as a root, and every function nested in an exported declaration is one, so only exported components were covered. Now a function literal a call invokes (an IIFE's callee) or receives as an argument (a project helper's callback, `[1].forEach(fn)`) is reached whenever the call is. Review: every function created in a reached function's body or parameter list is reached too, as every function nested in an exported declaration already is. Before, an arrow a private component's body only returned or stored (`(() => () => set(1))()`, `[() => set(1)]`) was certified. Reaching a function proves nothing about when it runs: the write and read roles still decide.
- **A false positive, already on #141.** `const wrapped = wrap(() => set(1)); wrapped()` was a violation even when `wrap` never runs the arrow. `functions_by_symbol` names the function nested in a call initializer for the binding, and the call graph took every call of the binding as a call of that function. The binding holds the call's result. The mapping stays as identity evidence, which server functions that a `query` wraps and `lazy` loaders' tags rely on. `SymbolFunction::Resolved` records `through_call`, and `CallSitesOf` drops such a symbol's call and render sites (census table `FileCallResultSymbols`). `lazy(loader)`'s result is not such a binding: its render runs the loader (ADR 0292).

Moved: `use-site-role`'s `UsesWrite`, violation -> uncertifiable. Its write runs in `writeOf`'s memo, but only the unsound call path proved it, and the use-site arm places no write. `Tdz.tsx`'s module-scope `wrap` arrow, `unplaced-site-obligations`, `write-scope-roots-rc3/rc9` and `uncertifiable-repro/sc1001` gain uncertifiable rows for newly reached code. Corpus: +1 violation (`prefer-for` on a `.map` in JSX inside a test's `mount` callback, a correct style finding), +36 uncertifiable. Fixtures `private-component-layers`, `call-result-binding`. Fuzzer: `--mounts root,child,show,for` on both grids; probes `write/private-child-iife`, `write/call-result-binding`, `write/private-returned-closure`, `write/private-array-element`. Not changed: a write a helper runs only on a branch its arguments rule out (`maybe(cb, false)`), in `[].forEach`, or in a component JSX renders in a dead branch is still claimed wherever it may run (the `SiteReach` convention, ADR 0457), as it already was in exported components. Corpus after the review: uncertifiable 6846 -> 7118.

<a id="adr-0507"></a>
## ADR 0507 — A child an inert parent renders is mounted as its parent is
ADRs 0503-0505 prove a first run under a module-scope `render` of the component itself. `module_render_sites` (`owners/async_context.rs`) now also returns a mount through parents, up to four deep (`MountSite::parents`). The child's tag must be:
- self-closing, with no attribute, spread or child;
- written in the parent's own body, in the argument of its one return (unconditional, straight-line) or its expression body;
- reached from there through intrinsic elements only, each tag a child of the next (`static_child_tag`).

The compiler evaluates such a tag (`createComponent`) while the parent builds its tree, so the child's body runs inside the parent's, outside any computation. The parent must run nothing else (`inert_parent`): its body is one `return <tree>` (review: a statement before it may coerce, iterate or read a getter that throws, ending the mount), with plain parameters, and no call, member read, tagged template, class, `throw`, nested function or fragment; no JSX but static intrinsic elements and the tag. An earlier pass of the same mount that went pending stages later passes (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:1690-1708`), and a throw ends the mount. ADR 0505's other premises (`render_runs_first`, the static tree) hold of the root render, unchanged. Not built: `<Show>`, `<For>` and other components, which run their children in a computation or later. Residual, as in ADRs 0503-0505 (a claim about a body that completes normally): an implicit throw in the component's own body before the site (a coercion through a throwing `toString`), and a loop that never ends. Fixture `child-mount-first-run` (violations `PlainParent` SC2001, `Grand` SC5001 through two parents, variant `effect-child` SC5001 through ADR 0505; uncertifiable: `CallingParent`, `ShowParent`, `PropParent`, `ConditionalParent`). Probe `write/child-mount-first-run`.

<a id="adr-0508"></a>
## ADR 0508 — `<Show when={true}>` and a one-row `<For>` render their child during the mount
ADR 0507's climb from a child's tag to its parent's returned tree passes intrinsic elements only. It may now also pass two holders:
- The dialect's `<Show when={true}>`: one attribute, `when`, the literal `true`; no spread; the one child is the climbed node (`shown_child`). Its value memo computes when `Show` is created, since `computed` runs a node that is not `lazy` at creation (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:4913-4932`). It reads `props.children` when the condition holds (`solid-js@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/solid.dev.js:1571-1600`), so the child renders inside that compute.
- The dialect's `<For each={[…]}>`: one attribute, `each`, an array literal of primitive literals with at least one element; no spread; the one child is a parameterless arrow whose expression body is the tag (`for_row`). `For` builds its `mapArray` on the first read of its result (`dist/solid.dev.js:1548-1562`), which the parent's insert makes on its first pass.

Either way the child's body runs during the parent's tree, under an owner (the memo or the row), and a render effect created there runs its first apply on the call (`dist/dev.js:1851-1869`). `inert_parent` admits the holders and the row arrow. Not built: a non-literal `when` or `each`, `keyed`, `fallback`, a row that takes the item, `<Switch>`/`<Match>`, `<Index>`. Fixture `child-mount-first-run`: `ShowParent` (uncertifiable under ADR 0507) and new `ForParent` become violations; new negatives `ShowBindingParent`, `ForEmptyParent`, `ForItemParent`. Probes `write/show-mount-first-run`, `pending-async/for-mount-first-run`. Review (Chrome-checked): whitespace text without a line break is a child (the compiler keeps it: `<For> {() => <C />} </For>` hands `For` an array, and the row never runs); and ADR 0503's first-run write claim now also needs no call written before the write in the apply (`apply_runs_nothing_before`), as ADR 0504's read does: a pending read before it throws first (fixture row `render-apply-first-run` `ReadThenWrite`; probes `write/for-inline-whitespace`, `write/apply-write-after-pending-read`).


<a id="adr-0509"></a>
## ADR 0509 — A value a caller hands over is followed, or its use stays open
The fuzzer's source axes (PR #149) hand the misuse component its signal, store, action or async memo through a prop, a context, a hook's return or a module export. Causes and fixes:
- **Setters and stores found by type alone were silent.** On rc.17 a setter not proven `createSignal`'s took the role that reports nothing (an optimistic setter meets no guard); it is now unplaced unless proven `createOptimistic`'s. The three store rules read only proven stores; a typed store is now an obligation. The demand plan types names destructured from a non-primitive call (`const [, set] = useCount()`).
- **Accessor transport carried reads only.** A hook's returned object or tuple slot and a context value now also resolve to `Callable::Setter`/`Callable::Action` (kept in `write_call_targets`, out of every read consumer's table); a post-pass writes or acts with the source's row, replacing a typed row's, and gives a transported async read its async row.
- **A call of a prop's value was dropped.** Pass 10 records per prop the exact sources visible sites pass (`PassedSources`) and whether a getter calls an async source; `props.name(…)` on the exact parameter, written directly in the component or a primitive's callback, reads, writes or acts with each (one witness suffices); deeper, or with any other value passed, it is an obligation (`closed-parameter-member-dispatch`).
- **One fail-closed net for the rest (`handed-member-dispatch`).** A call whose callee has no row and is, through members, aliases, destructuring or local literals, a value a caller supplied (props or a view, `useContext`, a project function's result, an alias of an imported source) is an obligation where it would report a write, an action or an untracked read, or may run in a leaf owner; a callee typed as a Solid accessor (declared in an installed Solid package, ADR 0509's `descriptor_type_role`) only for a read. The same net covers store writes past such a root, `refresh` of an unresolved target, reads after an `await` through helpers, argument closures and IIFEs, and an unproven helper call of a prop read. Pushed last, it yields to any other cause at its site. Correction: transport drops it only where the target's summary is complete (ADR 0686), and a hook's result used unbound or iterated is such a value too (ADR 0689).
- **A strict read of an async source that may meet it pending is unproven** (rc.17 throws first): an obligation, which fixes the module/context async wrong-rule class.
 Cost: two real-app clean repro cases (`clean-context-provider`, `clean-store-draft`) are uncertifiable. Not built: context proofs in an open program, props through views, the producer's empty origin module for published `.d.ts` files (typed rules elsewhere still need a stub's `declare module`).

<a id="adr-0510"></a>
## ADR 0510 — A module-scope or parent-handed async memo meets the first run pending; nothing in a layer runs before the read
ADR 0509 left an apply's read of an async source it could not see as an obligation. Three sources now meet ADR 0504/0505's first run pending (`pending_apply_first_read`, `execution_role/pending_sources.rs`); each is `createMemo` of one async function literal with no options, pending until a microtask (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:3640-3690`, `:4229-4242`):
- **A module-scope memo** of a project module whose scope only creates (`module_scope_only_creates`: each call a `const`'s whole initializer, `createMemo` of a literal compute, `createSignal` of at most one literal, `createContext`; nothing else runs), with a literal compute, read under a mount whose `render` passes `render_runs_first` (no TLA, every module seen), from an entry no project module imports (the `ModuleScopeRuns` census now also lists creation-only modules and imported files; the entry is then a root of evaluation, so every module it loads has evaluated, ECMAScript `InnerModuleEvaluation`), the component with plain parameters and nothing in its body before the effect (`body_runs_nothing_between` from the body's start). With no reader such a memo joins no transaction (`joined` false, `:4520`), and a parking seam skips a loading source (`:2194-2204`), so `render_runs_first` now admits such modules for ADR 0505 too.
- **A parent's memo** in a mount site's last parent: an exact prop (`<C data={data} />`, `props.data()` on the one plain parameter, never written) or one context provider of a module-scope `createContext` (`<Ctx value={{ k: data }}>`, `useContext(Ctx).k()`; the provider sets its value on a root before `children` builds the memos the parent's insert reads, `solid-js@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/solid.dev.js:11-15`; `getContext` finds it, `dist/dev.js:1811-1824`). ADR 0507's climb admits such parents: `const` creations before the one return, tag attributes naming them (`handed_attribute`), and the provider as a holder (`provided_child`).
- **A static tree** (ADR 0505) may hold string attributes, primitive-literal children (`insert` of a non-function places it with no effect, `@solidjs/web@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/web.dev.js:1135-1141`) and reads of the body's own signal or synchronous memo of a literal (`settled_expression_child`); an async memo's read still holds the flush.

Correction (false positives of ADRs 0503-0505, Chrome-confirmed): only the apply's own calls before the site were checked. `runs_nothing_before_in_layers` now walks each layer from the site up: no call of its own body before the position, none among the layer call's arguments before the function, and a project helper calls the argument first (`parameter_called_first`, ADR 0495); a wrapper's body too (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`). `(() => { JSON.parse("{"); a1(); })()` was SC5001 (a write there SC2001). Wrong rule: a read through a local function whose call local access placed no row (`wrapUse()` after an `action` creation) kept SC1001; it gets ADR 0509's opening row. Review (Chrome-confirmed false positives): only calls were checked before the site, so `const v = props.cfg.x;` (an `any`, undefined) before the effect, or `(v as any).a.b;` before the read in the apply, still claimed it though the run throws first; `throws_nothing_before` now also refuses a member read, computed member, spread or iteration, operator, `delete`, destructuring (but a creation's tuple or ADR 0505's local literal), tagged template, class, `throw` or loop in each layer and the body before the effect, and a layer with a pattern parameter. And a second root (a project file none imports, `warm.ts`: `import "./source"`) that reaches the source's module refuses: a page loading it before the entry settles the memo in the checkpoint between the two scripts (the census now records import edges); premise: no script outside the project's files imports the source. The body's name for an imported source is the read's own resolved callee, not an import matched by name. Fixture `pending-source-first-run`. Probes `pending-async/{module,prop,context}-source-*`, `settled-children-effect`, `apply-*-throw-first`, `wrapper-wrong-rule`, `write/apply-iife-throw-first`. Not built: aliases, tuples and destructurings of handed or imported sources (SC9012 before the proof), `<Loading>` holders, a hook of another module creating the memo, props read through layers.

<a id="adr-0511"></a>
## ADR 0511 — Handed and hook-created async memos meet the first run pending through layers and closed aliases
Shapes ADR 0510 left uncertifiable SC5001 although rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`:
- **Props read through layers.** ADR 0509 placed `props.k()` only directly in the component or a primitive's callback (`props_call_placed`); deeper it was an obligation. `props_call_pending_in_layers` (`local_access.rs`) places it where every source a visible site passes is an async accessor and `pending_apply_first_read` proves each from the call: the proof's climb and `runs_nothing_before_in_layers` already walk an IIFE, a named closure called once and a helper that calls its callback first. Fixture `pending-source-layers`. A wrapper the body defines and the apply calls once (`const wrapUse = () => { props.a1(); }`) is read at `wrapUse()`, as an own source's is: `discover_props_call` runs `push_call_attributed_async_reads` and leaves the wrapper's own site to it, and the proof takes the origin read for the handed member (`pending_apply_first_read_from`); a second call keeps the obligation.
- **A memo a hook of another module creates.** `const { a1 } = useVals()` or `const vals = useVals(); vals.a1()`: the transport already carried the read to the memo's declaration in the hook. `hook_source_pending` (`pending_sources.rs`) proves it where the hook is inert (`inert_hook`: a project function, not async or a generator, block body, no parameter, whose own code runs only inert creations, throws nothing, references its own names and the creations' callees, and whose one straight-line `return` is an object literal of data properties naming its bindings), creates the memo on every completion with a literal compute, and the body calls it once before the effect (`hook_call_in_body`: no argument, the callee resolved exactly to the hook and never written, on every completion, a `const` bound to a name or an object pattern). `BodyAdmits::hook` lets ADR 0505/0510's body premises admit that call and an object pattern of its result (`hook_result_destructuring`). The read must go through that call's own result (`read_through_hook_call`: `x.N` with `x` the call's `const`, or `N` a slot of its object pattern, the hook's literal holding the memo under `N`); a read of another instance (`shared.a1()` of a module-scope `useVals()`, settled before the mount) was SC5001 while rc.17 warns SC1001 (probe `hook-shared-instance`). Refused (checked): a getter or spread in the returned object, a parameter, a conditional return, `loadingValue`, any other call in the hook, a default in the pattern.
- **Closed aliases of handed values.** `const d = props.k`, `const d = ctx.k`, `const { d } = { d: props.k }` hit SC9012 before the proof. Two exact steps: the resolver's escape census (`census_rows`, `accessor_provenance/resolver_index.rs`) no longer counts as an escape a Get of a named member held whole by a `const` of one name, a data property or an array element (`gets`): it copies the member's value and hands the object nowhere, and a named write of the member keeps its veto; and an object pattern of an inline object literal follows the slot when the slot's own value is transported (`resolver_values.rs`; a local source keeps ADR 0463's alias facts). For props, `handed_member_alias` (`pending_sources.rs`) follows the same two forms to `props.k`, and `discover_call` treats the alias as the prop's call (ADR 0509's `discover_props_call`). The pending proof takes the alias declared in the body before the effect, `only_called_members` admits it, and `BodyAdmits::handed` lets the body read named members of the handed object (a data property of the object the proven mount passes). A passed function is followed through the alias: a write in it stays claimed at the passed arrow. Not built: tuples (ADR 0282's `tuple_iteration` veto holds in every program whose entry calls `render(…, document.body)`, an unresolved call handed an object), a local hook or wrapper over props (`const useThing = () => ({ f: props.k })`), and aliases of an imported source (`const d = a1`: ADR 0463's exact alias is a `const` of the file). Cost: alias reads in layers of tracked scopes go clean -> SC9012, as their direct twins already were. Corpus: of the 2,773 SC9012 ADRs 0509-0510 added, about 153 are closed alias, destructuring or tuple forms of a local value; the rest are member calls on values that are not closed (1,115 consts, 560 prop callbacks, 257 hook results).
- **ADR 0507's residual is closed; nothing built.** An implicit throw in the body before the site (a coercion through a throwing `toString`) or a loop that never ends: ADR 0510's `throws_nothing_before` already refuses every operator, template and loop in the body before the effect and in each layer before the read. Chrome: `pending-async/{body-coercion,body-template,apply-coercion}-throw-first` no defect, checker uncertifiable; `body-coercion-settles` (a `toString` that returns) throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` and stays uncertifiable, a precision cost.
- **`<Loading>` holders (research, then built).** rc.17's `Loading` returns `createLoadingBoundary(() => props.children, …)` (`solid-js@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/solid.dev.js:1654-1659`), whose boundary builds the children in `computed(fn, { name: "children" })` when created (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:9463-9468`); `runEffect` holds a queued run behind a showing fallback but not the synchronous first render (`:1947-1955`). Chrome: a child's render effect meets the parent's memo pending (`pending-async/loading-render`, also with a tracked read in its tree, `loading-render-tracked`), a user effect under a static tree too (`loading-effect`), and a render-effect write throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` (`write/loading-mount-first-run`); a user effect whose tree reads the memo is held to the reveal and warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` instead (`loading-effect-tracked`). Built: `loading_child` (`owners/async_context.rs`) lets ADR 0507's climb pass `<Loading fallback={<primitive literal>}>` with no `on`, no spread and one child; ADR 0505's static tree keeps the user-effect case to boundaries with nothing pending below. `suppress_findings_owned_by_enabled_rules` (`projection.rs`) drops an SC1001 at an SC5001's span only for a proven pending read; where both are uncertifiable, both stay (rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` on `loading-effect-tracked`).
- **Aliases of an imported source.** `const d = a1` and `const { d } = { d: a1 }` with `a1` a named runtime import: ADR 0463's exact alias is a `const` of the file, so these hit SC9012. A file's rows now record its module-scope `const`s of one name (`FileRows::module_consts`, analyzed files only) and mark such aliases (`AliasRow::import`, syntactic); the alias pass treats one as exact where the accessor it names is declared by one of those `const`s (an import is a live binding of it). `local_alias_mention_withheld` and the initialization census (`names_local_alias`) withhold a mention that may precede the alias's declarator, as for a local alias: an alias declared after the effect is in its temporal dead zone at the first run (Chrome: `module-alias-late` no defect). Tuples of an imported source stay open with the handed ones.
- **Tuples: not with the current census.** `const pair = [0, props.k] as const; const [, d] = pair;` (and of a context member or an imported source). A slot is followed only where no code may change the array iterator (ADR 0282's census), and that census refuses a slot whose value it cannot prove is not a code constructor: a handed member or an import is such a value (`string_code_reference`, "no element identity is guessed"). An array pattern over a `const` reads the holder's literal as one written in place where the holder is an immutable, never-written `const` the pattern is the one reference to; any other holder is a possible gateway (unit test `the_any_file_vetoes_follow_what_the_project_writes`). Building them needs the census to prove a slot's value is not a code constructor: an import of a module `const` a dialect primitive creates (cheap), a provider's value or the values callers pass (both computed after the census runs); not built for the small payoff (about 13 corpus tuples of a local value). Also seen: the fuzzer's harness entry calls `make(type)`, an unresolved function of a non-literal argument, which alone sets ADR 0282's veto in every grid program.

<a id="adr-0512"></a>
## ADR 0512 — The fuzzer's fast path is Chrome, driven by a Rust executor
The differential fuzzer's fast path runs its runner in headless Chromium, not Happy DOM: one Vite server with the Solid plugin serving the cases from memory, a reused page per worker navigated to a fresh document per program, on Chrome's virtual time. Projects, `tsrs -b` batches, checker runs and the pages (over CDP, with Playwright's switches) run in a Rust executor (`scripts/fuzz/exec`, its own workspace); verdicts stay in JS. Trust is measured, not argued: a stratified runtime differential against `feedback run` shows records identical to the host's (both keep 41 frames; the oracle runs `--source-instrumentation off`, whose `withOperation` wrapper frames moved 8 of 4,340 sampled records), and the hosts are compared record for record after each host change. Loops: `make fuzz-focus CODES=…`, `make fuzz-smoke` (4% of the programs, covering every behaviour five times and every core-axis pair per code, validated against an earlier checker), the full grids before a PR. `--fast-host happy-dom` remains. Owners: `scripts/fuzz/chrome-host.mjs`, `exec/`, `focus.mjs`, `smoke.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0513"></a>
## ADR 0513 — The fuzzer measures recall on axes the base grids do not draw
`--recall` grids (`mx-recall`, `lg-recall`) cross each new binding, callee form, layer, callback form, mount and file source with the base grid of every cell, and add recall-only contexts and a `LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD` template; mutation mode pairs each misuse shape with its legal twin, checker only. Opt-in so the base grids, their seeds and baselines stay byte-identical; not in `fuzz-focus` or `fuzz-smoke` by default, since their false cleans are the open-work misses this measures. Owners: `scripts/fuzz/misuse.mjs`, `mounts.mjs`, `matrix.mjs` (`recallAxes`), `mutation.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0514"></a>
## ADR 0514 — The fuzzer's browser is the pinned Playwright's; a Linux VM where process starts are gated
Without `--browser`, the oracle and the Chrome host run the headless shell the pinned Playwright names (`browsers.json` revision, the platform's cache and layout; `scripts/playwright-browser.mjs`) and stop when it is absent, instead of the newest build in the cache (invariant 7: a cache held Chrome 156 where Playwright 1.60.0 pins 148). `scripts/fuzz/vm.sh` runs the fuzzer in a Colima VM on a Mac whose security agents gate every process start; outcomes measured equal, 3.7x faster than the best macOS setting.

<a id="adr-0515"></a>
## ADR 0515 — Exact copies, named callbacks and assigned callees the fuzzer's recall grid found silent
Each is the same value or call by construction, so every rule follows it (fail-closed facts, no name matching): in `solid-facts`, a `const` initialized by a conditional both of whose arms are one `const` (`c ? r : r`) or by an inline identity arrow on it (`((x) => x)(r)`) is that variable's alias (ADR 0463's fact); a slot of `const { k } = N` over a `const N = S` holding a namespace import is `S.k` (ADR 0459's fact); a plain assignment to a binding as a callee, `(slot = f)()`, invokes `f` (ADR 0468's peel; not in an `async` function, whose after-await owner placement reads the operands and went silent on the peel). In the IR, a primitive's callback position names a function through an exact `const` copy only when every reference to the copy follows its declarator in the declarator's function (a copy reached from a nested function may run first and throw), and through the binding of a named function expression whose own name is unreferenced (reads and `by_name_position` alike); a read in a function nested in a named callback climbs the in-place layers to its call, as the inline callback's does.


<a id="adr-0516"></a>
## ADR 0516 — The recall grid's remaining false cleans fail closed, and four spellings are exact
Fable's read of the round-2 recall grid named ten causes. Where no sound proof exists the site is now an obligation, never silence: a function handed whole through a copy or an object-literal slot no callback edge follows has unplaced reads and an owner seed both owned and unowned (`function_handed_unfollowed`); a named write callback at several positions or behind an adjusted inline role is unknown (`write_role_walk`); `f.call`, `f.apply` and `f.bind(…)(…)` of a local setter, action or accessor are `reactive-dispatch-unresolved`, except in an event handler, where every rule they stand for is legal; a read in a loop that awaits is after a suspension from the loop's start (`loop_suspension_start`); a hook value handed on after a suspension is an SC1002 obligation. A sync generator called in place whose iterator is used (`generator_iterator_used`: the call's result is not a discarded statement) runs its body where it is stepped, which nothing follows: an owner seed both ways, an SC2008/SC6002 origin marked mixed, an SC2006 step and an SC1002 continuation marked uncertain, an open leaf-scope obligation; a discarded iterator stays silent. A named helper an action body calls, a named apply and a helper with callers outside the effect are reached (SC2006, SC2008) as uncertain unless every run is proven. Exact, so violations: a slot of a `let` destructuring of a namespace import that nothing writes or redeclares (a `var` is hoisted and stays out), an exported slot (it holds the export wherever it is read; an importer's mention is its own import binding), a pattern nested over a literal that holds the namespace once, a getter whose body is one `return` and a default the literal leaves unset (solid-facts schema 87), and `(x ||= h)()`'s operands. Not built: a namespace of a project module that `export *`s Solid, `LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD` (no rule). Costs, uncertifiable where silent: an exported generator iterator, a copy handed on before its declarator, `runWithOwner(null, named)` after an await, a read after a loop that may await. Gate review: callback targets resolve a `const` copy, a `const` function expression and an object literal's member handed whole (`exact_function_argument`, ADR 0358's receiver closure), replacing an owned-and-unowned seed; a stepped generator gets a `MayRun` edge (after a suspension, `MayBeUnowned`), and passes its call's role through for reads and, for writes, from a handler only; `f.call`/`f.bind()()`/array `f.apply` and a total composite callee are judged as `f()` at the written call (`discover_call_as`), and a non-total one is clean only where every operand's call is; an unwritten `let` function is current. Fable review: a default for a key `Object.prototype` supplies never applies, and a generator a scheduler calls runs none of its body (`MayBeUnowned`); fixture `dispatch-and-copies`. Settled cleanup (ADR 0488): an `onSettled` call that may follow an await (a loop's later iteration, a skippable await) takes the possible-continuation placement at the call, not at its callback: uncertifiable (fixture `settled-cleanup-after-await`, `LoopAwaitLater`). Effect phase by value (`effect_phase::named_layer`): an apply named by a named function expression's binding (ADR 0515's `named_expression_binding`), by its `const` copies (alias facts, transitively), or by an object literal's data-property slot (only through ADR 0358's receiver closure, never the property's symbol, which another object of the type may share) is followed as the binding is; any reference that is neither an effect-phase position nor a call the walk proves makes the origin mixed. SC6002 and SC2008 move from silent or `missing-owner` alone to their own rule (fixture `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback`: `applyCopy`, `expressionApply`, `applyBox`, uncertifiable `sharedCopy`, `sharedBox`). Leaf owner by value (`containing_leaf_owner`): a function whose every value position (`owners::exact_value_positions`: its binding, copies, a receiver-closed slot) is a leaf slot of one owner, with no other use, runs under that owner; reads there are judged as in the leaf (fixture `dispatch-and-copies`, `LeafByCopy`).


<a id="adr-0517"></a>
## ADR 0517 — The recall grid's certified false cleans: exact names, climbed layers, and obligations where no proof exists
The baseline of `0acd56c72` (all eight grids, every code) had 3,044 false cleans on `mx-recall` and none elsewhere, most of them certified; Chrome confirmed three false positives before a declarator. Each fix is exact or an obligation; none matches a name. Before a declarator: a callback position whose mention may precede the function's declarator (`const early = () => createMemo(write); early(); const write = …`) gives the function an unknown role, and a violation after an unordered mention written before its declarator in the same function (`[1].forEach(() => { r(); count(); })`) is uncertifiable. Namespace barrels: a static member of a namespace import of a project module (`import * as S from "./solid"` over `export *` or a named re-export) is the primitive its property's symbol resolves to; SC2008 and SC6002 take the package from the callee's exact symbol's declarations when Type Facts relocates no signature (one package, else nothing). After an await: the async computation is found through ADR 0516's `exact_function_argument` (a named function expression's binding, an object-literal slot), and a composite callee the producer never lists (`(slot = r)()`) is found in the function's own body (total, one accessor, dominated: a read; otherwise an obligation). A read summary carries `r.call(…)`, `r.bind(…)()` (decided) and composite callees (undecided). Named callbacks: a callback named at a `createRoot`/`runWithOwner` position is placed at the call when that is its sole entry (`by_name_position`), JSX it writes keeping the compiler's role, and unknown otherwise (not async callbacks); a read in its in-place layers climbs to the layer's call, past the callback's own awaits; `named_callback_write_role` climbs layers before scanning positions, and several owner-keeping positions with no rejection are unknown; `resolve`/`until` climbed into a function a tracked position names is SC2004/SC2005. `LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD`: rule `loading-on-outside-hold` (SC5006, warning) is an obligation only, at a `<Loading on>` (or spread) whose children render a component, hold an unproven read, or read an async source referenced outside it; a constant `on` never re-arms; the fuzzer claims the code, placeless. Open programs: the provider census runs, and a read it finds no provider for is SC4002 uncertifiable (amends ADR 0352). Probes: `scripts/fuzz/probes/{tdz,barrel,named-callback}/`, `read-after-await/exact-*`, `strict-read/dispatch-*`; fixtures `named-callback-tdz`, `namespace-solid-barrel`, `after-await-exact-callbacks`, `dispatch-read-through-closure`, `loading-on-outside-hold`, `named-inheriting-callback`, `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback/Barrels.ts`.


<a id="adr-0518"></a>
## ADR 0518 — A top-level `await` leaves pending-read findings unproven
SC5003 (`async-outside-loading-boundary`) claimed every rendered read of an async source outside a `Loading` boundary, as if the source were always pending at the read's first run. A top-level `await` breaks that: it suspends module evaluation (microtasks run, and timers too if it waits on one), so a node created before it (a module-scope memo, or a hook's instance created at module scope that a root already read) can have settled by the mount, and a settled read needs no boundary (rc.17 warns nothing). Found by the fuzzer's `module-hook-shared` source: 50 legal programs (`const shared = useVals(); createRoot(…shared.a1()…); await …; export function C1() { createRenderEffect(() => { shared.a1(); … }) }`) were false positives, confirmed in Chrome. Fix: `ModuleCensus::may_settle_before_mount` (a project module with a top-level `await`, or a load the census cannot see) sets `AsyncRead::settle_unproven`, and projection makes SC5003 uncertifiable then; SC5003's `ssrSource: "client"` wording throws on the server whatever the client's timing and stays. Premise: no dependency module awaits at its top level (the census does not scan installed packages; SC5001's apply proofs refuse any unseen load). Trade: in a project with any top-level `await`, every SC5003 is uncertifiable, even for a memo the reading body creates; which nodes the checkpoint settles is not modeled. Fixture variants `pending-source-first-run/{module-render-read,module-render-await}` (violation, uncertifiable). `scripts/fuzz/rediscovery.json` records the rediscovery. Review (Chrome-confirmed false positive): SC5001 on a component-body or root read had the same gap (`settled-body`: rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`, the checker proved SC5001); the flag now makes SC5001 uncertifiable too, except an apply's first-run row (ADR 0510's own proof). Those proofs (`render_runs_first`, `module_scope_only_creates`, `module_scope_may_run`) also count `for await` and `await using` now. Precision cost: an unread module memo stays pending through an `await` (`module-body-await` throws in Chrome) and is uncertifiable. The generator's `settled` variant also takes `body` and `root`. Variants `module-body-settled`, `module-body-await`, `module-for-await` (uncertifiable).


<a id="adr-0519"></a>
## ADR 0519 — The fuzzer's runs end with an answer: a supervised browser, deadlines, and disks that hold a full run
A night of full-grid runs hung or failed silently five ways: a browser that died stayed a zombie of the executor and the grid waited on it with no error; a disk out of inodes failed late with ENOSPC and harness rows; mx-recall in one process ran out of Node's heap; a VM paused by a sleeping host left a grid step waiting on no child; and editing the oracle's claim table re-ran every cached runtime record. The executor (`scripts/fuzz/exec`) now supervises its children: each is reaped when it exits, the exit and the browser's last stderr lines are logged, and the DevTools connection fails at once (every waiting and later command, and a page's event wait within a second); startup and the Vite helper's calls have deadlines; a batch whose browser died reruns the programs it failed on a new browser, at most three restarts per executor (each logged), then they are harness rows, never verdicts; shutdown kills and waits on both children. `exec.mjs` bounds the start, every request and close, and kills the executor on a missed deadline or malformed output, so a grid fails instead of hanging. `focus.mjs` streams each grid's log as it runs, kills a grid process after `--grid-timeout` (120 minutes), gives the recall grids a 12 GB heap, runs one process per code with `--split-codes`, deletes `cases/` with `--prune-cases`, and reports a grid that judged nothing. `vm.sh` puts runs and caches on the VM's data disk (a local block device, never the host's virtiofs share), refuses to sync below 10 GiB or 10% of the inodes, and `detach` runs a command that outlives the SSH session. The runtime cache key hashes `runtime-oracle.mjs` without its `codeRules` table (`oracleRecordHash`): the table only judges a record. The fuzzer's caches and focus outputs live under `$TMPDIR/*.noindex` (Spotlight indexing them slowed timed tests). The runtimes run on Node, never on the caller's `process.execPath` (`scripts/lib/node-runtime.mjs`): under the bun that runs the repository's tests, the fast path ran on bun, where rc.17 reported no `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT` for a read after an `await` through a wrapper, so `misuse-fire.test.mjs` failed on every machine; the runtime cache key takes the spawned Node's version. Outcomes are unchanged:


<a id="adr-0520"></a>
## ADR 0520 — An action body is placed by its callers, and a yielded promise detaches the next step
The fuzzer's `action` recall context (NO_OWNER_CLEANUP, 588 certified false cleans of 624) had the checker certify `onCleanup` in `const a1 = action(function* () { onCleanup(() => {}); })` clean when a button handler called `a1()`: the generator is a callback with no owner edge, so the component body's providing region answered for it by position. rc.17 runs the generator step by step on the stack of whoever calls the action (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev.js:1573-1607`), so the body's owner is the caller's — none in a handler — and the same held for `createEffect`, `useContext`, `Loading(props)` and an `onSettled` cleanup there (probed in Node, dev build). Fix: the stepped generator's owner node takes the symbol of the `const` that holds the action (`action_binding_name`), a call or callback argument naming that binding (through `const` copies, not a written binding) targets the generator (`named_action_generator`), and no providing region answers for a stepped generator, so its operations are judged on the graph: every caller unowned is a violation, a caller's owner is inherited (a leaf caller's leaf, where `onCleanup` is SC3001's claim; a children-capable caller throws `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` first and the body's context is owned), and a generator no binding holds, or whose binding reaches a component prop, a slot or an assignment (`action_binding_escapes`), is seeded open-world unowned: an obligation. Steps: a yielded native promise (`Promise.resolve()`, `new Promise(…)`, `Promise.all` and siblings, resolved by the standard library's declaration; the demand plan now asks it for a `yield` operand) is assimilated, and the next step runs from its continuation with no owner (`:1618-1636`), as does every step of an async generator; a plain value steps again synchronously (`:1637`). solid-facts records every `yield` with its operand and dominated block region (schema 88, `AstFacts::yields`); `action_step_resumption` makes an operation in the region proven unowned (`after_yield`, a new `OwnerRequirement` flag with its own evidence line) and one a possible-thenable `yield` may precede uncertain; a call after one carries the new `ResumesUnowned` edge. Legal twins stay silent: an action called only from a root or a body (the action-call rule reports), never called, from a tracked body, or after `yield 1`; a write in an action body is unchanged (execution roles are untouched). Residual: an action handed to a component prop is an obligation, not the violation the ADR 0319 census gives a plain helper (the census refuses generators); a stored prop called from a timer is certified clean for a helper and an action alike (pre-existing).

<a id="adr-0521"></a>
## ADR 0521 — A generator stepped at its own call places its write at the call
The recall grid's `generator` layer in `pos-handler-root` and `pos-handler-owner` (38 certified false cleans of REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE) had the checker certify `createRoot(() => { (function* () { setW1(7); yield 1; })().next(); })` in a button handler clean: ADR 0516's stepped-generator arm in the read-role walk (`callee_roles.rs`) answered a write with the read role at the creating call, the handler's `EventCallback`, which the write arm passed through as legal; the read walk knows no write adjustment, so the root between the handler and the call was skipped. rc.17 runs the first `next()` of a fresh generator synchronously up to its first `yield`, on the stack of the creating call, where `createRoot` (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev-shared.js:1537-1543`, through `runWithOwner` `:5758`, which sets `context` to the root) has made the root the ambient owner; `setSignal` (`:5619-5636`) and the action guard (`dist/dev.js:1545-1556`) throw under a children-capable owner. Fix: the write walk places such a write itself (`stepped_generator_in_place`, `write_roles.rs`): the generator's own, non-async, not a primitive's callback, its iterator the call expression itself and stepped there (`generator_iterator_used`, `iterator_stepped_at`), and the write's role is the *write* role of the creating call (so a root, a bound owner, `untrack`, `runWithOwner(null)` and the handler each answer as for a closure run in place). A rejecting role is the write's only when the one step is a `next` and no own `yield` may precede the site (none written before it outside an exclusive arm, none in a loop that repeats it, as ADR 0520's `action_step_resumption` reads them); a site a straight-line `yield` dominates (`YieldFact::region`), or a body only `return()`/`throw()` steps, never runs (legal, `Unreported`); any other `yield` leaves it unplaced. The read arm keeps its ADR 0516 answer and answers `Unknown` for a write. Action calls take the same path (`push_action_invocation`); ADR 0520's action bodies are generators the action owns, never stepped at a call, so they are untouched. Fixture `write-stepped-generator-in-root`. Residual: a write in a closure the generator's first step runs in place (`(function* () { [1].forEach(() => setW1(7)); yield 1; })().next()`) is still placed by the read arm, which stops at the generator.

<a id="adr-0522"></a>
## ADR 0522 — A read in layers that run in place is the enclosing function's, and a class member's calls are obligations
The recall grid's `ref-factory-body` context (1,030 certified false cleans of STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED; layers `helper`, `iife`, `closure+helper`, `module-helper`, every binding) had the checker certify `const dir1 = () => { h1(() => { r1(); }); return () => {}; }; <p ref={dir1()} />` clean, where rc.17 warns while the element is created (the JSX evaluates `dir1()` eagerly in the body's strict-read window; `@solidjs/web` dist/web.dev.js:1016-1022 applies the ref under `runWithOwner(null)`, which keeps the window). The read's role was right (`UntrackedRendering`, ADR 0469's climb); the drop was in the summaries: `direct_reference_contributions` gives a read to the node of its innermost function, here the anonymous callback, whose summary nothing consumes, so `dir1`'s summary had no read and `local_access` had declined the site as one inside a named non-component function. Not ref-specific: `dir1();` as a statement in the body was certified too. Fix: a read written in layers that run in place (ADR 0469's `layer_run`: an IIFE, a helper's callback the helper calls in its own body on every completion, a closure bound to a name and called once, in any module; the generators stepped at their own call too) is also a row of each node whose own body holds a call the climb passes (`in_place_run_calls`, `in_place_layer_owners` in `interproc/reads.rs`), at that call: `local` when the call is not on every completion, undecided when the climb to it crosses a possible await, a parameter list or a stepped generator, no row past an await on every path, and the same escapes asked of the call as of a read written directly (a primitive's callback, a fresh stack, a retained literal). The direct-read proof accepts such an origin when the layers' outermost call in the callee's body is written directly there (`body_site_runs_during_call_through_layers`), so the finding is a violation; a hook body with a helper layer, called in the body, is one too. Two more drops in the read rules fail closed: a summary consumer's call inside a class member's body (`wrapUse()` in a constructor or method, a hook's returned accessor there; 264 false cleans per class context) was returned silent at `call_result_reads`'s non-component gate and is now an obligation, ADR 0516's answer for a read written there; and a read in a generator stepped at its own call in a function an intrinsic `ref` applies kept the position's deferred role, because `ref_applied_read_role` climbed layers only (`climb_in_place_layers`), and now takes `in_place_run_site`, unplaced through the generator (14 and 7 false cleans per ref context for SC1001 and SC5001). Writes were never affected: the write walk places layers itself (ADR 0469, 0521). Fixture `read-in-place-layers-summary`. Costs and residuals: a class member's helper call is an obligation even where every construction is in a handler or a tracked attribute (as ADR 0516's direct read is); a layered read after an await in an async hook is an attributed obligation, as the direct read after the await already was; only the direct-call and `Function.prototype` rows take the layer climb (a store head Get in a layer, a read reaching the layer through a non-layer edge such as a helper called twice inside the callback, stay unclaimed at the enclosing function); a factory in a JSX attribute is tracked and silent, as before.

<a id="adr-0526"></a>
## ADR 0526 — A standard-library write of a store's proxy and a destructuring leaf are store writes
The store grid's recall variants (3,246 certified false cleans of STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER, 210 wrong-rule) had the checker certify `Object.assign(s, { v: 7 })`, `Reflect.set(s, "v", 7)` and `Object.defineProperty(s, "v", { value: 7 })` clean in every context, and report `({ v: s.v } = { v: 7 })` as a strict read (SC1001) in a body and nothing elsewhere. rc.17 warns STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER at each outside the store's setter and stores nothing: the `set` and `defineProperty` traps return `true` after the warning (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev.js:5420-5451`, `:5691-5697`, `:5743-5749`; 700 runs, no value committed, no STRICT_READ for the destructuring in a body). Root causes: `no_direct_mutation` reads `assignments`, whose target for a pattern is the whole pattern, so no member root resolved; no arm read the standard library's reflective writes at all; and the read filter in `local_access` excluded only a member that is itself a whole plain-assignment target, so a pattern leaf stayed a Get. Fix: `AstFacts::is_plain_write_target` and `destructuring_assignment_leaves` (a `write_targets` entry inside a plain assignment's object or array pattern, not itself an assignment's target) make each leaf a written member for the write arm and a non-read for `local_access` and the pre-suspension store reads; and `no_store_standard_library_write` reports a call whose callee Type Facts resolves to the standard library's `ObjectConstructor.assign`, `Reflect.set` or `ObjectConstructor.defineProperty` and whose first argument's root is the store's bare identifier, through the same exemptions as an assignment (`store_write_standing`: the store's own setter window, the draft) and the same handed-container obligation (ADR 0509); a shadowed `Object`, a project `assign`, a spread first argument, the store in any other argument (`Object.assign({}, s, …)` is a copy) and a copy target are silent; a target past the root (`Object.assign(s.o, …)`) is uncertifiable, and silent where a link is nullish under strictNullChecks (ADR 0465); on a release whose store root is `Readonly` the arm stays silent (unmeasured there, though the calls type-check). Fixture `store-standard-library-writes`. Cost: a standard-library write of a handed container (a hook result, a props object: `Object.assign(accessors, …)`, `Object.defineProperty(props, …)`) is an ADR 0509 obligation where it was silent (five in fixtures `compound-components-escapes`, `provider-array-vetoes`, `spread-prop-backing`), as `accessors.v = 1` already was. Residuals: `Object.defineProperties`, `Reflect.defineProperty`, `Reflect.deleteProperty` and a `for` head's destructuring pattern are not read (unmeasured); a past-root target gets no ADR 0491 literal-link proof.

## ADR 0527 — Refs, setup positions, flow bodies, store operations and action bodies enter the recall grids, with the placements they proved wrong
The recall grids (`--recall` only; the base grids and every random profile keep their programs) gain the shapes catalogue slice A ranked first, each cell fire-checked on rc.17's fast runtime before any checker run (misuse fires its code, legal is silent): seven intrinsic `ref` forms (`REF_CONTEXTS`: inline, named, array, conditional, a factory's apply, a factory's body, a `For` row), eleven setup positions (`POS_CONTEXTS`: a JSX attribute, a class static block, field, constructor and method, a body `forEach`, a handler expression, `flush(fn)`, a root, bound owner or `untrack` in a handler), the bodies of `For`, `Repeat`, `Show` and `Match` function children (`FLOW_CONTEXTS`, a one-parameter `Show`/`Match` child: rc.17 runs a function child only when `child.length > 0`), action bodies for the owner codes, legal cells only the recall grid lists (`recallLegal`: a write or an action in `onSettled`, a tracked effect, `runWithOwner(null)` and an action body), and 21 store operations plus 5 legal copy and draft forms (`recallVariants`, a `{ v, items: [1, 2] }` store for the order-changing mutators). Placements per code are tables in `misuse.mjs` (`REF_`, `POS_`, `FLOW_PLACEMENT`). They found, on the build before each fix, certified false cleans in every family and one false positive, now placed: a read in an intrinsic ref callback had the non-reporting `DirectiveApply` role (11,398 programs) and is its element's creation role when the ref yields the literal, otherwise unplaced; a ref factory's returned literal is applied with no owner; only a function an event attribute's expression yields is its handler, and a sequence yields its last operand (an IIFE there runs at setup); the flow arm places a read nested in a flow body at its in-place run site; a class member's read is an obligation (ADR 0516); a named leaf-owner callback keeps the write exemption (684 legal false positives; ADRs 0520-0522 and 0526 record the action, generator, layered-read and store-write fixes). `rediscovery.json` names old-build programs for each, and its test now reads the recall grids. The hand-off layers (`HANDOFF_FORMS`: the site's function handed on by name to an array callback, `.call`/`.apply`/`Reflect.apply`, a tagged template, a wrapper or a member) are built and held: they measure owner-region, read-hand-off and climb-bound false cleans that main has too (`docs/open-work.md`). Harness: `focus.mjs` stops when a grid cannot start (no executor, under 5 GiB or 5% of the inodes free) instead of judging every code at zero, and the executor's Vite helper exits with its parent. Callback props (`CBP_CONTEXTS`: a same-file child calls `props.run()` in its body, JSX, a handler, an effect apply, `onSettled` or never; `run` inline, named, forwarded, destructured or spread) take their base grid only (`CBP_PLACEMENT`).

## ADR 0532 — Exhausting a placement climb proves no role
The saturation ladder d11/d9/d2c/d2b/d2 writes inside a handler-created root at 0/8/15/16/17 IIFE layers. rc.17 throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` at all five; `detached_in_place` exhausted at 16 and fell back to the handler's legal compiler role. Exhaustion now answers `Unknown`, as do the root, owner-keeping, disposal and unwindowed-role climbs; an exhausted owner-return climb leaves an obligation. Shared in-place climbs carry `exhausted`: role consumers answer `Unknown`, proof consumers withhold proof, and reachability assumes a possible class-member run. Returning a partial call and recursively classifying it was insufficient: the flow-child arm kept `DeferredCallback` when that classification was unplaced. The apply first-read proof requires reaching its target; the cleanup census accepts a last step landing exactly on its target. Alias walks use the file's alias count, callback-result identity exhaustion retains typed obligations, and callback-chain exhaustion retains an unknown composition instead of falling back to the slot role. Fixture `write-climb-depth` pins the ladder, legal handler twins, helper and flow-read variants; boundary tests check layer, generator-step and callback-result alias exhaustion. Cost: legal deep handlers are obligations. Discovery/attribution caps that drop candidates or rows remain known misses, not role proofs (docs/open-work.md).

A named reader previously contributed only at direct calls or primitive callback positions; other references could certify clean. Resolve synchronous standard-library callback arguments, template tags and Function.call/apply receivers through exact facts and place their reads at the carrier. Any remaining hand-off is an obligation. Fresh-stack callbacks remain deferred. Fixture `read-handed-by-name` pins the saturation probes; stored getters and unknown carriers remain uncertifiable. Alias hand-offs conservatively remain obligations.

Never-read object members remain obligations; JSX boundaries keep their existing treatment. Amendment: an operation in an exported helper's own body keeps the exported-caller cause, since its open-world or namespace seed is its owner edge (`exported_seed`; fixture `owner-exported-seed-cause`, repro cases `exported-lazy-route`, `open-program-boundary`, `exported-function-reached-by-dynamic-import`). Verdicts unchanged.

 `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_ROLE_LOG=<file|dir>` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/role_log.rs`) writes one JSON line per outermost role decision (`read_execution_role`, `semantic_execution_role`, `semantic_write_execution_role`, an action call's role): its site, final role, every tagged arm it passed and the last one that returned that role. Arms are `role_arm!("<file stem>.<name>", role)` at each `return ExecutionRole::…` of `execution_role/` (named by meaning in the read and write walks) and `arm_id!` at each answer of the owner emission (`root-owned`, `context-0`, `after-await`, …); `default` marks a non-reporting answer given for want of a proof (an assigned member's function, an allowed region's unplaced nested function, a tracked root, incompatible returned invocations, owner context zero). The log is write-only and every hook returns its input, so no answer moves (`make coverage`: 0 moved); unset, a hook is one atomic load. `scripts/fuzz/roles.mjs` (and `focus.mjs --role-log`) folds a grid's log with its rows into `roles.json`: per-arm decisions, the arms never hit (the inventory is read from the source), and three numbers per slice: findings per 1,000 programs, arms first hit against `--roles-against`, and decisions silent by default. A slice whose new programs raise none of the three is saturated for its axis. Region coverage (`cargo llvm-cov`) stays the stronger, per-PR measure once installed; the arm log is its substitute for the classifiers only.
