## ADR 0539 — A read in an apply's returned cleanup is unplaced
full-7 certified clean 3,828 rc.17 SC1001 warnings (`effect-cleanup-rerun`, `render-cleanup-rerun`): an inline function an apply returns took the silent nested role. rc.17's `runEffect` opens the strict-read window before it calls the previous cleanup (dev.js:1991-2005); the owner's disposal calls it outside, and nothing orders the two. Dialect question `returned_cleanup_reruns_in_read_window` (createEffect and createRenderEffect applies; a tracked effect's cleanup runs in its tracking compute): a read in such a cleanup, in place or through a summary call, is an obligation, never a violation, also for an apply named by a binding. A compute with no call, no reference and literal returns never reruns: silent. Fixture `effect-cleanup-rerun-read`; fuzzer context `effect-cleanup-rerun-named`.

<a id="adr-0540"></a>
## ADR 0540 — An effect bundle's error arm and an ownerless callback's layers
full-7 reported 1,884 false positives. SC1001 (400): a bundle `{ effect, error }` is the apply argument, and its largest function was taken as the apply; rc.17 calls `error` outside the window (dev.js:1966-1989). `effect_apply_span` makes the apply an exact bundle's `effect` member; a non-exact bundle is unplaced. SC2001 (1,484) and SC2002 (550): a write or an action call in a layer a `createReaction` callback runs in place took the component's role; `detached_in_place` now detaches any callback the dialect runs with no owner whenever it runs (`CallbackOwner::None`: the reaction callback, the effect apply). Fixture `effect-window-arms`.

<a id="adr-0541"></a>
## ADR 0541 — The reaction callback, a render apply's reruns and returned functions in the effect phase
full-7 certified clean 7,686 SC6002/SC2008 misuses and gave 613 SC6002 programs SC4001 only (effect-window cells). rc.17's `createReaction` callback is a deferred user effect's apply (dev.js:2630-2675): `callback_runs_in_effect_phase` includes it (rc.17 only). `callback_reruns_in_effect_phase` (createRenderEffect's apply: first run owned, outside a flush) makes any origin an obligation. A function its layer returns (an apply's cleanup) was "no single answer", silent; it now walks the code around it, as ADR 0538's unplaced layer, so any origin is an obligation. Fixture `effect-phase-windows`; `primitive-in-effect-callback` and `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback` pins move to obligations.

<a id="adr-0542"></a>
## ADR 0542 — A callback that keeps its caller's context is in an undecided component's body
The undecided-component funnel (ADR 0328's "Option B") carved out every callback a primitive places, `flush(fn)`'s too, although `flush` runs its callback where the caller is (`callback_preserves_owner_write_context`): a read there in a component also handed out as a plain function (`[1].map(Row)`) was a violation, the same read in the body an obligation. Such a callback stays in the region, unless ADR 0530's census proves the body is entered only through an exact dynamic factory (its flush-layer claims stand, and full-7's 12 dynamic store rows stay ADR 0530's accepted wrong-rule). Fixture `undecided-flush-callback`.

<a id="adr-0543"></a>
## ADR 0543 — The fuzzer's warm checker: a retained daemon answers `--feedback-facts`
A cheap fuzz program's check took ~30 ms one-shot, about half of it starting the producer and opening its program; the retained daemon refused `--feedback-facts` (`carried`) and answered with `feedback_facts: false` at both enablement sites. Now the request carries the mode (`CheckRequest.feedback_facts`), both sites read it, a cached answer keys on it, and each mode is its own daemon (socket identity) that refuses the other. Feedback models mean the same retained: they are built from the same facts and rule options (`RuleOptions.development_feedback` is in the retained builder's identity), and the session and daemon process tests compare whole answers with fresh ones across A→B→A, repeats, permutations, mode switches, fresh and reused slots, abandoned checks and restarts. A feedback check is retained only when `SOLID_CHECKER_DAEMON=1` asks; the release default leaves `feedback run` one-shot. A directory whose stamp moved over the same entries (name, type, inode; an untracked entry's own stamp) is no longer a rebuild: the executor's batch type check creates and removes `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` in its slot, which rebuilt every program (20 of 20 at generation 1). Any entry created, removed, renamed or replaced still rebuilds; a tracked file's bytes stay its fingerprint's. `--stop-daemon` ends a daemon once it has answered what it accepted, or after `SOLID_CHECKER_DAEMON_STOP_SECS` (60) by the pid beside its socket. A daemon closes every descriptor it inherited above stderr as it starts, and the executor starts one process at a time: on macOS `std` marks a new pipe close-on-exec after creating it, so a client the executor started in between handed another command's stdout pipe to its daemon, and that command's reader waited for the daemon's end (three grid passes stalled on one `prepare`; the process test rediscovers it on the old build). Fuzzer: `--warm-checker` (executor `warmChecker`, off by default): one daemon per slot directory; `--warm-verify` compares each warm answer with a one-shot run at the same path and fails the program on a difference; a killed or failed warm check stops its daemon before the slot is reused; the executor stops its daemons at its end; not with the role log (ADR 0533). Measured on the Mac (`scripts/fuzz/warm-identity.mjs`, concurrency 7, both type checks included): NO_OWNER_CLEANUP 3,000 programs 128-132 → 168-169 programs/s, mean check 44 → 30 ms; STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED 2,000 programs 132 → 194 programs/s, 44 → 20 ms; in two rounds of each, every warm answer byte-identical to its one-shot answer at the same path (10,000) and every program equal across passes. A program whose import lines differ from the slot's previous one costs the producer's re-resolving update (~25 ms against ~12 ms run alone). Default stays off until a full cached grid under `--warm-verify` agrees. Default since warm-verify-1: three grids under `--warm-verify`, 48,973 programs with Chrome, 0 differences; `--no-warm-checker` opts out.

<a id="adr-0544"></a>
## ADR 0544 — Role work and reachability stay polynomial in nested layers
A read's role asks, of every named closure around it (`unrun_closure_within`, ADR 0452), whether all its calls are in tracked positions, and placing each call asks the same of the closures around that call: closures called once per layer cost 2^depth placements per read (debug, 189 s at depth 20). `closure_unplaced` (`execution_role/body_roles.rs`) keeps a placement for the outermost query and reuses it only where recomputing it would answer the same: its computation met no cut on a closure in progress before it began, none of the closures it entered is in progress now, and its context is equal (the read-recording frame, `base::reads::current_frame`; whether a role-log decision is open; the flow-cache state, `flow_cache_state`: whether a flow or write-flow answer computes and how often their per-answer caches were cleared; the callbacks `inherits_callback_role` places). It is not kept under a probe or a proof's deferred check. A reuse replays what the role log recorded. Placements now grow as depth·(depth+3); `BuildTimings::closure_placements` counts them and the complexity ladder gates them under 2·depth²·placements(1), to depth 20. Where a root reaches a call-graph cycle, reachability no longer walks every simple path (factorial in a dense cycle): `path_counts` counts on the condensation (`condensation_counts`, Tarjan), which equals the path count on an acyclic graph and is nonzero exactly where a path reaches the call. `ReactiveWrite::multiplicity` is read only as zero or not (projection ignores it), so no decision moves; its value on a cyclic graph is no longer the simple-path count.

<a id="adr-0545"></a>
## ADR 0545 — Warm checks: parsed files outlive a chain, the producer's build inputs are witnessed, the role log is each answer's
An edit whose imports move cannot replace its file in place (`reuse_program`), so the producer builds a fresh chain, which parsed every file again (bundled libraries and installed declarations: most of a ~25 ms warm check) and digested every text again (~1.9 ms). `file_regions::ParseCache` hands a new chain each file whose parse options are equal and whose text is byte-equal to what the host reads now; the chain adopts the file's region, so the file lives while any chain reads it, and the cache keeps a committed head's files only. A parse is a function of its options and text and a bound file is frozen (`unsafe_boundary` invariant 8); tsrs's watch mode and project system share files across programs the same way. A cached file's text digest is kept in its entry (its address is unique while the entry holds it). The randomized incremental oracle opens without the cache.

The daemon's resolution witness modelled bare and `#` imports only: a relative or `paths` resolution relied on tracked sources and directory stamps, so a `package.json` rewritten in place inside an imported directory kept the old resolution and the warm answer differed from one-shot (`warm_resolution_process` fails on the old build). The producer now records every question its builds ask below the overlay (the build scope's lookups and read paths; a new chain replaces them, an in-place update adds its own) and answers them on `Modules` with `buildInputs`. The witness observes each at least as finely as tsrs's answer reads it: kind following links, a stat's kind and file size (tsrs reads nothing else of it), the canonical path, a listing with each entry's followed kind, a read's bytes; a tracked source's bytes stay the overlay's. Absent probes of a directory whose `lstat` stamp is unchanged are still absent. `graph_kept` also says an in-place update asked nothing new, and a kept update fetches no inputs. The observation follows the build by a few milliseconds, as every witness entry does.

A warm slot keeps one directory, `slots/<n>/program/`: the per-program slot names (e34cb6e75) gave each program a socket, so a grid started a daemon per program (`warm-identity.mjs` sent no ids, which hid it). Each check names its program in `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_ROLE_LOG_PROGRAM`; path lines carry it as `g` and `roles.mjs` takes it over the path. The client sends its role log and program with the check; the daemon points the process-wide log at them for that answer alone (`role_log::configure`), analyzes it with a fresh diagnostic session and no cached answer, waits out a cancelled idle check and records none. Appends are whole buffers under an exclusive advisory lock; path ids never repeat in a process. Measured on the Mac, one slot, 400 programs paired with the base build: NO_OWNER_CLEANUP changed-import check p50 23.2 → 16.8 ms, same-import 10.6 → 10.5 ms; STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED 10.7 → 10.8 ms. `warm-identity.mjs` (concurrency 7, 2,000 programs, verify and cross-pass identical, role-log folds equal): NO_OWNER_CLEANUP warm 168.6 → 176.8 programs/s (mean check 30.4 → 27.3 ms); STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED 183.5 → 182.4 (noise); both codes interleaved with every consecutive pair's imports changed, 169.6 → 177.8 (p90 44 → 33 ms). Owners: apps/solid-typefacts/src/{file_regions,overlay,project,inputs}.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/{daemon,daemon_resolution}.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/role_log.rs, rust/crates/typefacts (`BuildInputs`), scripts/fuzz/{exec/src/main.rs,roles.mjs,warm-identity.mjs}.

<a id="adr-0546"></a>
## ADR 0546 — Walks over cyclic callers are computed once per function, not once per path
k local functions that all call each other took 2.1-3.7 s at k = 8, 38-52 s at k = 9 and over five minutes at k = 10 (release, f31337fa8); now 30-34 ms at k = 8-9 and 50-54 ms at k = 12, 42 MB: four walks enumerated every simple path through the callers. Each is now computed by a strategy whose answer equals the paths', and follows the paths where that is not shown. With the role log on (its arms are every path's), under a probe or a proof's deferred check, or with `solid_reactive_ir::follow_every_path` (a test hook), every walk follows its paths.
- The write walk's caller fold (`execution_role/write_components.rs`, `region_fold`). The answer is path-dependent in general (a function whose callers are all on the path folds to nothing; deciding that is a Hamiltonian-path question), but over a region whose edges are plain calls into loops that fold plainly, a rejecting entry ends every fold above it and a revisit reaches no function or entry its first visit did not, so the first rejection the paths meet is a depth-first search's with one visited set; without one, `Unknown` is absorbing, so each fold stops at its first `Unknown`, and a region where none can be `Unknown` folds to the role its first entries lead to. Each call site is placed once, in the walk's state at the root plus its own function, with every `visiting` query recorded (`visit_contains`, `visit_insert`); a placement that asked about another region function, an answered entry written in one, an edge whose loop is not plain or whose `Unknown` the fallback would place, or 2^20 steps make the walk follow the paths.
- The site-reach query (ADR 0458, `site_reach.rs`) iterates its least fixed point from all `false` when the program has fewer functions than its depth limit (64): the depth-first derivation then never stops at the limit and answers the same fixed point. At 64 or more it derives as before.
- ADR 0537's helper climb keeps each call's own answer (`handed_tail`, the climb's only rejection) for the climb; where no call within its depth rejects, a fold stops at its first unplaced entry, and where in addition none is unplaced and at most `depth` helpers have a call (no path can run out of calls), the climb answers none at once.
- The ordering proof (ADR 0339, `initialization.rs`) is a conjunction in which a function in progress counts as proved: it fails exactly when a failing mention is reachable, so a function proved once in a root's proof stays proved (`Proving`).
A component whose two named functions are each other's one call (ADR 0479) hung in `layers_run_inline` (found by the differential test below); a layer met twice now ends that climb unproven.
A rebuilt chain (an edit whose imports move, ADR 0545) scanned every module's exports again. Its files come from the parse cache, so a file the old and new programs share is the same parsed and bound file, its binder symbols the same addresses, and a module's exported identities are a function of its file and of every file it reaches over resolution edges with their resolutions. Now (`passes::Graph::rebuilt_export_rescan`) only the modules that reach, over the new program's module and type-reference edges, a file that is not that same file or whose resolution records differ are rescanned, as an in-place update rescans what reaches its file; every module is rescanned when membership or the compiler options differ, the configuration changed, or a file that differs joins the global scope or augments a module in either program. The crate's tests compare every update's kept identities with a fresh scan and fail on a difference (`identity::after_update`); the randomized incremental oracle (120 seeds × 30 steps × 2 tests) kept 111,183 module scans, all equal, and `warm_resolution_process` adds re-export chains, a re-export retargeted with an import edit, a global augmentation and a deep dependency edited under an import edit, each warm answer equal to one-shot. Measured (paired, one slot, 400 programs): NO_OWNER_CLEANUP changed-import check p50 16.9 → 15.2 ms, p90 17.6 → 16.0, same-import 10.4 → 10.4; the producer's update 6.0 → 4.4 ms, its exports scan 2.2 → 0.2. The rest of the gap is the fresh chain's build (its resolutions and reads, ~2.6 ms), which only an in-place update avoids.
Counters `BuildTimings::write_walk_steps` and `reach_evaluations`; the complexity ladder builds k = 2..12 dense components in three hosts, both declaration forms, and gates their sum under 2·(k/2)³·work(2) (k = 12: 3,082 steps in the body, 6,183 in the handler). `write_walk_strategies_process` (its own binary: the switches are process-wide) builds 500 randomized mutually recursive programs with the hook on and off (and every eighth with the role log) and requires equal IR.

## ADR 0547 — An action's first slice reads in its callers' windows
Fable's precision report (2026-11-02, probes R11, R13, R19) found SC1001 false cleans: an action body's read took the deferred role, but rc.17's `step` (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:1573-1600`) changes neither `strictRead` nor `tracking`, so from an effect apply or a `createReaction` callback the read warns "an effect callback"; a plain-value `yield` resumes on the same stack (`:1637`). Dialect question `action_step_inherits_calling_window` (rc.17 only). A read in the generator's body before any suspending `yield` (`action_step_resumption`) takes the role of the action's calls: a call whose guard throws first (`reports_disallowed_write`) runs no step, a reporting caller's role is the read's, an unplaced caller or an exported, escaping or unfollowed binding makes it an obligation, silent callers keep the deferred answer. Its run is proven only when every call is (`callee_callback_timing` follows the calls, with the apply-region census): an action called from an apply and a handler is an obligation. Fixture `action-step-window`; fuzzer contexts `action-from-apply`, `action-from-reaction`, `action-plain-yield-from-apply` and their legal twins.

Fable's precision report (2026-11-02, defect 3; probes R04, R08): an owner operation in an exact bundle's `error` member answered the region (ADR 0534's obligation), and a read in a `createReaction` bundle's `error` member took the reaction callback's reporting role (an obligation). The fuzzer's new `reaction-error-arm` cells found worse on f31337fa8: a write or an action call in an IIFE there climbed to the component body, SC2001/SC2002 false positives (98 at a 0.2 sample). rc.17's `runEffect` calls a user effect's `_errorFn` from the flush before `setStrictRead`/`setEffectCallback` (`dist/dev.js:1969-1985`); `createReaction` passes `effectFn.error` to its arm, a user effect (`:2653-2672`). Dialect question `bundle_error_member_runs_unowned` (`createEffect`'s apply and `createReaction`'s callback, rc.17), not `browser_callback_member_trigger`, which would also add reaction deliveries to the browser host graph. An exact bundle (`exact_object_literal`) names its members: the owner graph gets an edge to the `effect` member with the slot's kind and to the `error` member unowned, the `error` member is an allowed region (reads silent, writes and actions legal, in-place layers climb there), and the reaction's `untracked-argument` arm leaves it out. Fixture `effect-error-arm-owner`; fuzzer: the `effect-error-arm`, `reaction-error-arm` and `action-from-error-arm` NO_OWNER_* and SETTLED_CLEANUP_UNOWNED cells move from recall-gap to agree, the 98 false positives to clean (92) or obligations (6, hand-off layers).

## ADR 0549 — `createReaction`'s tracker creates its effect under the creation owner
Fable's precision report (2026-11-02, defect 4; probe R09): `track()` of a tracker created with no owner was silent; rc.17's `createReaction` captures `getOwner()` and each `track()` runs `runWithOwner(owner, () => effect(…))` (`dist/dev.js:2630-2651`), so `effect()` warns NO_OWNER_EFFECT at every `track()`. The returned-invocation memo now keeps the creating call, whether every binding the walk followed is a `const`, and whether the call is at its module's top level, and the cross-file proof digest hashes them. The owner pass places an `effect` requirement at the invocation, judged at the creating call's owner (same file) or with none (another module's top level). The walk also folds exact local value flows (second opinion, Fable 5.1): a member callee (`thing.useIt()`), a slot of an object or tuple pattern or member over a closed object literal, a tuple literal (also through a `const` naming it), or a `const` function's only, complete-literal return; a `let` on the way makes the requirement an obligation. A creating call whose result escapes where no invocation is named (no single-name binding, exported, handed whole to a call, a JSX attribute's value) is an obligation at that call, silent where its owner is proven. Not followed: a hook declared with `function`, one with several returns, a tracker created inside a function of another module (its creation obligation stands). ## ADR 0560 — Path-dependent walks end at a bound; the helper climb and site reach answer exactly
ADR 0546 left three walks exponential where its folds do not apply (ed30c5738, release): `untrack(() => fj(n - 1))` edges between k local functions 1.0 s at k = 7 and 38.7 s at k = 8, alias closures (`const gj = () => fj(n - 1); gj();`) 5.6 s at k = 7, site reach in a program of 64 or more functions 6.9 s at k = 7 and over 40 s at k = 8, a helper climb whose one rejecting call is its helper's last 0.68 s at k = 10 (about four times per helper).
- The helper climb (`local_access.rs`, `first_rejection`). Where a call it reaches rejects (ADR 0546's ball), a helper's climb rejects exactly when a path of at most its calls left, over helpers not in progress, reaches a call whose own answer (`handed_tail`) rejects; a shortest such path repeats no helper, so a breadth-first search decides it (`rejection_within`). The climb descends to the first call of each helper whose helper's climb rejects, else whose own answer does, in the order the fold tries them: the fold's first rejection. A descent that finds none follows the paths (not met). k = 10: 679 → 35 ms, k = 12 1,709 steps.
- Site reach (`site_reach.rs`). A derivation holds in progress only functions the answer may read: the function written around it and the functions each call, render or reference of its name or its class's name is written in. Where those are fewer than 64 and all the program's own (`reads_few`), no derivation from it reaches the depth limit, so the least fixed point answers at any program size. Elsewhere the fixed point answers a function it proves runs (the limit only adds `true` to a monotone system, so a derivation answers at least the fixed point) and keeps only those; the rest derive depth first. k = 12: 56 ms.
- The bound (`WALK_STEP_BOUND`, 2^16) where the answer depends on the path: the write walk where its region fold does not apply (which callers a fold reaches cut is a Hamiltonian-path question), the climb where it follows paths (the role log, a probe, a proof's deferred check, or no rejection), and the depth-first site-reach derivations. A write walk with a `visiting` set of its own counts its region and path steps (`bounded_walk`; a nested walk with a fresh set counts its own, so an answer is a function of where the walk starts) and past the bound answers `Unknown`, which no flow fallback replaces; a climb answers `Unknown`, which no hand-off tail replaces; a site-reach query that stops at the bound or the depth limit answers every later question undecided (`Reach::Undecided`, `function_runs` true): a violation there is uncertifiable with its own message (`UnreachedSite::undecided`), a cleanup-handle call unplaced, an owner operation kept. untrack k = 8 38.7 → 3.3 s, alias k = 8 >40 → 2.6 s, each write uncertifiable.
Census, identity against ed30c5738 (findings and full JSON per program): 8,000 grid programs (base and recall shapes, one in ninety), 2,123 depth cells, 600 random mutually recursive programs, 72 dense components; all equal, ed30c5738 stops at the depth limit in none (so wherever the new query takes the fixed point, the base derivation answered it), no walk reaches the bound (largest 1,359 steps); with the role log, 6,762 programs, equal folds (400,702 lines), largest 1,695 steps. Counters `BuildTimings::bounded_walk_peak` and `bounded_walks_exhausted` (also in `SOLID_CHECKER_TIMINGS` output). The complexity ladder builds the exact shapes (site reach past 64 functions entered from dead code or through `forEach` arrows, the last-call climb) at k = 2..12 under the cubic envelope with no walk at its bound; `write_walk_strategies_process` builds the bounded shapes with the bound lowered to 2^10 (`bound_walks`, a test hook): each walk ends within 2·bound, work stays under 8·k²·bound, no write comes out clean and an undecided query leaves its sites undecided.
ADR 0546's two climb short-circuits survived `write_walk_strategies_process` as mutants: answering none at once also where a path can run out of calls (no `called <= depth`), and also where a reachable call is unplaced. The generator now builds one program in six as a chain of 9 to 12 functions (longer than the climb's 8 calls) and adds a call in a function stored as a member's value, a named function handed to an inline carrier, a function handed by name as an effect's apply and second entries: the first mutant fails (program 119 of 500). The second answers the same in every shape built: a call whose own answer (`handed_tail`) is unplaced is written in a function stored as a member's value, which no call site or position places, and the write walk reaches it through the same call sites, so the write's own role is already `Unknown` (or rejects, which returns before the climb); argued, not proven for every program. `an_unplaced_call_the_climb_reaches_leaves_the_write_unplaced` pins the premise (each write stays `Unknown`). Removing the quick fold's break, and off-by-one mutants of the new descent, also answer the same: the break saves work only, and a descent that finds no rejection follows the paths.

## ADR 0561 — Inline carriers and template tags are owner edges
mx-recall CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND (644bd1a81): `[1].forEach(hf)`, `map`, `Array.from`, `sort`, `Reflect.apply` and ``hf`x` `` left `hf` with no owner edge (context zero, ADR 0534), so SC4002's owner-gated provider proof never ran: 120 SC4001 obligations where rc.17 throws ContextNotFoundError. The owner pass now gives a function handed whole to an `InlineCallback` row of `runtime_semantics` (ADR 0531's `HandOff::Inline` table; the compiler-selected standard-library declaration) and a template tag the edge its direct call has: the caller's owner, `call_owner_edge`'s kind, ADR 0468's may-run edge for a written or early-mentioned binding. No dialect question: a synchronous call keeps the current owner, the fact `.call`/`.apply` edges rest on. No edge for a generator, a primitive's call, a spread, or a carrier in an instance field or the owner's own JSX (`invocation_runs_under_owner`). A carrier that may run it zero times (`[].forEach(hf)`) gives no proof (ADR 0563). Files: `owners/owner_context.rs` (`inline_carrier_arguments`), `owners/owner_discovery.rs`. Fixture `owner-handed-inline`; moved: `owner-region-no-edge` f18 (obligation → violation, rc.17 warns), and two leaf fixtures lose an SC4001 obligation beside their SC3001 violation.

Not followed: a call in a nested function of the child (a handler, an `onSettled` callback: another owner), a forwarded, spread, defaulted or copied prop, a child in another file, a prop never called (no run, obligation). <a id="adr-0563"></a>
## ADR 0563 — A carrier proves a run only where it provably runs the callback
Owner decision (zero false positives): `InlineCallback` (ADR 0531's `HandOff::Inline`) is a timing claim, not a count; `[].forEach(hf)` runs `hf` zero times. `carrier_runs::carrier_provably_runs` answers, from the compiler-selected standard-library declaration only: `.call`/`.apply`, `Reflect.apply`, the `Promise` executor and a tagged template always run it; an `Array`/`ReadonlyArray` iteration method only on an array literal receiver (through parentheses and assertions) with an element and no spread or hole; `reduce`/`reduceRight` with two elements or one and an initial value; `sort` with two elements that cannot be `undefined` (number, string, boolean, `null`, array or function literals: `CompareArrayElements` orders `undefined` uncalled); `Array.from` (and the typed `from`s) with such a literal source. `Set`/`Map`/`NodeList` `forEach`, `String.replace`, `JSON.parse`'s reviver and every variable, property or call receiver may run it zero times. ECMAScript semantics, no dialect question. Consumers: ADR 0561's owner edges (otherwise the possible-unowned edge, so SC4001/SC4002 are obligations), SC3001's leaf walk (`guaranteed` only through a running carrier), and every async-read row (`AsyncRead::carrier_run_unproven`: SC5001/SC5002/SC5003/SC5005 uncertifiable, with its own message; `handed_async_read_role` prefers a running carrier). SC1001/SC2001 keep their recorded trade; SC1002 kept its feasible-path premise until ADR 0566. Fixtures `owner-carrier-provably-runs`, `async-carrier-provably-runs`, `leaf-carrier-provably-runs`; moved: SC3001 violations to obligations in `leaf-scope-host-callbacks` (3, signal lists) and `leaf-scope-host-timing` (2, `NodeList`), with SC4001's obligation back at each callback.

<a id="adr-0591"></a>
## ADR 0591 — The Linux checker identity is the image's GNU build id
A warm fuzz check in the Linux VM spent 26.8 of its 46.8 ms in the client (600 programs of mx-recall and lg-recall, shard 0/200, one slot), 96% of that reading and hashing the 40 MB executable for the daemon identity (`loaded_implementation_identity`, the executing-inode SHA-256 of 04a890520), once per invocation. Now, as LC_UUID names a Mach-O image, the one `NT_GNU_BUILD_ID` note of `/proc/self/exe`'s `PT_NOTE` segments names it (`gnu-build-id:`, `elf_build_id`): a few header reads of the executing inode. The linker computes it over the whole output, so a rebuild that changes the binary changes it; an image without exactly one build id of 16 to 64 bytes (an 8-byte `--build-id=fast`, a big-endian or 32-bit image, a malformed note) keeps the digest. The premise it shares with macOS: nothing rewrites a linked checker's code and keeps its build id. Measured: the check 46.8 → 23.0 ms (client 26.8 → 2.5 ms); the benchmark (warm caches, 24 slots on 12 vCPUs) 163 → 263 programs/s, checker p50 122 → 68 ms, p99 198 → 124 ms; every row of programs.jsonl and the full JSON answer of 600 programs equal.

<a id="adr-0592"></a>
## ADR 0592 — The fuzz executor hands a slot the program its daemon can update
A warm slot's daemon (ADR 0543) updates its program in place when the next program has the same file names and tsconfig.json, and reopens it (a new producer, every file parsed again) when they differ: 52.9 ms of daemon and producer CPU against 26.1 for an update. In grid order a third of the programs differ from the previous one (78% are `App.tsx` alone, the rest add `helpers.ts`, `lib.ts` or `solid-api.ts`), and with as many slots as step-1 workers a worker always got back the slot it had just freed. Now a worker takes the oldest of the first 64 queued requests whose shape (`project_shape`: names and tsconfig) a free slot last held, else the oldest, and a slot goes to the request of its shape first; no request is passed over more than four times the workers. Only the order of preparation moves, never an answer. Measured (the ADR 0591 benchmark, warm): 263 → 313 programs/s, checker p50 68 → 53 ms, p99 124 → 112 ms, checker busy 370 → 294 s; every row equal.

<a id="adr-0593"></a>
## ADR 0593 — Memo-graph shard work runs on the caller below 4,096 entries
A warm fuzz check started about 100 threads in the daemon (11 checks traced: 1,138 clones), 77 of them in `base::memo::each_shard`, which handed the 16 graph shards to min(cores, 16) fresh threads at every flush and graph sync however little they held; each start also re-read the cgroup quota (`available_parallelism`, uncached in `analysis_parallelism`). Now `each_shard` runs on the caller's thread when its items hold fewer than `SHARD_THREADS_MIN_WORK` (4,096) edges or nodes, as `parallel_slice_results` and `parallel_balanced_results` already do below 256 and 64 items, and the core count is read once per process. The inline arm is the existing sequential (wasm32-wasip1) one; results are in shard order either way and each item touches its own shard. A random 6,000-node graph keeps the worker arm under test against a fresh store. Measured (the ADR 0591 benchmark, warm): about 20 thread starts a check; 313 → 356 programs/s, checker p50 53 → 43 ms, busy 294 → 246 s; every row equal.

<a id="adr-0594"></a>
## ADR 0594 — The fuzz executor runs a check's own fan-outs on one worker
The executor runs a check per core (24 slots on 12 vCPUs warm), yet each check still spread its reactive IR, its producer's per-file passes and the producer's tsrs pool over every core: thread starts, futex wake-ups and `sched_yield` from 24 pools of 12. The checker it starts now gets `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_WORKERS`, `SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_WORKERS` and `RAYON_NUM_THREADS` at 1 (`CHECK_WORKERS`), unless its caller set them; its own `tsrs` type checks keep their threads. Answers do not depend on worker counts (`context_census_workers_process`; the full JSON of 600 programs equal either way). Measured (the ADR 0591 benchmark, warm): CPU per check 30.2 → 20.7 ms (one slot, 600 programs; a lone check reopening its program loses its parallelism, mean wall 19.7 → 21.9 ms), 360 → 381 programs/s, checker busy 243 → 224 s; every row equal.

<a id="adr-0597"></a>
## ADR 0597 — A grid prepares two chunks ahead
`matrix.mjs` and `legal.mjs` prepared chunk k + 1 (150 programs) only once chunk k's preparation had answered, so at every chunk's end the executor's queue ran dry: its workers idled until the chunk's slowest check answered (the VM stayed about 70% busy with every cache warm), and the slots of ADR 0592 took whatever program came last. `preparedAhead` (pipeline.mjs) now keeps chunks k + 1 and k + 2 preparing while chunk k finishes; each chunk's stage and the rows' order are as before. Measured (the ADR 0591 benchmark, warm): 382.6 → 433.4 programs/s, whole-VM CPU per program 22.0 → 20.0 ms (fewer reopened programs), checker p50 39 → 36 ms; every row equal.

<a id="adr-0598"></a>
## ADR 0598 — The recall grids are lean by default
`--recall` leaves out `LEAN_DEMOTED` (matrix.mjs): the mounts `show-fallback`, `show-keyed`, `for-signal`, `for-unkeyed`, `for-fallback`, `switch`, `repeat`, `loading`, `loading-on`, `errored`, `portal` and the callback forms `function`, `let`; `--recall-full` brings them back. On the VM (cd08cd408, seed 1, `--role-log`) a full run (mx-recall 754,182 and lg-recall 311,521 judged) and a lean run (438,426 and 185,791) both had 0 false positives, false cleans, wrong-rule, unexecuted and tsc-overlap; the lean run hit exactly the full run's 94 of 172 role arms (0 only in either). `redundancy.mjs` on the full run: each demoted mount answers alike `show-signal` on every program (27,528 misuse and 11,022 legal each, 0 differ), `loading` alike the root program, `function` and `let` alike `const`. The kept values differ (`dynamic` 11,784 misuse, `for-item` 4, `named` 310, `shared` 768). The nightly audit is lean; a Sunday run and a manual `recall: full` run are full, and a report is compared only with an earlier audit of its mode. Trade: a defect only a demoted value shows is found by the weekly run, not the nightly one; `rediscovery.json` `fullIds` pins such programs. The table is in scripts/fuzz/README.md ("Demoting saturated recall values").

<a id="adr-0599"></a>
## ADR 0599 — Editor latency: what a result chunk's stage proof holds, and per-declaration indexes
The editor path is the `solid-checker/eslint` plugin under oxlint's or ESLint's language server: per lint pass a `--format json --only-file` request to the retained daemon (the answer's size is no cost: `--only-file` and the full report take the same time). Measured on app-game (1,610 files, Linux VM, retained daemon), an edit took 787/1014/1075/1221 ms (end, mid, body, import edits), almost all Reactive IR. Three causes, none moving a finding:
- `SiteReach` (ADR 0458) found a declaration's references by scanning every identifier of the file with a binary search each, per function per query: 230 ms of final ordering. `AstFacts::reference_identifiers_to` answers from the lazy `references_by_declaration` index, re-checking each candidate with `reference_declaration` and yielding in `identifiers` order, so it is the scan's exact answer. Three more lazy indexes (`span_index.rs`) answer "the calls whose peeled callee is", "the returns whose peeled argument is" and "the bindings whose peeled initializer is" a span, in table order; 37 linear `calls` scans use the first.
- The result chunks' stage proof (ADR 0397) held what the direct-reference contributions and ADR 0522's in-place layer climb read: `function_call_sites` of every function. An edit that moved a function's span moved that proof and reran all 1,611 result chunks (200 ms). Both decide summary rows only, which a result chunk reads through the result view and compares by value (`dependency_matches`), as the graph chunks and the member-invocation refresh do; their reads now join `chunk_reads` (replayed into the stage after its proof is taken). Body, mid and import edits rerun 1 to 18 result chunks.
- Stale result chunks were recomputed one at a time; they now run on the workers (`parallel_balanced_results_from`, from two items).
`compute_uninitialized_references`' unlisted-binding fallback scanned the file's reference table per binding; it reads `references_to` per name. After: 542/618/697/800 ms on the same machine; `make ir-edit-fuzz` 0 mismatches. A second pass, same exactness: local-access chunk proofs are checked on the workers (49 to 28 ms); the in-place climb runs per node on the workers (propagation 31 to 18 ms); initialization order is warmed beside the other memos, decides each binding once instead of once per mention, and reads TypeScript references as a borrowed view (`SymbolReferenceList`) instead of a copy; the memo store's dirty marking uses hash sets for membership (15 to 9 ms); probes are claimed one at a time. 494 to 502 ms for an end edit. A census inside a whole-program memo must not run on the workers: a worker asking the lookup waits on the lock its caller holds (it hung). What remains is `docs/open-work.md`'s memo begin residue and late-stage passes.

<a id="adr-0600"></a>
## ADR 0600 — The daemon keeps a content digest while its file stamp holds
ADR 0400 (4) is reversed: a content digest is kept per path while the file's stamp (device, inode, length, modification and change times) is the one read before and after hashing it, the trust the daemon gives its other disk inputs. The 2026-10-25 audit measured app-game (under 6 ms); on sensor-sim's pnpm workspace the digests were 30 of a 47 ms cache hit, now 17 ms.

<a id="adr-0610"></a>
## ADR 0610 — A write that may follow an await takes a permissive entry role
A write its own async function may reach past an await or may not (a `catch` or `finally` whose `try` head is no proven inert async call, code after an await in a branch or a loop's second pass, a `for await`) was Unknown before the walk read its function's entries (`write_roles.walk-unknown-3`). It runs either on the stack the function was entered on, in the role its entries give it, or past the await on a later task, where rc.17's guard reads an ambient owner every entry point has restored (`dev-shared.js` `setSignal`, `devGuardStoreSetterWrite`): legal there. The walk now goes on to the entries (`LoopPlan::suspendable`, never folded as a plain edge) and keeps their role only when it is permissive (`past_possible_suspension`); a rejecting or unplaced one, and a named effect compute or tracked position, stay Unknown. The fuzzer measured the premise first: no write reports past an await on rc.17 in an async function, its loop, `for await` or `finally`, an async memo or effect compute, or a handler's async function (45 programs); those are SC2001 legal cells now (`async` base, the rest recall, `handler-async-*` new). Fixture `write-past-possible-await` (the corpus repro `write-after-await`, 256 sites, is this shape: a submit handler's `catch` and `finally`).

<a id="adr-0611"></a>
## ADR 0611 — A write past an await of a function it runs in place inside is legal
ADR 0610's SC2001 legal cells found 16,528 false positives on main (`lg-recall`, every `memo-async*` and `effect-async*` cell): `await p; (() => set(1))()` in an async memo or effect compute, the write in an IIFE, a helper's callback (same file or imported), a named closure's one call, or through module and depth layers, was `no-write-in-pure-computation` with the compute's lexical role (`callee_roles.tracked-callback`); the suspension checks read only the write's own function. rc.17 runs it on the later task with no owner and reports nothing. `suspension_through_in_place_layers` (write_roles.rs) climbs the layers `layer_run` proves run in place on their caller's stack and owner, and asks at each layer's call whether it follows an await of its own async function: past one on every path the write is `Unreported`; on some path, a rejecting role is no claim. A Solid primitive's callback is never an in-place layer, so a `createRoot` body past the await keeps its owner and stays a violation (rc.17 throws there). Fixture `write-past-layered-await`; `rediscovery.json` lists 16 of the grid's ids.

<a id="adr-0612"></a>
## ADR 0612 — Past an await an async compute has no observer and no owner
The fuzzer's async family (`ASYNC_FAMILY_PLACEMENT`, 476 probes on rc.17, then the recall grids) found three defects of one premise, the compute's synchronous run taken for all of it. SC2004/SC2005: `resolve`/`until` past a compute's await were violations (12,176 legal programs) though the guard reads `getObserver()`, which no later task has; `past_suspension` (own function, or a layer's call, ADR 0611) now clears a proven await and leaves a possible one an obligation. SC4001: `createEffect`, `onCleanup`, a boundary and a settled cleanup past a compute's await were silent (2,376 misuse programs, 1,140 certified false cleans, 190 more as a second code): the providing region answered `root_owned` before the after-await path; a region now owns only the synchronous run. That exposed `calls_after_await` counting a `catch` or `finally` past a `try` head that may throw before it suspends, where the code runs under the function's owner: such a site is a possible continuation unless `follows_try_head_await` proves the head (a plain async function's too). SC1002: the object of a written member (`s.a` in `s.a.v = 1`) past the await was skipped as part of the longer path (4,124 certified misses, as second codes of SC2003), the skip ADR 0498 removed from the strict-read window; `read_as_part_of_longer_path` is now the one test, and a member read in a function run in place past the boundary counts as read there, as an accessor call does (not through a library callback, which may run zero times: ADR 0566). Measured: a thrown code in an async compute only rejects its promise, so a cell there needs a `try` and a marker (README, Not built); three first placements were wrong that way, and the new legal cells all pass it. Fixture `past-await-owner-observer`; `eslint-plugin-corpus` gains the two proven SC4001s (`nestedScope`, `nestedReactiveScope`).

<a id="adr-0613"></a>
## ADR 0613 — A synchronous flush under an owner withholds the drained callbacks' ownerless proofs
A `createEffect` apply, a `createRenderEffect` apply after its first run, a `createReaction` callback and an effect bundle's `error` member run from the queue's drain. rc.17's `flush()` drains on its caller's stack and sets no owner (`dist/dev-shared.js:2646-2721`), so a flush under an owner runs them owned. Probed on the fast runtime: with a `flush()` in a component body, a memo compute or a root body after a `createEffect`, a `createEffect` in its apply raised nothing (the checker said SC4001 violation: a false positive) and a write there threw `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` (the checker certified it: a false clean); in a handler or `runWithOwner(null, …)` the throws stay. The dialect names those callbacks (`callback_runs_drained_by_flush`), and a census (`indexes/flush_census.rs`) lists every reference to the flush primitive. A call does not veto where its position proves no owner, by the dispatch census's classes (ADRs 0356, 0435, 0437, 0438) and an inline element handler the compiler reports (the owner graph's own unowned edge; the handler classes only while the dispatch veto is absent), or where the effect-phase walk proves it runs only on a `createEffect` apply of the same `solid-js` copy (the queue is running: it drains nothing). Any other call, and a reference that is not a call (`run(flush)`), vetoes program-wide: the `FlushVetoAnswer` memo and `FlushVeto` section, as ADR 0450 keeps the dispatch veto (a debug tripwire refuses a read while the census computes). Under the veto the drained callback's unowned edge is `DrainedByFlush` (possibly owned, never proven, and leaving a region written around the call: a plain conditional edge would let a `createRoot` around the effect answer for the apply, a false clean), SC6002 is uncertifiable, and a write in such a callback, in a layer it runs in place, in a render effect's later run, in the cleanup an apply returns (a rerun runs it from the drain), in a bundle member or with an apply role is unplaced (SC2001 uncertifiable). Narrowing to effects created before an owned flush can run is not built (order across components is not modelled). Not vetoed: a flush in dependency code, a package analyzed as source included (premise; `@solidjs/router`'s `preload` calls one), through a computed member or an escaped namespace object. Fuzzer: `FLUSH_DRAIN_CONTEXTS`. Fixtures `flush-veto-*`; eight older snapshots moved to uncertifiable, none to clean. The review of the stack added `onSettled`: called with no owner it takes the same queue, so under the veto its callback is possibly owned (`QueuedLeafDrained`) and its writes unplaced (a `missing-owner` false positive and a write false clean, probed); and a `flush()` in a parameter default is no module evaluation (the dispatch census's class too): it runs at its function's call. An `onSettled` called under an owner is a true leaf, but the write role does not tell its call sites apart: under the veto it is unplaced too, a recall cost (`leaf-owner`, `setter-in-root-*`, `write-scope-roots-*`, `eslint-plugin-corpus` move to uncertifiable).

<a id="adr-0614"></a>
## ADR 0614 — One read answers one way on every path
Certified second-code misses of `mx-recall` (focus-final, 2026-11-04: 7,110 rows; all on main's build too) and the census's first runs. Root cause of most: a read has parallel paths, and fixes reached one. `discover_member` and the spread walk kept `semantic_execution_role` after ADR 0520 moved accessor calls to `read_execution_role`, so a store or prop read in an intrinsic `ref` took the silent `DirectiveApply`; `gate_refuses` returned where ADR 0531's accessor path asks `handed_read_role`, so a store read in a named function a carrier runs was dropped (an escape with no follow-up too, ADR 0516); the store row of `reads.rs` had no ADR 0522 layer owners. All three now match the accessor's. SC1002's helper walk read accessor calls only: a store read in `wrapUse()` past the await and through a receiver holding a `const` (ADR 0481) are now read, keys compared after the root. An object's callable holding a carrier takes the hand-off (obligation), as any named function does.
Runtime premises the census and second codes measured wrong: `Loading`/`Errored` as functions and `resolve`/`until` create a computation, so rc.17 refuses them in an effect callback (SC6002) and a leaf owner (SC3001); SC2004's hint said to call `resolve` in an apply. The observer guard needs its own answer: an action's first step answers to its callers (a tracked caller reports no read, so ADR 0547's role is no proof), a flush-drained apply to the veto (ADR 0613), and a deferred role under tracked JSX proves nothing (all SC2004/SC2005 obligations). A pending read in an action step takes the leaf owner its every call has (SC5001). SC1002 of an async source is an obligation: settled it warns, pending it throws first (both measured). An apply, or its cleanup, in a flow child is no render (SC5003 false positive). Fixtures `store-read-placement`, `after-await-store-paths`, `after-await-async-source`, `boundary-in-effect-callback`, `observer-callers`, `observer-flush-drained`, `pending-read-action-step`, `async-read-under-jsx`.

<a id="adr-0615"></a>
## ADR 0615 — A defect fails where it is created
Owner's question: find an issue when it is created, not afterwards. Sampled the ledger's 72 rows and this stack: 7,264 flagged rows all fail on main (nothing new but the `flush-*` shapes); the gap from cause to detection ran from hours to three months (ADR 0585: `named_callback_execution_role`, 2026-07-31, found 11-03). Causes: (1) cells are hand tables per code, so a context reached a code only by a slice's choice: 2,334 of 4,100 code × context pairs had none (`memo-async` existed from 10-27, reached SC2001 on 11-04: 16,528 false positives). (2) Measured, failing nothing: second codes tallied since ADR 0498, `not-fired` rows judged no finding and computed no second code, another rule's violation at a site was never judged. (3) No generated program ran per pull request: CI and `make verify` run the fuzzer's unit tests only, `make fuzz-smoke` ran by hand, and 60 of 64 ledger rows named a grid with no manifest, so `existsSync` skipped them; ADR 0586's regression (5820be453) lived five hours to a hand run. (4) A fix reached one of parallel paths (ADR 0614). (5) A premise written down, not exercised: the flush premise, ADR 0483 to ADR 0613.
Built: **the census** (`mx-census`, `matrix.mjs censusCells`), a default grid: every pair no cell lists, in plain shapes, judged as any grid, so a new context or code is crossed with everything the run it lands, with no table entry (a thrown code where a promise swallows it, and pairs that halt before the driver, are out, each with its reason). It alone caught none of the store misses, which are in listed pairs. **The covering samples** (`cover.mjs`, owner decision): a greedy cover of every pair of axis values over the whole enumeration, misuse, legal and pair grids alike (`side` is an axis), computed from the shapes, so it is a function of the code and a new value is in the next sample. Three tiers: every pull request, the census and all pairs at a fixed seed (`fuzz-pr.yml`, `make fuzz-smoke`), so a failure belongs to the pull request; nightly or before a merge, the pairs within each code, the nightly lane on a seed of the day (`make fuzz-premerge`); weekly or before a release, the full grids. **The ledger's programs are required** in every sample: 60 of 64 rows had named a grid with no manifest. **A capped oracle** (`--runtime-diff-cap`): 25 a grid; the census's oracle is stratified by code and outcome (a census took 878 s, 870 of them in the oracle). **The judge reads all it saw**: a claiming violation on a `not-fired` site that ran, another rule's violation at a site, a second code on a `not-fired` program. **`store-link`** read variants for SC1001 and SC1002.
Measured on 25,186 known problem rows the grids still generate (15 categories, 93 category × context groups; misuse and legal; the pair grids have no recorded rows), five seeds: all pairs, 11,835 of 1,200,776 programs, chosen in about 30 s, hits 12-14 categories and 59-65 groups; pairs within each code, 60,156 programs, 14-15 and 85-88. A greedy cover took the owner's random-order sample of the mx rows from about 8,800 to 7,350 programs with no loss. The ledger's 1,508 programs make the pull-request sample 13,099. Not yet run on a runner: 16,300 programs at 6.4-11.4 programs a second (cold, cached) is 24-42 minutes before the build. The one category the top tier ever misses, 24 programs of `pos-flush-fn` × `dynamic`, is a four-way interaction, and `context × mount × layer` caught it on half the seeds.
Not built: test selection from role arms (only `execution_role` and owner arms are tagged, so ADR 0614's member path would have selected nothing); a premise ledger (no fact says a line of prose is a premise: review's job); `context × mount × layer` in the cover (29% more programs for a category it catches on half the seeds).

A hint that the runtime does not warn excused its finding on its own word; `window.mjs` now measures it: a control run reads one signal per counter, and rc.17 warns there exactly when a strict-read window is open (24 `pos-flush-fn` × `dynamic` rows: none; mounted plainly: all). No evidence excuses nothing. The pair grids join the audit. The sample found three older false positives, `delete store.key` past an await. The per-code tier found a pending read through a helper in a handler's `onSettled` proven SC5001 (332 of 1,284 programs on main): the read now takes the owner fixed point's `onSettled` gate.

<a id="adr-0616"></a>
## ADR 0616 — The runtime oracle runs its cases in `feedback batch` processes
Each oracle worker is one `feedback batch` process (packages/cli README) instead of one `feedback run` per case. Per case, a fresh run spent about 1.1 of 3.2 s on what does not depend on the case: Node and tooling imports (0.3 s), two hashes of the Chromium binary (0.13 s), Chromium's launch (0.23 s), parsing the TypeScript lib declarations (0.25 s) and process exit (0.3 s); the page's own pauses (1 s) are semantic and stay. A batch shares the loaded tooling, one Chromium process (a new context per case), the browser's pin (hashed at open and close, its file stamp checked after every run, ADR 0600's trust) and the execution cache's harness and tooling identities; every run keeps its own snapshot, native analysis, Vite server, context and input pins, and starts from the environment and working directory a fresh run has (Vite sets NODE_ENV). A process retires after a failed run, an application Vite config (application code in the process), a changed working directory, a lost browser or a context left open; one that refuses its close takes its cases with it. Declaration files are parsed once per process, keyed by path, options and bytes. On the 741 fixture cases (c8, cache off) the rows are identical and the run took 324 s against 840 s; 20 lg programs serially took 39.7 s against 64.9 s, and 10.6 s against 27.0 s on execution-cache hits; the cache keys are the same in both modes. `--session per-case` keeps the old shape.

<a id="adr-0617"></a>
## ADR 0617 — The oracle samples are split over the run's runners by size
`audit.mjs pick` gives each grid's oracle sample one job, then one more part at a time to the grid whose part is largest, while the run has fewer than 20 jobs (the account's concurrent runners) and that part is over 200 programs (every N-th id, as before). This replaces the by-name map (mx-recall 4, lg-recall 2). Run 41775917761 (full recall, every cache cold) waited on mx-census's 2,294 programs in one job (90.2 minutes) while every other oracle job ended within 24. A program took 1.0 to 2.3 s of a job's differential, and the same code was 1.6x apart between runners, so a part's size, not its grid, is its cost. That run never had more than 20 jobs at once and started its shards in no index order (shard 0 was 29th), so listing the large parts first would not keep them out of the queue; the budget does. Its samples give 20 jobs of at most 439 programs (census 6, both recall grids 3), about 17 minutes on the slowest runner before ADR 0616; the floor leaves a lean night's runners unused rather than add jobs whose setup and warm-state artifact cost more than they save. A changed part count makes that job's warm state cold once.

<a id="adr-0618"></a>
## ADR 0618 — Main's CI builds, and does not retest, a tree a pull request passed on
A pull request's passing CI run uploads `ci-tested-tree-<tree>`, the tree of the merge ref it tested. A push to main looks the artifact up by its own commit's tree and accepts it only from a `ci.yml` `pull_request` run in this repository whose whole run succeeded. Then `rust-engine` and `wasm-package` only build (clippy, `cargo test --no-run`, the debug checker, the WASM package), which is what writes main's caches; `wasm-parity` and `scripts-and-cli` are skipped; `rust-package` is unchanged. A missing, expired or unaccepted record runs everything, as does a merge after main moved (a different tree). CI cancels a pull request's superseded run; main's runs are never cancelled. Owner: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

<a id="adr-0619"></a>
## ADR 0619 — An unlabelled `untrack` keeps the pending throw for the body's own source
rc.21 `untrack` sets `strictPending` in an active strict window, and `read()` throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` on `strictPending && owner._parent === context` (#4630), so a pending memo, derived store or projection the strict body made throws through `untrack`, nested ones too. SC5001 reads that as the body's own read: the read's callback chain is exact unlabelled `untrack`s, the outermost runs in the strict window, and the source's binding is in the same function. A source made elsewhere, or a read under a child root, has another `context`. Answered by `Dialect::untrack_keeps_own_pending_throw` on the rc.21 triple. A hook's memo throws too but is not claimed: open work. Owners: `execution_role/read_context.rs` (`untrack_throws_own_pending_read`), `local_access.rs`.

<a id="adr-0620"></a>
## ADR 0620 — `deep(store)` is a read of the whole store
Measured on rc.21: `deep(s)` warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` in a strict window on any store, an empty or shallow one too, and throws for a pending derive; `snapshot` reads nothing. Rules-mining RS-R37. A proven SC1001 row at the call, uncertain over a pending derive. Owner: `local_access.rs` (`discover_whole_store_reads`).

<a id="adr-0621"></a>
## ADR 0621 — A whole-store read needs what it reads
Rules-mining CS-R15; every form measured on rc.21 in a strict window. `JSON.stringify` (`toJSON`), `Reflect.get`, `Array.from` and a template substitution read a key on any store; an iteration (`for…of`, array and call spread, array destructuring) reads `length`, so it needs an array literal; `{...s}`, `Object.values`, `entries` and `assign` read a key the store holds, so they need an object literal with one. `{...s}` on `{}` is silent, so the old proven `s spread` row on such a store was a false positive: a store of unknown shape is uncertifiable now. `Object.keys`, `in`, `hasOwn`, `for…in`, `!!s` and `snapshot` read none. Owner: `local_access.rs` (`store_initial_shape`). Correction (rules audit, reproduced in Chrome): `for…of` was certified: Type Facts keeps no entity at its operand, so `whole_store_source` asked nothing; it now takes the binder too. "Read none" holds for a settled store only: an uninitialized derive's traps throw (ADR 0635).

<a id="adr-0622"></a>
## ADR 0622 — `onFlush` and `from` are modelled on the rc.21 triple
`onFlush` ends in `queueMicrotask(callback)` on every path: deferred, no owner, no window. `from` calls nothing itself; the iterator a computation asks for runs the subscribe function in that pass, tracked and owned, and without a consumer it never runs. Whether a computation consumes the result is not known at the call, so `from`'s callback has a conditional owner, and a write in it is unproven. A mixed install keeps them in a scoped gap, so a namespace import of `solid-js` on a whole triple costs nothing. `from` left `V2_REMOVED_EXPORTS`, the 1.x name it was. Owner: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs.

<a id="adr-0623"></a>
## ADR 0623 — A tracked read in a compute is no obligation
`async_read_role` maps a tracked read in a computation's callback to `Unknown`, so it is no render for SC5003; the same `Unknown` also set `invocation_context_unproven`, so `createMemo(() => m())` over an async memo was an SC5001 obligation in every project since the flag was added. A pending source suspends such a read: `tracked_computation_read` separates it from a read no role places. Owner: `owners/async_context.rs`.

<a id="adr-0628"></a>
## ADR 0628 — A hook's memo under `untrack`, and a labelled `untrack`'s window
Measured on rc.21 in Chrome. A hook creates under its caller's owner, so `owner._parent === context` holds for the body that called it. A hook returning the creation itself (`return createMemo(...)`, an arrow's body) is a returned source like a bound one, so its plain read is SC5001, where it was SC1001 and the run throws; the summaries carry it (ADR 0486). `untrack_pending_read` answers per read: `Throws`, for a source a layer declares or a hook called directly in a layer creates (a whole value, or an object slot through `const h = useM()` or a pattern); `OtherOwner`, for a module-scope source, which suspends; `Unproven` for the rest, an obligation, a root's value and two creations in one hook among them. The layers are exact `untrack`s and what runs in place on their stack (an IIFE, a closure, a helper's callback). `untrack(m)` and `untrack(read)` read as `untrack(() => m())`. A helper that reads a parameter under `untrack`, and a hook's result no transport resolves, are obligations at the hand-off and the read, unless nothing the value reaches can be pending and this body's own: no call in the hook makes a computation (a signal or store of a data literal, a sync memo reading nothing), and a prop reached has plain signals from every call site, since its getter runs here and an inline memo would be this body's. A module `const` holding a hook's result, read directly or through local `const`s, belongs to no body's owner. An `untrack` in a body with no proven window (one `dynamic` mounts) stays an obligation for a source that body makes: the read re-runs the body onto a fresh pending memo and the page never settles; "no window" was read as "not under `untrack`", which certified it. A memo over an async source is pending with it, which no row states: under `untrack` it throws and was certified, and a plain read was a proven SC1001 where the run throws; both are obligations. `untrack(fn, "label")` opens a window wherever it runs (`strictRead = label`), so it reports a read and throws on any pending source, in a handler too; `false`, `undefined` and `""` open none, and any other expression is unplaced. A body re-run onto a fresh pending memo (`a1-hook-root`, a prop's memo under `untrack`) never ends and reports no code, so no grid can measure it: such a read stays an obligation. Owners: `execution_role/read_context.rs` (`untrack_layers`, `untrack_pending_read`, `untrack_label`), `pending_sources.rs`, `source_discovery/returned_sources.rs`, `Dialect::reports_untracked_reads_at`.

Tracked and event code reads it freely. The shape of the path's value is not the store's, so a whole-value use stays uncertain. Owner: `local_access.rs`.

So an uncaught throw leaves the pass and reports nothing (`a(); try { a() } catch {}`, measured in Chrome), and the condition is per pass, not per block. Proven (`caught_read_proven`) for a read of a source the body around the compute created with a literal `async` compute, on the stack of a sync compute that runs where it is written (a `lazy` memo waits for its first read), in a `try` whose `catch` has no `throw`, with every other read on that pass that may throw inside such a `try`. Any other such read in a `try` is an obligation: a memo may throw its error, and a value nothing resolves may be pending. <a id="adr-0639"></a>
## ADR 0639 — The strict window and the DOM event, measured in Chrome
The rc.21 window read from source (`strictReadEvent`, `dispatchingEvent`) ran in real Chrome through the fuzzer's host, 30 programs, against the checker. Silent, and clean: a sync handler (delegated, and a listener a `ref` adds), a handler past an await, one dispatched from another handler or an effect's apply, a timer or microtask scheduled from a handler or an apply, a focus handler an apply fires. Warns, and SC1001: an apply's read, with the apply run by a `flush()` in a handler, and after the apply dispatched something. Warns, uncertifiable: a listener dispatched from a body or a render effect's apply (`untrack(fn, label)` sets `ANY_EVENT`, so the body's window takes the dispatch), and an owned write there throws, also uncertifiable. A delegated handler dispatched from a body does not run: the element is not attached yet. The hand-written programs showed no disagreement. The event axis (`evt-*`, `timer-from-*`, `micro-from-*`, misuse.mjs) that now generates them found one: `flush()`'s guard takes no event, unlike the strict window and the primitive guard, so a flush in a handler an apply dispatches warns `FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`, and was certified. A flush in a function that is no component is an SC2008 obligation while any dispatch of the program has no placed caller (`DispatchSite::withholds_flush_proofs`, the callers that exclude a strict-read window; the receiver does not, a listener on `window` flushes too). So a dispatch in a helper or a body keeps it, and a harness dispatching after an await, or a handler dispatching, costs nothing; nor does a `flush()` no delivery reaches (a timer, past an await, a reviewed test runner). The first version took any visible dispatch and moved 8,732 legal lg-recall programs from clean to uncertifiable. `element.click()` was no hazard at all until now: an argumentless call had no resolved-call demand. Now it is one, a dispatch directly in a `createEffect`'s apply is its own caller class (`DispatchCaller::EffectApply`): no window, as measured, so a handler's read keeps its proof, while owner and flush proofs stay withheld. Any other unplaced `click()`, a body's or a helper's, withholds handler read proofs across the program, as `dispatchEvent` always did.
