## ADR 0640 — Obligations an exact fact decides
The review of #173's added obligations found them wider than their evidence, so each became a proof. A `click()` on an element the function creates and attaches only under `document.body` or the root element reaches no project listener, unless the program holds a `click` listener on the path, a handler on a JSX `body` or `html`, an `onclick` property, or a listener for an event nothing names on a target the guard cannot rule out (`DispatchReceiver::NoListener`). Caller classes now apply to every receiver, since the flush veto reads a `window` dispatch's caller. The test-only and, for a dispatch's own function, the event-only checks hold in an open program: an export refuses, and unseen code cannot name what a module keeps. A `flush()` on the stack of a call no delivery reaches, through `createRoot` or a helper's inline callback, is no obligation. rc.21 emits the untracked-read diagnostic before it throws, so a `catch` keeps SC5001; SC5003 and SC5005 keep their demotion, unmeasured (`AsyncRead::caught`). A store read in layers that run only in place takes the role they land on. SC2009's reach is a call path, not a mention: the slot of a hook's closed result (an expression-body arrow's too), callees, identifiers handed on, a `let`'s assignments. A value in an unwritten `let`, a pattern or member of a fresh literal (a spread of one, a quoted computed key) or a local hook's returned slot is that source (`closed_source`), under an `untrack` that keeps the throw. A catch that is one `throw` leaves the pass on every path, and a setter is no read. `s.c.m()` is a proven SC2003 when `c` is the instance of a class in the store's literal, the setter is never named, the store is local, and the class has no `extends` or computed member. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `indexes/flush_census.rs`, `execution_role/event_literals.rs`, `local_access.rs`, `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`, `projection.rs`.

<a id="adr-0641"></a>
## ADR 0641 — What the fuzzer's judge left unjudged
A review of the 898 findings the judge did not settle found a false positive and a wrong rule every grid passed. SC5003 proved `<C a={m()} />`, but rc.21 warns from a render effect that hits the pending value (`dist/dev.js:2680-2705`) and a component prop is a getter: an unread prop warns nothing (Chrome). It is now uncertifiable at any component tag's prop or children, with the blocker in its message; following the prop to the child's consumer is the proof, in `docs/open-work.md`. A control-flow tag reads in a render and stays proven. `resolve` and `until` under `createTrackedEffect` were `leaf-owner-forbidden-call`, but rc.21 throws the observer guard's "Cannot call resolve inside a reactive scope", so on rc.21 they are SC2004 and SC2005 (`Dialect::leaf_call_throws_observer_guard`); only `createTrackedEffect` has an observer, so under `onSettled`, which runs its callback under `untrack`, the same call stays `leaf-owner-forbidden-call` and reports `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE`; rc.17 is measured otherwise (ADR 0614). The judge: an `agree` row now judges every other violation on its lines, so a wrong rule cannot hide behind a claiming one (of the 39 rows that surfaced, 34 are masked: Chrome reports only the row's code, and each twin with the first throw removed reports the second rule's, so they go in `besideCode`; 5 are `primitive-in-directive-application` beside a sync memo in a `ref`, where no run shows the rule's claim and only its documented silence excuses it); a finding with no counter on its line is judged by its component's entry; a placeless code is judged by file; `gate` fails on a rise per (rule, verdict, reason), since a blind spot that grows is a regression; every row with an unsettled entry runs in Chrome; `source` and `mount` are back on the differential's axes, whose omission is why 7 of those 898 rows had been compared. `runtime-oracle.mjs` no longer waits forever for a batch process that printed its closing line and did not exit. Unexecuted violations stay reported and are no false positive by themselves: a rule's claim is "whenever this runs" (ADR 0458), the twin (`effect-apply-guard-open`, `cbp-apply`) shows the claim true once it runs, and the gate holds their count down. `deadsite.mjs`'s "real false positive" stands for code the program builds and can never run: a `--confirm-with-chrome` question, not a grid verdict.

<a id="adr-0642"></a>
## ADR 0642 — What delivers a listener on its caller's stack
Supersedes ADR 0329's "tree mutation stays trusted" for `remove`, and ADR 0639's `click`/`dispatchEvent` scope. The dispatch hazard held only those two, so the program-wide veto missed everything else that runs a listener synchronously, and a handler's read, write and pending read were certified clean from a body, a compute, `untrack` in a compute and `onSettled`, where Chrome warns or throws. Measured: `focus`, `blur`, `select` (`focus`, `focusin`; only the `select` event is queued), `remove` of a focused element and `removeChild` (`blur`), `checkValidity`, `reportValidity`, `requestSubmit`, `HTMLFormElement.reset`, `HTMLDialogElement.show`, `showModal`, `close`, `requestClose`, and `showPopover`, `hidePopover`, `togglePopover`, `Document.execCommand` (`input` on a focused editable), `ElementInternals.checkValidity` and `reportValidity`, and `HTMLSelectElement.remove`. `other.focus()` delivers the element that loses focus too, so the handler at risk need not be on the receiver. `delivers_synchronously` names exact declarations, with the type facts' own names (`HTMLOrSVGOrMathMLElement.focus`, `ChildNode.remove`), so a project method called `focus` is no hazard; a `click` or `dispatchEvent` on `any`, an interface or a type literal's signature names no declaration, and is an `Unknown` site, since the runtime receiver may be an element; the existing caller classes place them. Whether focus moves is a runtime fact, so each is taken to deliver, an unfocused `remove` included. ADR 0640's created element is no exclusion here: a `remove` of one is a hazard like any other, since `autofocus`, a selection or a tab key can focus an element no use of it names. The list cannot be complete, so a handler keeps the host-delivery proof only if its event is on a measured list (`event_keeps_delivery_proof`), read at the delivering element, not at a component's prop, and one denied delivery denies the function. A state change delivers `focus`, `blur`, `focusin`, `focusout`, `change`, `compositionend`, `beforetoggle`, `load`, `formdata` with no call to name: `t.disabled = true`, `replaceWith`, an `append` that moves, `new FormData`, a `popover` write, `Selection.collapse`, and Solid's own unmount, on the stack of `flush()` in a body or `createRoot`, `render()`'s disposer in a body, a memo, a compute or `untrack`, and `insert()`. Those handlers are uncertifiable in any program, `flush()` in one is SC2008's obligation, a hazard in one has no handler's stack, and a name nobody measured is denied too. Not hazards: `form.submit` (it delivers `formdata`, which the family holds), `setSelectionRange`, `setRangeText`, `scrollIntoView`, `details.open` (nothing synchronous). What the veto now withholds in the corpus is in `docs/open-work.md`. An apply's delivery keeps its read proof only while no flush drains under an owner: under one it has that owner, and the handler's write throws (`g10`). Owners: `runtime_semantics.rs`, `base/contributions.rs`, `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `cleanup.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs`.

<a id="adr-0643"></a>
## ADR 0643 — What installs a member no rule follows
A Get or a write of a plain local is no reactive operation to any rule, so what makes it one was invisible. A store as a prototype makes each Get and write of the object the store's, and a signal pair installed as an accessor makes `o.a = 2` the setter's call. Measured in Chrome on rc.21, all certified before: `({ __proto__: store }).v`, the quoted key, `merge({ __proto__: store }, …).v`, `Object.create(store)`, `Object.setPrototypeOf(o, store)` and `Reflect`'s, `o.__proto__ = store`, `F.prototype = store`, a method that reparents `this`, `set("__proto__", store)` reaching `o[k] = v`, the receiver of `Reflect.set(o, k, v, store)` (`STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`, `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`), and `Object.defineProperty`, `defineProperties`, `Reflect.defineProperty`, `Object.create`'s descriptors, aliased, destructured, by `.call`, by a literal key or through `globalThis` (`REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`, a strict read through `get`). The literal is no source, so `closed` (already false for that key) never reaches the question; the Get goes silent before any closedness test. `installed_members.rs` answers at the install, wherever the object goes: it is flow insensitive, so an escape, a later call and a helper change nothing. An install is clear when what it installs is a function with a body of its own (its role is judged there) or a prototype proved plain: `null`, a closed literal of functions and primitives, a `.prototype`, `Object.getPrototypeOf(x)`, `Object.create` of one. Anything else is now uncertifiable at the install, SC9012, whose blocker is that no Get or write is followed through an installed member. `Reflect.defineProperty` on a store is proved SC2003. A computed `["__proto__"]` key and a shorthand define an own property and are not installs. A data descriptor is `o.k = value` by another name, so it stays with the store-held-in-a-container gap in `docs/open-work.md`. Owner: `installed_members.rs`.

<a id="adr-0644"></a>
## ADR 0644 — `render` drains on its caller's stack
`render` is `createRoot`, `code()`, then `flush()` (`@solidjs/web@2.0.0-rc.21` `dist/web.dev.js:551`), and ADR 0613's census listed only `flush`. In Chrome, with `render` in a component body, an apply queued before it runs under the body's owner: its signal write throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` (certified), and its `onCleanup` is silent, which SC4001 proved a violation. A wrapper, a root, a memo and `forEach` do the same, and so does an effect in the nested tree. `Dialect::drains_queue_on_caller_stack` names `flush`, `render` and `hydrate` for the census, whose caller classes are unchanged: a module-scope `render`, a handler, a timer and past an await stay owner-free, so ordinary apps keep their proofs. These applies are now uncertifiable: SC2001 and SC4001 stay obligations (the false positive is withdrawn), as for `flush()`. `render` in an apply or in `onSettled` throws what `createRoot` throws there (`PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`, `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE`), so it joins `cleanup_rule` and `throws_creating_in_effect_callback`. `hydrate` needs server output to run, so its census entry is read from the bytes, and it stays out of those two tables. The other `flush()` calls of `solid-js` and `@solidjs/web` are hydration's own. Owners: `solid_2.rs`, `indexes/flush_census.rs`.

<a id="adr-0645"></a>
## ADR 0645 — A listener's reach, and what hears a created element
Three shipped false cleans, reproduced in Chrome. `program_listens_for_click` read direct calls of a member, so a bare `addEventListener`, a `.call` of the method and a literal key certified (`FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`); it now reads every way to the method, an `onclick` key and a computed key on a DOM global. ADR 0640 took any tag: a created `label` delivers its control's JSX handler and a created checkbox fires `change`, both on the apply's stack, so it now needs `createElement("a")` with that literal. Code a listener reaches took no role from the registration: an object's method, a bound member, a `handleEvent` object, and a function that is also a JSX handler were certified while Chrome warned and threw, and `window`'s exclusion from the delivery proofs is about JSX handlers, while a listener is on `window`. `listener_reach.rs` asks whether any dispatch may run with an owner or a window (`withholds_listener_proofs`: every receiver but ADR 0640's) and, if so, whether a registered listener calls project code: such a registration, and any read of the method that loses its arguments, is now uncertifiable, SC9012, whose blocker is that no proof follows a listener to the reads and writes it reaches. A default event does not bubble from `body` to `window` (Chrome is silent where the arbiter's jsdom run warned), and with `bubbles: true` it warns. Which event a site delivers and which target it reaches would decide most of these; ADR 0642's list is under review, so nothing here depends on its members. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `listener_reach.rs`.

<a id="adr-0646"></a>
## ADR 0646 — A hook's function slot, and a named function a carrier may not run
`returned_slot_is_literal_function` dropped the handed-member net for a slot written as a function literal, on the claim that the call graph follows it. With an unrelated member of that name written anywhere, the name veto stops the resolver, so nothing followed it: `const { read } = useThing(); read()` certified while Chrome warned `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`. The shortcut is gone: where the transport resolves, ADR 0509's drop removes the net and the read is proved; where it does not, it is now uncertifiable, with the name veto as its blocker. A read in `const hf = …; [].forEach(hf)` was a proven SC1001 where Chrome runs nothing: ADR 0563's rule held for a literal and an async read, not a named sync read. It now holds at the end of a file's read rows, on every path. The predicate does not know that `JSON.parse("{}", reviver)` runs the reviver, so that read is uncertifiable, not proved. Owners: `local_access.rs` (`named_carrier_may_not_run`).

<a id="adr-0650"></a>
## ADR 0650 — SC5007 reads every form of `on`, and only a non-function is silent

The flip's first rule. `LOADING_ON_ACCESSOR` fires on what `on` evaluates to, so the only silent answer is a proven non-function. `Callable` needs a value domain with no undefined, since callability skips a nullish constituent and the warning needs the function on the first run. A spread after the last `on` is closed only by a complete declared key set without `on`, under ADR 0436's premise; `Loading(...)` needs an exact literal argument. Everything else is an obligation, blocker `loading-on-value-unproven`. A primitive handed as a value (`<Dynamic component={Loading}>`) is already SC9012's. Owner: `loading_hold.rs`; the demands are in `demand_plan.rs`.

<a id="adr-0651"></a>

`has_children` and "the attribute is written" assumed both sources put content there. Chrome says otherwise: `textContent={undefined}` keeps the child, `innerHTML` beside `{null}` or whitespace keeps the HTML, while `innerHTML` and `innerText` wipe a real child whatever their value, and a filled `textContent` loses to any child in the source. A violation now needs a pair proven to lose something; a value not proven filled is uncertifiable, `content-source-value-unproven`. The `.unwrap_or_default()` over a non-literal spread read nothing, so `{...p}` holding `innerHTML` certified: any spread not closed by an object literal or a complete declared key set without a content key is uncertifiable, `spread-content-unproven`, alone too, since one object can hold two sources. The `children` pair alone stays TS2710's. Owner: `upstream_compat/syntax.rs`.

<a id="adr-0652"></a>

The rule needed a proven accessor and exempted every argument, so `String(c)`, `JSON.stringify({ c })`, `{...{ "data-c": c }}`, `title={c as any}`, an erased `==` and a hook's or prop's accessor in a template certified. Each is now uncertifiable, `accessor-value-coerced`. A parameter the compiler proves value-only cannot call the argument, unless the call keeps the value (`Object.assign`, `Object.defineProperty`, by declaration). A shorthand property carries the property's symbol, so its binding names the accessor. The `!x` guard needs a value domain with no undefined, since callability skips a nullish constituent. On the warning side, a member write on the accessor refuses the proof. These operands demand callability without a type descriptor: a descriptor is evidence other consumers read, and asking one moved an unrelated SC9012 (ViewerToolbar). Owners: `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`, `demand_plan.rs`.

<a id="adr-0659"></a>
## ADR 0659 — Which build runs a site, and `--rendering hydrated`
No selection could describe a hydrated app: `csr` is false for it, node hid client rules, and SSR with browser is refused. `--rendering hydrated` selects both: it is not a server build for client rules, and it proves server rendering for server rules. Packages resolve as the client, so `browser` joins its conditions and node is refused. `ExecutionPaths` holds two facts. `client_root_present` is any `render` or `hydrate` import, by name, since only its absence proves anything: under a server selection with no root a client claim is silent, and with a root it stands (D13), while only server entries and no selection make it uncertifiable, `client-root-unproven`. A build arm is a region under a proven `isServer`, silent for a client claim, or under a constant nothing decides, uncertifiable, `build-constant-arm`. Both apply once over final findings, for the rules `claims_client_runtime` names, so no rule's code changes. Owners: `execution_paths.rs`, the rules catalog. Limits are in `docs/open-work.md`.

Supersedes ADR 0642's "Not hazards: `form.submit`". `computation_provenance` answers `Async`, `Sync` or `Open` for a source's first argument: `Sync` needs a data value, a non-callable type, or a function with a closed return that names no `NotReadyError`, directly or through a call. `Open` joins `async_sources` with `opaque` and `provenance_open`, so SC5001 and SC5003 are uncertifiable, blocker `async-provenance-unproven`, and the read's SC1001 goes with them. `computation_is_async` stays a proof of a Promise for the static rules. A parameter compute (`createMemo(options)`) is open though its type may close it: no fact carries a value's signature. `SC8015`'s gate reads a getter's open source as a reactive read: `pending_open` is not `uncertain` about the backing. An object method, class member or array slot has no binding to follow, so a read of a possibly async source there gets two rows, SC5001 and SC5003, both uncertifiable. A function a primitive holds in an options bundle, and a tag function, keep their own place.

<a id="adr-0673"></a>
## ADR 0673 — A read only a hidden call runs is placed nowhere

A hidden call (getter, `toString`, iterator, tag, field, `Map` slot) has no call node, so a plain helper it runs had no row and no obligation:

`discover_member`, role `Unknown`, blocker by the existing read wording. The helper changes for all its readers: it also asks the hand-off census, since
`SiteReach` calls a method only a coercion runs never entered, and it reads this file's calls of the binding, since no edge leaves such a method.
Each helper is visited once: the walk fans out over every caller, and without the set `solid-groove` went from 5 s to the 900 s kill. So a
diamond past the depth bound may answer from its first path. ADR 0674 removes the bound. This is `sc1001.md` steps 2 and 3, at the site rather than at the call.

<a id="adr-0674"></a>
## ADR 0674 — The caller walk answers only by finishing

`runs_unplaced_through_callers` answered "placed" wherever it stopped: at its depth, at a helper already visited on a longer
path, at a parameter default, at an await on some paths, at a generator, at a function with no name. Each stop was a silent
clean, and four were live (`hidden-generator`, `hidden-generator-held`, `hidden-may-await`, `hidden-param-default`: certified,
Chrome warns). It is now a worklist with no depth: each function once, so order cannot change the answer, and a 4,096-site
budget that answers "unplaced" past it. Only an await on every path ends a branch as placed. `visited` terminates it; the
budget only guards a pathological graph.

The walk read calls, so a helper in a member only a coercion runs, reached by `h.call(null)`, `[1].map(h)`, a copy or a tag,
certified, in one file or across two. It now reads every reference of the binding. In another file a hand-off is unplaced,
since no role reads a carrier there; a proven component stops the question, because its render enters it however its value
travels; an intrinsic element's attribute is the handler roles' to place. A type query is `HandOff::Value`: erased, so nothing
runs there. That removes 208 SC6002 sites, 5 SC1001 and 1 SC2006 that rested only on `ReturnType<typeof useThing>`.

A named function no edge enters, handed to a carrier in a body, had no read row: `[1].map(h)` certified. A carrier that provably
runs it now proves SC1001 (`handed_async_read_role`), and one that may not leaves it uncertifiable: the zero-run trade of ADR 0531
does not extend to this arm. W's nets take the same walk.

<a id="adr-0675"></a>

`inheriting_call_role`'s reporting tail trusted the role at the call. Up the in-place layers from it: a scheduler's callback has
no window, proved silent (`n06`, and the 21 rows `sc1001.md` counts); a function nothing places proves no role; a component's body
keeps it. `reporting_role_where_the_call_runs`, reads only. `:817` is untouched: direct reads rely on it.
A read in a window-inheriting callback in a named handler also called in place (`h(); <button onClick={h} />`) certified, because
the handler role answered first: a reporting in-place call now wins. `n13 n22 n23` hold, so `sc1001.md`'s step 5 is not needed.

<a id="adr-0676"></a>
## ADR 0676 — A setter runs its callback on its stack

`setSt((d) => …)`, `setN((p) => …)` and a `reconcile` key that is argument 0 run on the setter's stack, so the setter's call places a
read there, in discovery rather than `layer_run`, which W's write walk reads. In an apply: proved V, where it certified (`r11 r18
r19 z01`). A key applied to the draft (`reconcile(v, key)(d)`) counts too. Where the write guard throws first the callback never runs:
no read claim, SC2001 stands (`z04 r16 r17`). A setter callback in a tracked effect runs, quietly (`t1 t2`). A key on a `reconcile` held
anywhere else, and anything else built for the setter (`storePath`), is unplaced: `r13`, `r14` lose a false V.
A store's own Gets in its setter's callback are silent in any window: `dev.js` checks `!inDraft`, measured (`z05`, `z08` through a
`const`). The test is identity of the tuple binding, so another store warns (`z09`), and so does a signal's own read (`z10`).
`CloudStorageContext:238` moves V to U: read in `setState(storePath(…))`. rc.21 exports no `storePath`, so the file does not load and
no Chrome truth exists; `sc1001.md` predicted this move. Tell the owner.

<a id="adr-0677"></a>
## ADR 0677 — A function only an arm reaches is in the arm; `NoHydration` is one
`ExecutionPaths` arms were lexical, so a helper only `if (isServer)` calls, or a tag only `isServer ? <X /> : null` renders, kept every client claim: `SC2003` and `SC2001` violations on code a client build never runs (ADR 0659's limit), and a helper a `clientOnly` body calls the same way (ADR 0664's). `arm_only_functions` runs to a fixpoint from the functions an arm names: a function all of whose references lie in arms joins the weakest of them. A deferred arm passes only a call, because a tag's body has its window back. A server-only answer for a function other files may import is open, `build-constant-arm`, not silent. A function with no symbol stays out and keeps its finding. `<NoHydration>` is a new arm where a hydrating pass may run (the selection, or a `hydrate` call): it returns `undefined` while hydrating and a plain mount renders its children, so claims there are uncertifiable, blocker `no-hydration-subtree`; with no hydrating pass they stand. An `export function f` holds all of `f`, so only export specifiers count as naming a binding.

<a id="adr-0678"></a>
## ADR 0678 — `render(Local, el)` is a root, not a component body
`render` calls its first argument as `code()` under `createRoot`, so a function it names has no strict window, as `render(() => ..., el)` has none; `<Local />` goes through `createComponent` and does. The named form took the component's role: SC1001 on a read Chrome is quiet about, and a pending read only uncertifiable. A read in a function every reference of which is such a call now takes the role of the call, which proves SC1001 silent and SC5001 true at module level. Reads only: a write under the root keeps SC2001. A `<Local />` anywhere, an alias, an export or a use in another file keeps the old answer, so `render(App, el)` with an imported `App` stays uncertifiable (D15, Chrome: an uncaught `NotReadyError`): the query is one file's, and the walk is recorded per file.

<a id="adr-0679"></a>
## ADR 0679 — A window-inheriting call in a function not placed proves no role
ADR 0675 climbed the in-place layers around `createRoot` and `runWithOwner`, then kept the role at the call. Written in a component, that role is lexical: `const go = () => createRoot(...); setTimeout(go, 0)` was a violation, and so was a helper called only from two handlers; Chrome is quiet on both. A function written in a component now answers from where it runs: a compiler or primitive callback keeps its role; a named one takes the roles of its direct calls and its in-place hand-offs, where one reporting position proves the claim and positions none of which reports prove it silent; one only handed to fresh-stack schedulers is silent; a holder with no name, or handed anywhere else, is unplaced (`function-run-unplaced`). A direct call in a body (`h(); h()`) and `[1].forEach(h)` keep the true warning.

<a id="adr-0680"></a>
## ADR 0680 — Every uncertifiable row names its blocker
The flip's rule asks for a clear uncertifiable with a named blocker, and only rows the flip added had one: 9,216 of the corpus's 18,502 carried a function's name or nothing in `analysisContext`, with the reason only in their wording (SC1001 4,792, SC2001 3,220, SC9012 558, SC2008 237). `project_finding` now derives the blocker from the row's own holes, in the order the proof meets them, and each backend escalation (`instance-field-run-unplaced`, `component-identity-unproven`, the four site-reach answers, `binding-before-initialization`, `compiler-refused-file`) names its own. A final pass names a row from its rule. `proof-step-unnamed` is the last resort and no blocker: `check.mjs` fails on it or on any unnamed row, and `scripts/flip/blockers.mjs` does so over a sweep. The context the row had stays as detail, `blocker:context`. No finding moves.

`createEffect`'s apply has no owner on any run (rc.21, measured: `getOwner()` null on the first run and a rerun, `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP` on both), so `onCleanup` there registers nothing, and a read reached only through that callback claimed what never happens. `refused_entry` now treats the callback as a refused entry: the site is uncertifiable, `refused-creation-unrun`, as a creation refused in an apply is. `createRenderEffect`'s apply is owned on its first run and keeps its answer, through the dialect's `apply_never_owned`. An intrinsic's `ref` runs during the body that creates the element, so `dynamic_component_entry` steps out of it, and the `ref` path now answers through `dynamic_read_role`: under `<Dynamic>` its read is no longer a claimed warning. It ends uncertifiable, `callee-callback-timing`.

<a id="adr-0682"></a>
## ADR 0682 — A read attributed at a call has the window of the body it is in
`dynamic_read_role` ran for direct reads, so a helper's pending read in a body `<Dynamic>` mounts kept the lexical `UntrackedRendering` and SC5001's violation under `<Loading>`, where Chrome retries and renders. The post-pass over `async_reads` now gives an attributed row the same role and the boundary cover a direct read has. The helper's own site stays uncertifiable: a read in it is unplaced, `function-run-unplaced`. With no boundary (Chrome: `ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY`) and with a mixed mount (Chrome: throws) the program stays uncertifiable.

<a id="adr-0683"></a>
## ADR 0683 — A method a project subclass may replace is not the standard one
TypeFacts resolves `held.forEach` to `Array.forEach` when `held: number[]`, and every in-place, fresh-stack and carrier proof reads that answer, so `class Late extends Array` overriding `forEach` with a microtask left a violation Chrome contradicts (`d05 d06 g24`). A class extending `Array`, `Map`, `Set`, `WeakMap`, `WeakSet` or `Promise` (the global, a `globalThis.` member, or a `const` alias) raises a recorded hazard per member it declares, or for every member when a key is computed. While it is raised, `resolved_callee_call` answers nothing for that method of that container, in the lookup and in the memo context, so all of those proofs fail closed and the rows are uncertifiable. By member, not by container: the corpus's `ogl` vectors extend `Array`, and a container-wide veto moved 3 true SC8014 violations and 279 rows on methods no subclass replaces. An array-literal receiver is exempt, as a local proof it is no instance of a subclass. Limits: a class made by a mixin, or a prototype patched, raises nothing: premises, as `Object.prototype` writes are. Cost: +68 uncertifiable in `app-game`, all but one SC9012, where classes overriding `Map` and `Set` members leave plain maps with no proof; a receiver bound to `new Map()` could be proved the same way as an array literal.

<a id="adr-0684"></a>
## ADR 0684 — A component's references are all uses
`props_reactivity` lists a component's references to prove its props closed. Two exemptions skipped them by position: anything inside the component's own function, and any same-named reference inside one of its elements. So `saved = C`, `me={C}`, `<C>{C({ run: setN })}</C>` and `<Dynamic component={C} run={setN} />` written inside `<C>` handed the component on, and it stayed certified while Chrome throws (H-D5). Only the name and the element's own opening and closing tag are now exempt; any other reference escapes, and the reads are uncertifiable. The census's JSX row rests on this.

<a id="adr-0685"></a>
## ADR 0685 — `valueOf` and `__defineGetter__` are no slot calls
The closure census took any named member call for a receiver that hands the object nowhere. `x.valueOf()` hands `x` out, and `__defineGetter__` and `__defineSetter__` write it, so a closed object's slot could be replaced and the read stayed proved: V where Chrome is quiet, certified where it warns (H-D4). Those three no longer count. They are the members that matter because `Object.prototype`'s are the only names `tsc` accepts beyond a closed literal's own slots.

<a id="adr-0686"></a>
## ADR 0686 — A resolved call drops its obligation only if the target's summary is complete
ADR 0509 let a call transport resolved exactly drop its handed obligation. The summary does not see what the target reads through a captured parameter, so `useT(c)` then `x.read()` was certified and Chrome warns or throws. `interproc/completeness.rs` answers whether a target's summary is complete: default open, a captured parameter (any shape), `arguments`, `this`, a rebound binding, or a `const` walked to one, is incomplete, through calls, with a cycle adding nothing and a bound answering open. A parameter annotated with primitive types only is followed, since no accessor, store or props object fits one; `id as any` is the program's own cast. Setters, actions and non-async accessors stay exact. It is read only in `reactive_analysis.rs`, so no result chunk holds a cross-file answer. Left to F0's later steps: `handed_value` on a call expression (`f01 f12`) and `ArrayMembers` at a reporting role (`f02`).

<a id="adr-0687"></a>
## ADR 0687 — A helper whose callers differ is not a nullable owner
`conditional_owner` meant "owned and unowned contexts both reach this", and every such row said `runWithOwner` received a nullable owner. Track W's mixed hooks (ADR 0714) are the other case: a function an owned caller and an unproven unowned one reach, with no `runWithOwner` anywhere. A site in a callback whose owner the dialect calls `Conditional` keeps that wording; any other gets `owner_varies_by_caller`, its own wording and the blocker `owner-varies-by-caller`. No kind moves.

Every call now asks: `.call` or `.apply` resolving to the standard declaration, invoking, with no function or source row for its receiver, and a store, accessor, setter or action (or a member of one) among its arguments, is SC9012, `function-call-target-unresolved`. <a id="adr-0689"></a>
## ADR 0689 — A hook's result is handed where no binding holds it
`handed_value` followed a `const` holding a call, and nothing else that was a call, so `useT(p.n).read()`, `useT(n)()()` and `useT(n).forEach((f) => f())` opened no obligation and certified; Chrome warns on all three (`v08`, `f01`, `f12`, `f02`). A call is now handed wherever it stands, a call of a call is its inner call's answer, and a callback's parameter holds an element of its standard-library call's receiver. A transported array's member call (`x.forEach((e) => e.m())`) whose target's summary is incomplete raises the obligation no std call had to keep. Now uncertifiable, all of it.

They are sources now, over-async as a memo is, and `createMemo(fn)()` is a read at the call: a violation, or `created-accessor-read-unproven` when the compute may suspend. `.toArray()` is a handed call: uncertifiable. ## ADR 0691 — A destructure takes the read's role
`may_snapshot_at_setup` asked the semantic role, which answers a `ref`'s layers and rows with a deferred callback before any `ref` arm, so a store destructure there certified and Chrome warns: 152 false cleans. It asks the read's role now. Uncertifiable, all of them.

<a id="adr-0692"></a>
## ADR 0692 — The value-use census
What a program does with a reference of a proven source was decided nowhere: a form no table named was a silent clean, so `try { throw c; } catch (e) { (e as () => number)(); }` certified with Chrome warning. `value_use.rs` walks every reference of an accessor, setter, action, store root or props root, and every creating call with no binding, to a position the analysis follows, or answers an SC9012 uncertifiable naming that position. The default is the obligation: a form no table holds is `value-use-unclassified`.

Item 1c of the end-stage report is confirmed: an export's span is its whole statement, so the draft's arm answered "followed" for every position in an `export function`, and `throw c` there certified while the same form in a plain function was open. `exported_directly` takes only `export { c }` and `export default c`. The fixture holds each form at module scope, in a plain function and in the three export forms. Measured with the old arm rebuilt: `throw c` certified at all three exports, Chrome warned at all three.

A `Followed` answer needs a proof, and the probes found five that had none: a method's or a slot's returned value (no reference names its callers), a call reached through a pattern over a holder (`const { c: h } = o; h()`: no row read it), a JSX tag (`<H />` calls what `H` holds), a helper reading `arguments`, a helper destructuring a root. A walked call now needs a read, write or action row, or a tracked position for an accessor, so the census runs after rows merge. Exported holders are walked in other files. A context handed on (`useCtx(Ctx)`) is open. `createRoot` answers its callback's value (`returned_callback_value`), so a memo a module root returns is followed.

Facts schema 90: `inert_operands`, a statement's value and the operands of `void`, `typeof`, `===`, `!==`. A plain assignment's target holds no value. Positions met twice on one walk answer what they answered; only a position met while it is being answered is a cycle.

A holder's slot is kept by its static key, so another slot's Get, or a pattern naming another key, holds nothing of the source; a computed key, an escaped or non-canonical numeric key (`0x1`, `1.0`), a slot past a spread and a rest element hold every slot. `{ __proto__: v }` installs `v` as the prototype, so it is open; a shorthand `{ __proto__ }` is an own slot. `{ ...holder }` in an object literal keeps the slots, one member step away. A walk crosses up to 8 returns, props and contexts and 512 positions; past either it is open (`value-forwarded`, `value-use-unclassified`). A walked call is followed when a row or an obligation stands at its callee or at the call itself (not one inside or around it), or when its role clears what the walked value does there: an accessor's read, a setter's or action's write, both for a tuple or root. A function's callers are followed only when every call reference of it is a site the call graph resolves to it, and an exported function's only when the program is closed and no namespace hands it out unnamed (ADR 0203's `entered_only_through_call_expressions`); else `value-to-unresolved-callee` or `value-forwarded`. An empty caller list proves nothing by itself. `mapArray`'s source goes where its result goes (the dialect's `callback_requires_return_invocation`). A census row at the span of upstream's `package-callee` row is dropped: one SC9012 per span.

W's adversarial review found five more `Followed` answers with no proof, each a program Chrome warns on: `export default` of a literal or creating call (`value-exported-unnamed`); a pattern inside a parameter hop (only the hop's own reference is exempt from a row); a pending accessor at a tracked position (a tracked role excuses only a source not in `async_sources`, or a created one whose computation ADR 0665 proves Sync); a store or props root's member past the walk's first step (a row on its chain, unless the alias is a source walked on its own, ADR 0463); a bare JSX child (an intrinsic parent inserts it, a component reads `props.children`, anything else is open). The holder, reference and row maps are ordered, so a walk's order is the source's.

Proved: nothing new is a violation. Now uncertifiable: rc.21 corpus 27,229 to 28,428 U, 377 V unchanged, 0 sites cleared against the pre-census base, +946 sites (app-game 491, donegeon-client 104, kui 60, viviana-ui-web 60, compass-ui 51, solid-groove 40, inferay 38, jandibat-web 20, skyjtx-website 16, ai-memory-ui 12, ten more under 10); W's fixes are 162 of them (donegeon-client 83, app-game 40, compass-ui 11, solid-groove 11, kui 6), and two rows moved from an alias's declaration to its use. An audit traced every census row the slot, hop and row steps removed from the fixtures (106, and 4 only moved a line): 23 went back to uncertifiable through the three guards above, 83 each end at a named proof (a row at the reached call, another slot, a pattern naming no slot, `Object.keys`, an inert or target position, a role, a parameter hop, a closed program's uncalled export). Most are hook results whose callers hand the object on; the blocker names the position. Open patterns 90 to 8 (7 with ADR 0820). Fuzz, 1,782 `use-*` programs: 772 certified false cleans on the old build, 0 on this one, 1,706 runtime-confirmed answers uncertifiable.

ADR 0683 withheld a method a project subclass replaces, unless the receiver is an array literal. A subclass, an alias of it, a shadowing name and a rebound `let` keep the veto. Fixture `plain-container-receiver`.

Owner's ruling on SC1001 in `flush(fn)` in a body only `dynamic()` mounts, which ADRs 0530 and 0542 left a violation. A real read there (`const x = s.a`) keeps its violation though Chrome stays quiet: a violation means wrong if it runs. A Get that only serves a write's path (`s.a` in `s.a.v = 7`, `s.items.push(1)`, `delete s.a.o`, `Object.assign(s.a, x)`) is uncertifiable, `write-path-read-unproven`, because the same write in the body is, so its path claims no more. `+=` reads its value, so it keeps the violation; a chain through a call is a read. `serves_write_path` asks SC2003's own tables by exact declaration; `via` carries the mark, and a row at a helper's call asks the Get it stands for. `allowed-unplaced-nested` answered silent for any function in an allowed region. It answers `Unknown` now, unless a primitive's own arm places the function, a scheduler runs it on a fresh stack, only a host keeps it in a project that dispatches nothing itself (ADR 0535), or it is a compute's returned closure the apply only `void`s; so `untrack(fn)` and `onSettled(fn)` stay silent, and an action's step keeps its proved SC5001. A cleanup keeps its silence only while nothing in the project can dispose an owner on a chosen stack: a `getOwner` or `createOwner`, a `createRoot` that takes `dispose`, a kept `render`, `hydrate` or `onCleanup` result. The test is project-wide and part of `Section::ProjectPrimitives`' value: the draft asked one file, and an unrelated `getOwner` answered for it (`b1`, `b4`). A cleanup whose handle may be called is `Unknown`.

Bounds W's ADRs 0750-0752 left: `dynamic_component_entry` past 32 climbs names no entry, `Unknown` (33 nested `flush(fn)` promised a warning Chrome does not give); `may_throw_not_ready` walks each function once; a forwarded callback is an effect-phase layer; a sync helper hands on its chain's flight; `derived_objects` stands for what it did not read. A write rooted at a plain parameter `handed_container` does not place walks back (depth 6, 64 sites): a named function's call sites; for a literal handed to a project function, its calls of the parameter; a non-project callee passes its arguments and receiver. An element is a link (ADR 0465): uncertifiable, `unproven-container-root`; only the root itself returned is a violation. Corpus +108 uncertifiable. Fixture `store-element-flow`. Owners: `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs` (`handed_container`).

`const [now] = useNow()` over `return [now] as const` had no target, so a tracked read took ADR 0775's obligation while `const { now }` was exact. A slot now follows the accessor as it follows a setter: proved rows for an async source, certified for a settled one, and a body read is a proved SC1001. ADR 0282's veto would have refused nearly every program: any call it cannot name holds it, and `props.onChange(value)` is one, as is dependency JavaScript. A local `const [a] = createSignal(0)` rests on the same protocol with no census, so a slot asks less: whether the program obtains what iteration lives on. `array_intrinsic_obtained` lists the spellings (`Array` as a value, `Array.prototype` with no plain member after it, a global object's `Array`, `__proto__`, `getPrototypeOf`, `.constructor`, `eval`, `Function`, a timer's string), per file as the base hazard `ArrayIntrinsicObtained`. **Premise** (owner, 2026-11-06, accepted as written; the final review attacks it): a program that obtains no array intrinsic cannot patch iteration, whatever it writes, and a script element or a frame is outside the model. ADR 0282's census does not stand in for it: a `Function` handed to a parameter and called with a string passes that census and patches iteration (fixture `tuple-slot-function-obtained`, Chrome warns). A callee that is a source in its own right (ADR 0222) is read there, so the slot adds no second row at that call. A setter passes an object to nothing, so `setNow(new Date())` leaves the full veto too. Setter and closure slots keep the full veto.

Such a read gets an open row, `async-provenance-unproven`. A resolved call of such an accessor keeps its obligation, and a tracked call that only holds one (`let m = useM(g)`, `box.m()`) is held and decided there. A computation's own read suspends, so the question moves to its reader. A proved strict read stands. A parameter every caller fills with a settled value is uncertifiable: the arguments are not followed yet.

## ADR 0803 — A primitive's array pattern is exact only while iteration is intact
`const [s, set] = createSignal(1)` took both slots as the primitive's own beside `Array.prototype[Symbol.iterator] = f`. Chrome is quiet there: the patched iterator handed back a no-op setter, and SC2001 was a false positive (`types/s04`). Now discovery records a primitive's array-pattern slots, and they are withdrawn when either side fires. (1) Visible: a write that can reach the protocol, by assignment, `delete`, the reflective mutators or `setPrototypeOf`: an iterator key (`Symbol.iterator`, `next`, `return`) on a target not proven separate, or an unknown key or `__proto__` on `Array.prototype` or `Object.getPrototypeOf([][Symbol.iterator]())` (resolved through ADR 0282's alias walk). An unresolved call alone is not evidence. (2) Hidden: ADR 0801's census, code from a string (`eval`, `Function`, a timer's string) or an intrinsic obtained as a value (`Array.prototype` handed on, `getPrototypeOf`, `.constructor`, `__proto__`, a global's member under an unknown key). Two uses are no hand-out: a `getPrototypeOf` result that is only compared or tested, also through a binding (`!(p = Object.getPrototypeOf(o)) || p === Object.prototype`), and `Array.prototype` as the target of a discarded `Object.assign`/`defineProperty`/`defineProperties` or a `Reflect.set`/`defineProperty`/`deleteProperty`, which (1) decides. The first landing asked ADR 0349's spelling and ADR 0282's census together; it withdrew on test-harness prototype writes that cannot reach iteration (`EventTarget.prototype.dispatchEvent =`) and missed a Function run with a string (patterns s02, s03: SC2001 where Chrome is quiet). A withdrawn slot stays in the source tables, so transport, the handed net and the census still reach every use; what rests on it is held instead (`hold_withdrawn_slot_rows`): a read keeps its row, uncertain (`withdrawn_slot`), and a write or an async read becomes SC9012 uncertifiable, `tuple-iteration-written`, at its own place. A prop a caller passes from a withdrawn slot proves nothing either way (props reactivity: unknown, no exact source, no accessor value), and a compute that calls one stays open (ADR 0665). Taking the slots out of the tables instead (the first landing) left 35 corpus uses with no row: an IIFE's tuple, a `let`, a `const` alias, a hook's object, an object or class holder, a prop. Never a silent clean. ADR 0282 alone refuses every call it cannot name (96 more corpus V→U); full fail-closed on ADR 0801's spellings without the two exemptions cost 78 more V→U, mostly test files and read-only comparisons in dist code. A withdrawn slot's references and the calls transport carries it to (a hook's object, a destructure, an import) are each held. Corpus: 47 V→U, 0 silent, the same as (1) alone. Review of a9fcbe8f1: `Object.getPrototypeOf([])`, `[].__proto__` and `[].constructor.prototype` are exactly `Array.prototype` to the visible side, and `array_iteration_intact` (after_await, pre_await) is false whenever slots are withdrawn; a static row (upstream_compat's uncalled accessor, a store write) whose span holds a withdrawn reference is held too (`hold_withdrawn_static_rows`); the global object as a value is obtained when read through to `Array`, `Function` or `eval` (`const g = globalThis; g.Array`) or exported, and a destructuring of the global object, or by key `getPrototypeOf`/`constructor`/`__proto__`/`Array`/`Function`/`eval`, counts. Step 2: a call that receives the global object, `Function` or an intrinsic is recorded per file (`IntrinsicHandedAtCall`) and decided over the project (`handed_to_unseen_code`): it counts unless the callee is seen code that neither calls nor keeps it: a standard-library declaration on a closed list (`SEEN_STANDARD`, the read-only reflection and tests, index 0 only but `Object.is`), `call`/`bind` followed to their target with the arguments shifted, or a project or analyzed-dependency function whose parameter, followed three levels with the same rules, hands nothing on. The dialect's runtime is no exemption: a primitive hands back what it keeps (`createSignal(Array.prototype)`, review D1). The global object or an intrinsic stored or returned counts (reviews R2, R7). Reviews D3, R12: a method called on the window runs with it as `this`, recorded at the call; seen only on a closed list (`THIS_SEEN`: `getComputedStyle`, `matchMedia`, the scrolls, `postMessage`, `removeEventListener`) or as `addEventListener` with a listener that can reach neither its `this` nor its event (every parameter a name nothing reads; a non-arrow mentions no `this`, `super` or `arguments`; nothing in it runs a string). An `on*` handler written on the window, by member or bare, passes the same test or is `null`; a timer's function callback counts unless it is an arrow or never mentions `this`. `top`, `parent`, `frames` and `opener` name the window, and a value Type Facts types `Window` (`defaultView`, `view`, `contentWindow`, `source`, `open()`, a destructured key, demanded at those spans) is the global object. An optional-chain `.bind` with no declaration is unseen: error-menu-web and two repro projects withdraw (open-work, `held` cases). **Premise**, as ADR 0335's: a program that neither writes visibly toward iteration nor obtains an intrinsic or code from a string in an analyzed file leaves iteration intact. Outside the model: a script element, a frame, and code not analyzed (a dependency the project does not import from source). Owners: `indexes/intrinsics.rs` (`array_slots_withdrawn`), `accessor_provenance/mutation_sites.rs` (`iteration_intrinsic_target`), `reactive_analysis.rs` (`hold_withdrawn_slot_rows`), `source_discovery/props_reactivity.rs`; fixture `iteration-written-slots` and variants; flip-read.mjs `iter-*` and `wd-*`.

Step 2, review R19 and D8. Seen code is the called value, not the declaration a resolution names: `project_callee_at_parameter` with its exact answer (ADRs 0221, 0278, 0279), or `function_value_is_current` on a name for a listener, a timer's callback and a `call`/`bind` target. A value a call made, a written binding, a written or overridden method, and a conditional are unseen; `targets` proves nothing there, since `c ? noop : patch` with equal signatures reduces to one and has no target set. Every list needs the member unwritten: by name on any receiver, and by `patch_veto`, which now counts a bare global write (`scrollTo = f`). `property_may_be_defined_reflectively_as` is not asked: any open-key `defineProperty` answers yes for every name, and a definer on a standard root is a patch already. `patch_veto` of a window member also asks a global write under an open key. `Object.keys(x)` is recorded, not dropped, in its own slot: the census has no project to ask, and a key argument is an ordinary one, since it is coerced through program code (`Object.hasOwn({}, window)`). A platform caller as a value (`const st = setTimeout`, `window.setTimeout`, `[f].forEach(setTimeout)`), and `Object` or `Reflect` as a value, go on as `Function`. `window.setTimeout(f)` takes a timer's test. `composedPath()` holds the window: its result is `Obtained::Path`, quiet only for `includes`, `indexOf`, `lastIndexOf`, `length` and a `some` whose arrow only compares its parameter; R19's plain `some` hands the window to the callback. `only_compared` no longer answers for an operand that goes on past the span (`x === window.Function(code)()`). All of these are narrowings: now uncertifiable, not proved. The window's `this` list grows, proved: `clearTimeout`, `clearInterval`, `cancelAnimationFrame`, `cancelIdleCallback`, `alert`, `confirm`, `prompt` call nothing, and `dispatchEvent` runs listeners each decided where added, for an event nothing else can read (it keeps `target`; review D21); a span in a type query is no value. A method of the global object no declaration resolves (`(window as any).__log?.(x)`) is absent when its literal key starts with `__`, is none of the five `__` members `Object.prototype` defines, and nothing analyzed can install it. Premise, stated: a browser defines no other `__` member on the window; enumerated on Chromium 148's window prototype chain (1,002 keys), Firefox and WebKit not checked: no write of the name, none on `Object.prototype` or a window prototype, no reflective definition, no declaration in a classic script: seen (review D24). Any other any-cast method stays unseen: `valueOf` returns the window, `__defineGetter__` installs a global, `requestAnimationFrame` skips the timer's test. Review D11: a timer's callback is seen when it is a parameter of a `new Promise` executor (the sleep idiom): the engine's settling functions ignore `this`. Exactly the callback and a delay, the reference that parameter, unwritten, an executor that reads no `arguments` and has no name (review R35), the global `Promise` unwritten and standard.

Step 2, the follow-up that recovers proofs. A listener or timer callback is read at the call that hands the window on, not over every caller: another call with another target hands no window, so an exported helper needs no closed world. Followed through a parameter by what that call gave it (plain name, unwritten, no default, no `arguments`), a `const` by what its initializing call returns on every path, an arrow's expression body included (review R51), a `let` whose every write is a plain literal, and a function entered only by direct calls in its file, never exported and with no name of its own (review R52). A listener may read its event through a closed list of input facts; `data` and `detail` are on it because `handed_on` counts the window stored in any object literal, an event's init among them, and only the three flag methods may be called, since a called value runs with the event as `this` (review R53); a tagged template is a call. A value of kind Global uses its key against the closed lists whatever declaration resolves, `@types/node`'s or ADR 0840's impostor veto of it, because the receiver is the window. Also `(window.requestIdleCallback || setTimeout)(f)`, each arm by itself, and `path.find((el) => el instanceof Node && ...)` while `Node` and `instanceof` are intact.

## ADR 0781 — A lying cast or a patched global is not the standard method
`resolved_callee_call` answered from the written type, so `(t as unknown as Promise<void>).then(fn)` certified with a thenable that runs `fn` on the spot, `window.setTimeout = f` certified, and `(t as unknown as number[]).forEach(cb)` with a deferring `t` was a violation Chrome contradicts. Type text decides nothing (ADR 0367), so `library_types` now carries `typefacts::LIBRARY_TYPE_OTHER` beside a library name when another value constituent stands next to it. A write to a global, a prototype or a constructor member, by assignment, alias, `defineProperty`, `Reflect.set` or `Object.assign`, raises `Hazard::StandardPatch`, keyed by owner and member; a computed key is read from the types (a symbol replaces nothing). This replaces ADR 0683's premise, which also held for `Object.prototype`. Limits, both in docs/open-work.md: an unknown key on a global vetoes functions and constructors, not instance methods; an assertion away from the receiver (a parameter, a field, a return) raises nothing, three held patterns. Fixtures `lying-assertion`, `standard-patches`.

<a id="adr-0782"></a>
## ADR 0782 — A component a cleanup mounts writes inside that disposal
rc.21 skips the write guard while `disposalDepth` is above zero, so `onCleanup(() => { createRoot(() => <Inner />); })` with a write in `Inner` was an SC2001 violation Chrome contradicts (`e703`, `g12`). The patterns report placed the fix at `write_roles.rs:799`, which a component body never reaches: `walk-direct-1` decided first. A write the walk calls rendering now asks where its component mounts: all sites in a disposal, with nothing else holding the function, is proved legal; any site outside one keeps the violation; the rest is uncertifiable. `DisposalReach::Only` keeps its meaning, every run inside a disposal, so the bounds work reads it unchanged. A handler literal that renders is no possible component. Fixture `component-mounted-in-disposal`.

<a id="adr-0810"></a>
## ADR 0810 — Own keys hold no value
`Object.keys` and `Object.getOwnPropertyNames` of a store, a props root or a holder run its `ownKeys` and, for `keys`, an untracked descriptor read, and answer only names: the census follows them (`runtime_semantics::reads_keys_only`, by exact standard-library declaration). Measured in Chrome (rc.21): both quiet in a component body; `Object.entries` and `Object.values` warn `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` and stay SC1001 violations. Under an observer `keys` tracks the key set, which is a tracked read. An accessor handed to `Object.keys` keeps SC1005's coercion obligation. Fixture `value-use-census` (`Followed`, `InNamed`).

<a id="adr-0820"></a>
## ADR 0820 — A generator a computation steps runs on its flight
`for await (const v of gen())` in an async compute with a read past the generator's first suspension: Chrome warns, certified (`f02`, P9). The operand sits before its own suspension, so no region reached it, and helpers stopped at a generator. A member now follows every generator it calls, on `for await` as an `async` helper: past an `await` the read is proved, past a `yield` it is an obligation, blocker `generator-stepped`. Any other stepping (a wrapper, `next()`, `yield*`, an inline generator) and a sync generator stepped across an await are obligations too. A generator's straight-line reads are a window only until its first `yield`. Stay clean: the first segment under `for await`, a sync generator a loop drains before any suspension. Measured, kept: in `createMemo(async function* …)` the runtime watches no flight (`watchAsyncTail` takes promises only), so a read after an `await` gives no diagnostic, and a later write does not rerun the compute; SC1002 V there states a true claim.

<a id="adr-0821"></a>
## ADR 0821 — An exported const copy holds its source
`export const read = c`, `export const api = st`, used in another module: certified, Chrome warns (`d8-pkg-types-lie-plain`; project twins). `LocalAliasBindingFact` leaves out an exported local, because its reference census assumes one file, and the accessor copy asked for that fact. The alias row asks what the binding holds: a `const` of one name whose initializer is a `const` variable holds it, exported or not. Proved: reads and store writes through the copy. Corpus: ai-memory-ui `export const useLocale = localeSignal`, +343 U (SC5003 266, SC5001 65, SC1001 12), the source's own open provenance; 0 V moved.

<a id="adr-0822"></a>
## ADR 0822 — What a package's source does not close is open
A Get on a package's value was checked against literal shapes only, so an accessor installed at run time, a class getter, a `Proxy` or `Object.create` was certified where Chrome warns (`d8-pkg-getter-export`). The walk now records where it ends inside a selected package: `dependency-value-unfollowed`, SC9012 U, in a pass of its own (`interproc/dependency_values.rs`). Clean: a closed literal, a value descriptor, a key that is not the member, a class with no `extends` and no getter of that name, a standard container. A package that installs a function that may read a source on a global is `dependency-global-read` at its import, whether or not it is called. A side-effect import now selects its package (`dependency_sources.rs`), assets excepted: its source is the only thing that says what it installs. Corpus: +18 SC9012 U (`useParams()` Gets, a window size), 0 V moved.

<a id="adr-0823"></a>
## ADR 0823 — A sealed holder's member tag is a call site; a leaf-owner prop read needs its callers
`<ui.C a={a()} />` resolved no function, so `C` escaped through its holder and its prop read in `createTrackedEffect` had no row: certified, Chrome warns PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE. Pass 10 now resolves a member tag of a same-file `const` holder whose value ADR 0342's census accounts (`compound_member_target`), and the holder's reference to `C` is a render when every other reference to the holder is such a tag of that file and, outside a closed program, the holder is not exported. Proved: `C`'s callers are those tags (`async-prop-getter`, SC1001 V, a literal clean). Now uncertifiable: a prop read in a leaf owner unless every caller is enumerated and none may pass a pending getter, `prop-pending-unproven`. Owners: `source_discovery/post_rows.rs`, `props_reactivity.rs` (`compound_render_reference`, `pending_excluded`), `local_access.rs`.

<a id="adr-0824"></a>
## ADR 0824 — A props destructure asks the pending question
`const { v } = props` in `createTrackedEffect`, or in an IIFE in the body, with a caller passing a pending getter: certified, Chrome warns PENDING_ASYNC_*. SC1003 skips tracked roles and body helpers, and nothing asked whether a slot may be pending. The destructure now asks ADR 0509's question for the slots it binds (`PropsPending`, a recorded discovery read): an async getter outside tracked JSX or in a leaf owner is `async-prop-getter`; an open caller set in a leaf owner is `prop-pending-unproven` (ADR 0823). The leaf-owner walk passes through an IIFE written around the read to the named function that holds it; any other anonymous function stops it, as before. Owners: `static_rules.rs` (`component_props_destructure`), `local_access.rs` (`may_run_in_leaf_owner`), `late_chunks.rs`.

<a id="adr-0825"></a>
## ADR 0825 — A primitive used as a value is named only when an import confirms it
SC9012 `primitive-value` took the primitive from the symbol the producer put at the reference, so 78 corpus rows said `createOwner` at values no source wrote it for (`css` from an absent generated module, `sharedConfig`, a project class). The row now asks ADR 0711's identity check (`primitive_identity_proven_at`): a named import of a dialect module whose imported name is the primitive, a namespace member of one, or ADR 0459's namespace slot. Otherwise the row stays an obligation, `primitive-identity-unproven`, named by the value's own text. Owners: `static_rules.rs` (`primitive_value_references`), `cleanup.rs`.

<a id="adr-0826"></a>
## ADR 0826 — An unresolved import names no primitive
ADR 0825's `createOwner` was traced: `symbol_names` named an alias root after any declaration in a Solid package, and `@solidjs/router`'s `import { createOwner } from "solid-js"` (and `sharedConfig`, `storePath`) misses rc.21's typings, so its root is the checker's one unknown symbol, the root every unresolved project import shares. Every unresolved import then read as `createOwner`. A root with no declaration is named by nothing, as `add_solid_import_names` already required; the incremental patch asks the same. Corpus: 0 V moved; 82 U rows gone (SC9012 78, SC3001 2, SC6001 2: `storePath`, `recordUiTimings`), 8 SC9012 U appear that one-row-per-start had hidden. No renamed alias lands on another primitive's name. Owner: `symbols.rs`.

<a id="adr-0827"></a>

After ADR 0826 an import the compiler did not resolve (a missing module, an export its target lacks, an import under `@ts-ignore`) certified wherever it was used: called in JSX or the body, handed to `createEffect`, used as a tag, written through. Some had been U only through the bogus `createOwner` name; most never were. Invariant 2: a missing symbol is never a clean. Every runtime reference of an import binding whose root has no declaration, or that has no entity, is now SC9012 uncertifiable `import-unresolved` (`static_rules.rs`, `unresolved_import_references`; the read is noted through `SemanticLookup::symbol_undeclared`). Type positions are erased and skipped. Corpus: +7,109 U in 11 projects (viviana-ui-web 5,423 at unbuilt workspace packages, ai-memory-ui 941 and probus-hk 388 at the ungenerated `~/paraglide/messages`, hoxxes-briefing 197 at `styled-system/*`), 0 V moved. Fixture `import-unresolved`.

W's early grid (research-w-grids-early.md, 775ee26d1): 76 certified false cleans, REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE in `pos-handler-root` and `pos-handler-owner`, layers `forward-2`/`forward-13`: a write in a closure handed down a chain of forwarding helpers inside `createRoot` or `runWithOwner(boundOwner, …)` inside an `onClick` handler. `write_region_adjustment` needs the write directly in the callback, and `layer_run` calls the forwarding helper `Elsewhere`, so the walk took the handler's lexical EventCallback role (`walk-direct-1`): silent. ADR 0471 already refused that role for a named closure in a root body; `handed_closure_in_owner_callback` refuses it for any closure handed to a call that is no primitive and no reviewed standard-library call, inside a callback that runs under a created root or an owner `runWithOwner` names (`Creates`, `Conditional`); the climb stops at a JSX attribute function or a fresh-stack layer. The site's entries then decide. Proved: the 38 root programs, V (the walk follows the forward chain). Now uncertifiable: the 38 owner programs. Legal forward twins (2,242): 0 false positives, unchanged. Corpus 0 moved. Owner: `write_roles.rs`.

P8 (ADR 0791): `delete st["a"]`, `st.rec[key]`, `st.rec.k`, `st.items[0]`, a required `T | undefined` and `unknown` were certified though tsc accepts them and the proxy warns STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER, because `writableOptionalProperty: false` could not tell them from a TS2790 operand, which must stay silent. Type Facts now also answers `deleteOperandRejected` (entity row bit 18, Wire table schema v25, protocol 90): the producer's mirror of `check_delete_expression` on the access's resolved symbol under the program's own options (TS18011, TS2704, TS2790 with or without `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`; `any`, `unknown`, `never` pass), demanded at static and computed operands. `no_store_delete` is silent on a proven rejection in a file tsc checks, keeps its proof for a writable optional operand, and makes every other operand uncertifiable, `delete-operand-unproven`. Proved: nothing new. The 13 producer shapes and both TS2790 ownership cases hold. Corpus +19 U (computed deletes through unproven roots), 0 V moved.

<a id="adr-0830"></a>
## ADR 0830 — A rejection nothing reports is no reason for silence
Coordinator's item after P8: several arms stay silent because `tsc` rejects the code (invariant 1), but under `// @ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error` or `@ts-nocheck` (or in JavaScript `tsc` may not check) nothing reports it, and Chrome warns. Probed (tsc-clean, real Chrome, checker): `delete store.v!` and `delete (store.v as T)` (TS2703; STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER), a one-argument `createEffect` (TS2554; MISSING_EFFECT_FN), `createEffect(c, 5)` (REACTIVITY_HALTED), an apply returning a number and `onSettled(() => 1)` (the retired SC3004 and SC4004 return claims; REACTIVITY_HALTED), `refresh(() => t())` and `affects(memo, "name")` (the retired SC7003/SC7004 claims; INVALID_REFRESH_TARGET, INVALID_AFFECTS_TARGET): all certified. AST facts (schema 91; the census took 90 on its branch) now list every TypeScript directive comment with its first and last line and whether tsc 5 and tsgo agree on it (`exact`: one line, the canonical spelling), the leading `@ts-check`/`@ts-nocheck` pragma, and any `@ts-nocheck` comment. `FileFacts::tsc_silence_at` walks upwards from a diagnostic's line as both compilers do (a directive line suppresses, a line neither blank nor `//` ends the walk): `Silent` only where both suppress, `Maybe` where one may (a two-line block directive, a whitespace class only tsc trims, unchecked JavaScript), else `Reports`. A violation needs `Silent` on the line of the diagnostic the arm defers to; an uncertifiable result may use `Maybe` anywhere in the span (the file-wide premise ADR 0829 already uses). SC2003's delete under an assertion is judged as the member it wraps where TS2703 is silent, and a TS2790/TS2704 rejection silent on its line makes the operand proven. SC7001 judges a rejected call as typed where TS2554 (at the callee) or TS2345 (at the apply argument, on one line) is silent. Every other rejected primitive call, and a cleanup-accepting primitive's callback returning a value that is no function literal on such a line, is SC9012 U `solid-call-rejected-unchecked`; no retired rule returns as a violation. A directive elsewhere in the file proves nothing, so the call stays TypeScript's and silent. Proved: the suppressed one-argument `createEffect` and the suppressed delete under an assertion (fuzz shapes: 180 false cleans on the build before, 180 agree now), `createEffect(c, 5)`, `@ts-nocheck`. Now uncertifiable: `refresh`/`affects` targets, an apply's or `onSettled`'s returned non-cleanup, a two-line block directive, JavaScript. Corpus 0 moved. Owners: `suppression.rs`, `FileFacts::tsc_silence_at`, `effect_api.rs`, `static_api.rs`, `no_store_delete`. Then, on the coordinator's word, the same U for SC1005's positions TypeScript closes and SC7007's arity arm (`uncalled_accessor`, `server_rules.rs`); `innerhtml` stays silent (the runtime writes it as an attribute), `no-implicit-draggable` is retired, and SC9012's invalid call is a plain TypeError. Upstream eslint-plugin-solid at 6d3bc311 (`reactivity.ts:480-540`) reports `badSignal` as an error at an uncalled signal in a template literal, a binary arithmetic or comparison operand (`in` included), a unary `-`/`+`/`~`, a computed key and a DOM element's non-handler JSX attribute, and has no class-object-value arm. The checker keeps those positions TypeScript closes silent where tsc reports them, and U (never V) where it may not; the arm only adds U, so no ownership pin.
